p p -adic L L -functions for U ⁡ ( 2 , 1 ) × U ⁡ ( 1 , 1 ) \mathrm{U}(2,1)\times\mathrm{U}(1,1)
Michael Harris, Ming-Lun Hsieh, Shunsuke Yamana

Abstract
. We construct the five-variable p p -adic L L -function attached to Hida families on U ⁡ ( 2 , 1 ) × U ⁡ ( 1 , 1 ) \mathrm{U}(2,1)\times\mathrm{U}(1,1) , interpolating the square-root of Rankin-Selberg L L -values in the shifted piano range. Our construction relies on a new theta operator and its p p -adic variation which plays a role analogous to the classical Ramanujan-Serre theta operator in Hida’s p p -adic Rankin-Selberg method. The interpolation formula, including the modified Euler factors at p p and at the real place, is consistent with the conjectural shape of p p -adic L L -functions predicted by Coates and Perrin-Riou.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
11F67, 11F33

1.
Introduction

In the pioneering paper
[
BDP13
]
, Berolini, Darmon and Prasanna established a formula between the
p
-adic Abel-Jacobi image of generalizaed Heegner cycles and the values outside the
p
-adic interpolation of some anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-function.
The aim of this project is to carry out the first step toward a generalization of this work to the diagonal cycles of unitary groups and
p
-adic
L
-functions (cf.
[
RSZ20
]
). Using the
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg method, we construct a five-variable
p
-adic
L
-function interpolating a square root of the algebraic part of central values of the
L
-series attached to a pair of Hida families on the quasi-split unitary groups
\mathrm{U}(2,1)
and
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
in the shifted piano position.
In contrast, the
p
-adic
L
-function in the piano position, in the sense of
[
GHL
, Definition 5.6]
, was constructed in
[
HY25
]
.

Moreover, we obtain the explicit interpolation formulae, which is consistent with the conjectural framework described in
[
CPR89
,
Coa89b
,
Coa89a
]
.
A crucial novelty of our construction is the introduction of a new theta operator and its connection with complex differential operators.

Let
E
be an imaginary quadratic field.
Throughout this paper we fix a prime number
p>3
which
splits
in
E
and an isomorphism
\iota_{p}:{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
, where
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
is a fixed algebraic closure of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
We write
{\mathfrak{p}}
for the prime ideal induced by the restriction of
\iota_{p}
to
E
.
We denote the rings of adèles of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and
E
) by
{\mathbb{A}}
and
{\mathbb{E}}
, and the rings of finite adèles by
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}
and
\widehat{E}
, respectively.

1.1.
Galois representations associated to Hida families

Let
x\mapsto x^{c}
be the non-trivial automorphism of
E
.
Fix a Hermitian matrix
T
in
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(E)
of signature
(r,s)
.
For
g\in\mathrm{M}_{n}(E)
we define
g^{\ddagger}:=T^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!g^{c}T
.
The unitary group
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
associated with
T
is the algebraic group defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
by setting

\mathrm{U}(r,s)(R)=\{g\in\mathrm{M}_{n}(E\otimes_{{\mathbb{Q}}}R)\mid g^{\ddagger}g=1\}

for any
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra
R
.

Fix a finite extension
F
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and denote its maximal compact subring by
{O}
.
For each positive integer
n
, let
T_{n}\subset\mathrm{GL}_{n}
be the diagonal torus.
Let

\Lambda_{n}:={O}\llbracket{T_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\rrbracket=\varprojlim_{m\geq 1}{O}\left[T_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{m}{\mathbb{Z}})\right]

be the completed group algebra, and
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
a semi-local and normal
\Lambda_{n}
-algebra finite and flat over
\Lambda_{n}
.
We say that an
{O}
-algebra homomorphism
\underline{Q}:{\mathbf{I}}_{n}\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
is locally algebraic if its restriction to
T_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is of the form
\underline{Q}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}^{k_{Q_{i}}}\epsilon_{Q_{i}}(z_{i})
with
(k_{Q_{1}},\dots,k_{Q_{n}})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}
and characters
\epsilon_{Q_{i}}:{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}^{\times}
of finite order.
We call
k_{\underline{Q}}=(k_{Q_{1}},\dots,k_{Q_{n}})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}
the weight of
\underline{Q}
and
\epsilon_{\underline{Q}}=(\epsilon_{Q_{1}},\dots,\epsilon_{Q_{n}})
the finite part of
\underline{Q}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}
be the set of locally algebraic points in
\mathrm{Spec}{\mathbf{I}}_{n}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p})
.
We say that
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}
is dominant if
k_{Q_{1}}\leq k_{Q_{2}}\leq\dots\leq k_{Q_{n}}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}^{+}
be the subset of locally algebraic points of dominant weights.
Put

{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}^{\mathrm{cls}}:=\{\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}^{+}\mid k_{Q_{n}}-k_{Q_{r}}>r\}.

Let
N
be a positive integer only divisible by primes
q\neq p
split in
E
.
Choose an ideal
{\mathfrak{N}}
of the ring
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
of integers of
E
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N{\mathbb{Z}}
.
This ideal
{\mathfrak{N}}
shall be referred to as the tame level.
Hida theory produces the
\Lambda_{n}
-module
{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{\mathrm{U}(r,s)}({\mathfrak{N}},\Lambda_{n})
of ordinary
\Lambda_{n}
-adic cusp forms, which is free of finite rank equipped with a faithful action of the universal ordinary Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathrm{U}(r,s)}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N,\Lambda_{n})
for the unitary group
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
.
Roughly speaking, ordinary
\Lambda_{n}
-adic cusp forms are
p
-adic families of
p
-ordinary cusp forms on
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
invariant by the mirabolic subgroup of level
{\mathfrak{N}}
.
An
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
-adic Hida family
\boldsymbol{f}
on
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
is a non-zero Hecke eigenform in

{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{\mathrm{U}(r,s)}({\mathfrak{N}},{\mathbf{I}}_{n}):={\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{\mathrm{U}(r,s)}({\mathfrak{N}},\Lambda_{n})\otimes_{\Lambda_{n}}{\mathbf{I}}_{n},

which induces a
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
-algebra homomorphism
\lambda_{\boldsymbol{f}}:{\mathbf{T}}^{\mathrm{U}(r,s)}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N,{\mathbf{I}}_{n})\to{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
.

Denote the absolute Galois group of a field
L
by
\Gamma_{L}
and its cyclotomic character by
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{m}}
be a maximal ideal of
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
.
To each
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
-adic Hida family
\boldsymbol{f}
, one can associate the residual semisimple Galois representation
\bar{\rho}_{\boldsymbol{f}}:\Gamma_{E}\to\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbf{I}}_{n}/{\mathfrak{m}})
.
If
\bar{\rho}_{\boldsymbol{f}}
is absolutely irreducible, then we can further obtain the Galois representation
\rho_{\boldsymbol{f}}:\Gamma_{E}\to\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbf{I}}_{n})
unramified outside primes dividing
Np
and primes
l
where
\mathrm{U}(r,s)({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
is ramified (see
[
Ger19
, Propositions 2.28, 2.29]
for more details).
Denote by
V_{\boldsymbol{f}}
the free
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
-module of rank
n
on which
\Gamma_{E}
acts via
\rho_{\boldsymbol{f}}
.

For each
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}
, the specialization
V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}:=V_{\boldsymbol{f}}\otimes_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n},\underline{Q}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
is the geometric
p
-adic Galois representation associated with some automorphic representation
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q},v}
of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)({\mathbb{A}})
.
Let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\mathrm{temp}}
be the set of points
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}^{\mathrm{cls}}
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}
is everywhere tempered.
The representation
V_{\boldsymbol{f}}
is
conjugate self-dual
in the sense that

V_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\vee}\simeq V_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{c}\otimes\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}^{n-1}.

Moreover, by the local description of
p
-adic Galois representations
[
Ger19
, Corollary 2.33]
at
p
combined with
[
TU99
, Lemma 7.2]
, there exists a filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}})\}_{i=1}^{n}
of
\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-stable lattices

\{0\}=\mathrm{Fil}_{0}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}})\subset\mathrm{Fil}_{1}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}})\subset\dots\subset\mathrm{Fil}_{n}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}})=V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}}

such that for every
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}
, the specialization
V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
is Hodge-Tate and each graded piece

\mathrm{gr}_{i}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}}):=\mathrm{Fil}_{i}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}})/\mathrm{Fil}_{i-1}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}})

has Hodge-Tate weights

\displaystyle-k_{Q_{i}}-s-i+1

\displaystyle\;\;(1\leq i\leq r),

\displaystyle-k_{Q_{i}}+r-i+1

\displaystyle\;\;(r+1\leq i\leq n).

Here the Hodge-Tate weight of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}(1)
is 1 in our convention.
Likewise there exists a filtration
\{\mathrm{Fil}_{i}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}})\}_{i=1}^{n}
of
\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}
-stable lattices in
V_{\boldsymbol{f}}
such that such that for every
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{+}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{n}}
, each graded piece

\mathrm{gr}_{i}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}})=\mathrm{Fil}_{i}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}})/\mathrm{Fil}_{i-1}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}})

is Hodge-Tate of weight

\displaystyle k_{Q_{n-i+1}}-r-i+1

\displaystyle\;\;(1\leq i\leq s),

\displaystyle k_{Q_{n-i+1}}+s+1-i

\displaystyle\;\;(s+1\leq i\leq n).

1.2.
The
L
-series for
\mathrm{U}(2,1)\times\mathrm{U}(1,1)

Define the skew Hermitian matrices
S
and
S^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&1\\
0&\sqrt{-D_{E}}&0\\
-1&0&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle S^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix},

where
D_{E}
is the absolute value of the discriminant of
E
.

Let
G=\mathrm{U}(S)
and
H=\mathrm{U}(S^{\prime})
be the quasi-split unitary groups attached to
T
and
T^{\prime}
.
Let
{G}=\mathrm{GU}(S)
and
{H}=\mathrm{GU}(S^{\prime})
be the groups of unitary similitudes attached to
T
and
T^{\prime}
.
Fix prime-to-
p
natural numbers
N
and
N^{\prime}
that satisfy the following condition:

(splt)

all the prime factors of
NN^{\prime}
are split in
E
.

Fix a decomposition
N^{\prime}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}={\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime c}
such that
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}+{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime c}={\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.

Though it may be more natural to work with Shimura varieties attached to the unitary groups, which are not of PEL type (cf.
[
Har21b
,
RSZ20
]
), we start with Hida families

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbf{I}}_{3}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbf{I}}_{2})

on the unitary similitude groups
{G}
and
{H}
, and restrict them to unitary groups
G
and
H
.
We further assume that the residual Galois representations
\bar{\rho}_{\boldsymbol{f}}
and
\bar{\rho}_{\boldsymbol{g}}
are both absolutely irreducible. Let
V_{\boldsymbol{f}}
and
V_{\boldsymbol{g}}
be the Galois representations of
\Gamma_{E}
associated with the Hida families
\boldsymbol{f}
and
\boldsymbol{g}
respectively.
Consider the tensor product representation
V_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}:=V_{\boldsymbol{f}}\otimes V_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\vee}
of rank six over the five variable Iwasawa algebra
{\mathbf{I}}_{3}\widehat{\otimes}_{{O}}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}
, where
V_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\vee}
is the dual representation of
V_{\boldsymbol{g}}
.

Define the induced representation
{\mathbf{V}}
of
\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}
by

{\mathbf{V}}:=\operatorname{Ind}_{\Gamma_{E}}^{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}}(V_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}).

For each prime number
q
we denote the Weil-Deligne group of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
by
W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}
.
For each
{Q}=(Q,Q^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{3}}^{+}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{2}}^{+}
, let
{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}
be the specialization of
{\mathbf{V}}
at
{Q}
and define the complex
L
-series of the
p
-adic Galois representation
{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}
by the Euler product

L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},s)=\prod_{q}L_{q}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},s)

of the local
L
-factors attached to
\mathrm{WD}_{q}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q})\otimes_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p},\iota_{p}^{-1}}{\mathbb{C}}
, where
\mathrm{WD}_{q}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q})
is the Weil-Deligne representation of
W_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
associated to
{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}
.
Let

\displaystyle(\lambda_{Q_{1}},\lambda_{Q_{2}},\lambda_{Q_{3}})

\displaystyle=(-k_{Q_{1}},-k_{Q_{2}}-1;-k_{Q_{3}}+1);

\displaystyle(\mu_{Q_{1}^{\prime}},\mu_{Q_{2}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\biggl(-k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2};-k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\biggl),

be the Harish-Chandra parameters of
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\vee}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
respectively. Define the archimedean
L
-factor of
{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}
by

\Gamma({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},s)=\prod_{i=1,2,3}\prod_{j=1,2}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}\biggl(s+\frac{1}{2}+|\lambda_{Q_{i}}-\mu_{Q_{j}^{\prime}}|\biggl),

where
\Gamma_{{\mathbb{C}}}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)
.
We are interested in the algebraic part of the value of
L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},s)
at
s=0
.
Note that
L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},0)
are central values as
{\mathbf{V}}^{\vee}\otimes\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}\simeq{\mathbf{V}}
is self-dual.
With the assumption (
splt
), the specializations of the Hecke eigensystems
\underline{Q}\circ\lambda_{\boldsymbol{f}}
and
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\circ\lambda_{\boldsymbol{g}}
determine unique unitary automorphic representations
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
H({\mathbb{A}})
, and we have

\Gamma({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},s)L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},s)=L\biggl(s+\frac{1}{2},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}\times\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}\biggl).

The right hand side is the
complete
automorphic
L
-function defined by

L(s,\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}\times\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee})=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\mathrm{BC}(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}})\times\mathrm{BC}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee})),

where
\mathrm{BC}(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}})
(resp.
\mathrm{BC}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee})
) is the functorial lift of
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}
(resp.
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}
) to an automorphic representation of
\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{GL}_{3}({\mathbb{A}})
(resp.
\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
) (cf.
[
Lab11
, Corollary 5.3]
).
Here
\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{GL}_{n}
denotes the general linear group over an imaginary quadratic field
E
, regarded as an algebraic group over
{\mathbb{Q}}
by restricting scalars.
The
L
-function in the right hand side has been defined by the Rankin-Selberg convolution whose local and global analytic theories were established by Jacquet, Piatetski-Shapiro and Shalika in
[
JPSS83
]
.

The set of critical points in the shifted piano range is defined by

{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}=\{(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\mathrm{temp}}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{2}}^{\mathrm{cls}}\mid k_{Q_{1}}\leq k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\leq k_{Q_{2}},\;k_{Q_{3}}\leq k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\}.

1.3.
The algebraicity of central values

Fix
(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
.
Put

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}},

\displaystyle\underline{k}

\displaystyle=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3}):=k_{\underline{Q}},

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\underline{k}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime}):=k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}.

Let
L(s,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})
and
L(s,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})
be the
complete
adjoint
L
-functions of
\sigma
and
\pi
, respectively.
These are the Asai and twisted Asai
L
-functions of
\mathrm{BC}(\sigma)
and
\mathrm{BC}(\pi)
(cf. Remark
7.3
).
With the assumption (
splt
) we denote the conductor of
\pi
by
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}
, and the conductor of
\sigma
by
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}
(cf. §
8.2
).

Let
f^{\circ}\in S^{G}_{\underline{k}}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
be a vector-valued
holomorphic

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational newform associated to
\pi
(see Definition
5.18
).
A perfect bilinear pairing
(\,,\,)_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}}
on the space of modular forms is introduced in §
7.11
.
We write
\Omega_{\infty}
for the complex period of a CM elliptic curve introduced in
[
HT93
, (4.4 b)]
(see §
6.7
and (
6.3
)).
For
a,b\in{\mathbb{C}}
we write
a\sim b
if
b\neq 0
and
a/b\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
We will prove the following theorem in in Section
7.6
.

Theorem 1.1
.

Notation being as above, we have

L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)\sim\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{\pi}\biggl)^{2(k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})}\frac{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})}{(f^{\circ},f^{\circ})_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}}}L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})^{2}.

In Appendix
D
we will see that this theorem is compatible with Theorem 5.19 of
[
GHL
]
.

1.4.
The periods

Let
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}:{\mathbb{A}}/{\mathbb{Q}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be the additive character with the archimedean component
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}(z)=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}z}
and
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
the local component of
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
at the prime number
p
.
Define the additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{E}
of
{\mathbb{E}}/E
by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{E}(x)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(x/\sqrt{-D_{E}}))
.

To make our interpolation formula more precise, we explain the periods for critical
L
-values associated with the Galois representation
{\mathbf{V}}_{{Q}}
.
Let
g^{\circ}\in S^{H}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma},\chi^{\prime},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
be the normalized newform associated to
\sigma
.
Put

\Omega(\sigma):=D_{E}^{-1}2^{2}(-2\sqrt{-1})^{k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}\|g^{\circ}\|_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}}^{2}{E}(\sigma_{p},\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}),

where
\|\cdot\|_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}}
denotes the normalized Petersson norm and
{E}(\sigma_{p},\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})
is the modified Euler
p
-factor for the adjoint motive attached to
\sigma
, defined in Definition
8.7
.
The period
\Omega(\sigma)
is analogous to Hida’s canonical period considered in
[
Hsi21
, Definition 3.12]
.
Corollary
C.2
shows that

L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})\sim\Omega(\sigma).

Let
f^{\circ}
be the Picard newform associated with
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
in the sense of Definition
4.8
.
In addition to
\Omega(\sigma)
we introduce a non-zero complex number
\Xi_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}
in §
7.11
.
Roughly speaking, the period
\Xi_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}
is given by the ratio

\Xi_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}\sim\frac{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})}{(f^{\circ},f^{\circ})_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}}}.

Let
f^{\rm gen}
be the normalized vector-valued
generic
newform in the global
L
-packet of
\pi
with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{E}
.
In
[
SY25
, Theorem 4.1]
, Shih-Yu Chen shows that the Petersson norm
\langle f^{\rm gen},f^{\rm gen}\rangle_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}}
of
f^{\rm gen}
is equal to the adjoint
L
-value
L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})
, using the Lapid-Mao conjecture.
Therefore,
\Xi_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}
is essentially the ratio
\frac{\langle f^{\rm gen},f^{\rm gen}\rangle_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}}}{(f^{\circ},f^{\circ})_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}}}
between the Petersson norms of normalized generic forms and holomorphic forms in the
L
-packet of
\pi
. The form
f^{\rm gen}
is normalized so that the valued of the
\psi^{E}
-Whittaker function of highest weight component of
f^{\rm gen}
at
\mathrm{diag}({\sqrt{-D_{E}},1,-\sqrt{-D_{E}}^{-1}})
is given by

K_{k_{2}-k_{3}+2}(4\sqrt{2}\pi),

where
K_{s}(z)
is the modified
K
-Bessel function.

1.5.
The modified Euler factors

For
{Q}=(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
we view the algebraic number

\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2(k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}+k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}})-2(k_{Q_{1}}+k_{Q_{2}}+k_{Q_{3})}}\frac{\Gamma({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},0)L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},0)}{\Xi_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}\Omega(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})^{2}}

through the embedding
\iota_{p}
, as a
p
-adic number.
We are interested in the
p
-adic behavior of this ratio when
{Q}
varies in
{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
. A general conjecture on
p
-adic
L
-functions predicts that, after inserting a suitably modified Euler factor at
p
(recalled below), this ratio varies
p
-adic analytically.

We consider the rank three
\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-invariant and
\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}
-invariant subspaces of
V_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}_{1}V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes V_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\vee}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}}+V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\otimes\mathrm{Fil}_{1}V_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\vee}|_{\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}};

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Fil}_{2}V_{\boldsymbol{f}}|_{\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}}\otimes\mathrm{Fil}_{1}V_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\vee}|_{\Gamma_{E_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}}},

and define the six dimensional
\Gamma_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
-invariant subspace of
{\mathbf{V}}
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}_{\mathfrak{p}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}\oplus\mathrm{Fil}^{+}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}{\mathbf{V}}_{\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{g}}\right)\otimes\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}.

The pair
(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}},{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}})
satisfies the Panchishkin condition in
[
Gre94
, p. 217]
in the sense that all the Hodge-Tate numbers of
\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}
are positive but none of the Hodge-Tate numbers of
{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}/\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}
is positive for
{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
.

For each
p
-adic representation
V
of
\Gamma_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
, recall that the
\gamma
-factor
\gamma(V)
is defined by

\gamma(V)=\varepsilon({\rm WD}_{p}(V),{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})\frac{L_{p}(V^{\vee},1)}{L_{p}(V,0)},

where
\varepsilon({\rm WD}_{p}(V),{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})
is the local constant defined in
[
Del73
, §4]
.
Following the recipe of Coates and Perrin-Riou, we define the modified factor at
p
by

{E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q})=\frac{1}{\gamma(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q})L_{p}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},0)}.

In the theory of
p
-adic
L
-functions we also need the modified factor
{E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q})
at the archimedean place as observed by Deligne.
It is explicitly given by

{E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q})=(-\sqrt{-1})^{k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}+k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{Q_{1}}-k_{Q_{2}}-k_{Q_{3}}}.

1.6.
Five-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions

Given a ring
R
, we write
\mathrm{Frac}(R)
for the total ring of fractions of
R
.
Let
\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the
p
-adic CM period attached to an elliptic curve with complex multiplication by
E
(cf. §
6.7
and
[
HT93
, (4.4a)]
).
Put
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}=\{\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm cls}_{R}\;|\;\underline{Q}(\boldsymbol{f})\neq 0\}
.
The subset
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime\prime}
consists of
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}
such that
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
is new outside
p
.
For a technical reason we consider its subset

{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}=\{(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}\mid k_{Q_{1}}=k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\}.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
M
be the conductor of
\chi^{\prime}
.
We assume (
splt
) and assume that

(
H_{3}
)

M^{2}\text{ is divisible by }N^{\prime},

Then there is a unique element
{\mathscr{L}}_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}\in{\mathbf{I}}_{3}\widehat{\otimes}_{O}\mathrm{Frac}({\mathbf{I}}_{2})
such that

\frac{{Q}({\mathscr{L}}_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}^{2})}{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2m_{{Q}}}}=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2m_{{Q}}}\frac{\Gamma({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},0)L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q},0)}{\Xi_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}}\Omega(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})^{2}}{E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}){E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q})

for
{Q}=(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}\cap({\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime\prime}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{2}}^{\mathrm{cls}})
,
where

m_{Q}=k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}+k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-(k_{Q_{1}}+k_{Q_{2}}+k_{Q_{3}}).

Remark 1.3
.

(1)

We note that the global root number

\varepsilon\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}\times\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}\biggl)=+1\text{ for }(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}

for
(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
by (splt) and Remark
B.4
(
2
).

(2)

The assumption (
H_{3}
) is technical.
We denote the central character of
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},l}
by
\omega_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},l}}
.
When
N^{\prime}
is odd, we can choose an auxiliary automorphic character
\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}=\prod_{v}\varrho_{v}
of
\mathrm{U}(1)({\mathbb{A}})
of finite order which ramifies precisely at prime factors of
N^{\prime}
so that

c(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},l}\otimes\varrho_{l})=2c(\omega_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},l}\otimes\varrho_{l}})

for all
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{2}}
and prime factors
l
of
N^{\prime}
by stability of the epsilon factor.
Fix
{Q}_{0}=(\underline{Q}_{0},\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{0})\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}\cap({\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{2}}^{\mathrm{cls}})
.
Let
f_{\varrho}
and
g_{\varrho}
be
p
-stabilized newforms associated to
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}_{0}}\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{0}}\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}
.
Theorem
4.4
allows us to lift
f_{\varrho}
and
g_{\varrho}
to Hida families
\boldsymbol{f}_{\varrho}
and
\boldsymbol{g}_{\varrho}
to which Theorem
1.2
can apply.
The specialization
{Q}_{0}({\mathscr{L}}_{\boldsymbol{f}_{\varrho},\boldsymbol{g}_{\varrho}})
involves the central value
\Gamma(0,{\mathbf{V}}_{{Q}_{0}})L(0,{\mathbf{V}}_{{Q}_{0}})
.

(3)

If
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{I}}_{3}}^{\mathrm{cls}}
satisfies
k_{Q_{1}}<k_{Q_{2}}
, then the weights of
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q},\infty}
are regular, and so by the endoscopic classification of cuspidal automorphic representations on
\mathrm{U}(2,1)

[
Rog90
]
(see also
[
BC04
, 3.2.3, p. 618]
) combined with the Ramanujan conjecture
[
Car12
, Theorem 1.2]
we have
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\mathrm{temp}}
.

(4)

Hsieh and Yamana
[
HY25
]
have constructed five-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions for the Rankin-Selberg convolution
\mathrm{BC}(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}})\times\mathrm{BC}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee})
in the piano case, where the weights satisfy the interlacing relation

k_{Q_{1}}\leq k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\leq k_{Q_{2}}\leq k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\leq k_{Q_{3}},

using Hida families on definite unitary groups
\mathrm{U}(3)\times\mathrm{U}(2)
.

(5)

We expect the interpolation formula in Theorem
1.2
holds for all
(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{\mathbf{V}}}^{\rm crit}
, but at this moment, we only obtain the interpolation formula at
{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
due to our limited understanding of the integral properties of vector-valued Fourier-Jacobi coefficients.

1.7.
One-variable
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3})
be a tuple of integers such that
k_{1}\leq k_{2}\leq k_{3}-2
.
Let
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
be an irreducible tempered automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
which is unramified at
p
and all the non-split primes and such that
\pi_{\infty}
is a discrete series or limit of discrete series with Blattner parameter
-\underline{k}
.
We identify
\pi_{p}
with an irreducible principal series
I(\nu_{p},\rho_{p},\mu_{p})
with unramified characters
\nu_{p},\rho_{p},\mu_{p}
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Fix a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational
p
-stabilized newform
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in S^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
associated to
\pi
with respect to
\nu_{p},\rho_{p},\mu_{p}
, by which we mean that

\displaystyle U_{p}(\alpha_{1})f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}

\displaystyle=p\nu_{p}(p)^{-1}f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},

\displaystyle U_{p}(\beta_{1})f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}

\displaystyle=p\mu_{p}(p)f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}

(see Proposition
8.6
).
When
\nu_{p}(p),\rho_{p}(p),\mu_{p}(p)
are mutually different, one can obtain
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}
by applying the
p
-stabilization to a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational newform
f_{\pi}^{\circ}\in S^{G}_{\underline{k}}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
associated to
\pi
(cf.
[
MY14
,
HY24a
]
).

Take a finite extension
F
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
in such a way that
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in S^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi},\chi,F)
via
\iota_{p}
.
Let
{O}
be the maximal compact subring of
F
and
\varpi
a prime element of
{O}
.
We may normalize
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}
so that
S^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi},\chi,{O})
contains
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}
but does not contain
\varpi^{-1}f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}
.

Let
{\mathbf{I}}
be a semi-local normal ring finite and flat over
\Lambda={O}\llbracket{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\rrbracket
.
Let
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{H}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbf{I}})
be a one-variable cuspidal Hida family on
{H}
such that
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}_{Q}\in\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}S_{(k_{1};k_{Q})}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\epsilon_{Q}^{\downarrow},{\mathbf{I}}(Q))
for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbf{I}}
with
k_{Q}-k_{1}\geq 2
, where the character
\epsilon_{Q}^{\downarrow}
is defined in (
8.1
).
We denote the Galois representation of
\Gamma_{E}
associated to
\pi
by
V_{\pi}
.
Put

{\mathbf{V}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Ind}_{\Gamma_{E}}^{\Gamma_{\mathbb{Q}}}(V_{\pi}\otimes V_{\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}).

We write
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q}\simeq\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q,v}
for the irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
H({\mathbb{A}})
associated to
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}_{Q}
in the sense of §
5.4
.
Let

{E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})={E}(\pi_{p},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q,p}^{\vee})

be the modified factor relative to
\mu_{p}
(see Definition
7.11
).
To remove the assumption (
H_{3}
), we use the simplified period
\Omega^{(N^{\prime})}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q})
introduced in Definition
7.19
.

Theorem 1.4
.

Notation and assumption being as above, if all the prime factors of
N^{\prime}
are odd and split in
E
, then there exists a unique element
{L}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}
such that

\frac{Q({L}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}^{2})}{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}}=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}\frac{\Gamma({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime},0)L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime},0)}{\Xi_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}}\Omega^{(N^{\prime})}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q})^{2}}{E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime}){E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})

for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbf{I}}
with
k_{Q}\geq k_{3}
, where

m_{Q}^{\prime}=k_{Q}-(k_{2}+k_{3}).

Remark 1.5
.

(1)

The most surprising and confusing point in our construction of the theta element
{L}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}
is that it does not impose any ordinary hypothesis on
\pi
, even though the Panchishkin condition requires
\pi
to be
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-ordinary, namely, a
\Gamma_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}
-stable lattice
\mathrm{Fil}_{1}V_{\pi}
with Hodge-Tate weight
-k_{1}-1
to exists.
Note that

\mathrm{Fil}_{2}V_{\pi}^{c}=\{u\in V_{\pi}^{c}\;|\;\langle v,u\rangle=0\text{ for }v\in\mathrm{Fil}_{1}V_{\pi}\},

where
\langle\;,\;\rangle:V_{\pi}\otimes V_{\pi}^{c}\to\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{\rm cyc}^{-2}
is a perfect pairing.
Actually, we need the
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-ordinary hypothesis when we vary
\epsilon_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}

(2)

When
k_{1}=k_{2}=k_{3}-2
, the range of interpolation includes the points
Q
such that
k_{Q}=k_{1}+2
, i.e., the restriction of
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}_{Q}
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
is an elliptic cusp form of weight
2
.
The specialization of the five-variable
p
-adic
L
-function at such points will be interesting (cf.
[
DR14
,
DR17
]
).

We give a brief outline of the construction of
{L}_{f^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}
and a proof of the interpolation formula.
The main ingredients are followings:

•

geometric theory of Fourier-Jacobi coefficients of Picard modular forms;

•

Shimura’s theory of differential operators;

•

Bannai-Kobayashi theory of algebraic CM theta functions;

•

the Ichino–Ikeda–Neal-Harris (IINH) formula.

Below we describe these ingredients and their role in more detail.

1.8.
Fourier-Jacobi coefficients of Picard modular forms

For simplicity we suppose that
k_{1}=k_{2}=0
and
\pi_{p}
is unramified.
Put
k:=k_{3}
and
\underline{k}_{0}=(0,0;k)
.
The Hermitian symmetric domain associated to
G({\mathbb{R}})
is

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\;\biggl|\;\eta(Z)>0\biggl\},

\displaystyle\eta(Z):=\sqrt{-1}(\tau^{c}-\tau)-\frac{ww^{c}}{\sqrt{D_{E}}}.

The group
G({\mathbb{R}})
acts on
{\mathfrak{D}}
by
\alpha\begin{bmatrix}Z\\
1\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\alpha Z\\
1\end{bmatrix}\mu(\alpha,Z)
for
\alpha\in G({\mathbb{R}})
and
Z\in{\mathfrak{D}}
.
Put
q:=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\tau}
.
The space
S_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
consists of functions
f:{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})\to{\mathbb{C}}
which are holomorphic in
Z
, satisfy

f(\gamma Z,\gamma gu)=\chi(u)^{-1}\mu(\gamma,Z)^{k}f(Z,g)

for
\gamma\in G({\mathbb{Q}})
,
g\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
,
u\in K_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})
, and have Fourier-Jacobi expansions

f\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix},{\mathbf{d}}(a)\biggl)=\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)q^{m}

for finite idèles
a\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
such that
a_{\mathfrak{l}}=1
for
{\mathfrak{l}}|pN
, where
{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}:=({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}\cap{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}
,
{\mathbf{d}}(a):=\mathrm{diag}(a^{c},1,a^{-1})
and
{\mathfrak{a}}
is the fractional ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
associated to
a
.
It is important to note that Fourier-Jacobi coefficients are CM theta series, namely, there is a lattice
L_{0}\subset E
such that for every
l\in{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}

\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w+l,f)=e^{\frac{2\pi m}{\sqrt{D_{E}}}\bigl(w+\frac{l}{2}\bigl)l^{c}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f).

Let
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p)}
denote the integral closure of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}={\mathbb{Q}}\cap{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
Let
f\in S_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
be
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p)}
-integral in the sense of Definition
3.5
.
Then
\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)
can be identified with an integral section of a pullback of Poincarè bundle of a CM elliptic curve over
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p)}
by Proposition
3.13
(cf.
[
Lan12
]
).

The holomorphic function
w\mapsto\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigl(\frac{w}{\Omega_{\infty}},f\bigl)
has algebraic Taylor coefficients, and moreover, the coefficients
\biggl(\dfrac{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{n}\dfrac{\partial^{n}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}}{\partial w^{n}}(0,f)
are
p
-integral by the work
[
BK10
]
of Bannai and Kobayashi.
We associate to this
p
-integral power series the
p
-adic measure
\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f)_{0}^{\Omega_{\infty}})
on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.

1.9.
Shimura’s differential operator

Shimura has introduced a theory of nearly homomorphic modular forms and differential operators on Hermitian symmetric domains in his paper
[
Shi86
]
.
His differential operator
\delta_{\underline{k}}
preserves near holomorphy and
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rationality.
At the same time, the first named author gave an equivalent (and more general) construction in
[
Har86
]
, using algebraic geometry.

If
f\in S_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, then
\delta_{\underline{k}_{0}}f
is a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational nearly holomorphic modular form valued in the dual space of the global holomorphic tangent space
{\mathfrak{T}}={\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}\frac{\partial}{\partial w}
of
{\mathfrak{D}}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{H}}
be the upper half plane.
We regard
{\mathfrak{H}}
as a subdomain of
{\mathfrak{D}}
via the map
\tau\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
0\end{bmatrix}
which is compatible with the embedding
\iota:H\hookrightarrow G
defined by

\iota\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}a&0&b\\
0&1&0\\
c&0&d\end{bmatrix}.

Appendix
A
computes iteration of this operator and evaluates it at
w=0
to produce a nearly holomorphic cusp form on
{\mathfrak{H}}
of weight
(0,k+n)
.
More precisely, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{n}f\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\biggl)\biggl|_{\mathfrak{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(Z)^{-k}}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}-\frac{w^{c}}{\sqrt{-D_{E}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\right)^{n}\eta(Z)^{k}f(Z)\biggl|_{w=0}

\displaystyle\equiv(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-n}\frac{\partial^{n}f}{\partial w^{n}}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
0\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\pmod{y^{-1}},

where we write
y
for the imaginary part of
\tau
.
We denote the holomorphic projection from the space of nearly holomorphic modular forms on
{\mathfrak{H}}
by
\mathrm{Hol}
, and Hida’s ordinary projection on
p
-adic modular forms on
H
by
\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}
.
We deduce an important identity

\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\mathrm{Hol}\biggl(\delta_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{n}f\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\biggl)\biggl|_{H}\biggl)=\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\biggl((2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-n}\frac{\partial^{n}f}{\partial w^{n}}\biggl|_{H}\biggl)

from the simple relation stated in
[
Hid93
, (3), p. 311]
.

Shimura’s differential operators can be reformulated algebro geometrically in terms of the Gauss-Manin connection (cf. Section 6 of
[
Eis12
]
).

1.10.
The
\Theta_{\mathfrak{p}}
-operator

Given
w\in{\mathbb{E}}
and
z\in{\mathbb{A}}
, we define an element of
G({\mathbb{A}})
by

{\mathbf{n}}(w,z)=\begin{bmatrix}1&w^{c}&z+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{-D_{E}}ww^{c}\\
0&1&\sqrt{-D_{E}}w\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}.

Fix a sufficiently small ideal
{\mathfrak{c}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
prime to
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Take a set
{X}_{n}
of representatives for
{\mathfrak{p}}^{-n}{\mathfrak{c}}/{\mathfrak{c}}
.
Section
6
associates the operator

(1.1)

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}f(Z,g)=\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{x\in{X}_{n}}\sum_{z\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\phi(z){\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(zx_{\mathfrak{p}})f(Z,g{\mathbf{n}}(x_{\mathfrak{p}},0))

to an
{O}
-valued function
\phi
on
({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}
.
Here we view
x
as a principal adèle and denote its
{\mathfrak{p}}
-component by
x_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Building on the work
[
BK10
]
of Bannai-Kobayashi, we show that
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}
can be interpreted in terms of
p
-adic translation by
{\mathfrak{p}}
-power torsion points by the pull-back of theta functions to the formal group of a CM elliptic curve and see that

\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\phi(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f))=\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(0,\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}f)

for locally constant functions
\phi
on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
.
In particular, the right hand side of (
1.1
) is independent of
n
and bounded.
Furthermore, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}x^{n}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f))=\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{n}\frac{\partial^{n}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}}{\partial w^{n}}(0,f)

for non-negative integers
n
.
We obtain an element
{J}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)\in\Lambda
such that for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda}
with
k_{Q}\geq k

Q({J}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f))=\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{k_{Q}-k}\frac{\partial^{k_{Q}-k}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}}{\partial w^{k_{Q}-k}}(0,\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q}}f).

Now we can define formal power series
{J}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)\in\Lambda_{2}\llbracket{q}\rrbracket
by

{J}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}}{J}^{mp^{j!}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)q^{m}.

The set
\{{J}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)\}_{{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E}}
forms a
p
-adic family
{J}(f)
of
p
-ordinary cusp forms on
H
such that
for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda}
with
k_{Q}\geq k

Q({J}(f))=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{k_{Q}-k}\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\mathrm{Hol}\biggl(\delta_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{k_{Q}-k}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q}}f\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\biggl)\biggl|_{H}\biggl).

1.11.
The Ichino-Ikeda formula

Let
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{H}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbf{I}})
be the one-variable cuspidal Hida family on
{H}
.
Note that the restriction of
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
is a usual Hida family of elliptic cusp forms.
We denote its restriction to
H
also by
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}
and write
1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N^{\prime},{\mathbf{I}})\otimes_{\mathbf{I}}\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}
for the idempotent corresponding to
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}
.
For simplicity we assume that
N^{\prime}=1
and
\chi^{\prime}=1
.

Define
\varsigma^{(p)}=(\varsigma^{(p)}_{l})\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

\displaystyle\varsigma

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&0&1\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\varsigma^{(p)}_{l}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}^{-1}(\varsigma)&\text{if $l$ splits in $E$ and differs from $p$, }\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{3}&\text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

Define the
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-depletion of
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}
by

[{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{0}f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}](Z,g)=\frac{1}{p\mu_{p}(p)}\sum_{y\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\sum_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}\left(Z,g\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\left(\begin{bmatrix}p&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\right).

Let
{F}(Z,g)=[{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{0}f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}](Z,g\varsigma^{(p)})
.
Then
{J}({F})\in{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{H}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E},{\mathbf{I}})
.
Writing the spectrum decomposition of
J({F})
, we define the theta element by

{L}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}:=\text{the first Fourier coefficient of }1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}{J}({F}).

The evaluation of
{L}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}
at
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda}
with
k_{Q}\geq k
is roughly given by

\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{k_{Q}-k}\frac{\Big\langle\delta_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{k_{Q}-k}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q}}{F}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\Big)\Big|_{H},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}_{Q}\Big\rangle}{\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}_{Q}\|_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q}}}^{2}},

where
\langle\;,\;\rangle
is the Petersson pairing on
H
.

The Ichino-Ikeda conjecture has been formulated for orthogonal groups in
[
II10
]
as a refinement of the global Gross–Prasad conjecture formulated in
[
GP92
]
.
The unitary analogue was formulated in
[
GGP12
,
Har14
]
and proved in
[
Zha14
,
BPLZZ21
,
BPCZ22
]
(cf. Theorem
7.4
).
The pairing above is the Gan-Gross-Prasad period integral, and by the celebrated Ichino–Ikeda–Neal-Harris (IINH) formula, its square is written as the product of the central
L
-value
L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q}^{\vee}\bigl)
and local zeta integrals.

A big chunk of this paper is to make explicit evaluations of these local zeta integrals including at
p
and the archimedean place.
Appendix
B
computes the archimedean integral, which proves the algebraicity of the
L
-value mentioned above, together with Shimura’s theory on
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rationality of nearly holomorphic modular forms.
We will compute the local integral at
p
in Section
8
.
A local key ingredient is the splitting lemma which has been proved in
[
LM14
,
Zha14
]
(see Lemma
8.5
).
This lemma relates the local integral to a square of the JPSS integral at split primes.

When
N^{\prime}>1
, we will apply the operator
{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}
(see §
6.4
).

1.12.
Theta operators

We conclude this introduction by remarking that in addition to the explicit evaluation of local integrals, a novelty of this work is the introduction of a new theta operator
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}
.
Since
\phi\mapsto\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}f
is a
p
-adic measure on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
valued in the space of
p
-adic modular forms, we can speak of operators
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}:=\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi_{n}}
for integers
n
, where
\phi_{n}(z)=z^{n}

(z\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times})
.

This theta operator acts directly on Fourier-Jacobi coefficients, different from the one studied in
[
dSG16
,
dSG19
]
, and plays a role analogous to the classical Ramanujan-Serre theta operator
\theta
in Hida’s
p
-adic Rankin-Selberg method for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}
.
For instance, if
g
and
h
are elliptic modular forms, then Hida has proved the miraculous identity

\boldsymbol{e}_{\rm ord}(g\cdot\theta^{n}h)=\boldsymbol{e}_{\rm ord}\mathrm{Hol}(g\cdot\delta^{n}h)

for non-negative integers
n
, where
\boldsymbol{e}_{\rm ord}
is the Hida ordinary projector on
p
-adic modular forms on
\mathrm{GL}(2)
and
\delta
is the Maass-Shimura differential operator (see
[
Hid93
, (2), p. 330]
).
We discover an analogous identity

\frac{1}{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}f|_{H})=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{n}\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\mathrm{Hol}\biggl(\delta_{\underline{k}_{0}}^{n}f\biggl(\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\biggl)\biggl|_{H}\biggl).

Our one-variable
p
-adic
L
-function in Theorem
1.4
roughly interpolates the value
1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{k-k_{3}}f|_{H})
, where
\boldsymbol{g}_{k}^{\prime}
is an elliptic modular of weight
k
in a Hida family of elliptic modular forms.
In a future work on the
p
-adic IINH formula for
\mathrm{U}(2,1)\times\mathrm{U}(1,1)
we shall evaluate this one-variable
p
-adic
L
-function at
k=k_{3}-1
and relate this value to the
p
-Abel-Jacobi image of the diagonal cycle in the product of a Picard modular surface and a modular curve.

One may also ask about the relation between our theta operator and the
p
-adic differential operators constructed in
[
EFMV18
]
.
The space of differential operators that share homogeneity properties with these operators is necessarily one-dimensional, so the two types of operators differ by a scalar multiple. This can be determined by relating the Serre-Tate coordinates used at an ordinary point in
[
EFMV18
, §2.7.1]
with the local coordinates at a boundary point (which also belongs to the ordinary locus). This comparison also determines the relation between the element
{L}_{f_{\pi}^{\rm ord},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}
, constructed in Theorem 1.4, with the
L(F,\tau)
defined in
[
Har21a
, Theorem 6.6]
, under a Gorenstein condition, as an element of the localized Hecke algebra. We hope to clarify this relation in a future article.

Notation

We will fix some notation that will be used in what follows.
Besides the standard symbols
{\mathbb{Z}}
,
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
{\mathbb{R}}
,
{\mathbb{C}}
,
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
,
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
we denote by
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
the valuation ring
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\cap{\mathbb{Q}}
, by
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{\times}
and
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}
the groups of strictly positive real and rational numbers, and by
{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
the group of complex numbers of absolute value
1
.
Given a field
F
, we write
\overline{F}
for an algebraic closure of
F
.
We denote the integral closure of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
by
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p)}
.
For a ring
R
we put
R_{(p)}=R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}
be the rational adèles ring and
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}
its finite part.
We denote by
\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}=\prod_{q}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
the maximal compact subring of
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}
, and by
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(p)}
the restricted product
\prod^{\prime}_{q}{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
over rational primes
q\neq p
.
Given a place
v
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
, we write
{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
for the completion of
{\mathbb{Q}}
with respect to
v
.
We shall regard
{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
,
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(p)}
(resp.
{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}^{\times}
) as subgroups of
{\mathbb{A}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
) in a natural way.
We denote by the formal symbol
\infty
the real place of
{\mathbb{Q}}
and do not use
q,l
for the infinite place.

Define
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}(z)=e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}z}
for
z\in{\mathbb{R}}
and by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(x)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}(-y)
for
x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with
y\in{\mathbb{Z}}[p^{-1}]
such that
y-x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
.
Then
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}=\prod_{v}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}
defines a character of
{\mathbb{A}}/{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We associate to
m\in{\mathbb{Q}}
the global additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}
defined by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}(mz)
for
z\in{\mathbb{A}}
.
For
a\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
we define an additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}^{a}
of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}^{a}(z)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}(az)
for
z\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
.

Let
\mathrm{d}z_{v}
be the Haar measure on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}
self-dual with respect to the pairing
(z_{v},z_{v}^{\prime})\mapsto{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}(z_{v}z_{v}^{\prime})
.
Note that
\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}}\mathrm{d}z_{q}=1
for each rational prime
q
and that
\mathrm{d}z_{\infty}
is the usual Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
\mathrm{d}z=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{v}
.
Then
\mathrm{d}z
is the Haar measures on
{\mathbb{A}}
such that
{\mathbb{A}}/{\mathbb{Q}}
has volume 1.
Let
\mathrm{d}^{\times}t=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{v}
be the Haar measure on
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
, where
\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{v}
is the Haar measure on
{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}^{\times}
normalized by
\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}^{\times}}\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{q}=1
if
v=q<\infty
, and
\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{\infty}=\frac{\mathrm{d}t_{\infty}}{|t_{\infty}|}
.

For positive integers
m,n
and a commutative ring
R
we denote
\mathrm{M}_{m,n}(R)
the
R
-module of all
m\times n
-matrices with entries in
R
, by
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(R)
the group of all invertible elements of
\mathrm{M}_{m}(R)=\mathrm{M}_{m,m}(R)
, by
T_{m}(R)
the subgroup of diagonal matrices in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(R)
, by
B_{m}(R)
the subgroup of upper triangular matrices in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(R)
, by
N_{m}(R)
the subgroup of upper unipotent triangular matrices in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(R)
.
If
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}
are square matrices, then
\mathrm{diag}[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}]
denotes the matrix with
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}
in the diagonal blocks and 0 in all other blocks.

Let
E
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{E}
with integer ring
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Put
\delta=\sqrt{-D_{E}}
.
Then
{\mathfrak{d}}_{E}=\delta{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
is the different of
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Set

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}

\displaystyle=E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{A}},

\displaystyle E_{v}

\displaystyle=E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{v},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,q}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}.

Denote by
\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}=\prod_{v}\epsilon_{E_{v}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}
the quadratic character of
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
associated to
E
.
We write
x\mapsto x^{c}
for complex conjugation on
E
, i.e., the generator of
\mathrm{Gal}(E/{\mathbb{Q}})
, which induces an involution on
R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}E
by
(r\otimes x)^{c}=r\otimes x^{c}
for a ring
R
.
Let
\,{}^{t}\!x\in\mathrm{M}_{n,m}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}E)
be the transpose of a matrix
x=(x_{ij})\in\mathrm{M}_{m,n}(R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}E)
and put
x^{c}=(x_{ij}^{c})
.

Once and for all we fix an odd rational prime
p
that is split in
E
.
Fix an algebraic closure
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
containing
E
.
We can identify
E
with the subfield of
{\mathbb{C}}
in exactly two ways.
We fix an embedding
\iota_{\infty}:\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
and an isomorphism
\iota_{p}:{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
, where
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
is the completion of an algebraic closure of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
We denote the restriction of
\iota_{\infty}
to
E
by the same symbol and write
\iota_{\infty}^{c}
for the other injection of
E
into
{\mathbb{C}}
.
We view
\iota_{p}\circ\iota_{\infty}
as a
p
-adic place of
E
and write
{\mathfrak{p}}
for the corresponding prime ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Let
\mathrm{ord}_{p}
be the
p
-adic valuation on
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
normalized so that
\mathrm{ord}_{p}p=1
.

Given an algebraic number field
L
, we regard
L
as a subfield in
{\mathbb{C}}
(resp.
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
) via
\iota_{\infty}
(resp.
\iota_{p}\circ\iota_{\infty}
).
We denote by
{\mathfrak{o}}_{L}
the ring of integers of
L
, by
\Gamma_{L}=\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/L)
the absolute Galois group, and by
L_{\mathrm{ab}}
the maximal abelian extension of
L
.
If
X
is a scheme over
L
, then
\mathrm{R}_{L/{\mathbb{Q}}}X
is the restriction of scalar of
X
from
L
to
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

If
R
is any
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,(p)}
-algebra and
M
is any
R
-module with a commuting
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-action, then
M
becomes an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes R
-module and we have a canonical type decomposition
M=M(\varSigma)\oplus M(\varSigma^{c})
, where
M(\varSigma)=e_{\varSigma}M
is the part of
M
on which
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
acts via the canonical homomorphism
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,(p)}\to R
, and
M(\varSigma^{c})=e_{\varSigma^{c}}M
is the part on which it acts via the conjugate homomorphism.
The idempotents
e_{\varSigma},e_{\varSigma^{c}}\in{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
are explicitly given in (1.2) of
[
dSG16
]
.
Since
p
is split in
E
, we can view
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
as an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,(p)}
-algebra via
\iota_{p}\circ\iota_{\infty}
.
For an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-module
M
we put

\displaystyle M_{p}

\displaystyle=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p},

\displaystyle M_{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=e_{\varSigma}M_{p},

\displaystyle M_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}

\displaystyle=e_{\varSigma^{c}}M_{p}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$e_{\varSigma},e_{\varSigma^{c}}$}}}

Part I
Geometric Theory

2.
Shimura varieties for unitary groups

2.1.
Unitary groups

Let
r,s
be non-negative integers such that
r\geq s
.
Put
n=r+s
and
t=r-s
.
When
t\geq 1
, we fix
\gamma_{0}\in\mathrm{GL}_{t}(E)
such that

(C
1
)

-\sqrt{-1}\iota_{\infty}(\gamma_{0})
is a positive definite Hermitian matrix;

(C
2
)

\gamma_{0}\in\mathrm{GL}_{t}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,(p)})
;

Let
V=E^{n}
be an
E
-vector space equipped with a skew Hermitian form

\displaystyle S_{\gamma_{0}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=xS_{\gamma_{0}}\,^{t}\!y^{c},

\displaystyle S_{\gamma_{0}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&-\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\\
0&-\gamma_{0}&0\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0&0\end{bmatrix}.

Define the alternating pairing
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s}
on
V
by
\langle x,y\rangle_{r,s}=\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(S_{\gamma_{0}}(x,y))
.
Then
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s}
is Hermitian in the sense that
\langle ax,y\rangle_{r,s}=\langle x,a^{c}y\rangle_{r,s}
for all
x,y\in V
and
a\in E
.
Let
{G}=\mathrm{GU}(V)=\mathrm{GU}(S_{\gamma_{0}})
be the unitary similitude group attached to the skew Hermitian space
(V,S_{\gamma_{0}})
.
As an algebraic group over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, the
R
-points of
{G}
are given by

{G}(R)=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R)\;|\;gS_{\gamma_{0}}\,^{t}\!g^{c}=\nu(g)S_{\gamma_{0}}\text{ for some }\nu(g)\in R^{\times}\}

for a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra
R
.
The morphism
\nu:{G}\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m/{\mathbb{Q}}}
is called the similitude character.
The unitary group
G=\mathrm{U}(V)=\mathrm{U}(S_{\gamma_{0}})
is defined by

G(R)=\{g\in{G}(R)\;|\;\nu(g)=1\}.

Remark 2.1
.

If
n
is even, then
\nu({G}({\mathbb{A}}))={\mathbb{A}}^{\times}
.
If
n
is odd, then
\nu({G}({\mathbb{A}}))=\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathbb{E}}^{\times})
and hence
{G}({\mathbb{A}})={\mathbb{E}}^{\times}G({\mathbb{A}})
(cf. Proposition 5.3 of
[
Shi97
]
).

Let
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the standard basis of
V
.
Put

\displaystyle W_{0}

\displaystyle=\oplus_{i=s+1}^{r}Ee_{i},

\displaystyle Y_{E}

\displaystyle=\oplus_{i=1}^{s}Ee_{i},

\displaystyle X_{E}

\displaystyle=\oplus_{i=r+1}^{n}Ee_{i},

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=Y_{E}\oplus X_{E}.

Then
Y_{E}
and
X_{E}
are maximal totally isotropic subspaces, which are non-degenerately paired by
S_{\gamma_{0}}
.
Let
H=\mathrm{U}(J_{s})
and
{H}=\mathrm{GU}(J_{s})
, where
J_{s}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\\
-\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0\end{bmatrix}
.
The natural inclusion
\iota:H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{U}(W)\subset G
is given by

(2.1)

\iota\left(\begin{bmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{bmatrix}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}A&0&B\\
0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{t}&0\\
C&0&D\end{bmatrix},

where
A,B,C,D
have size
s
.

Define the alternating pairing
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{t,0}
on
W_{0}
by
\langle x,y\rangle_{t,0}=-\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(x\gamma_{0}\,^{t}\!y^{c})
.
Let
G_{0}=\mathrm{U}(W_{0})
and
{G}_{0}=\mathrm{GU}(W_{0})
be the unitary and unitary similitude groups of the definite skew Hermitian space
(W_{0},\gamma_{0})
.
Let
{P}={M}{N}
be stabilizer of
X_{E}
in
{G}
, where
{N}
is the unipotent radical of
{P}
and
{M}=\mathrm{GL}(X_{E})\times{G}_{0}
is the standard Levi subgroup of
{P}
.
Here we regard
\mathrm{GL}(X_{E})\times{G}_{0}
as a subgroup of
{G}
by the morphism

(2.2)

(A,g_{0})\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}A&&\\
&g_{0}&\\
&&\nu(g_{0})(\,^{t}\!A^{-1})^{c}\end{bmatrix}.

2.2.
Lattices and polarization

Fix an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-lattice
L_{0}
in
W_{0}
on which we impose the following conditions:

(
C_{3}
)

L_{0}
is integral in the sense that
\langle L_{0},L_{0}\rangle_{t,0}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}
;

(
C_{4}
)

L_{0}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}=\sum_{i=s+1}^{r}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,p}\;e_{i}
.

Let
Y=\oplus_{i=1}^{s}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}e_{i}
and
X^{\vee}=\oplus_{i=r+1}^{n}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}e_{i}
be the standard
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-lattices in the
E
-vector spaces
Y_{E}
and
X_{E}
.
Let

M=Y\oplus L_{0}\oplus X^{\vee}

be the
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-lattice in
V
.
Then
\langle M,M\rangle_{r,s}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}
and
M_{p}
is self-dual with respect to the alternating form
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s}
by (C
2
).

An ordered polarization of
M_{p}=M\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is a pair
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}=\{N^{-1},N^{0}\}
of sublattices of
M_{p}
which are maximal totally isotropic submodules of
M_{p}
and which are dual to each other with respect to
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s}
and satisfy

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}N^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rank}N^{0}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}=r,

\displaystyle\mathrm{rank}N^{-1}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rank}N^{0}_{\mathfrak{p}}=s.

We define the standard polarization
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}^{0}=\{M^{-1},M^{0}\}
by

\displaystyle M^{-1}

\displaystyle=Y_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus L_{0,{\mathfrak{p}}}\oplus Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},

\displaystyle M^{0}

\displaystyle=X^{\vee}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}\oplus L_{0,{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}\oplus X^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{p}},

following
[
Hsi14
]
.
Observe that

\displaystyle M^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=Y_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus L_{0,{\mathfrak{p}}},

\displaystyle M^{-1}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}

\displaystyle=Y_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},

\displaystyle M^{0}_{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=X^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{p}},

\displaystyle M^{0}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}

\displaystyle=X^{\vee}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}\oplus L_{0,{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}.

Given a lattice
L
in
V
and
g\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\widehat{E})
, we denote by
Lg
the lattice in
V
such that
(Lg)_{q}=L_{q}g_{q}
for every rational prime
q
.
Put

\displaystyle K^{0}

\displaystyle=\{g\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})\;|\;Mg=M\},

\displaystyle K^{0}_{q}

\displaystyle=\{g\in{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})\;|\;M_{q}g=M_{q}\}.

Note that
K^{0}=\prod_{q}K^{0}_{q}
.

2.3.
Abelian schemes

An abelian variety
A
is an algebraic group over a field
k
whose underlying variety is complete and connected.
Given a line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}
on
A
, we define a morphism
\phi_{\mathfrak{L}}:A\to A^{\vee}
by
\phi_{\mathfrak{L}}(x)=T_{x}^{*}{\mathfrak{L}}\otimes{\mathfrak{L}}^{-1}
, where
A^{\vee}=\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(A)
denotes the dual abelian variety,
T_{x}
is the translation by
x
and
T_{x}^{*}
denotes the pullback of the line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}
by
T_{x}
.
A polarization of
A
is a homomorphism
\lambda:A\to A^{\vee}
such that
\lambda=\phi_{\mathfrak{L}}
for some ample line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}
on
A
over
\bar{k}
.
The poincaré bundle
P_{A}
is a line bundle on
A\times A^{\vee}
with the following properties:

(1)

For every
\alpha\in A^{\vee}(\bar{k})
the line bundle
P_{A}|_{A\times\{\alpha\}}
represents that element of

\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(A)(\bar{k})
given by
\alpha
;

(2)

P_{A}|_{\{0\}\times A^{\vee}}
is trivial.

A morphism
f:A\to B
of abelian varieties gives a dual morphism
f^{\vee}:B^{\vee}\to A^{\vee}
, which satisfies
(1\times f^{\vee})^{*}P_{A}\simeq(f\times 1)^{*}P_{B}
.
Put
\mathrm{End}^{0}(A)=\mathrm{End}(A)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
.
The Rosati involution associated to a polarization
\lambda
is the involution on
\mathrm{End}^{0}(A)
defined by
f\mapsto\lambda^{-1}\circ f^{\vee}\circ\lambda
.

An abelian scheme
{\mathscr{A}}
over a base scheme
S
is a smooth group scheme whose geometric fibres are abelian varieties.
We denote by
{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee}
the dual abelian scheme.
A polarization of
{\mathscr{A}}
is a homomorphism
\lambda:{\mathscr{A}}\to{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee}
such that for every geometric point
s
the induced homomorphism
\lambda_{s}:{\mathscr{A}}_{s}\to{\mathscr{A}}_{s}^{\vee}
is a polarization of
{\mathscr{A}}_{s}
.

A semi-abelian variety is a commutative group variety which is the extension of an abelian variety by a connected torus.
A semi-abelian scheme is a smooth group scheme whose geometric fibres are semi-abelian varieties.

When
S
is locally noetherian and the prime number
p
is invertible on it, we write
T_{p}(A)
for the
p
-adic Tate module of
A
.
When
S
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
-scheme, we similarly write
T^{(p)}(A)=\prod_{q\neq p}T_{q}(A)
.

The Rosati involution of
\mathrm{End}(A)^{0}:=\mathrm{End}(A)\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
induced by
\lambda
sends
f\in\mathrm{End}(A)^{0}
to
\lambda^{-1}\circ\hat{f}\circ\lambda
.
The Weil pairing
e_{N}:A[N]\times A^{\vee}[N]\to\boldsymbol{\mu}_{N}
composed with
\lambda
gives an alternating pairing
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{\lambda}:T^{(p)}(A)\times T^{(p)}(A)\to\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(p)}
after taking the limit with respect to
N
.

2.4.
Shimura varieties associated to
\mathrm{GU}(r,s)

For a finite set
\Box
of prime numbers we put

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}_{(\Box)}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}\cap\bigcap_{q\in\Box}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q},

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(\Box)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{q\notin\Box}{\mathbb{Z}}_{q},

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(\Box)}

\displaystyle={\prod_{q\notin\Box}}^{\prime}{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}.

Let
U\subset K^{0}
be an open compact subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(\Box)})
.

Put
{\mathscr{O}}={\mathfrak{o}}_{E,(p)}
.
An
{\mathscr{O}}
-algebra
R
is called a base ring, and similarly a scheme
S
over
\mathrm{Spec}{\mathscr{O}}
is called a base scheme.
Let
S
be a locally noetherian connected
{\mathscr{O}}
-scheme and
\bar{s}
a geometric point of
S
.
An
S
-quadruple
\underline{A}=(A,\iota,\bar{\lambda},\bar{\eta}^{(\Box)})_{S}
of level
U^{(\Box)}
consists of the following data:

•

A
is an abelian scheme of dimension
r+s
over
S
,

•

\iota:{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{S}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{(\Box)}
is an embedding of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(\Box)}
-algebra,

•

\lambda:A\to A^{\vee}
is a prime-to-
\Box
polarization over
S
and
\bar{\lambda}
the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(\Box),+}
-orbit of
\lambda
, where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(\Box),+}={\mathbb{Z}}_{(\Box)}\cap{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}
,

•

\bar{\eta}^{(\Box)}=\eta^{(\Box)}U
is a
\pi_{1}(S,\bar{s})
-invariant
U^{(\Box)}
-orbit of isomorphisms of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-modules
\eta^{(\Box)}:M\otimes\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(\Box)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}T^{(\Box)}(A_{\bar{s}}):=H_{1}(A_{\bar{s}},\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(\Box)})
.

Here we define
\eta^{(\Box)}u(x)=\eta^{(\Box)}(xu^{-1}\nu(u))
for
u\in U
.

In addition, the
S
-quadruple
\underline{A}
satisfies the following conditions:

(K
1
)

The Rosati involution induced by
\lambda
is given by
\iota(a)\mapsto\iota(a^{c})
,

(K
2
)

\eta^{(\Box)}
takes the pairing
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s}
on
M
to a
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(\Box)\times}
-multiple of
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{\lambda}
,

(K
3
)

The determinant condition: for
b\in{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
we have

\det(X-\iota(b)|_{\mathrm{Lie}A})=(X-\iota_{\infty}^{c}(b))^{r}(X-\iota_{\infty}(b))^{s}\in{O}_{S}[X].

Two
S
-quadruples
\underline{A}=(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(\Box)})_{S}
and
\underline{A}^{\prime}=(A^{\prime},\iota^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime},\bar{\eta}^{\prime(\Box)})_{S}
are equivalent
if there is an isomorphism
\phi:A\to A^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\phi\circ\iota

\displaystyle=\iota^{\prime}\circ\phi,

\displaystyle\phi^{\vee}\circ\bar{\lambda}^{\prime}\circ\phi

\displaystyle=\bar{\lambda},

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{\prime(\Box)}

\displaystyle=\phi\circ\bar{\eta}^{(\Box)}.

See Section 1.4 of
[
Lan13
]
for the comparison with another moduli problem.

Assume that
U
is neat in the sense of Definition 1.4.1.8 of
[
Lan13
]
.
Let
{\mathfrak{S}}_{U}^{(\Box)}(S)
be the collection of equivalence classes of
S
-quadruple
\underline{A}=(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(\Box)})_{S}
of level
U^{(\Box)}
.
Then
{\mathfrak{S}}_{U}^{(\Box)}
becomes a contravariant functor from the category of locally Noetherian
{\mathscr{O}}
-schemes to the category of sets.

The reflex field associated to the Shimura datum is contained in
E
as
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
is normal.
If
\Box=\emptyset
, then the functor
S\mapsto{\mathfrak{S}}_{U}^{(\emptyset)}(S)
is represented by a quasi-projective scheme
S_{G}(U)_{/E}
over
E
by the theory of Shimura and Deligne.
If
\Box=\{p\}
and
U=K^{0}_{p}\times U^{(p)}
, where
U^{(p)}
is an open compact subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(p)})
, then
{\mathfrak{S}}_{U}^{(p)}
is representable by a quasi-projective scheme
S_{G}(U)_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
over
{\mathscr{O}}
by the work
[
Kot92
]
of Kottwitz.

Fix a smooth toroidal compactification
\overline{S}_{G}(U)_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
of
S_{G}(U)_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
, which is a proper smooth scheme over
{\mathscr{O}}
containing
S_{G}(U)_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
as an open dense subscheme.
The abelian scheme
{\mathscr{A}}/S_{G}(U)
extends with the
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-action to a semi-abelian scheme
{\mathscr{G}}
over the toroidal compactification
\overline{S}_{G}(U)
by Theorem 6.4.1.1 of
[
Lan13
]
.
In addition, the universal quadruple
\underline{{\mathscr{A}}}
of level
U^{(p)}
over
S_{G}(U)
extends to a quadruple
\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}=({\mathscr{G}},\iota,\lambda,\eta)
over
\overline{S}_{G}(U)
.

2.5.
Igusa schemes associated to
\mathrm{GU}(r,s)

Define the isomorphism
\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}:G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
by
\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(g)=g|_{V_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
We write
\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(g)=\begin{bmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{bmatrix}
according to the standard filtration of
M_{\mathfrak{p}}=M_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\oplus M_{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
defined in
[
Hsi21
, (1.6)]
.
Let
U=K^{0}_{p}\times U^{(p)}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
For positive integers
\ell
we define

\displaystyle U^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{u\in U\;\biggl|\;\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(u_{p})\equiv\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{r}&*\\
0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix}\pmod{p^{\ell}}\biggl\},{\@index{\@indexphrase{$U^{\ell}$}}}

\displaystyle U^{\ell}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{u\in U\;|\;\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(u_{p})\in N_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\pmod{p^{\ell}}\}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$U^{\ell}_{1}$}}}

We introduce a notion of the Igusa scheme, which is a model of
S_{G}(U^{\ell})
over
{\mathscr{O}}
(see
[
Hsi21
, Lemma 2.3]
).
Let
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}=\{N^{-1},N^{0}\}
be an ordered polarization of
M_{p}
.
An
S
-quintuple
(\underline{A},j)_{S}=(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(p)},j)_{S}
of level
K^{\ell}
with respect to
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}
consists of an
S
-quadruple
\underline{A}
of level
K^{(p)}
and a level-
p^{\ell}
structure
j
of
A
with respect to
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}
, which is a monomorphism
j:\boldsymbol{\mu}_{p^{\ell}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}N^{0}\hookrightarrow A[p^{\ell}]
as
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-group schemes over
S
.

Two
S
-quintuples
(\underline{A},j)_{S}
and
(\underline{A}^{\prime},j^{\prime})_{S}
are equivalent if there is an isomorphism
\phi:\underline{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\underline{A}^{\prime}
such that
\phi\circ j=j^{\prime}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{I}}_{K,\ell,\mathrm{Pol}_{p}}^{(p)}(S)
be the set of equivalence classes of
S
-quintuple
\underline{A}=(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(p)})_{S}
of level
U^{\ell}
with respect to
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}
.
The functor
S\mapsto{\mathfrak{I}}_{U,\ell,\mathrm{Pol}_{p}}^{(p)}(S)
is relatively representable over
S_{G}(U)_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
.
Recall the standard polarization
\mathrm{Pol}_{p}^{0}=\{M^{-1},M^{0}\}
of
M_{p}
.
Let
I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell})_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
be the scheme representing
{\mathfrak{I}}_{K,\ell,\mathrm{Pol}_{p}^{0}}^{(p)}
over
{\mathscr{O}}
.

Definition 2.2
.

Put

{\mathbf{H}}=\mathrm{GL}(M^{0},{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,p})=\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{H}}$}}}

The group
{\mathbf{H}}
acts on
I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell})
over
\overline{S}_{G}(U)
by
h\cdot j(m)=j(mh)
.
Let

\displaystyle{\mathbf{N}}

\displaystyle=N_{r}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\times N_{s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{T}}

\displaystyle=T_{r}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\times T_{s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}){\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{N}}$}}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{T}}$}}}

be subgroups of
{\mathbf{H}}
with respect to the
p
-adic basis of
M^{0}
defined in §
2.2
.
Define
I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})=I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell})/{\mathbf{N}}
.

2.6.
Hermitian symmetric domains

We hereafter suppose that
s\geq 1
.
We follow the notation of
[
Shi97
,
Hsi14
]
.
The Hermitian symmetric domain associated to
{G}({\mathbb{R}})
is given by

{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}=\biggl\{Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in\mathrm{M}_{r,s}({\mathbb{C}})\biggl|\;\tau\in\mathrm{M}_{s}({\mathbb{C}}),\;w\in\mathrm{M}_{t,s}({\mathbb{C}}),\;\eta(Z)>0\biggl\},{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}$}}}

where

\eta(Z)=\sqrt{-1}(\,^{t}\!\tau^{c}-\tau-\,^{t}\!w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}w).

Let
{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
be the subgroup of
{G}({\mathbb{R}})
consisting of elements whose similitude factor is positive.
The action of
{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha(Z)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}(a\tau+bw+c)(h\tau+lw+d)^{-1}\\
(g\tau+ew+f)(h\tau+lw+d)^{-1}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}a&b&c\\
g&e&f\\
h&l&d\end{bmatrix}\in{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}

with
a,d\in\mathrm{M}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
and
e\in\mathrm{M}_{t}({\mathbb{C}})
.
The automorphy factors are defined by

\displaystyle\lambda(\alpha,Z)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}h^{c}\,{}^{t}\!\tau+d^{c}&h^{c}\,{}^{t}\!w-\gamma_{0}^{c}l^{c}\\
-(\gamma_{0}^{-1}g)^{c}\,{}^{t}\!\tau-(\gamma_{0}^{-1}f)^{c}&-(\gamma_{0}^{-1}g)^{c}\,{}^{t}\!w+(\gamma_{0}^{-1}e\gamma_{0})^{c}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\mu(\alpha,Z)

\displaystyle=h\tau+lw+d,\qquad\qquad J(\alpha,Z)=(\lambda(\alpha,Z),\mu(\alpha,Z)).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\lambda(\alpha,Z)$}}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mu(\alpha,Z)$}}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$J(\alpha,Z)$}}}

Remark 2.3
.

If
r+s
is odd, then
{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}={G}({\mathbb{R}})
(see Remark
2.1
).

We define a maximal compact subgroup
\mathrm{GL}_{m}({\mathbb{C}})
by

\mathrm{U}_{T}=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{m}({\mathbb{C}})\;|\;\,^{t}\!gTg^{c}=T\}

for a positive definite Hermitian matrix
T
of size
m
.
Following
[
Shi97
,
Shi00
]
, we define the origin of
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
and the standard maximal compact subgroup of
G({\mathbb{R}})
by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{i}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{-1}\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\\
\hbox{\bf 0}_{t,s}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},

\displaystyle{K}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in G({\mathbb{R}})\;|\;\alpha({\mathbf{i}})={\mathbf{i}}\}.

Put
T^{\prime}=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&\\
&\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\gamma_{0}^{c}\end{bmatrix}
.
The isomorphism
{K}_{\infty}\simeq\mathrm{U}_{T^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{U}_{{\bf 1}_{s}}
is given by

(2.3)

\alpha\mapsto J^{-}(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}}):=(\overline{\lambda(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})},\mu(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}}))

by (
A.5
).
Recall that

(2.4)

\det\alpha\cdot\det\lambda(\alpha,Z)=\det\mu(\alpha,Z)

(see (6.3.9) of
[
Shi97
]
).

2.7.
Complex uniformization

Put
{G}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}={G}({\mathbb{Q}})\cap{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
.
Let
U
be a neat open compact subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
We denote the point represented by
(Z,g)\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
in the quotient

M_{G}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},U)={G}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}\backslash{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})/U,

which is a complex manifold, by
[Z,g]\in M_{G}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},U)
.

We now recall the construction of the universal quadruple
\underline{{\mathscr{A}}}
of level
U^{(p)}
over
S_{G}(U)/{\mathbb{C}}
.
Define the
E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{C}}
-module
{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}:={\mathbb{C}}^{r}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}^{s}
so that
e_{+}({\mathbb{C}}^{r,s})={\mathbb{C}}^{s}
and
e_{-}({\mathbb{C}}^{r,s})={\mathbb{C}}^{r}
, where
{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
and
{\mathbb{C}}^{s}
are regarded as spaces of row vectors.
We define the map
c_{r,s}:{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}
by

\displaystyle(u,u^{\prime})c_{r,s}

\displaystyle=(u^{c},u^{\prime})

\displaystyle(u

\displaystyle\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r},\;u^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s}),

where
u^{c}=(u_{1}^{c},\dots,u_{r}^{c})
is the complex conjugation of
u=(u_{1},\dots,u_{r})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
.

The embedding
\iota_{\infty}
induces an isomorphism
E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}
by which we regard
V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}}
as a
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector space of row vectors according to
the
E
-basis
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
Assuming that
s>0
, we put

B(Z)=\begin{bmatrix}\,{}^{t}\!\tau^{c}&\,{}^{t}\!w^{c}&\tau\\
0&\gamma_{0}&w\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$B(Z)$}}}

For each
Z\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
we define the map
p_{Z}:V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}
by
p_{Z}(v)=vB(Z)c_{r,s}
.
Then
V
acts on
(Z,u)\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}
by

v\cdot(Z,u)=(Z,p_{Z}(v)+u).

We define a left action of
{G}
on
V
by
g*v:=vg^{-1}\nu(g)
.
Put

\displaystyle M_{[g]}

\displaystyle=g*M,

\displaystyle M_{[g]}(Z)

\displaystyle=p_{Z}(M_{[g]}).

We associate to each point
(Z,g)\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
a
{\mathbb{C}}
-quadruple
\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)=({\mathscr{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\bar{\lambda}_{Z},\iota_{\mathbb{C}},\bar{\eta}_{g})
of level
U
in the following way:

•

the abelian variety
{\mathscr{A}}(V)_{g}(Z):={\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}/M_{[g]}(Z)\simeq V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}}/M_{[g]}
;

•

\bar{\lambda}_{Z}
is the
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}
-orbit of the polarization induced by the unique Riemann form
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{Z}
on
{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}
such that
\langle p_{Z}(v),p_{Z}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{Z}=\langle v,v^{\prime}\rangle_{r,s}
for
v,v^{\prime}\in V
;

•

\iota_{\mathbb{C}}:{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathscr{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)
is induced by the
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-action on
V
via
p_{Z}
;

•

the level structure
\eta_{g}:M\otimes\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}M_{[g]}\otimes\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(p)}=H_{1}({\mathscr{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
is defined by
\eta_{g}(x)=g*x
for
x\in M
.

Since

(2.5)

p_{\alpha(Z)}(v)=p_{Z}(v\alpha)J(\alpha,Z)^{-1}=p_{Z}(\alpha^{-1}*v)\nu(\alpha)J(\alpha,Z)^{-1}

for
\alpha\in{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
by (
A.4
), we see that

\underline{{A}}(V)_{\gamma g}(\gamma(Z))\sim\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)

for
\gamma\in{G}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}
.
Actually,
\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)
is equivalent to
\underline{{A}}(V)_{g^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})
if and only if
[g,Z]=[g^{\prime},Z^{\prime}]
.
Moreover, a
{\mathbb{C}}
-quadruple is equivalent to
\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)
for some
(Z,g)
(see Theorem 4.8 of
[
Shi00
]
).
Therefore the set of complex points of
S_{G}(U)
is identified with
M_{G}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},U)
.

We define the prime-to-
p
level structure
\eta_{g}^{(p)}
similarly.
As for the level structure at
p
we fix a primitive
p^{\ell}
th root
\zeta=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{p^{\ell}}}
and define

j_{\zeta}:M^{0}\otimes\boldsymbol{\mu}_{p^{\ell}}\simeq M^{0}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}/p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)[p^{\ell}]=M_{[g]}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}/p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}

by
j_{\zeta}(x^{0})=g*x^{0}
for
x^{0}\in M^{0}
, where
\zeta
is used to pin down an isomorphism
\zeta^{-1}:\boldsymbol{\mu}_{p^{\ell}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}}
.

2.8.
Igusa schemes associated to
\mathrm{U}(r,s)

We consider a subvariety associated to an open subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
containing
G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
We follow the notion introduced in Section 2.5 of
[
Hsi21
]
(cf. Appendix A.1 of
[
Har21a
]
).
Let
\mathrm{Cl}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{+}(U)
be a set of representatives of the group
{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}\backslash\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}/\nu(U)
in
\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{(p)\times}
.
For
{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathrm{Cl}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{+}(U)
we consider the following condition:

(K
2,c
)

\eta^{(p)}
takes the pairing
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s}
on
M
to
u\langle\;,\;\rangle_{\lambda}
for some
