u\in{\mathbf{c}}\nu(U)
.

We consider the functor

{\mathfrak{I}}_{U,\ell,\mathrm{Pol}_{p}^{0},{\mathbf{c}}}^{(p)}(S)=\biggl\{(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(p)},j)_{S}\;\biggl|\;\begin{matrix}(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(p)})_{S}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{U}^{(p)}(S)\\
\lambda\text{ and }\eta^{(p)}\text{ satisfy (K${}_{2,{\mathbf{c}}}$)}\end{matrix}\biggl\}/\sim

It is proved in
[
Hid04
, p. 136]
that the equivalence class of
(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(p)},j)_{S}
is independent of the choice of
\lambda
in
\bar{\lambda}
.
Then
{\mathfrak{I}}_{U,\ell,\mathrm{Pol}_{p}^{0},{\mathbf{c}}}^{(p)}
is represented by a scheme over
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E_{U},(p)}
, which we denote by
I^{0}_{G}(U,{\mathbf{c}})
.
The set of complex points of
I^{0}_{G}(U,{\mathbf{c}})
is given by
M_{G}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},{{}^{\mathbf{c}}U})
, where we write
{}^{\mathbf{c}}U=g_{\mathbf{c}}Ug_{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}\cap G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, taking an element
g_{\mathbf{c}}\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that
\nu(g_{\mathbf{c}})={\mathbf{c}}
.
As explained in §4.2.1 of
[
Hid04
]
for Hilbert modular varieties, we have

S_{G}(U)=\bigsqcup_{{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathrm{Cl}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{+}(U)}S_{G}(U,{\mathbf{c}}).

We will write
I^{0}_{G}(U)
for
I^{0}_{G}(U,{\mathbf{c}})
if
{\mathbf{c}}=1
.

2.9.
Morphisms between Igusa schemes

Put
L_{1}=Y\oplus X^{\vee}
.
Recall the decompositions

\displaystyle(V,\langle\;,\;\rangle_{r,s})

\displaystyle=(W,\langle\;,\;\rangle_{s,s})\oplus(W_{0},\langle\;,\;\rangle_{t,0}),

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=L_{1}\oplus L_{0}.

Define the hyperspecial open compact subgroups of
{G}_{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
by

\displaystyle U^{L_{0}}_{p}

\displaystyle=\{g\in{G}_{0}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\;|\;(L_{0}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})g=L_{0}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\},

\displaystyle U^{L_{1}}_{p}

\displaystyle=\{h\in H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\;|\;(L_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})h=L_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\}.

Define the subgroup
{H}^{\prime}
of
{H}\times{G}_{0}
by

{H}^{\prime}=\{(h,g)\in{H}\times{G}_{0}\;|\;\nu(h)=\nu(g)\}.

We naturally view
{H}^{\prime}
as a subgroup of
{G}
.
When we view
H
and
{G}_{0}
as subgroups of
{G}
by the morphisms (
2.1
) and (
2.2
), the subgroup
{H}^{\prime}
is identified with
H{G}_{0}
.
Take open compact subgroups
U_{L_{0}}=U^{L_{0}}_{p}\times U_{L_{0}}^{(p)}
of
{G}_{0}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
U_{L_{1}}=U^{L_{1}}_{p}\times U_{L_{1}}^{(p)}
of
H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
so that
U_{L_{1}}U_{L_{0}}\subset U
.
For simplicity we let
t=1
.
Define an open compact subgroup
{U}_{L_{1}}
of
{H}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

{U}_{L_{1}}=\biggl\{\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&\\
&u\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix}k\;\biggl|\;u\in\nu(U_{L_{0}}),\;k\in U_{L_{1}}\biggl\}.

Then we have a natural morphism

i_{W,W_{0}}:I_{H}^{0}({U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell})_{/{\mathscr{O}}}\times I_{G_{0}}^{0}(U_{L_{0}}^{\ell})_{/{\mathscr{O}}}\to I_{G}^{0}(U^{\ell})_{/{\mathscr{O}}}

(see (2.12) of
[
Hsi21
]
).
Fix
\underline{E}_{0}=(E_{0},\iota_{0},\lambda_{0},\bar{\eta}_{0}^{(p)},j_{0})_{S}\in I^{0}_{G_{0}}(U_{L_{0}})
.
We restrict
i_{W,W_{0}}
to obtain a morphism
\iota_{W}:I^{0}_{H}({U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell})_{/{\mathscr{O}}}\to I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell})_{/{\mathscr{O}}}
, which is given by

\iota_{W}([(B,\iota,\lambda,\eta^{(p)}{U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell},j)_{S}])=[(B\times E_{0},\iota\times\iota_{0},\lambda\times\lambda_{0},(\eta^{(p)}\times\eta_{0}^{(p)})U^{\ell},j\times j_{0})_{S}].{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\iota_{W}$}}}

We give an explicit expression of the morphism
\iota_{W}
in terms of the complex uniformization constructed in §
2.7
.
Let
E_{0}
be an elliptic curve with CM by
E
together with a complex uniformization
E_{0}({\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{C}}(\varSigma^{c})/(L_{0}\gamma_{0})^{c}
by which we define the level structure
\eta_{0}:L_{0}\otimes\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}H_{1}(E_{0},\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})
.
As is well-known,
E_{0}
is defined over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
, and extends to an abelian scheme over
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p)}
.

Recall the embedding
\iota:H\hookrightarrow G
defined in (
2.1
).
Put
{\mathscr{H}}_{G}=\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}
.
Define the embeddings
\jmath:{\mathfrak{D}}_{s,s}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}_{s+1,s}
and
\iota^{\prime}:{\mathscr{H}}_{H}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{H}}_{G}
by

\displaystyle\jmath(\tau)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
\hbox{\bf 0}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\iota^{\prime}(A,B)

\displaystyle=\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}A&\\
&1\end{bmatrix},B\biggl),

where
A,B
have size
s
.
Then for
h\in H({\mathbb{R}})

(2.6)

\displaystyle\iota(h)\jmath(\tau)

\displaystyle=\jmath(h\tau),

\displaystyle J(\iota(h),\jmath(\tau))

\displaystyle=\iota^{\prime}(J(h,\tau)).

Since

\displaystyle{\mathscr{A}}(V)_{\iota(h)}(\jmath(\tau))

\displaystyle={\mathscr{A}}(W)_{h}(\tau)\times E_{0},

\displaystyle\lambda_{\jmath(\tau)}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\tau}\times\lambda_{0},

\displaystyle\eta_{\iota(h)}

\displaystyle=\eta_{h}\times\eta_{0}

for
\tau\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{s,s}
and
h\in H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, we find that

(2.7)

\iota_{W}(\underline{{A}}(W)_{h}(\tau))=\underline{{A}}(V)_{\iota(h)}(\jmath(\tau)).

3.
Modular forms on unitary groups

3.1.
Geometric modular forms

We recall the theory of geometric modular forms and
p
-adic modular forms on unitary groups from the viewpoint of Katz and Hida.
In order to introduce modular forms of non-scalar weight, we consider the algebraic group
{\mathscr{H}}=\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}
over
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

The Hodge bundle
\omega=\omega_{{\mathscr{G}}/\overline{S}_{G}(U)}:=e^{*}\Omega_{{\mathscr{G}}/\overline{S}_{G}(U)}
is the pull-back of the relative cotangent sheaf
\Omega_{{\mathscr{G}}/\overline{S}_{G}(U)}
of the universal semi-abelian scheme
{\mathscr{G}}
over
\overline{S}_{G}(U)
via the zero section
e
of
{\mathscr{G}}/\overline{S}_{G}(U)
.
Then
\omega
is a locally free coherent
{O}_{\overline{S}_{G}(U)}
-module.
The structure morphism
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\to{O}_{\overline{S}_{G}(U)}
makes
\omega
into an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-module.
The embedding
\iota
makes the relative tangent sheaf
{\mathfrak{t}}_{{\mathscr{G}}/\overline{S}_{G}(U)}
into an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-module.
The action of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
on
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in\omega
is defined by
(a\boldsymbol{\omega})(l)=\boldsymbol{\omega}(\iota(a^{c})l)
for
a\in{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
and
l\in e^{*}{\mathfrak{t}}_{{\mathscr{G}}/\overline{S}_{G}(U)}
.

We can decompose
\omega=\omega(\varSigma)\oplus\omega(\varSigma^{c})
.
Define

\displaystyle{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}^{+}

\displaystyle:=\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}({O}^{r}_{\overline{S}_{G}(U)},\omega(\varSigma)),

\displaystyle{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}^{-}

\displaystyle:=\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}({O}^{s}_{\overline{S}_{G}(U)},\omega(\varSigma^{c})).

Then
{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}:={E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}^{+}\times{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}^{-}
is an
{\mathscr{H}}
-torsor over
\overline{S}_{G}(U)
with the structure map
\bar{\pi}:{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}\to\overline{S}_{G}(U)
.
Let
S=\mathrm{Spec}R
be a base scheme.
An
R
-point in
{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}
is a pair
(\underline{A},\boldsymbol{\omega})
which consists of an
R
-point
\underline{A}=(A,\iota,\lambda,\bar{\eta}^{(p)})_{S}
in
\overline{S}_{G}(U)
and
\boldsymbol{\omega}=(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})
with

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}

\displaystyle\in{E}_{\underline{A}}^{+}=\mathrm{Isom}(e_{\varSigma}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes R^{r}),\omega_{A/R}(\varSigma)),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}

\displaystyle\in{E}_{\underline{A}}^{-}=\mathrm{Isom}(e_{\varSigma^{c}}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes R^{s}),\omega_{A/R}(\varSigma^{c})).

We will write

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}_{i}^{+}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}(e_{i})\quad(1\leq i\leq r),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}_{i}^{-}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}(e_{i})\quad(r+1\leq i\leq r+s).

Giving an element
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}
(resp.
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}
) is equivalent to giving an ordered basis
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{i}^{+}\}
of
\omega_{A/R}(\varSigma^{c})
(resp.
\{\boldsymbol{\omega}_{i}^{-}\}
of
\omega_{A/R}(\varSigma)
).
The action of
h=(h^{+},h^{-})\in{\mathscr{H}}
on
\boldsymbol{\omega}=(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})
is given by
h\boldsymbol{\omega}(v^{+},v^{-})=(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}(v^{+}h^{+}),\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}(v^{-}h^{-}))
.

Let
(\rho,{L})
be an algebraic representation of
{\mathscr{H}}
defined over
{\mathscr{O}}
.

Definition 3.1
.

A geometric modular form
f
of weight
\rho
and level
U
defined over
{\mathscr{O}}
is a rule, defined for all
{\mathscr{O}}
-algebras
R
, which associates to a pair
(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})
of an
R
-quintuple
(\underline{A},j)
together with
\boldsymbol{\omega}\in{E}_{\underline{A}}
an element
f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})\in{L}(R)
, satisfying the following conditions:

(G
1
)

f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})
depends only on the
R
-isomorphism class of
(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})
;

(G
2
)

f(\underline{A},j,h\boldsymbol{\omega})=\rho(\,^{t}\!h^{-1})f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})
for every
h\in{\mathscr{H}}(R)
;

(G
3
)

f(\underline{A}_{/R^{\prime}},j_{/R^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{/R^{\prime}})=\varphi(f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega}))
for every
{\mathscr{O}}
-algebra homomorphism
\varphi:R\to R^{\prime}
;

(G
4
)

if
r=s=1
, then

f({\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes\mathrm{Tate}(q),\iota_{\mathrm{can}},{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes\lambda_{\mathrm{can}},{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes\eta_{\mathrm{can}}^{(p)},{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes j_{\mathrm{can}},{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{can}})\in R\llbracket{q}\rrbracket,

where
(\mathrm{Tate}(q),\lambda_{\mathrm{can}},\eta_{\mathrm{can}}^{(p)},j_{\mathrm{can}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{can}})
is the Tate elliptic curve
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}/q^{\mathbb{Z}}
with the canonical level structure
\eta_{\mathrm{can}}^{(p)}
and the canonical differential
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathrm{can}}
over
{\mathbb{Z}}(\!(q)\!)
.

We sometimes write
f(\underline{A},j,\{\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}_{i}\};\{\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}_{i}\})
instead of
f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})
.
We define

\omega_{\rho}={E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}\times^{\rho}{L}:={E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{G}}}}\times{L}/\sim,

where the relation
\sim
is given by
(\boldsymbol{\omega},l)\sim(h\boldsymbol{\omega},\rho(\,^{t}\!h^{-1})l)
for
h\in{\mathscr{H}}
.
For a base ring
R
flat over
{\mathbb{Z}}_{(p)}
we set

{\mathbf{M}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R):={\rm H}^{0}(I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})_{/R},\omega_{\rho}).{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{M}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)$}}}

A geometric modular form associates to every
{\mathscr{O}}
-algebra
R
an element of
{\mathbf{M}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
, which is compatible with base change.
If
R
is a field, then

{\mathbf{M}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)={\rm H}^{0}(\overline{S}_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})_{/R},\omega_{\rho})

by the Koecher principle unless
r=s=1
(cf.
[
Shi97
, Proposition 4.5]
).

Let
{D}
be the Cartier divisor
\overline{S}_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})-S_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})
, equipped with its structure of reduced closed subscheme.
The
R
-module of cusp forms on
{G}
over
R
is the submodule

{\mathbf{S}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R):={\rm H}^{0}(I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})_{/R},\omega_{\rho}(-{D})).{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{S}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)$}}}

The diagonal torus
{\mathbf{T}}
of
{\mathbf{H}}
acts on
{\mathbf{M}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
by

[t].f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})=f(\underline{A},t\cdot j,\boldsymbol{\omega}).

3.2.
Classical modular forms on
\mathrm{GU}(r,s)

Let
\mathrm{Her}^{+}_{s}({\mathbb{Q}})
(resp.
\mathrm{Her}^{0}_{s}({\mathbb{Q}})
) be the set of positive definite (resp. positive semi-definite) Hermitian matrices of size
s
over
E
.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
We call a function
f:{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})\to{L}({\mathbb{C}})
a modular form on
{G}
of weight
\rho
and level
U
if it is holomorphic in
Z
and satisfies

\displaystyle f(\gamma Z,\gamma gu)

\displaystyle=\rho(\nu(\gamma)^{-1}J(\gamma,Z))f(Z,g)

\displaystyle(\gamma

\displaystyle\in{G}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+},\;g\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}),\;u\in U),

and has a Fourier-Jacobi expansion

f\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix},\beta\biggl)=\sum_{B\in\mathrm{Her}_{s}^{0}({\mathbb{Q}})}\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{B}_{\beta}(\,^{t}\!w,f)e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{tr}(B\tau)}.

A modular form
f
is called a cusp form if
\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{B}_{\beta}(\,^{t}\!w,f)=0
unless
B\in\mathrm{Her}^{+}_{s}({\mathbb{Q}})
.
Let
M_{\rho}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
(resp.
S_{\rho}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
) be the space of modular (resp. cusp) forms on
{G}
of weight
\rho
and level
U
.

For
g\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and a function
{F}
on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
we set

[r(g){F}](Z,\beta)={F}(Z,\beta g).

For a character
\chi
of
U
we set

S_{\rho}^{G}(U,\chi,{\mathbb{C}})=\{f\in S_{\rho}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})\;|\;r(u)f=\chi(u)^{-1}f\text{ for }u\in U\}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$S_{\rho}^{G}(U,\chi,{\mathbb{C}})$}}}

Remark 3.3
.

Put
\Gamma^{\beta}={G}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}\cap\beta U\beta^{-1}
for
\beta\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
Let
f\in M_{\rho}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
.
Define a function
f_{\beta}
on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
by
f_{\beta}(Z)=f(Z,\beta)
.
Then
f_{\beta}
satisfies

f_{\beta}(\alpha Z)=\rho(\nu(\alpha)^{-1}J(\alpha,Z))f_{\beta}(Z)

for
\alpha\in\Gamma^{\beta}
.
Let
{B}
be a finite subset of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})=\bigsqcup_{\beta\in{B}}{G}({\mathbb{Q}})\beta U
.
One can identify the modular form
f
with the collection of holomorphic functions
\{f_{\beta}\}_{\beta\in{B}}
satisfying the functional equations above.

We denote the first
r
complex coordinates of
{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}
by
z_{V}^{(1)}(\varSigma),\dots,z_{V}^{(r)}(\varSigma)
, and the remaining
s
complex coordinates of
{\mathbb{C}}^{r,s}
by
z_{V}^{(r+1)}(\varSigma^{c}),\dots,z_{V}^{(r+s)}(\varSigma^{c})
.
Let
\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{V}=\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{V}(\varSigma)\cup\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{V}(\varSigma^{c})
be an ordered basis of
\Omega_{{\mathscr{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)}
, where

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{V}(\varSigma)

\displaystyle=\{\mathrm{d}z_{V}^{(i)}(\varSigma)\}_{i=1}^{r},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{V}(\varSigma^{c})

\displaystyle=\{\mathrm{d}z_{V}^{(r+j)}(\varSigma^{c})\}_{j=1}^{s}.

We define
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z)=(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}_{g}(Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}_{g}(Z))\in{E}_{\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z)}
by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+}_{g}(Z)_{i}

\displaystyle=2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{d}z_{V}^{(i)}(\varSigma)

\displaystyle(1\leq i\leq r),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-}_{g}(Z)_{i}

\displaystyle=2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{d}z_{V}^{(i)}(\varSigma^{c})

\displaystyle(r+1\leq i\leq r+s).

We see from (
2.5
) that for
\beta\in{G}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}

\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\beta g}(\beta Z)=\nu(\alpha)\,^{t}\!J(\beta,Z)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z).

It follows that if
U
is neat and
f\in{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
, then

\displaystyle f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{\beta g}(\beta Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\beta g}(\beta Z))=

\displaystyle f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\nu(\alpha)\,^{t}\!J(\beta,Z)^{-1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho(\nu(\alpha)^{-1}J(\alpha,Z))f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z)).

Thus the function
(Z,g)\mapsto f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z))
is a modular form of weight
\rho
and level
U
.
Actually, this is a one-to-one correspondence.

Lemma 3.4

(cf.
[
Hsi21
, Lemma 3.7]
,
[
CEF+16
, Lemma 3.7]
)
.

If
U
is neat, then the association

f\mapsto f(Z,g):=f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z))

defines an isomorphism
{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}(U^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}M_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}})
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
{U}=K^{0}_{p}\times{U}^{(p)}
be a not (necessarily neat) open compact subgroup of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
containing
U
.
The space
{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}\bigl)
is defined as the subspace of
{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}\bigl(U^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}\bigl)
that is the inverse image of
M^{G}_{\rho}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}\bigl)
.
For an
{\mathscr{O}}
-subalgebra
R
of
{\mathbb{C}}
we regard
{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},R\bigl)
as a submodule of
{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}}\bigl)
.
We define
M_{\rho}^{G}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},R\bigl)
as the image of
{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\rho}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},R\bigl)
under the isomorphism in Lemma
3.4
, and define the submodule
S_{\rho}^{G}\bigl({U}^{\ell}_{1},R\bigl)
similarly.

3.3.
Algebraic representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}

Let
{\mathscr{T}}=T_{r}\times T_{s}
be the diagonal torus of
{\mathscr{H}}
and
N_{\mathscr{H}}=N_{r}\times N_{s}
a unipotent subgroup of
{\mathscr{H}}
.
Put
E^{\prime}=E(\sqrt{-1})
.
We enlarge the base ring
{\mathscr{O}}
to
{\mathscr{O}}^{\prime}={\mathfrak{o}}_{E^{\prime},(p)}
.
Then
T^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathscr{O}}^{\prime})
by (C
2
) (see §
2.6
for the definition of
T^{\prime}
).

Let
R
be a an
{\mathscr{O}}^{\prime}
-algebra.
Let
\underline{k}=(k_{1},\dots k_{r};k_{r+1},\dots,k_{r+s})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r+s}
be a tuple with
k_{1}\leq k_{2}\leq\dots\leq k_{r}
and
k_{r+1}\leq\dots\leq k_{r+s}
.
We define an algebraic character
\underline{k}
of
{\mathscr{T}}
by

\underline{k}(t)=t_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots t_{r}^{k_{r}}\cdot t_{r+1}^{k_{r+1}}\cdots t_{r+s}^{k_{r+s}}

for
t=\mathrm{diag}(t_{1},\dots,t_{r+s})\in{\mathscr{T}}
.
The irreducible algebraic representation
L_{\underline{k}}(R)
can be realized as the schematical induction module

L_{\underline{k}}(R)=\{f\in R[\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}]\;|\;f(tug)=\underline{k}(t)f(g)\;(t\in{\mathscr{T}},\;u\in N_{\mathscr{H}})\},{\@index{\@indexphrase{$L_{\underline{k}}(R)$}}}

where
R[\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}]
is the ring of polynomial functions on
\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}
with coefficients in
R
.
Put
\Bbbk=(k_{r},\dots,k_{1};k_{r+s},\dots,k_{r+1})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r+s}
.
Let
{\mathscr{H}}(R)
act on
L_{\underline{k}}(R)
by

\rho_{\Bbbk}(h_{1},h_{2})f(g_{1},g_{2})=f(g_{1}h_{1},g_{2}h_{2}).

Define the distinguished functional
l_{\underline{k}}:L_{\underline{k}}(R)\to R
by
f\mapsto f(1)
.
Then
l_{\underline{k}}
is the vector of maximal weight
-\underline{k}
in
L_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}(R):=\mathrm{Hom}(L_{\underline{k}}(R),R)
.
On the other hand, we define
{\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}[\mathrm{GL}_{r}]
by

{\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}=(\det X)^{-k_{r}}(\det Y)^{-k_{r+s}}\prod_{l=1}^{r-1}(\det X_{(l)})^{k_{l+1}-k_{l}}\prod_{m=1}^{s-1}(\det Y_{(l)})^{k_{m+r+1}-k_{m+r}},

where
X=(X_{i,j})\in\mathrm{M}_{r}
and
X_{(l)}:=(X_{r-l+i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq l}
.
We find that
{\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}\in L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{Z}})^{N_{\mathscr{H}}}
is a vector of the highest weight
-\underline{k}
, where we set

\underline{k}^{\vee}=(-k_{r},\dots,-k_{1};-k_{r+s},\dots,-k_{r+1}).

If
R
is an integral domain of characteristic zero and
F
is its fractional field, then
L_{\underline{k}}(F)
is an irreducible representation of
{\mathscr{H}}(F)
and there is a unique isomorphism
L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(F)\simeq L_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}(F)
sending
{\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}
to
l_{\underline{k}}
. Let

\ell_{\underline{k}}:L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(F)\otimes L_{\underline{k}}(F)\to F{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\ell_{\underline{k}}$}}}

be the canonical invariant pairing such that
l_{\underline{k}}(f)=\ell_{\underline{k}}({\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}\otimes f)
.

Suppose that
T^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GL}_{r}(R)
.
We define representations
\rho_{\underline{k}}
of
{\mathscr{H}}(R)
on
L_{\underline{k}}(R)
by
\rho_{\underline{k}}(\alpha,\beta)=\rho_{\Bbbk}(T^{\prime-1}\,{}^{t}\!\alpha^{-1}T^{\prime},\beta)
for
\alpha\in\mathrm{GL}_{r}(R)
and
\beta\in\mathrm{GL}_{s}(R)
.
When
R={\mathbb{C}}
, we view
\rho_{\underline{k}}
as a representation of
{K}_{\infty}
by pulling back via the homomorphism
J^{-}(-,{\mathbf{i}}):{K}_{\infty}\to{\mathscr{H}}({\mathbb{C}})
.
By definition we have
\rho_{\underline{k}}(J(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}}))=\rho_{\Bbbk}(J^{-}(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}}))
for
\alpha\in{K}_{\infty}
.

3.4.
Automorphic forms on
\mathrm{GU}(r,s)

We write
{\mathscr{A}}({G})
for the space of automorphic forms on
{G}
, i.e., smooth,
K
-finite,
{\mathfrak{z}}
-finite, slowly increasing functions on
{G}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{G}({\mathbb{A}})
, where
{\mathfrak{z}}
is the centre of the universal enveloping algebra of the complexfied Lie algebra of
{G}({\mathbb{R}})
.
We call an autormophic form a cusp form if it is rapidly decreasing.
We denote the space of cusp forms on
{G}
by
{\mathscr{A}}^{0}({G})
.

We associate to a
{L}({\mathbb{C}})
-valued function
f
on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
satisfying the relation in Definition
3.2
a function
\varPhi_{\rho}(f):{G}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{G}({\mathbb{A}})\to{L}({\mathbb{C}})
by

\displaystyle\varPhi_{\rho}(f)(g)

\displaystyle=\rho(\nu(g_{\infty})J(g_{\infty},{\mathbf{i}})^{-1})f(g_{\infty}({\mathbf{i}}),\hat{g})

for
g=(g_{\infty},\hat{g})
with
g_{\infty}\in{G}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
and
\hat{g}\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
Note that

\varPhi_{\rho}(f)(g\alpha)=\rho(\nu(\alpha)J(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})^{-1})\varPhi_{\rho}(f)(g)

for
g\in{G}({\mathbb{A}})
and
\alpha\in{K}_{\infty}
.

When
R
is an
{\mathscr{O}}^{\prime}
-algebra,
\rho=\rho_{\underline{k}}
and
{L}=L_{\underline{k}}(R)
, we will write

\displaystyle\omega_{\underline{k}}

\displaystyle=\omega_{\rho_{\underline{k}}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,R)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{M}}_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{G}(U,R),

\displaystyle M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,R)

\displaystyle=M_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{G}(U,R),

\displaystyle\varPhi_{\underline{k}}

\displaystyle=\varPhi_{\rho_{\underline{k}}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{S}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,R)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{S}}_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{G}(U,R),

\displaystyle S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,R)

\displaystyle=S_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{\mathbf{G}}(U,R)

for an open compact subgroup
U
of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
We associate to
f\in M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
and
{\mathbf{v}}\in L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{C}})
a scalar valued cusp form
\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathscr{A}}({G})
defined by
\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{\mathbf{v}}(g)=\ell_{\underline{k}}({\mathbf{v}}\otimes\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)(g))
.
If
f\in M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
is not zero, then
{\mathbf{v}}\mapsto\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{\mathbf{v}}
gives a
{K}_{\infty}
-equivariant embedding
L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{C}})\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{A}}({G})
.

Let
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
.
If
f^{\circ}\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
gives an embedding
L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{C}})\hookrightarrow\pi
and if
f^{\circ}
corresponds to a new vector of
\pi_{q}
at all rational primes
q
in a suitable sense (cf. Definition
8.2
and
[
AOY24
]
), then we call
f^{\circ}
a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational newform associated to
\pi
.

Remark 3.6
.

We define the highest weight part
f_{0}
of
f
by
f_{0}(Z)=l_{\underline{k}}(f(Z))
.
Put
\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{0}=\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{{\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}}
.
Let
{T}_{\infty}
be the subgroup which consists of
\alpha\in{K}_{\infty}
such that
J^{-}(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})
is diagonal.
Observe that for
g\in{G}({\mathbb{A}})
and
t_{\infty}\in{T}_{\infty}

\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{0}(gt_{\infty})=\underline{k}(J^{-}(t_{\infty},{\mathbf{i}}))^{-1}\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{0}(g).

3.5.
p
-adic modular forms

Let
{\mathbb{W}}={\mathbb{W}}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
be the ring of Witt vectors associated with
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
.
We view
{\mathbb{W}}
as an
{\mathscr{O}}
-algebra.
Put
{\mathbb{W}}_{m}={\mathbb{W}}/p^{m}{\mathbb{W}}
for positive integers
m
.
We will identify
{\mathbf{H}}
(resp.
{\mathbf{N}}
,
{\mathbf{T}}
) with the group
{\mathscr{H}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
(resp.
N_{\mathscr{H}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
{\mathscr{T}}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
) with respect to the
p
-adic basis of
M^{0}
fixed in §
2.2
.
Let
F
be a number field which contains
E^{\prime}
.
We denote by
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}={\mathfrak{o}}_{F,(p)}
the localization of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{F}
at
p
and the
{\mathfrak{p}}
-adic completion of
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}
by
{O}
.

The ordinary locus
T_{0,m}
of
\overline{S}_{G}(U)_{/{\mathbb{W}}_{m}}
is the complement of the zero set of a lift of some power of the Hasse invariant.
Since
p
splits in
E
, it is known that
T_{0,m}
is open and dense in
\overline{S}_{G}(U)_{/{\mathbb{W}}_{m}}
(cf.
[
Wed99
]
).
Set
T_{\ell,m}:=I_{G}(U^{\ell})_{/{\mathbb{W}}_{m}}
for positive integers
\ell
.
As we let
\ell
vary, we obtain a tower of finite étale and Galois coverings of
T_{0,m}
, called the Igusa tower.
Then
T_{\infty,m}:=\varprojlim_{\ell}T_{\ell,m}
is a Galois cover of
T_{0,m}
with Galois group
{\mathbf{H}}
.
We define

\displaystyle V_{\ell,m}

\displaystyle={\rm H}^{0}(T_{\ell,m/{O}},{O}_{T_{\ell,m}}).

The module of
p
-adic modular forms on
{G}
of level
U^{(p)}
is

V({G},U)=\varprojlim_{m}\varinjlim_{\ell}V_{\ell,m}^{\mathbf{N}}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$V({G},U)$}}}

Let
\ell\geq m
.
Each element
f\in V_{\ell,m}
can be viewed as a function
(\underline{A},j)\mapsto f(\underline{A},j)\in{\mathbb{W}}_{m}
, where
(\underline{A},j)
is a
{\mathbb{W}}_{m}
-quintuple.
Each element
h\in{\mathscr{H}}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}})
acts on elements
f\in V_{\ell,m}
via
(h\cdot f)(\underline{A},j)=f(\underline{A},hj)
.
When
A
is
p
-ordinary, we get an isomorphism
j^{\vee}:A[p^{\ell}]^{\rm{et}}\simeq({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}})\otimes N^{-1}
by taking the dual of
j:\boldsymbol{\mu}_{p^{\ell}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}N^{0}\hookrightarrow A[p^{\ell}]
as
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-group schemes.
Since
\omega_{A/{O}}\simeq A[p^{\ell}]^{\rm{et}}\otimes{O}
(see
[
CEF+16
, (9)]
), we can view
j^{\vee}
as an element
\boldsymbol{\omega}(j)\in{E}_{\underline{A}}
and define the canonical morphism

\displaystyle{\rm H}^{0}(T_{\ell,m}{}_{/{O}},\omega_{\underline{k}})

\displaystyle\to V_{\ell,m}^{\mathbf{N}},

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\widehat{f}_{0}(\underline{A},j)=l_{\underline{k}}(f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega}(j))).

We call
\widehat{f}_{0}
the
p
-adic avatar of
f
.

Let
\zeta
be a character of
{\mathscr{T}}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{\ell}{\mathbb{Z}})
.
We define the locally algebraic character
\underline{k}_{\zeta}
of
{\mathbf{T}}
by
\underline{k}_{\zeta}(t)=\underline{k}(t)\zeta(t)
.
Assume that
{O}
contains the values of
\zeta
.
Put

\displaystyle{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},{O})

\displaystyle:={\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},{\mathscr{O}}_{F})\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}_{F}}{O},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}_{\zeta}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{0},{O})

\displaystyle:=\{f\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},{O})\;|\;[t].f=\zeta(t)f\text{ for }t\in{\mathbf{T}}\}.

We therefore have a map

\displaystyle{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}_{\zeta}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{0},{O})

\displaystyle\to V({G},U)[\underline{k}_{\zeta}],

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\widehat{f}_{0},

where
V({G},U)[\underline{k}_{\zeta}]
is the
\underline{k}_{\zeta}
-eigenspace of the torus
{\mathbf{T}}
(cf. Remark
3.6
).

3.6.
The
{U}_{p}
-operators

Define elements
\alpha_{i},\beta_{j}\in G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
by

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{r+s-i}&\\
&p\hbox{\bf 1}_{i}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\beta_{j}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}p^{-1}\hbox{\bf 1}_{j}&\\
&\hbox{\bf 1}_{r+s-j}\end{bmatrix}

for
i=1,2,\dots,s
and
j=1,2,\dots,r
.
Put
{I}_{p}=\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(U_{1}^{\ell})
.
Following Proposition 3.15
1
1

1

We here let
a_{i}=-k_{i}

(1\leq i\leq r)
and
b_{j}=k_{r+j}

(1\leq j\leq s)
.
Since the similitude group
\mathrm{GU}(r,s)
is considered in
[
Hsi14
]
, the factor
\underline{k}(\alpha_{v})^{-1}
should be replaced by
\underline{k}(\alpha_{v})
.
of
[
Hsi14
]
, we define the normalized
U_{p}
-operators

{U}_{p}(\alpha_{i}),\,{U}_{p}(\beta_{j})\in C_{c}^{\infty}({I}_{p}\backslash G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/{I}_{p})

by

\displaystyle{U}_{p}(\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle:=p^{-ri+\sum_{l=1}^{i}k_{r+s+1-l}}[{I}_{p}\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\alpha_{i}){I}_{p}],

\displaystyle{U}_{p}(\beta_{j})

\displaystyle:=p^{-sj-\sum_{l=1}^{j}k_{l}}[{I}_{p}\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{j}){I}_{p}].{\@index{\@indexphrase{${U}_{p}(\beta_{1})$}}}

Let
{\mathbf{T}}^{G}(N,R)
be the universal Hecke algebra over a commutative ring
R
defined by

{\mathbf{T}}^{G}(N,R)=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{l\nmid pN}C_{c}^{\infty}({K}_{l}\backslash{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})/{K}_{l},R)\bigotimes R\left[{U}_{p}(\alpha_{i}),{U}_{p}(\beta_{j})\right]_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,s,\\
j=1\dots,r\end{subarray}}.

As is well-known,
{\mathbf{T}}^{G}(N,{\mathbb{C}})
is commutative and acts naturally on
M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U_{1}^{\ell},{\mathbb{C}})
.
More explicitly, we have

\displaystyle[{U}_{p}(\alpha_{i})f](Z,\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{ri-\sum_{l=1}^{i}k_{r+s+1-l}}}\sum_{u\in N_{r+s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\alpha_{i}^{-1}N_{r+s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\alpha_{i}}f(Z,\beta\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(u\alpha_{i}^{-1})),

\displaystyle[{U}_{p}(\beta_{j})f](Z,\beta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{sj+\sum_{l=1}^{j}k_{l}}}\sum_{u\in N_{r+s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\beta_{j}^{-1}N_{r+s}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\beta_{j}}f(Z,\beta\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(u\beta_{j}^{-1}))

for
f\in M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U_{1}^{\ell},{\mathbb{C}})
.
The Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}^{G}(N,{O})
also acts on the module
{\mathbf{S}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U_{1}^{\ell};{O})
of cusp forms over
{O}
and on the module of
p
-adic modular forms.
We put
{U}_{p}=\prod_{i=1}^{s}{U}_{p}(\alpha_{i})\prod_{j=1}^{r}{U}_{p}(\beta_{j})
and define the projector

\boldsymbol{e}=\lim_{m\to\infty}{U}_{p}^{m!}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\boldsymbol{e}$}}}

If
f\in M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U_{1}^{\ell},{\mathbb{C}})
, then we call
f
an
p
-ordinary eigenform if
f
is an eigenvector of
{U}_{p}
with eigenvalue
a_{p}(f)
such that
\iota_{p}(a_{p}(f))\in\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{p}^{\times}
.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
generated by
\Phi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{0}
with a Hecke eigenform
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
Denote the Harish-Chandra parameter of
\pi_{\infty}
by
(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{n})
.
Note that
\lambda_{j}=-k_{j}+\frac{r-s+1}{2}-j
for
1\leq j\leq r
and
\lambda_{r+i}=-k_{r+i}+\frac{r+s+1}{2}-i
for
1\leq i\leq s
.
Assume that
\pi_{p}|_{\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}
is a subquotient of a principal series
I(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
with locally algebraic characters
\iota_{p}\circ\mu_{i}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Put
\alpha_{i}=\iota_{p}(\mu_{i}(p))
.
We order
\mu_{i}
so that
\mathrm{ord}_{p}\alpha_{1}\geq\mathrm{ord}_{p}\alpha_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\mathrm{ord}_{p}\alpha_{n}
.
We say that
\pi
is
p
-ordinary with respect to
\iota_{p}
if
\mathrm{ord}_{p}\alpha_{i}=-\lambda_{n-i+1}
for
i=1,2,\dots,n
(cf. Conjecture 4.1 of
[
CPR89
]
).

The ordinary vector
h_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in\pi_{p}
constructed in §
8.4
has the eigenvalue whose
p
-adic order is smallest.
Proposition
8.6
shows that
h_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
is the unique simultaneous eigenvector whose
{U}(\alpha_{i})
and
{U}(\beta_{j})
eigenvalues are
p
-units for
1\leq i\leq s
and
1\leq j\leq r
.

3.7.
Theta functions

Let
W_{0}=E^{t}
.
Put
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}:=W_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}}={\mathbb{C}}^{t}
.
Let
\gamma_{0}\in\mathrm{M}_{t}(E)
be a matrix which satisfies
\,{}^{t}\!\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{0}=-\gamma_{0}^{c}
and such that
\iota_{\infty}(-\sqrt{-1}\gamma_{0})
is positive negative.
We regard
W_{0}
as a vector space over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and equip
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
with the real linear alternating form defined by

\ll w_{1},w_{2}\gg=\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(w_{2}^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w_{1}).

Let
L_{0}
be an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-lattice in
W_{0}
as in §
2.2
.
Put

\displaystyle L_{0}^{c}

\displaystyle=\{l^{c}\;|\;l\in L_{0}\},

\displaystyle L_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=L_{0}^{c}\gamma_{0}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$L_{0}^{\prime}$}}}

For simplicity we will impose a stronger assumption

(
C_{3}^{+}
)

\ll\;,\;\gg\text{ takes values in $2{\mathbb{Z}}$ on $L_{0}^{\prime}$}

(cf. (
3.1
)).
Define the positive definite Hermitian form on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
by

H_{0}(w_{1},w_{2})=2w_{2}^{c}\sqrt{-1}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w_{1}.

Note that

E_{0}(w_{1},w_{2}):={\rm Im}H_{0}(w_{1},w_{2})=\ll w_{1},w_{2}\gg.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$E_{0}$, $H_{0}$}}}

Fix a fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
and a positive rational number
m
such that
m{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}\subset{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Let
L_{\mathfrak{a}}
be a lattice contained in
{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime}
.
Then
mE_{0}
takes values in
2{\mathbb{Z}}
on
L_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
In particular, it is a Riemann form on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
relative to
L_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

Given
l\in L_{\mathfrak{a}}
, we define the function
e_{l}^{m}:{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
by

e_{l}^{m}(w)=e^{\pi mH_{0}\bigl(w+\frac{l}{2},l\bigl)}.

We define a line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}
on the complex abelian variety
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{C}}):={\mathbf{W}}_{0}/L_{\mathfrak{a}}
by
L_{\mathfrak{a}}\backslash({\mathbf{W}}_{0}\times{\mathbb{C}})
with the action of
l\in L_{\mathfrak{a}}
given by

l\cdot(w,x)=(w+l,e_{l}^{m}(w)x).

Let
(\rho,{L})
be a finite dimensional representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{t+1}
.
For
l\in{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
and any
{L}({\mathbb{C}})
-valued function
\Theta
on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
we define

[A_{m,\rho}(l)\Theta](w)=e^{-\pi mH_{0}\bigl(w+\frac{l}{2},l\bigl)}\rho\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
-\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!l^{c}&\hbox{\bf 1}_{t}\end{bmatrix}\right)\Theta(w+l).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$A_{m,\rho}(l)$}}}

The operators
A_{m,\rho}(l)
fulfill the basic commutation relation

(3.1)

A_{m,\rho}(l_{1})A_{m,\rho}(l_{2})=e^{\pi\sqrt{-1}mE_{0}(l_{2},l_{1})}A_{m,\rho}(l_{1}+l_{2}).

For any scalar-valued function
\theta
on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
we define

[A_{m}(l)\theta](w)=e^{-\pi mH_{0}\bigl(w+\frac{l}{2},l\bigl)}\theta(w+l).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$A_{m}(l)$}}}

Definition 3.8

(Theta functions)
.

The space
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})
consists of holomorphic functions
\theta
on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
which satisfy the functional equations
\theta(w+l)=e_{l}^{m}(w)\theta(w)
for every
l\in L_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
By definition we can identify
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})
with the space
{\rm H}^{0}({Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{C}}),{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m})
of global sections.
If
t=0
, then we define
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})={\mathbb{C}}
.

More generally, the space
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\rho}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})
consists of
{L}({\mathbb{C}})
-valued holomorphic functions
\Theta
on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
such that
A_{m,\rho}(l)\Theta=\Theta
for all
l\in L_{\mathfrak{a}}
.

Put
N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}=\biggl\{\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
*&\hbox{\bf 1}_{t}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathrm{GL}_{t+1}\biggl\}
.
Let
{L}(R)_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}
be the module of
N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}(R)
-coinvariants for the action of
\rho
in
{L}(R)
.
For
\Theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\rho}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})
we write
\Theta_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}(w)
for the image of
\Theta(w)
in
{L}({\mathbb{C}})_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}
.
Then the function
w\mapsto\Theta_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}(w)
belongs to
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{L}({\mathbb{C}})_{N^{1}_{\mathscr{H}}}
.

3.8.
Analytic Fourier-Jacobi coefficients

In the rest of this section we shall restrict ourselves to the simple case
s=1
.
Let
{P}={M}{N}
be the standard maximal parabolic subgroup of
{G}
whose unipotent radical
{N}
consists of matrices of the form

{\mathbf{n}}(w,z)=\begin{bmatrix}1&-w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}&z-\frac{1}{2}w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w\\
0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{t}&\,{}^{t}\!w\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}

with
w\in W_{0}
and
z\in{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Observe that

(3.2)

{\mathbf{n}}(w_{1},z_{1}){\mathbf{n}}(w_{2},z_{2})={\mathbf{n}}\left(w_{1}+w_{2},z_{1}+z_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\ll w_{1},w_{2}\gg\right).

Thus
{N}
is the Heisenberg group associated with
(W_{0},\ll\;,\;\gg)
.

g_{0}^{-1}{\mathbf{n}}(w,z)g_{0}={\mathbf{n}}(w\,^{t}\!g_{0}^{-1},\nu(g_{0})z)

We form the group
{S}={G}_{0}\ltimes{N}
.
For
\xi\in{\mathbb{E}}^{\times}
we put

{\mathbf{d}}(\xi)=\begin{bmatrix}\xi^{c}&0&0\\
0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{t}&0\\
0&0&\xi^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\in G({\mathbb{A}}).

For
\varPhi\in{\mathscr{A}}({G})
we put

\displaystyle\varPhi^{m}_{\beta}(b)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varPhi({\mathbf{n}}(0,z)b\beta)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)}\,\mathrm{d}z

for
b\in{P}({\mathbb{A}})
,
\beta\in{G}(\widehat{\mathbb{Q}})
and a positive rational number
m
.

Given a finite idèle
\alpha=(\alpha_{\mathfrak{q}})\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
, we write
\alpha{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
for the fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{q}}=\alpha_{\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E_{\mathfrak{q}}}
for all prime ideals
{\mathfrak{q}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
We will consider Fourier-Jacobi coefficients at the cusp labeled by
{\mathbf{d}}(\alpha)\in{M}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

Definition 3.9
.

Take a finite set
\Box
of prime numbers so that
U\supset K^{0}_{l}
if
l\notin\Box
.
Let
{\mathfrak{a}}
be a prime-to-
\Box
fractional ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Take a finite idèle
\alpha\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
so that
\alpha{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}={\mathfrak{a}}
and
\alpha_{q}=1
for all
q\in\Box
.
For
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
we write

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)

\displaystyle=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(\alpha)}(w,f),

\displaystyle\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=l_{\underline{k}}\left(\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}}(w,f)\right).

It is clear that
\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)
does not depend on the choice of
\alpha
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
f\in M_{\rho}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
.
We have

\varPhi_{\rho}(f)_{g_{0}{\mathbf{d}}(\xi)\beta}^{m}({\mathbf{n}}(w,0))\\
=e^{-\pi m\bigl(2\nu(g_{0})^{-1}\xi\xi^{c}+\frac{1}{2}H_{0}(w,w)\bigl)}\rho\left(\begin{bmatrix}\xi^{c}&0\\
-\,^{t}\!g_{0}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w^{c}&\,{}^{t}\!g_{0}\end{bmatrix},\xi\right)\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta}(w,f)

for
w\in{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
,
\xi\in E_{\infty}^{\times}
,
\beta\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
g_{0}\in{G}_{0}({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
.

Proof.

Let
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Put

\alpha={\mathbf{n}}(w,x)g_{0}{\mathbf{d}}(\xi)=\begin{bmatrix}\xi^{c}&-w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}g_{0}&\frac{\nu(g_{0})}{\xi}(x-\frac{1}{2}{w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w})\\
0&g_{0}&\frac{\nu(g_{0})}{\xi}\,^{t}\!w\\
0&0&\frac{\nu(g_{0})}{\xi}\end{bmatrix}.

We remind the reader of the assumption
\,{}^{t}\!\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{0}=-\gamma_{0}^{c}
.
Since

\displaystyle\alpha({\mathbf{i}})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\xi\xi^{c}}{\nu(g_{0})}\sqrt{-1}-\frac{w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w}{2}+x\\
\,{}^{t}\!w\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\lambda(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})

\displaystyle=\nu(g_{0})\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{\xi^{c}}&0\\
\frac{1}{\xi^{c}}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w^{c}&\,{}^{t}\!g_{0}^{-1}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\mu(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\nu(g_{0})}{\xi},

we see that
\varPhi_{\rho}(f)({\mathbf{n}}(w,x)g_{0}{\mathbf{d}}(\xi)\beta)
equals

\rho\left(\begin{bmatrix}\xi^{c}&0\\
-\,^{t}\!g_{0}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w^{c}&\,{}^{t}\!g_{0}\end{bmatrix},\xi\right)f\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\xi\xi^{c}}{\nu(g_{0})}\sqrt{-1}-\frac{w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!w}{2}+x\\
\,{}^{t}\!w\end{bmatrix},\beta\biggl)

by definition.
Now one can easily check the stated identity.
∎

Given a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-lattice
L
, we write
\widehat{L}=L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
When
L
is small, the set

\displaystyle{N}(L)

\displaystyle=\{{\mathbf{n}}(l,z)\;|\;l\in\widehat{L},\;z\in\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}\}{\@index{\@indexphrase{${N}(L)$}}}

is an open compact subgroup of
{N}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
It is worth noting that
{N}(L_{0}^{\prime})\subset K^{0}
.
For an integral ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
we put

{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}=({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}\cap{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}$}}}

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
f\in M_{\rho}^{G}(U,{\mathbb{C}})
.
Assume that
U
contains
{N}(L_{0}^{\prime})
.
Let
a\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
.
Put
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.

(1)

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)=0
unless
m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}\cup\{0\}
.

(2)

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\rho}({\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime},{\mathbb{C}})
and
\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}({\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime},{\mathbb{C}})
for
m\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{+}^{\times}
.

Proof.

If
z\in\widehat{\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})}
, then

\displaystyle\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)

\displaystyle=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(0,\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(a)^{-1}z)}(w,f)

\displaystyle=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathbf{n}}(0,z){\mathbf{d}}(a)}(w,f)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f),

which proves (1).
Given
l\in W_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{A}}
, we denote its finite and infinite parts by
l_{\mathbf{f}}
and
l_{\infty}
.
If
l\in W_{0}
, then

\varPhi_{\rho}(f)_{{\mathbf{d}}(a)}^{m}({\mathbf{n}}(w+l_{\infty},0))=e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}m\ll l_{\infty},w\gg}\varPhi_{\rho}(f)_{{\mathbf{d}}(a)}^{m}({\mathbf{n}}(w-l_{\mathbf{f}},0))

by (
3.2
).
It follows from Lemma
3.10
that

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathbf{n}}(-l_{\mathbf{f}},0){\mathbf{d}}(a)}(w,f)=e^{-\pi mH_{0}\bigl(w+\frac{l_{\infty}}{2},l_{\infty}\bigl)}\rho\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
-\gamma_{0}^{-1}\,{}^{t}\!l_{\infty}^{c}&\hbox{\bf 1}_{t}\end{bmatrix}\right)\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w+l_{\infty},f).

If
l\in{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime}
, then since
a^{-1}l_{\mathbf{f}}\in\widehat{L_{0}^{\prime}}
, we have
{\mathbf{n}}(-a^{-1}l_{\mathbf{f}},0)\in U
by assumption, which completes the proof of (2).
∎

3.9.
Integral theta functions

Suppose that
t>0
.
By the standard theory of abelian varieties with complex multiplication there is a CM abelian variety
B_{0}
defined over a number field such that
B_{0}({\mathbb{C}})={\mathbf{W}}_{0}/L_{0}^{\prime}
after extending scalars to
{\mathbb{C}}
via
\iota_{\infty}
.
Assume that
B_{0}
can be extended to an abelian scheme
{\mathscr{B}}_{0}
over
{\mathscr{O}}
.

We write
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*}
for the space of
{\mathbb{C}}
-antilinear forms on
{\mathbf{W}}_{0}
.
Define the
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map
\Lambda_{0}:{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\to{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*}
by
\Lambda_{0}(w):w^{\prime}\mapsto H_{0}(w,w^{\prime})
.
Let
{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee}
be the dual abelian scheme of
{\mathscr{B}}_{0}
.
It is important to note that
{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee}({\mathbb{C}})={\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*}/L_{0}^{\prime\perp}
, where
L_{0}^{\prime\perp}:=\{l\in{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*}\;|\;{\rm Im}l(L_{0}^{\prime})\subset{\mathbb{Z}}\}
.
Denote by
\lambda_{0}:{\mathscr{B}}_{0}\to{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee}
the polarization such that
\lambda_{0/{\mathbb{C}}}
is induced by
\Lambda_{0}
.

Let
{\mathscr{P}}_{0}
be the Poincaré bundle over
{\mathscr{B}}_{0}\times{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee}
.
After scalar extension to
{\mathbb{C}}
the complex line bundle
{\mathscr{P}}_{0/{\mathbb{C}}}
is characterized by the Riemann form

E_{{\mathscr{P}}_{0}}((z_{1},l_{1}),(z_{2},l_{2}))={\rm Im}l_{1}(z_{2})-{\rm Im}l_{2}(z_{1})

for
(z_{i},l_{i})\in{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\times{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*}

(i=1,2)
relative to
L_{0}^{\prime}\times L_{0}^{\prime\perp}
.
Set

L_{\mathfrak{a}}={\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime}\cap\Lambda_{0}^{-1}({\mathfrak{d}}_{E}{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime\perp}).

Let
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}
be the CM abelian scheme such that
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{C}})={\mathbf{W}}_{0}/L_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
Given a positive rational number
m
such that
m\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})\subset{\mathbb{Z}}
, we write
m=\sum_{i}\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(a_{i}b_{i}^{c})
with
a_{i}\in{\mathfrak{a}}^{-1}
and
b_{i}\in{\mathfrak{a}}^{-1}{\mathfrak{d}}_{E}^{-1}
.
Then the map

\displaystyle[m]

\displaystyle:{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\to{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\times{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*},

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{i}(a_{i}w,\Lambda_{0}(b_{i}w))

induces a morphism

[m]:{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}\rightarrow{\mathscr{B}}_{0}\times{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee}.

We redefine the line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}
by

{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}:=[m]^{*}{\mathscr{P}}_{0}.

The Riemann form associated with the complex line bunle
{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}{}_{/{\mathbb{C}}}
is given by

\displaystyle E_{{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}}(w_{1},w_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}E_{{\mathscr{P}}_{0}}((a_{i}w_{1},\lambda_{0}(b_{i}w_{1})),(a_{i}w_{2},\lambda_{0}(b_{i}w_{2})))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}(E_{0}(b_{i}w_{1},a_{i}w_{2})-E_{0}(b_{i}w_{2},a_{i}w_{1}))=mE_{0}(w_{1},w_{2}).

We consider the
{\mathscr{O}}
-module of global sections
{\rm H}^{0}({Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ},{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m})\subset{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})
.

Definition 3.12
.

For an
{\mathscr{O}}
-algebra
{O}
we put

{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{O}):={\rm H}^{0}({Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ},{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}}{O}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{O})$}}}

This provides an integral structure of
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})
in the sense that

{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{O})\otimes_{O}{\mathbb{C}}={\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathbb{C}})

when
{O}
is a subring of
{\mathbb{C}}
.
When
t=0
, we set
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{O})={O}
.

3.10.
Arithmetic Fourier-Jacobi coefficients

Recall that

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{o}}_{E}e_{1},

\displaystyle X^{\vee}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{o}}_{E}e_{n}.

Let
\alpha
be a finite idèle
\alpha\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
such that
\alpha_{q}=1
for all
q\in\Box
.
Put

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle={\mathbf{d}}(\alpha),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{a}}

\displaystyle=\alpha{\mathfrak{o}}_{E},

\displaystyle Y_{\beta}

\displaystyle=Y\beta^{-1}=({\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}e_{1},

\displaystyle X_{\beta}^{\vee}

\displaystyle=X^{\vee}\beta^{-1}={\mathfrak{a}}e_{n}.

Let

X_{\beta}=\{y\in Y_{E}\;|\;\langle y,X_{\beta}^{\vee}\rangle_{r,1}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}\}={\mathfrak{d}}_{E}^{-1}({\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}e_{1}

be the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-dual of
X_{\beta}^{\vee}
.
We denote by
i:Y_{\beta}=({\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}e_{1}\hookrightarrow X_{\beta}={\mathfrak{d}}_{E}^{-1}({\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}e_{1}
the inclusion map.
Let
I_{\beta}
be the subgroup in
X_{\beta}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}Y_{\beta}
generated by

\displaystyle x\otimes i(x^{\prime})-x^{\prime}\otimes i(x);

\displaystyle xb\otimes y-x\otimes b^{c}y

\displaystyle(x,x^{\prime}\in X_{\beta},\;y\in Y_{\beta},\;b\in{\mathfrak{o}}_{E})

Let
{\mathscr{S}}_{\beta}=S(X_{\beta}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}Y_{\beta})
be the maximal free quotient of the group
X_{\beta}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}Y_{\beta}/I_{\beta}
.
The
{\mathbb{Z}}
-dual
{\mathscr{S}}_{\beta}^{\vee}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}({\mathscr{S}}_{\beta},{\mathbb{Z}})
is the module of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-valued symmetric and Hermitian bilinear forms on
{\mathfrak{d}}_{E}^{-1}({\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}\times({\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}
.
Therefore
{\mathscr{S}}_{\beta}^{\vee}
is isomorphic to the fractional ideal
\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})=({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{c})\cap{\mathbb{Q}}
, and hence
{\mathscr{S}}_{\beta}
is the fractional ideal
\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathfrak{a}})^{-1}
.
Let

{\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\ell}=\biggl\{\sum_{0\leq m\in p^{-\ell}{\mathscr{S}}_{\beta}}\theta^{m}\,q^{m}\;\Big|\;\theta^{m}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathscr{O}})\biggl\}

be the ring of formal
q
-series with
m
th coefficients in
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathscr{O}})
.

Put
U_{P}^{\beta}=\beta U\beta^{-1}\cap{G}_{0}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
Let
({\mathscr{B}}_{0},\bar{\lambda}_{0},\iota_{0},\bar{\eta})
be the quadruple associated to
[\hbox{\bf 1}_{{G}_{0}}]\in S_{{G}_{0}}(U_{P}^{\beta})
, where
\hbox{\bf 1}_{{G}_{0}}
is the identity element of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
Following §2.7.4 of
[
Hsi14
]
, we consider the group scheme

{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}=\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(X_{\beta},{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee})\times_{\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(Y_{\beta},{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee})}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(Y_{\beta},{\mathscr{B}}_{0}),

which consists of pairs
(c,c^{\vee})\in\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(X_{\beta},{\mathscr{B}}_{0}^{\vee})\times\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(Y_{\beta},{\mathscr{B}}_{0})
such that
c(i(y))=\lambda_{0}(c^{\vee}(y))
for
y\in Y_{\beta}
.
After scalar extension to
{\mathbb{C}}
we have

{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})=\{(w^{\prime},w)\in{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{*}/{\mathfrak{d}}_{E}{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime\perp}\times{\mathbf{W}}_{0}/{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime}\;|\;w^{\prime}-\Lambda_{0}(w)\in{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime\perp}\}.

We redefine
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}
as the connected component of
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
Then
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}({\mathbb{C}})={\mathbf{W}}_{0}/L_{\mathfrak{a}}
in view of the exact sequence

0\to{\mathbf{W}}_{0}/L_{\mathfrak{a}}\to{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})\to{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime\perp}/({\mathfrak{d}}_{E}{\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime\perp}+\Lambda_{0}({\mathfrak{a}}L_{0}^{\prime}))\to 0.

Let
\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta}
be an
{\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta}
-quintuple of level
U
, called the Mumford quadruple at the cusp labeled by
\beta
.
Fix a
p^{\ell}
-level structure
\jmath_{\mathscr{M}}
on
{\mathscr{M}}_{\beta}
lifted from the tautological
p^{\ell}
-level structure
\jmath_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}
on
{\mathscr{B}}_{0}
and the natural
one on
X_{\beta}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.
Then
(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},\jmath_{\mathscr{M}})
is an
{\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\ell}
-quintuple, called the Mumford quintuple.

Let
\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{W_{0}}=\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{W_{0}}(\varSigma)
be the ordered basis of
\Omega_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}
.
There are an
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{O}}
-basis
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}
of
\Omega_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}
and a pair
(\Omega_{{G}_{0},\infty},\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}})\in\mathrm{GL}_{t}({\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{t}({\mathbb{W}})
such that

\Omega_{{G}_{0},\infty}\mathrm{d}\underline{z}_{W_{0}}=\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}=\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}}\boldsymbol{\omega}(\jmath_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}){\@index{\@indexphrase{$\Omega_{\infty}$}}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}$}}}

(see §2.8 of
[
Hsi14
]
), where
{\mathbb{W}}={\mathbb{W}}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
is the
p
-adic completion of the ring of integers of the maximal unramified extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Assuming that
\Omega_{{G}_{0},\infty}
and
\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}}
can be taken to be diagonal, we write

\displaystyle\Omega_{{G}_{0},\infty}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Omega_{1,\infty}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\Omega_{t,\infty}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\Omega_{1,{\mathfrak{p}}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\Omega_{t,{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{bmatrix}.

There is an exact sequence of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-modules

0\to\Omega_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}\to\Omega_{{\mathscr{M}}_{\beta}}\to\Omega_{X_{\beta}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to 0.

Let
\mathrm{d}^{\times}t
be the canonical
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
-basis of
\Omega_{X_{\beta}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
.
We denote a canonical lifting of
e_{\varSigma^{c}}\mathrm{d}^{\times}t
in
\Omega_{{\mathscr{M}}_{\beta}}
by
\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{-}
.
Choose a lifting
\mathrm{d}^{\times}t^{\prime}_{+}
of
e_{\varSigma}\mathrm{d}^{\times}t
in
\Omega_{{\mathscr{M}}_{\beta}}
.
Then
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}}:=(\mathrm{d}^{\times}t^{\prime}_{+},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}};\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{-})\in{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta}}
.

When
f\in{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\underline{k}}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
with an
{\mathscr{O}}
-subalgebra
R
of
{\mathbb{C}}
, we can consider the analytic Fourier-Jacobi expansion of
f(Z,g):=f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z))\in M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
(see Lemma
3.4
).
We have the following important result:

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
f\in{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\underline{k}}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
.
Then

\prod_{j=1}^{t}\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{j,\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\biggl)^{k_{j+1}}\cdot\;\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},R).

Proof.

Let
h\in{\mathbf{H}}
.
We evaluate
f
at
(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}})
to obtain

f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}})\in L_{\underline{k}}({\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\ell})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}}R.

Its image
f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}})_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}
in
L_{\underline{k}}({\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\ell})_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}}R
only depends on the choice
of
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}}
.
We write

f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}})_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}=\sum_{m}\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,h}(f)_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}\,q^{m},

where

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,h}(f)_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}\in L_{\underline{k}}(R)_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}}{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathscr{O}}).

We presently have the following relation between analytic and arithmetic Fourier-Jacobi coefficients:

(3.3)

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{h\beta}(f)_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}=\rho_{\underline{k}}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-1}\Omega_{{G}_{0},\infty}\end{bmatrix},1\biggl)\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,h}(f)_{N_{\mathscr{H}}^{1}}

(cf.
[
Lan12
]
,
[
Hsi14
, (3.12)]
).
We complete the proof by letting
h=1
and applying the evaluation map
l_{\underline{k}}
.
∎

We define the Fourier-Jacobi expansion of a
p
-adic modular form
{F}\in V({G},U)
at the
p
-adic cusp
(\beta,h)
by

{F}(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}})=\sum_{m}\widehat{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,h}({F})\,q^{m}\in{\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\infty},

where
{\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\infty}=\varprojlim_{\ell}{\mathscr{R}}^{0}_{\beta,\ell}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
When
{F}
is the
p
-adic avatar of a classical modular form, we have the following relation between analytic and
p
-adic Fourier-Jacobi coefficients:

Corollary 3.14
.

Let
f\in{\mathbf{M}}^{G}_{\underline{k}}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
.
Then

\widehat{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,1}(\widehat{f}_{0})=\prod_{j=1}^{t}\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{j,\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{j,{\mathfrak{p}}}}\biggl)^{k_{j+1}}\cdot\;\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}.

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{\omega}(\jmath_{\mathscr{M}})=(\mathrm{d}^{\times}t^{\prime}_{+},\boldsymbol{\omega}(\jmath_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}});\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{-})
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{0}(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}})

\displaystyle=l_{\underline{k}}(f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}(\jmath_{\mathscr{M}})))

\displaystyle=l_{\underline{k}}(f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},(\mathrm{d}^{\times}t^{\prime}_{+},\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{B}}_{0}};\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{-})))

\displaystyle=l_{\underline{k}}\biggl(\rho_{\underline{k}}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}}\end{bmatrix},1\biggl)f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}})\biggl)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{t}\Omega_{j,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-k_{j+1}}\cdot\;l_{\underline{k}}(f(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{\beta},h^{-1}\jmath_{\mathscr{M}},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\mathscr{M}})),

from which we get
\widehat{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,h}(\widehat{f}_{0})=\prod_{j=1}^{t}\Omega_{j,{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-k_{j+1}}\cdot\;l_{\underline{k}}(\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta,h}(f))
for
h\in{\mathbf{H}}
.
The proof is complete by (
3.3
).
∎

4.
Hida theory for
\mathrm{GU}(r,1)

In this section we continue to assume
s=1
and
n=r+1
.

4.1.
Open compact subgroups

Let
l
be a split prime, i.e.,
l{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}={\mathfrak{l}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{c}
.
Then
V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}=V_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus V_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{c}}
, where
V_{\mathfrak{l}}
and
V_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{c}}
are vector spaces over
{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}
and in duality under
\langle\;,\rangle_{r,s}
.
The projection
g\mapsto g|_{V_{\mathfrak{l}}}
gives an isomorphism
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}:G({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
Fix a maximal compact subgroup
{K}=\prod_{q}{K}_{q}
of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}({K}_{l})=\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{l}}})
for every split prime
l
.

Define open subgroups of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{l}}})
by

\displaystyle{K}_{0}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{l}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{ij})\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{l}}})\;|\;g_{nj}\in{\mathfrak{l}}^{k}\text{ for }j=1,2,\dots,n-1\},

\displaystyle{K}_{1}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{l}}^{k})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{ij})\in{K}_{0}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{l}}^{k})\;|\;g_{nn}-1\in{\mathfrak{l}}^{k}\}.

Fix a positive integer
N
whose prime factors are split in
E
.
We take an ideal
{\mathfrak{N}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let

\displaystyle{K}_{0}({\mathfrak{N}})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{q})\in{K}\;|\;\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}(g_{l})\in{K}_{0}^{(n)}(N{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{l}}})\text{ for }l|N\},

\displaystyle{K}_{1}({\mathfrak{N}})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{q})\in{K}\;|\;\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}(g_{l})\in{K}_{1}^{(n)}(N{\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{l}}})\text{ for }l|N\}

be open compact subgroups of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

For each positive integer
\ell
we define open subgroups of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{p}}})
by

\displaystyle{I}_{0}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{ij})\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,{\mathfrak{p}}})\;|\;g_{ij}\in{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}\text{ for }i>j\},

\displaystyle{I}_{1}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{ij})\in{I}_{0}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})\;|\;g_{ii}-1\in{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}\text{ for }i=1,2,\dots,n\}

and open compact subgroups of
{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

\displaystyle{K}_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{q})\in{K}_{0}({\mathfrak{N}})\;|\;\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{p})\in{I}_{0}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})\},

\displaystyle{K}_{1}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})

\displaystyle=\{(g_{q})\in{K}_{1}({\mathfrak{N}})\;|\;\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(g_{p})\in{I}_{1}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})\}.

Let
{O}
be a
p
-adic ring.
Put
\Lambda={O}\llbracket{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\rrbracket
.
We say that an
{O}
-algebra homomorphism
Q:\Lambda\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
is locally algebraic if its restriction to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
is of the form
Q(t)=t^{k_{Q}}\epsilon_{Q}(t)
with
k_{Q}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and a finite order character
\epsilon_{Q}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda}
be the set of locally algebraic homomorphisms of
\Lambda
.

Put
\Lambda_{n}={O}\llbracket{T_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\rrbracket
.
Let
{R}
be a normal ring finite flat over
\Lambda_{n}
.
We say that an
{O}
-algebra homomorphism
\underline{Q}:{R}\to\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
is locally algebraic if its restriction to
T_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is of the form
\underline{Q}(t)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}(t_{i})
with
Q_{i}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda}
for
t=\mathrm{diag}(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})
.
We write
\underline{Q}=(Q_{1},Q_{2},\dots,Q_{n})
and call
k_{\underline{Q}}:=(k_{Q_{1}},\dots,k_{Q_{n}})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}
the weight of
\underline{Q}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{R}
be the set of locally algebraic homomorphisms of
{R}
.

For
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{R}
we put
\ell=\max\{1,c(\epsilon_{Q_{1}}),c(\epsilon_{Q_{2}}),\dots,c(\epsilon_{Q_{n}})\}
and define finite order characters of
{K}_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})
by

\epsilon_{\underline{Q}}(t_{p})=\epsilon_{Q_{1}}(t_{p,1})\epsilon_{Q_{2}}(t_{p,2})\cdots\epsilon_{Q_{n}}(t_{p,n}),

where
\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}(t_{p})=\mathrm{diag}(t_{p,1},t_{p,2},\dots,t_{p,n})
with
t_{p,i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
.
Let
\wp_{\underline{Q}}
be the ideal of
{R}
corresponding to
\underline{Q}
and
{R}(\underline{Q})
the image of
{R}
under
\underline{Q}
.

4.2.
\Lambda_{n}
-adic families on
\mathrm{GU}(r,1)

Let
F
be a number field which contains
E^{\prime}
and the values of
\chi
.
We denote the
p
-adic closure of
\iota_{p}(\iota_{\infty}({\mathfrak{o}}_{F}))
in
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
by
{O}
.
Put
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}={\mathfrak{o}}_{F,(p)}
.
For a character
\chi
of
{K}_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})
we define

\displaystyle S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathscr{O}}_{F})

\displaystyle=\{f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}({K}_{1}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}),{\mathscr{O}}_{F})\;|\;r(u)f=\chi(u)^{-1}f\;(u\in{K}_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}))\},

\displaystyle S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})

\displaystyle=S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{\mathscr{O}}_{F})\otimes{O}.

For a local ring
R
finite flat over
{O}
we set

\boldsymbol{e}S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;R)=\boldsymbol{e}S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})\otimes R.

Let
\Box
be the set of prime factors of
pN
.
We denote the class number of
E
by
h_{E}
.
Fix a set
C_{E}:=\{{\mathfrak{a}}_{1},{\mathfrak{a}}_{2},\dots,{\mathfrak{a}}_{h_{E}}\}
of representatives of the ideal class group of
E
which consists of prime-to-
pN
fractional ideals of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Take a finite idèle
\alpha_{i}\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
so that
\alpha_{i}{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}={\mathfrak{a}}_{i}
and
\alpha_{q}=1
for all
q\in\Box
for
i=1,2,\dots,h_{E}
.

We consider the finite set

\underline{{D}}(U)={G}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})/U

of double cosets.
We can take the set
{D}(U)
of representatives for
\underline{{D}}(U)
from
{M}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Box})
by strong approximation in special unitary groups.
For
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})
and
g\in{D}(U)
we write

\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{g}(f)_{0}(w)=l_{\underline{k}}\left(\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{g}(w,f)\right)=\ell_{\underline{k}}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}\otimes\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{g}(w,f)\right).

One can similarly define the CM abelian scheme
{Z}_{g}^{\circ}
and the line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}_{g}^{m}
and extend Proposition
3.13
and Corollary
3.14
to Fourier-Jacobi coefficients
\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{g}(f)_{0}
at those cusps (see §2.7.4 of
[
Hsi14
]
).

Remark 4.1
.

If
n
is even and
\nu(U)=\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\times}
, then we can take
{D}(U)
from the set
\{{\mathbf{d}}(\alpha_{i})\}_{i=1}^{h_{E}}
.
If
n
is odd and
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})
has central character, then
f
is uniquely determined by
\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}}^{m}(w,f)
for
m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}_{i}}^{+}
and
i=1,2,\dots,h_{E}
(see Remark
2.1
).

Taking Corollary
3.14
into account, we define the notion of
p
-adic families of cusp forms in terms of Fourier-Jacobi coefficients.

Definition 4.2
.

An ordinary
\Lambda_{n}
-adic cusp form
\boldsymbol{f}
on
{G}
of tame level
{\mathfrak{N}}
and nebentypus
\chi
is a collection of theta functions
\Theta_{g}^{m}\in{\rm H}^{0}({Z}_{g}^{\circ},{\mathfrak{L}}_{g}^{m})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}}\Lambda_{n}
indexed by
g\in{D}(U)
and
0<m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{g}

such that for each sufficiently regular weight
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda_{n}}
there is a unique ordinary Picard cusp form

\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}\in\boldsymbol{e}S_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{G}(p^{\ell_{\underline{Q}}}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi\epsilon_{\underline{Q}};\Lambda_{n}(\underline{Q}))

which satisfies

\prod_{j=1}^{r-1}\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{j,\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{j,{\mathfrak{p}}}}\biggl)^{k_{Q_{j+1}}}\cdot\;\mathrm{FJ}_{g}^{m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}=\underline{Q}(\Theta_{g}^{m}).

Remark 4.3
.

(1)

The above definition of
\Lambda_{n}
-adic forms is a restatement of
[
Hsi14
, Definition 4.24]
.

(2)

When
r=2
, we will write

\displaystyle\Omega_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{{G}_{0},\infty}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times},

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{{G}_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}}\in{\mathbb{W}}^{\times}

for the complex and
p
-adic CM periods.
We remark that
(\Omega_{\infty},\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}})
are precisely the periods defined in
[
HT93
, (4.4 a,b) page 211]
up to a
p
-adic unit.

4.3.
Classicality

Let
{\mathbf{S}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\Lambda_{n})
be the
\Lambda_{n}
-module of ordinary
\Lambda_{n}
-adic cusp forms on
{G}
of tame level
{\mathfrak{N}}
and nebentypus
\chi
, which is equipped with the action of
\Lambda_{n}
-adic Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}(N,\chi,\Lambda_{n})
.
The ordinary projector
\boldsymbol{e}=\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}{U}_{p}^{m!}
converges in
\mathrm{End}_{\Lambda_{n}}({\mathbf{S}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\Lambda_{n}))
.
Put

{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\Lambda_{n})=\boldsymbol{e}{\mathbf{S}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\Lambda_{n}).

Let
{\mathbf{T}}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N,\chi,\Lambda_{n})
be the image of
{\mathbf{T}}(N,\chi,\Lambda_{n})
in
\mathrm{End}_{\Lambda_{n}}({\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\Lambda_{n}))
.

For a normal ring
{R}
finite flat over
\Lambda_{n}
we put

{\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})={\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\Lambda_{n})\otimes_{\Lambda_{n}}{R}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})$}}}

The set of classical points is defined by

{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{R}=\{\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{R}\;|\;k_{Q_{1}}\leq\dots\leq k_{Q_{r}}<k_{Q_{r+1}}-r\}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{R}$}}}

Theorem 4.4

(Control theorem)
.

(1)

{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})
is an
{R}
-module of finite rank.

(2)

For
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm cls}_{R}
the specialization induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})/\wp_{\underline{Q}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\boldsymbol{e}S_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi\epsilon_{\underline{Q}};{R}(\underline{Q})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}

\displaystyle\mapsto\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}$}}}

Proof.

Part (1) is proved by Hida (see also Theorem 4.21 of
[
Hsi14
]
).
Part (2) is also due to Hida when
k_{\underline{Q}}
is very regular (c.f. Corollary 4.23 of
[
Hsi14
]
).
In general, the specialization
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
of
\boldsymbol{f}
at
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{R}
is an ordinary
p
-adic modular form.
Ordinary
p
-adic modular forms of cohomological weight are overconvergent by Proposition 5.4 and Théorème A.3 of
[
Pil12
]
, and any overconvergent modular form of weight
k_{\underline{Q}}=(k_{Q_{1}},\dots,k_{Q_{r}};k_{Q_{r+1}})
with
k_{Q_{r+1}}-k_{Q_{r}}>r
is classical by Théorème and Remarque, p.977 of
[
BPS16
]
.
We therefore find that
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}\in\boldsymbol{e}S_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi\epsilon_{\underline{Q}};{R}(\underline{Q}))
if
k_{Q_{r+1}}-k_{Q_{r}}>r
.
∎

Definition 4.5

(Hida families)
.

We call a non-zero
{R}
-adic cusp form
\boldsymbol{f}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})
an
{R}
-adic Hida family if
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
is a simultaneous eigenform of Hecke operators away from
pN
for
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm cls}_{R}
.
Let

{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}=\{\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\rm cls}_{R}\;|\;\underline{Q}(\boldsymbol{f})\neq 0\}{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}$}}}

be a Zariski dense subset of the rigid generic fiber of
\mathrm{Spf}{R}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Assume that the space of cuspidal automorphic forms on
{G}
has multiplicity one.
If
\boldsymbol{f}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})
is an
{R}
-adic Hida family, then
\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}
(see Remark
3.6
) generates an irreducible
p
-ordinary cuspidal automorphic representation of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
for
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\pi}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\tilde{\pi}_{v}
be an irreducible constituent of the cuspidal automorphic representation generated by
\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}
.
If
l
and
Np
are coprime, then
\tilde{\pi}_{l}
is determined by the Hecke eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
.
Thus
\tilde{\pi}
belongs to the A-packet associated to these eigenvalues.
Moreover,
\tilde{\pi}_{q}
is uniquely determined for each prime factor
q
of
N
by the assumption on
N
as the associated local A-packet is a singleton for each split prime.
Since
\tilde{\pi}_{\infty}
is the holomorphic discrete series with highest weight
-k_{\underline{Q}}
, the equivalence class
\tilde{\pi}
is determined by
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
.
Thus
\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}
generates an irreducible representation of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
by the multiplicity one for unitary groups.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

When
n=2
, the multiplicity one holds in view of the accidental isomorphism (see Remark
5.2
).
Moreover, the group
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
has multiplicity one (see §6.2 of
[
LM15
]
).
When
n=3
, the multiplicity one holds by Theorem 13.3.1 of
[
Rog90
]
and Proposition
5.3
(
2
) below.

If
\boldsymbol{f}
is a Hida family, then
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
is a simultaneous eigenform of the operators
{U}_{p}(\alpha_{1})
and
{U}_{p}(\beta_{j})

(1\leq j\leq r)
for
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}
, and we denote by
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}
the cuspidal automorphic representation of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
associated to
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
, which is
p
-ordinary with respect to
\iota_{p}
(see Definition
3.7
).
Let
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}}
be the largest ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that there exists a non-zero automorphic form in
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}
fixed by
{K}_{1}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}})
.
Such an automorphic form corresponds to an essential vector at each split prime
l
(see Definition
8.2
).

Definition 4.8
.

The subset
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime\prime}
consists of
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}
such that
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
is new outside
p
, namely,
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
corresponds to an essential vector at every split prime
l\neq p
.
Let us fix a factorization
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q},v}
.
For
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime\prime}
we can write
\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}=\otimes_{v}W_{v}
by multiplicity one for new and ordinary vectors (see
[
Hid04
, Theorem 5.3]
), where
W_{p}=h_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}},p}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q},p}
is the normalized ordinary vector.
We call
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}^{\circ}\in S_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}},\chi\epsilon_{\underline{Q}};{R}(\underline{Q}))
the newform associated to
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
if
\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}^{\circ})_{0}=W_{p}^{\circ}\otimes\bigotimes_{v\neq p}W_{v}
, where
W_{p}\in\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q},p}
is the normalized essential vector.

4.4.
Algebraic representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}\times\mathrm{GL}_{1}

In our later discussion we will specifically consider the case in which
r=2
and
s=1
.
We give an explicit expression of the schematical module
L_{\underline{k}}
and the pairing
\ell_{\underline{k}}
introduced in §
3.3
in this case.

For a commutative ring
R
of characteristic
0
and a non-negative integer
\kappa
we write
{L}_{\kappa}(R)
for the space of two variable homogeneous polynomials of degree
\kappa
over
R
.
The group
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)
acts on this space
{L}_{\kappa}(R)
by

\rho_{\kappa}(\alpha)P(X,Y)=P((X,Y)\alpha).

For
\lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}
we will write
\rho_{(\kappa+\lambda,\lambda)}=\rho_{\kappa}\otimes\det^{\lambda}
.
Define the perfect pairing

\ell_{\kappa}:{L}_{\kappa}(R)\otimes{L}_{\kappa}(R)\to R

by

(4.1)

\ell_{\kappa}(X^{j}Y^{\kappa-j}\otimes X^{\prime i}Y^{\prime\kappa-i})=\begin{cases}(-1)^{i}\binom{\kappa}{i}^{-1}&\text{if $i+j=\kappa$, }\\
0&\text{if $i+j\neq\kappa$. }\end{cases}

Let
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
be a positive definite Hermitian maxtrix of size
2
.
We view
\mathrm{U}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
as a subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
and regard
{L}_{\kappa}({\mathbb{C}})
as an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{U}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
of dimension
\kappa+1
.
If
\sigma
is an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{U}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
of dimension
\kappa+1
, then there is an integer
\lambda
such that
\sigma\simeq\rho_{(\kappa+\lambda,\lambda)}
.
Let

\displaystyle\underline{k}

\displaystyle=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{3},

\displaystyle k_{1}\leq k_{2}\leq k_{3}.

We write
{L}_{\underline{k}}(R)
for an
R
-module on which
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)
acts as the representation
\rho_{(k_{2},k_{1})}
.
We define the representation
\rho_{\underline{k}}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)\times R^{\times}
on
{L}_{\underline{k}}(R)
by

\rho_{\underline{k}}(\alpha,\beta)=\rho_{(k_{2},k_{1})}(\,^{t}\!\alpha^{-1})\beta^{k_{3}}=(\det\alpha)^{-k_{1}}\rho_{\kappa}(\,^{t}\!\alpha^{-1})\beta^{k_{3}}

for
\alpha\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)
and
\beta\in R^{\times}
, where we put
\kappa=k_{2}-k_{1}
.

When we view
{L}_{\underline{k}}({\mathbb{C}})
as a representation of the maximal compact subgroup
{K}_{\infty}
of
\mathrm{U}(2,1)
, it has highest weight
(k_{2},k_{1};k_{3})
(cf. (
A.2
)).
Define the representation
\rho_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)\times R^{\times}
on
{L}_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}(R)={L}_{\kappa}(R)
by

\displaystyle\rho_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}(\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle=(\det\alpha)^{k_{1}}\rho_{\kappa}(J\alpha J^{-1})\beta^{-k_{3}},

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix}.

We write
\ell_{\underline{k}}=\ell_{\kappa}
.
Then for
\alpha\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)
and
\beta\in R^{\times}
and
P,Q\in{L}_{\kappa}(R)

\ell_{\underline{k}}(\rho_{\underline{k}}(\alpha,\beta)P\otimes\rho_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}(\alpha,\beta)Q)=\ell_{\underline{k}}(P\otimes Q).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\ell_{\underline{k}}$}}}

Remark 4.9
.

When
r=2
, the isomorphism
{L}_{\underline{k}}(R)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}L_{\underline{k}}(R)
is given by

P\mapsto f_{P}(g)=(\det g)^{k_{1}}P((0,1)g).

Observe that the isomorphism
{L}_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(R)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(R)
sends
X^{k_{2}-k_{1}}
to
{\mathbf{v}}_{\underline{k}}
.

Let

{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}}=(X\otimes Y^{\prime}-Y\otimes X^{\prime})^{\kappa}\in{L}_{\underline{k}}(R)\otimes{L}^{\vee}_{\underline{k}}(R){\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}}$}}}

be the vector
{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}}
fixed by the diagonal action of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(R)
.
One can easily show that

(4.2)

\ell_{\underline{k}}({\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}})=\dim\rho_{\underline{k}}=\kappa+1.

4.5.
The restriction of Picard modular forms

Recall the morphism
\iota_{W}:I^{0}_{H}({U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell})\to I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell})
constructed in §
2.9
.
Let
\rho
be an irreducible representation of
{\mathscr{H}}=\mathrm{GL}_{r}\times\mathrm{GL}_{1}
.
Assume that the restriction of
\rho
from
{\mathscr{H}}
to
{\mathscr{H}}^{\prime}=\mathrm{GL}_{r-1}\times\mathrm{GL}_{1}
has an irreducible quotient isomorphic to
\rho^{\prime}
.
We denote by
\omega_{\rho^{\prime}}
the automorphic sheaf of weight
\rho^{\prime}
over
I^{0}_{H}({U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell})
.
Fix a projection
\varpi_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}:\rho\to\rho^{\prime}
.
The morphism
\varpi_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}
induces a morphism
r_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}:\iota_{W}^{*}\omega_{\rho}\to\omega_{\rho^{\prime}}
of sheaves over
I^{0}_{H}({U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell})
.
See Section 6 of
[
CEF+16
]
for a general framework for the unitary Shimura varieties.
Section 2 of
[
Har21a
]
studies the pullback

r_{\rho,\rho^{\prime}}\circ\iota_{W}^{*}:{\mathbf{M}}_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)={\rm H}^{0}(I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell}_{1})_{/R},\omega_{\rho})\to{\rm H}^{0}(I^{0}_{H}(({U}_{L_{1}})_{1}^{\ell})_{/R},\omega_{\rho^{\prime}})

of global sections of the automorphic sheaves, which corresponds to the restriction
M_{\rho}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},{\mathbb{C}})\to M_{\rho^{\prime}}^{H}(({U}_{L_{1}})_{1}^{\ell},{\mathbb{C}})
in view of (
2.7
) if
R={\mathbb{C}}
.

We will explicate this map in the case when

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle=2,

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle=\rho_{\underline{k}},

\displaystyle\underline{k}

\displaystyle=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3}),

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\rho_{\underline{k}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\underline{k}^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=(k_{1}+i;k_{3})

for
i=0,1,2,\dots,k_{2}-k_{1}
.
Henceforth we will abbreviate

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle:=k_{2}-k_{1},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle:={\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{H}}

\displaystyle:={\mathfrak{D}}_{1,1}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathfrak{D}},{\mathfrak{H}}$}}}

We write
f\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
as

f(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}f_{i}(\underline{A},j,\boldsymbol{\omega})X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}

for
R
-quintuples
(\underline{A},j)\in I^{0}_{G}(U^{\ell})
.
Let

f(Z,g):=f(\underline{{A}}(V)_{g}(Z),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{g}(Z))\in M_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)

(see Lemma
3.4
).
For
\beta\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
m\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}
we will write

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta}(w,f)=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\beta}(f)_{i}(w)X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}.

We now consider the pullback of
f
via the morphism
\iota_{W}
.

Definition 4.10
.

Fix a fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
E
.
Let
E_{0}({\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathbb{C}}(\varSigma^{c})/\iota_{\infty}^{c}({\mathfrak{a}})
be a complex elliptic curve, which can be descended to an elliptic curve
E_{0}
over a number field
F
by the theory of complex multiplication.
Since
E_{0}
