has potentially good reduction everywhere at all finite places of
F
by Proposition VII.5.5 and Corollary C.11.1.1(b) of
[
Sil09
]
, by enlarging the base field
F
, we can further descend
E_{0}
to an abelian scheme
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}
over the ring
{\mathfrak{o}}_{F,{\mathfrak{P}}}
obtained by the localization
{\mathfrak{o}}_{F}
of at some prime ideal
{\mathfrak{P}}
above
p
.
Fix a Néron differential
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}
of
{\mathscr{E}}_{0}
defined over
{\mathfrak{o}}_{F,{\mathfrak{P}}}
.
The complex period
\Omega_{\infty}
is an element of
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}/\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
defined by
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0}=\Omega_{\infty}\mathrm{d}w
, where
\mathrm{d}w
is the standard invariant differential on
{\mathbb{C}}(\varSigma^{c})/\iota_{\infty}^{c}({\mathfrak{a}})
.

Define
\iota_{W}^{\prime}:{E}_{\underline{B}}\to{E}_{\iota_{W}(\underline{B})}
by
\iota_{W}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})=(\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
f\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U^{\ell}_{1},R)
.
If
R
is an
{\mathscr{O}}
-subalgebra of
{\mathbb{C}}
, then

(2\pi\sqrt{-1}/\Omega_{\infty})^{i-k_{2}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\beta}(f)_{i}(0)\in R

for
m\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}
,
\beta\in{G}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, and
i=0,1,2,\dots,k_{2}-k_{1}
.

Proof.

Let
R
be a
\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{(p)}
-algebra.
For an
R
-point
(\underline{B},\jmath)\in I^{0}_{H}({U}_{L_{1}}^{\ell})
and one forms
\boldsymbol{\omega}^{\pm}\in{E}^{\pm}_{\underline{B}}
defined over
R
, we have

f_{i}(\iota_{W}(\underline{B},\jmath),\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})\in R.

Given
a,b,c\in R^{\times}
, we have

\displaystyle f(\iota_{W}(\underline{B},\jmath),a\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+},b\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0};c\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{\underline{k}}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a^{-1}&0\\
0&b^{-1}\end{bmatrix},c^{-1}\biggl)f(\iota_{W}(\underline{B},\jmath),\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k_{2}-k_{1}}a^{k_{1}+i}b^{k_{2}-i}c^{-k_{3}}f_{i}(\iota_{W}(\underline{B},\jmath),\boldsymbol{\omega}^{+},\boldsymbol{\omega}_{0};\boldsymbol{\omega}^{-})X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}.

Thus
((\underline{B},\jmath),\boldsymbol{\omega})\mapsto f_{i}(\iota_{W}(\underline{B},\jmath),\iota_{W}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}))
is a geometric modular form on
H
of weight
(k_{1}+i;k_{3})
.

It follows from (
2.7
) that

\displaystyle f_{i}(\jmath(\tau),\iota(h))

\displaystyle=f_{i}(\underline{{A}}(V)_{\iota(h)}(\jmath(\tau)),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{\iota(h)}(\jmath(\tau)))

\displaystyle=f_{i}(\iota_{W}(\underline{{A}}(W)_{h}(\tau)),\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}^{+}(\tau)_{1},2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{d}w;\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}^{-}(\tau)_{2})

\displaystyle=(2\pi\sqrt{-1}/\Omega_{\infty})^{k_{2}-i}f_{i}(\iota_{W}(\underline{{A}}(W)_{h}(\tau)),\iota_{W}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{h}(\tau))).

Let
(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{h},\jmath_{{\mathscr{M}}_{h}})
be the Mumford quintuple at the cusp labeled by
h
, and
\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{h}}=(\mathrm{d}^{\times}t^{\prime}_{+};\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{-})\in{E}_{\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{h}}
.
Since the analytic and algebraic Fourier expansions coincide, we have

(2\pi\sqrt{-1}/\Omega_{\infty})^{i-k_{2}}\sum_{m}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\iota(h)}(0)_{i}q^{m}=f_{i}(\iota_{W}(\underline{{\mathscr{M}}}_{h},\jmath_{{\mathscr{M}}_{h}}),\iota_{W}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\omega}_{{\mathscr{M}}_{h}}))\in R\llbracket{q}\rrbracket,

which completes our proof.
∎

Part II
Analytic Theory

5.
Modular forms on
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
and
\mathrm{U}(2,1)

5.1.
The basic setting

In this section we let
s=1
and
\gamma_{0}=\delta
in the setting of §
2.6
.
Thus
G
is a quasi-split unitary group in three variables.
Let

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=\{g\in\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{GL}_{3}\;|\;gS_{\delta}\,^{t}\!g^{c}=S_{\delta}\},

\displaystyle S_{\delta}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&-1\\
0&-\delta&0\\
1&0&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle=\{h\in\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}\;|\;hJ\,^{t}\!h^{c}=J\},

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix}

be quasi-split unitary groups in three and two variables.
The associated unitary similitude groups are denoted by
{G}=\mathrm{GU}(T)
and
{H}=\mathrm{GU}(J)
.

We define the embedding
\iota:H\hookrightarrow G
by

\iota\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}a&0&b\\
0&1&0\\
c&0&d\end{bmatrix}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\iota$}}}

We regard
H
as the subgroup of
G
via
\iota
.
Recall the embedding
\jmath:{\mathfrak{H}}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}
defined by
\tau\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
0\end{bmatrix}
is compatible with the embedding
\iota
in the sense of (
2.6
).
The reader is reminded that for
Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle\eta(Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}(\tau^{c}-\tau-w^{c}\delta^{-1}w),

\displaystyle\eta(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}(\tau^{c}-\tau).

We will frequently add
′
to the notation for various objects to indicate that they are attached to
T^{\prime}
.
Recall the standard Borel subgroups
{P}={M}{N}
of
{G}
and
{P}^{\prime}={M}^{\prime}{N}^{\prime}
of
{H}
.
We denote the Borel subgroups of
G
and
H
by
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{M}}{N}
and
{\mathbf{P}}^{\prime}={\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}{N}^{\prime}
, and the additive and multiplicative groups in one variable over
{\mathbb{Q}}
by
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}=\mathrm{M}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}=\mathrm{GL}_{1}
.
Define isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{M},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{M}^{\prime},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{n}}:\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\ltimes{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{N},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}:{\mathbb{G}}_{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{N}^{\prime}

by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}(a,b)=\mathrm{diag}(a,b,(a^{-1})^{c}bb^{c}),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}(a,t)=\mathrm{diag}(a,t(a^{-1})^{c}),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{n}}(w,z)=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\delta^{-1}w^{c}&z-\frac{w^{c}w}{2\delta}\\
0&1&w\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z)=\begin{bmatrix}1&z\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}.

We will write
{\mathbf{d}}(a)={\mathbf{m}}(a^{c},1)
and
{\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}(a)={\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}(a^{c},1)
.
Put

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle={K}\cap G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}),

\displaystyle K_{l}

\displaystyle={K}_{l}\cap G({\mathbb{Q}}_{l}),

\displaystyle K_{i}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})

\displaystyle={K}_{i}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})\cap G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})

for
i=0,1
.
Define the maximal compact subgroup
K^{\prime}=\prod_{l}K^{\prime}_{l}
of
H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by
K^{\prime}_{l}=H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\cap\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,l})
.
We similarly define an open subgroup
K_{i}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})
of
K^{\prime}
.

5.2.
Restriction from
\mathrm{GU}(1,1)
to
\mathrm{U}(1,1)

Fix a finite prime
q
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
For
t\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
we define the character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}^{t}:{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}^{t}(x)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}(tx)
.
Given an admissible representation
\sigma
of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
, we put

\displaystyle\mathrm{Wh}^{t}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}(\sigma\circ{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}^{t}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Wh}(\sigma)

\displaystyle=\{t\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}\;|\;\mathrm{Wh}^{t}(\sigma)\neq 0\}

We say that
\sigma
is
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}
-generic if
1\in\mathrm{Wh}(\sigma)
.

We associate to characters
\mu:E_{q}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
and
\rho:{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
the space
\tilde{I}^{\prime}(\mu,\rho)
which consists of functions
f^{\prime}:{H}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})\to{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying

f^{\prime}({\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}(a,t)uh)=\mu(a)\rho(t)|t^{-1}\mathrm{N}_{E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}(a)|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}^{1/2}f^{\prime}(h)

for
a\in E_{q}^{\times}
,
t\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}
,
u\in{N}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
and
h\in{H}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
, where
{H}
acts by right translation.
We denote its restriction to
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
by
I^{\prime}(\mu)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that
q
does not split in
E
and that
\mu,\rho
are unitary.

(1)

\tilde{I}^{\prime}(\mu,\rho)
is irreducible.

(2)

I^{\prime}(\mu)
is reducible if and only if
\mu|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}=\epsilon_{E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}
.

(3)

If
\mu|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}}=\epsilon_{E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}
, then
I^{\prime}(\mu)=\sigma_{+}\oplus\sigma_{-}
, where

\displaystyle\mathrm{Wh}(\sigma_{+})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{N}_{E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}(E_{q}^{\times}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Wh}(\sigma_{-})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}\setminus\mathrm{N}_{E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}(E_{q}^{\times}).

If
\mu
is unramified, then
\sigma_{+}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}^{\prime}
-invariant vector.

Proof.

Since the restriction of
\tilde{I}^{\prime}(\mu,\rho)
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
is the principal series associated to a unitary character of
T_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
, it is irreducible and so is
\tilde{I}^{\prime}(\mu,\rho)
.
Part (
2
) is a special case of Theorems 1.1, 1.2 and Lemma 4.4 of
[
KS97
]
.
We will prove (
3
).
Note that
\sigma_{+}
is a theta lift of the trivial representation of the orthogonal group associated to the norm form of
E_{q}
.
If
E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
is unramified, then it admits a non-zero
K_{q}^{\prime}
-invariant vector.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

(1)

The group
{H}
is the quotient of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}\times\mathrm{Res}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}
by
\mathrm{GL}_{1}
embedded diagonally (cf.
[
Shi97
, Lemma 4.4]
,
[
Har93
, §3.1]
).
An irreducible representation of
{H}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
is given by a pair
(\sigma^{\star},\chi)
, where
\chi
is a character of
E_{v}^{\times}
and
\sigma^{\star}
is an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
whose central character is
\chi^{-1}|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}^{\times}}
.
The functorial lift of
(\sigma^{\star},\chi)
is the base change of
\sigma^{\star}
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(E_{v})
twisted by
\chi
.

(2)

Put
\tilde{\sigma}=\tilde{I}^{\prime}(\mu,\rho)
.
The restriction of
\tilde{\sigma}
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
is the principal series
I(\rho\cdot\mu|_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}^{\times}},\rho)
.
If
E_{q}\simeq{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}\oplus{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
and if we write
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
, then
\tilde{\sigma}\circ\imath_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}\simeq I(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}^{-1})
.
Observe that
\imath_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}(h)=(h,J\,^{t}\!h^{-1}J^{-1})=(h,(\det h)^{-1}h)
for
h\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{\mathfrak{q}})
, where
J=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix}
.

Proposition 5.3
.

(1)

An irreducible representation of
{G}({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
remains irreducible upon restriction to
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
.

(2)

An irreducible automorphic representation
\tilde{\pi}
of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
remains irreducible upon restriction to
G({\mathbb{A}})
.
Moreover, if
\tilde{\varphi}\in\tilde{\pi}
is not zero, then its restriction to
G({\mathbb{A}})
is not zero.

Proof.

Part (
1
) is a special case of Theorem 9(a) of
[
AP19
]
.
The result stays same for
{G}({\mathbb{R}})
, from which the first part of (
2
) follows.
The restriction gives a non-zero
G({\mathbb{A}})
-equivariant map
\tilde{\pi}\to{\mathscr{A}}(G)
, which is injective (cf. Remark
2.1
).
∎

5.3.
Hida families on
\mathrm{U}(1,1)

Definition 5.4
.

We call a function
g:{\mathfrak{H}}\times H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})\to{\mathbb{C}}
a modular form on
H
of weight
\underline{k}^{\prime}=(k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime})
, level
p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
and nebentypus
\chi^{\prime}
if it is holomorphic in
\tau
and satisfies

(5.1)

g(\gamma\tau,\gamma hu)=\chi^{\prime}(u)^{-1}\lambda(\gamma,\tau)^{-k_{1}^{\prime}}\mu(\gamma,\tau)^{k_{2}^{\prime}}g(\tau,h)

for
\gamma\in H({\mathbb{Q}})
,
h\in H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
u\in K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})
.
A modular form
g
is called a cusp form if it has a Fourier-Jacobi expansion

g(\tau,h)=\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}}\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{h}(g)e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}m\tau}

for every
h\in H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
We write
S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbb{C}})
for the space of cusp forms on
H
of weight
\underline{k}^{\prime}
, level
p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
and nebentypus
\chi^{\prime}
.

Remark 5.5
.

By (
2.4
) the identity (
5.1
) is equivalent to the following identity

g(\gamma\tau,\gamma hu)=\chi^{\prime}(u)^{-1}(\det\gamma)^{k_{1}^{\prime}}\mu(\gamma,\tau)^{k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}g(\tau,h).

Recall the automorphic form
\varPhi_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(g)
on
H
associated to
g
defined by

\varPhi_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(g)(h)=\lambda(h_{\infty},\sqrt{-1})^{k_{1}^{\prime}}\mu(h_{\infty},\sqrt{-1})^{-k_{2}^{\prime}}g(h_{\infty}(\sqrt{-1}),\hat{h})

for
h=(h_{\infty},\hat{h})
with
h_{\infty}\in H({\mathbb{R}})
and
\hat{h}\in H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

Let
\Box^{\prime}
be the set of prime factors of
pN^{\prime}
.

Definition 5.6
.

For any subring
{O}
of
{\mathbb{C}}
the space
S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime};{O})
consists of
g\in S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbb{C}})
such that
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\alpha}(g)\in{O}
for every
\alpha\in{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Box^{\prime}})
.
For a local ring
R
finite flat over
{O}
we set

S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},R)=S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{O})\otimes_{O}R.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},R)$}}}

We write
\alpha={\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}(a)\in{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Box^{\prime}})
and put
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Since
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\alpha}(g)
depends only on the fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
, we will denote it by
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(g)
.
Recall that

[{U}_{p}(\beta_{1})g](\tau,\alpha)=p^{-1-k_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}f\biggl(\tau,\alpha\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}p&z\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\biggl)

(see §
3.6
).
Take a positive integer
j
so that
({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}
is a principal ideal.
Let
\xi
be a generator of
({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}
.
Then we can show that

\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({U}_{p}(\beta_{1})^{j}g)=p^{-jk_{1}^{\prime}}(\xi^{c})^{k_{1}^{\prime}}\xi^{-{k_{2}^{\prime}}}\mathrm{FC}^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(g)

for
m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}
at the cost of replacing
j
with its suitable multiple if necessary (cf. Lemma
6.5
).
It follows that

(5.2)

\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}g)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathrm{FC}^{mp^{j!}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(g).

Definition 5.7
.

An ordinary
\Lambda_{2}
-adic cusp form
\boldsymbol{g}
of tame level
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
and nebentypus
\chi^{\prime}
is a collection of
A_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}\in\Lambda_{2}

indexed by
0<m\in({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{a}}^{c})^{-1}
and fractional ideals
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
prime to
pN^{\prime}
such that for each
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{cls}}

\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}(\tau,\alpha):=\sum_{m}\underline{Q}^{\prime}(A^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}})q^{m}\in\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}S_{k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{H}(p^{\ell_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\epsilon_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}};{R}^{\prime}(\underline{Q}^{\prime})).

We will also write
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{g})=A^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}
.
Let
{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},\Lambda_{2})
be the
\Lambda_{2}
-module of those
\Lambda_{2}
-adic cusp forms.

Let
{R}^{\prime}
be a normal ring finite flat over
\Lambda_{2}
.
Put

{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})={\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},\Lambda_{3})\otimes{R}^{\prime}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})$}}}

Let
\boldsymbol{g}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})
be an
{R}^{\prime}
-adic Hida family on
{H}
.
Since

(5.3)

{H}({\mathbb{A}})={H}({\mathbb{Q}}){\mathbb{R}}^{\times}H({\mathbb{A}}){K}^{\prime}_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}),

the restriction of
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
to
{\mathfrak{H}}\times H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
is not zero and denoted also by
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
.
We therefore view
\boldsymbol{g}
as an element of
{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})
.

5.4.
Automorphic representations associated to Hida families

In view of Remark
5.2
(
1
) we can associate to a Hida family

\boldsymbol{g}=\{A^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}\}_{{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E},\;m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})

an
{R}^{\prime}
-adic Galois representation
\rho_{\boldsymbol{g}}:\Gamma_{E}\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}({R}^{\prime})
unramified outside primes dividing
N^{\prime}p
.
For
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{cls}}
the specialization
\rho_{\boldsymbol{f}}\otimes_{{R}^{\prime},\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
is associated with a
p
-ordinary irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\simeq\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},v}
of
{H}({\mathbb{A}})
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\simeq\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},v}
be the irreducible component of the restriction of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
to
H({\mathbb{A}})
such that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series and such that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}
is
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}
-generic for all finite primes
q
.

The ordinary
{R}^{\prime}
-adic cuspidal Hecke algebra
{\mathbf{T}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})
is defined as the
{R}^{\prime}
-subalgebra of
\mathrm{End}_{{R}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\mathrm{ord}}^{H}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})
generated over
{R}^{\prime}
by the Hecke operators in
C_{c}^{\infty}(K^{\prime}_{l}\backslash H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})/K^{\prime}_{l},{O})
with
l\nmid pN^{\prime}
and
{U}_{p}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
\boldsymbol{g}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})
be an
{R}^{\prime}
-adic Hida family on
{H}
.
Then
\boldsymbol{g}
is an eigenform for all operators in
{\mathbf{T}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{R}^{\prime})
.
If
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\prime}
, then
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
is generated by
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Fix a non-split prime
q
.
Let
T_{q}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(K^{\prime}_{q}\backslash H({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})/K^{\prime}_{q},{O})
.
In view of Remark
5.2
and the rigidity stated in
[
Hsi21
, §3.2(1)]
there are only finitely many points
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{{R}^{\prime}}
such that
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}
is reducible.
Hence there is an element
\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{q}\in{R}^{\prime\times}
such that
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}|T_{q}=\underline{Q}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{q})\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
for all but finitely many points
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{{R}^{\prime}}
, which shows that
\boldsymbol{g}|T_{q}=\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{q}\boldsymbol{g}
.

Let
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\prime}
.
Then
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\in\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}S_{k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{H}(p^{\ell_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\epsilon_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}};{R}^{\prime}(\underline{Q}^{\prime}))
is not zero.
Put

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}^{K^{\prime}_{q}}=\{v\in\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}\;|\;\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}(k)v=v\text{ for }k\in K_{q}^{\prime}\}.

Since
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}^{K^{\prime}_{q}}\subset\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}
for every non-split prime
q
by Lemma
5.1
(
3
), we conclude that
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
.
∎

We define the global
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{a}
-Whittaker functional on
{\mathscr{A}}^{0}(H)
or
{\mathscr{A}}^{0}({H})
by

W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{a}}(\varphi)=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varphi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&x\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(ax)}\,\mathrm{d}x

for
a\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
.
Let
\sigma
be an irreducible automorphic representation of
H({\mathbb{A}})
.
We say that
\sigma
is
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{a}
-generic if there is
\varphi\in\sigma
such that
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{a}}(\varphi)\neq 0
.
By definition
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
is
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
-generic.

Proposition 5.9
.

If
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\prime\prime}
, then
\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})\neq 0
.

Proof.

Put
W(\tilde{h})=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}(\tilde{h})\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}))
for
\tilde{h}=(\tilde{h}_{v})\in{H}(\widehat{{\mathbb{A}}})
.
By assumption we have a factorization
W(\tilde{h})=\prod_{v}W_{v}(\tilde{h}_{v})
by the uniqueness of
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
-Whittaker functionals.

For split primes
l
we view
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},l}
as a representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
via
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}
.
Since
{N}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})=\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}^{-1}(N_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l}))
(cf. Remark
5.2
), the restriction of
W_{l}
to
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
is a local Whittaker function associated to an essential vector in
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},l}
for split primes
l\neq p
, and hence
W_{l}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})\neq 0
(cf. Definition
8.2
).
Since
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
is a stabilized vector at
p
(see Remark
8.9
), we have
W_{p}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})\neq 0
.
By (splt) and the formula for spherical Whittaker functions
W_{q}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})\neq 0
for non-split primes
q
.
We conclude that
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}))\neq 0
as claimed.
∎

For a split prime
l
we denote by
c_{l}=c(\sigma_{l})
the exponent of the conductor of
\sigma_{l}
(see Definition
8.2
).

Definition 5.10
.

Put
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}=\prod_{{\mathfrak{l}}|{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{c_{l}}
.
Let
g^{\circ}_{\sigma}\in S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma},\chi^{\prime};{\mathbb{C}})
be the newform associated to
\sigma
, normalized so that
\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(g_{\sigma}^{\circ})=1
.
We call the prime-to-part of
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}
the tame conductor of
\sigma
.

Put

\displaystyle N_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{N}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\varPhi_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(g_{\sigma}^{\circ}),

where
\mathrm{N}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})
denotes the absolute norm of
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}
.

Definition 5.11
.

Let
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{{R}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{cls}}
.
We call
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
the normalized
p
-stabilized newform associated to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
if it generates
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
, is new outside
p
and is a
{U}_{p}^{\prime}
-eigenform with unit eigenvalue, and satisfies
\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ord}})=1
.
Put

\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ord}}:=\varPhi_{k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ord}})\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}.

Remark 5.12
.

(1)

We see from Lemma
5.1
(
3
) and Proposition
5.8
that if
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime},q}
is reducible and
m\notin\mathrm{N}_{E_{q}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{q}}(E_{q}^{\times})
, then
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})=0
.

(2)

If
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\prime\prime}
, then
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{ord}}:=\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})^{-1}\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
.

(3)

Let
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\star}
be the restriction of
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
.
The restriction
\boldsymbol{g}^{\star}
of
\boldsymbol{g}
to
{\mathfrak{H}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
is a Hida family on
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
of tame level
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
.
However,
\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\star}
may not be primitive even if
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\prime\prime}
(cf. Remark
5.2
(
2
)).
Nevertheless, Proposition
5.9
holds.

5.5.
Line bundles and Fourier-Jacobi coefficients

Let
\Box
be the set of prime factors of
pN
.

Definition 5.13
.

Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
\chi
a character of
U
.
We call a function
f:{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})\to{L}_{\underline{k}}({\mathbb{C}})
a modular form on
G
of weight
\underline{k}
, level
U
and nebentypus
\chi
if it is holomorphic in
Z
and satisfies

f(\gamma Z,\gamma gu)=\chi(u)^{-1}\rho_{\underline{k}}(J(\gamma,Z))f(Z,g)

for
\gamma\in G({\mathbb{Q}})
,
g\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
u\in U
.
A modular form
f
is called a cusp form if it has a Fourier-Jacobi expansion

f\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix},\alpha\biggl)=\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}}\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\alpha}(w,f)e^{2\pi\sqrt{-1}m\tau}

for
\alpha\in{\mathbf{M}}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Box})
.
Let
S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
be the space of cusp forms on
G
of weight
\underline{k}
, level
U
and nebentypus
\chi
.

Let
a\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
be a finite idèle which satisfies
a_{\mathfrak{q}}=1
for prime ideals
{\mathfrak{q}}
dividing
pN
.
Since
\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a)}(w,f)
depends only on the fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
, we will denote it by
\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)
.

For
g\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and a function
{F}
on
{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
we set

[r(g){F}](Z,\beta)={F}(Z,\beta g).

Namely,
r(g){F}
is the right translation of
{F}
by
g
.
Taking Proposition
4.11
into account, we will rewrite an
{L}_{\underline{k}}({\mathbb{C}})
-valued function
{F}
on
{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
as

{F}(Z,\beta)=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}{F}_{i}(Z,\beta)(-1)^{i}\binom{\kappa}{i}X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$f_{0},\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{0}$}}}

(cf. (
4.1
)).
We will also rewrite

(5.4)

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(w,f)=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\mathrm{FJ}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f)_{i}(w)(-1)^{i}\binom{\kappa}{i}X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}$}}}

We define the different of
E
by
{\mathfrak{D}}_{E}
.
Fix a prime-to-
{\mathfrak{p}}
fractional ideal
{\mathfrak{a}}
of
E
such that

{\mathfrak{f}}({\mathfrak{a}},m)={\mathbb{Q}}\cap m\delta^{-1}{\mathfrak{D}}_{E}^{-1}\mathrm{N}({\mathfrak{a}})

is integral, where
\mathrm{N}({\mathfrak{a}})
denotes the absolute norm of
{\mathfrak{a}}
.
Let

mH_{0}(w,w^{\prime})=2m\sqrt{-1}\delta^{-1}ww^{\prime c}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$E_{0}$, $H_{0}$}}}

be a positive Hermitian form on
{\mathbb{C}}
whose imaginary part is a Riemann form
mE_{0}=m\mathrm{Im}H_{0}
on an elliptic curve
E_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})={\mathbb{C}}/\iota_{\infty}({\mathfrak{a}})
and given by the alternating form
m\ll\;,\;\gg
defined in §
3.7
with
\gamma_{0}=\delta
.
We define a semi-character
\alpha_{\mathfrak{a}}:{\mathfrak{a}}\to\{\pm 1\}
as in §7.2.3 of
[
Hsi14
]
and a cocycle
l\mapsto e_{l}
of
{\mathfrak{a}}
with values in the group of invertible holomorphic functions on
{\mathbb{C}}
by

e_{l}(w)=\alpha_{\mathfrak{a}}(l)e^{\pi mH_{0}\bigl(w+\frac{l}{2},l\bigl)}

to which we associate a line bundle
{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}=L(mH_{0},\alpha_{\mathfrak{a}})
on
E_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})
as the quotient of the trivial bundle
{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{C}}
over
{\mathfrak{a}}
by the action of
{\mathfrak{a}}
given by
l\cdot(w,z)=(w+l,e_{l}(w)z)
.

Remark 5.14
.

Put
n=m\mathrm{N}({\mathfrak{a}})
.
The line bundle
{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}
is associated to the divisor
n[0]
of
E_{\mathfrak{a}}
(see Example 1.9 of
[
BK10
]
).
Hence
{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}
is defined over an algebraic number field, symmetric and
\iota_{\infty}
-admissible in the sense of
[
BK10
]
.

The space of global sections of this line bundle
{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}
coincides with the space
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})
that consists of reduced theta functions
\theta
on
{\mathbb{C}}
satisfying the equation

\theta(w+l)=e_{l}(w)\theta(w)

for
l\in{\mathfrak{a}}
.
The line bundle
{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}
is ample and
\dim{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})=f({\mathfrak{a}},m)D_{E}
, where
f({\mathfrak{a}},m)
is the positive generator of the ideal
{\mathfrak{f}}({\mathfrak{a}},m)
.

By the theory of complex multiplication the pair
(E_{\mathfrak{a}},{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}})
descends to
({\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathscr{L}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}})
over a discrete valuation ring
R_{F}=\overline{{\mathbb{Z}}}_{p}\cap F
for some number field
F
so that
({\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}},{\mathscr{L}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes_{R_{F}}{\mathbb{C}}\simeq(E_{\mathfrak{a}},{L}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}})
, where we use the fixed isomorphism
\iota_{p}:{\mathbb{C}}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
.
We similarly define
{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({O})
for any
R_{F}
-algebra
{O}
.

Let
L_{0}^{\prime}
be an ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Therefore hereafter we will rewrite
{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}:=L_{0}^{\prime}
.
The following criterion is due to Finis (see Lemma 3.1 of
[
Fin06
]
).

Lemma 5.15

(Finis)
.

Let
{\mathfrak{a}}
be an ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
and
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Then
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(R_{F})
if and only if
[A_{m}(l)\theta](0)\in R_{F}
for all
l\in E
.

Proposition 5.16
.

If
f\in M^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},R_{F})
, then for
{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E}

\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\biggl)^{k_{2}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}(R_{F}).

Proof.

Recall the line bundle
{\mathfrak{L}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}
on the elliptic curve
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}
associated to
{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E}
introduced in Sections
3.9
and
3.10
.
Since
{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}\subset L_{\mathfrak{a}}
, there is a natural isogeny
{Z}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\circ}\to{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}
.
Put
\theta=\Big(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\Big)^{k_{2}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}
.
Proposition
3.11
states that
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Since
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}(L_{\mathfrak{a}},R_{F})
by Proposition
3.13
, Lemma
5.15
shows that
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}(R_{F})
.
∎

5.6.
Hida families on
\mathrm{U}(2,1)

We define the notion of arithmeticity of cusp forms on
G
by means of the arithmetic property of the Fourier-Jacobi coefficients. See
[
Lan13
]
for the general arithmetic theory of Fourier-Jacobi expansions on PEL type Shimura varieties.

Definition 5.17
.

Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
which contains
{N}({\mathfrak{b}}_{0})
.
For any
{\mathscr{O}}
-subalgebra
{O}
of
{\mathbb{C}}
the space
S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\chi,{O})
consists of
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
such that for every
{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E}

\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\biggl)^{k_{2}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}({O}).

For a
p
-adic ring
R
finite flat over
{O}
we set

S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\chi;R):=\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{k_{2}}S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\chi;{O})\otimes R.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,\chi;R)$}}}

Put

\displaystyle S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U;R)

\displaystyle=S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U,1;R),

\displaystyle S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;R)

\displaystyle=S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(K_{0}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}),\chi;R).

Let
{R}
be a normal ring finite flat over
\Lambda_{3}
.
Let

\boldsymbol{f}=\{\Theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}\}_{{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E},\;m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{R})

be an
{R}
-adic Hida family on
{G}
(cf. Remark
4.1
).
Denote the restriction of
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
to
{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
also by
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}\in\boldsymbol{e}S_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{G}(p^{\ell_{\underline{Q}}}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi\epsilon_{\underline{Q}};{R}(\underline{Q}))
for
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{R}^{\mathrm{cls}}
.
The reader is reminded that when
\alpha={\mathbf{d}}(a)\in{\mathbf{M}}(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\Box})
and
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
,

(5.5)

\underline{Q}(\boldsymbol{f}):=\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}}\underline{Q}(\Theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}})\,q^{m}=\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}\biggl)^{k_{Q_{2}}}\bigl(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}\bigl)_{0}(Z,\alpha).

For
\underline{Q}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{f}}^{\prime}
Lemma
4.6
associated to
\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}}
an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{\underline{Q}}
of
{G}({\mathbb{A}})
.
We denote by
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{\underline{Q}}
its restriction to
G({\mathbb{A}})
, which is irreducible as guaranteed by Proposition
5.3
(
2
).

Let
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
be an irreducible cuspidal tempered automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
whose archimedean part
\pi_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series with minimal weight
-\underline{k}
and such that
\pi_{q}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}
-invariant vector for every non-split prime
q
.
For each split prime
l
we denote the conductor of
\pi_{l}
by
c(\pi_{l})
in the sense of (
8.2
).

Definition 5.18
.

Put
N_{\pi}=\prod_{l}l^{c(\pi_{l})}
.
We take ideals
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N_{\pi}{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Then there exists
f^{\circ}\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
such that
{\mathbf{v}}\mapsto\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f^{\circ})_{\mathbf{v}}
gives a
{K}_{\infty}
-equivariant embedding
{L}_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}({\mathbb{C}})\hookrightarrow\pi
.
We call
f^{\circ}
a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational newform associated to
\pi
.
We call
f^{\circ}_{0}
a highest weight newform associated to
\pi
.
Note that
f^{\circ}
is defined up to multiplication by
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
.

6.
Theta operators on Picard modular forms

6.1.
The
{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})
-operator

Recall that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{n}}(w,z)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&-\delta^{-1}w^{c}&z-\frac{w^{c}w}{2\delta}\\
0&1&w\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{d}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\xi^{c}&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&\xi^{-1}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}p^{-1}&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&p\end{bmatrix}.

for
w\in{\mathbb{E}}
,
z\in{\mathbb{A}}
and
\xi\in{\mathbb{E}}^{\times}
.
We will write

\displaystyle{U}({\mathfrak{p}})

\displaystyle={U}_{p}(\alpha_{1}),

\displaystyle{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})

\displaystyle={U}_{p}(\beta_{1}).{\@index{\@indexphrase{${U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})$}}}

We see from §
3.6
that

[{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})f](Z,\beta)=p^{-1-k_{1}}\sum_{y,z\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}f\left(Z,\beta\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\left(\begin{bmatrix}p&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\right).

Let
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})
be a Hecke eigenform.
We write
\pi
for the cuspidal automorphic representation associated to
f
.

Definition 6.1
.

We say that
\pi
is
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-ordinary with respect to
\iota_{p}
if the local representation
\pi_{p}
, viewed as a representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, is a subquotient of a principal series
I(\nu_{p},\rho_{p},\mu_{p})
of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(see §
8.4
for notation), and

k_{1}=\mathrm{ord}_{p}\iota_{p}(\mu_{p}(p))\leq\min\{\mathrm{ord}_{p}\iota_{p}(\rho_{p}(p)),\mathrm{ord}_{p}\iota_{p}(\nu_{p}(p))\}.

Remark 6.2
.

Put
\gamma_{p}=\mu_{p}(p)
.
There exists a cusp form
f\in\pi
such that

{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})f=p^{-k_{1}}\gamma_{p}f

(see Proposition
8.6
).

6.2.
The
{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}
-operator

We denote by
{C}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
the space of
{O}
-valued locally constant functions on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}
. We write
\mathrm{Dist}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
for the space of all
{O}
-linear functionals on
{C}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
.
As is well-known, there is the canonical ring isomorphism between
\mathrm{Dist}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
and
\Lambda={O}\llbracket{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\rrbracket
.

Let
{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}
be a sufficiently small prime-to-
{\mathfrak{p}}
integral ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
with respect to the fixed set
C_{E}
of representatives of the ideal class group of
E
.
Take a set
{B}_{j}
of representatives for
({\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j})^{\times}
from
{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}
.
Let
\breve{\phi}\in{C}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
.
Let
j
be sufficiently large so that
\breve{\phi}
factors through the quotient
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\to({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{j}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}
.
Then we view it as a function on
({\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j})^{\times}
.
Given
b\in{\mathbb{E}}
and a place
v
of
E
, we here denote by
b_{v}\in E_{v}
the
v
-component of
b
and write
b_{p}=(b_{\mathfrak{p}},b_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}})\in E_{p}
.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})
be such that
{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})f=p^{-k_{1}}\gamma_{p}f
.
When
\mathrm{ord}_{p}\iota_{p}(\gamma_{p})=k_{1}
, we define the
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-depletion of
f
with respect to
\breve{\phi}
by

[{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f](Z,g)=\frac{1}{p^{j}\gamma_{p}^{j}}\sum_{b\in{B}_{j}}\sum_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{j}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\breve{\phi}(b^{c})f\left(Z,g\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\left(\begin{bmatrix}p^{j}&b_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\right).{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}$}}}

The definition of
{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f
is independent of the choice of
j
by Lemma
8.10
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
{O}
be a
{\mathscr{O}}
-subalgebra of
{\mathbb{C}}
and
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{O})
satisfies
{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})f=p^{-k_{1}}\gamma_{p}f
with
\mathrm{ord}_{p}\iota_{p}(\gamma_{p})=k_{1}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E}
.
Take a sufficiently small open compact subgroup
U
of
\widehat{E}^{\times}
and a natural number
j
so that we can write
\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})={\mathbf{d}}(\xi_{\mathbf{f}}u)
with
\xi\in E^{\times}
and
u\in U
.
Then we have
\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)=0
unless
m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}
, in which case

\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)_{0}(w)=\frac{(\xi^{c})^{k_{1}}}{\gamma_{p}^{j}\xi^{k_{3}}}\sum_{b\in{B}_{j}}\breve{\phi}(b^{c})\biggl[A_{mp^{j}}\left(\delta\frac{b}{\xi}\right)\mathrm{FJ}^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}\biggl]\left(\frac{w}{\xi}\right).

In particular,
\Big(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\Big)^{k_{2}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)_{0}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}({O})
.

6.3.
Proof of Proposition
6.4

When the class number of
E
is greater than one, it is complicated to describe the action of
{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})
on Fourier-Jacobi expansions.
One can use the following result to compute the action of a suitable power
{U}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}
in terms of Fourier-Jacobi coefficients.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
and
a\in\widehat{E}^{\times}
. Let
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\in C_{E}
.
Take a sufficiently small open compact subgroup
U
of
\widehat{E}^{\times}
and a natural number
j
so that we can write
\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})={\mathbf{d}}(\xi_{\mathbf{f}}u)
with
\xi\in E^{\times}
and
u\in U
.
Let
z\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and
l\in{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}
.
Then for a positive rational number
m

\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})}(w,f)\\
=\frac{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)}{\xi^{k_{3}}}e^{\pi\sqrt{-1}m\delta^{-1}l^{c}(2w-l)}\rho_{\underline{k}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}(\xi^{-1})^{c}&0\\
\delta^{-1}(\xi^{-1}l)^{c}&1\end{bmatrix},1\right)\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi^{-1}(w-l),f).

Proof.

Let
\xi=g_{0}=1
and
\beta={\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})
in Lemma
3.10
.
Then

\varPhi(f)^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})}({\mathbf{n}}(w,0))\\
=e^{-\pi m(2+\sqrt{-1}w^{c}\delta^{-1}w)}\rho_{\underline{k}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
-\delta^{-1}w^{c}&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})}(w,f).

Since
{\mathfrak{a}}
and
p
are coprime, the left hand side equals

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varPhi(f)({\mathbf{n}}(w,x){\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j}))\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varPhi(f)({\mathbf{n}}(w+l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},x+z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j}){\mathbf{d}}(a))\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varPhi(f)\biggl({\mathbf{n}}\biggl(w-l_{\infty},x+z-\frac{\ll l_{\infty},w\gg}{2}\biggl){\mathbf{d}}(\xi_{\mathbf{f}}){\mathbf{d}}(a)\biggl)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Since the ideal of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
generated by
\xi
is
({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}
, we have
\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\xi)=p^{j}
.
The integral above equals

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}\biggl(z-\frac{\ll l_{\infty},w\gg}{2}\biggl)\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varPhi(f)({\mathbf{n}}(\xi_{\infty}^{-1}(w-l_{\infty}),p^{-j}x){\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{d}}(\xi_{\infty}^{-1}))\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)e^{\pi m\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(l^{c}\delta^{-1}w)}\varPhi(f)^{mp^{j}}_{{\mathbf{d}}(\xi_{\infty}^{-1}){\mathbf{d}}(a)}({\mathbf{n}}(\xi_{\infty}^{-1}(w-l_{\infty}),0))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)e^{\pi m\sqrt{-1}\delta^{-1}(l^{c}w-lw^{c})}e^{-\pi m(2+\sqrt{-1}(w-l)^{c}\delta^{-1}(w-l))}

\displaystyle\times\rho_{\underline{k}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}(\xi^{-1})^{c}&0\\
-\delta^{-1}(\xi^{-1}(w-l))^{c}&1\end{bmatrix},\xi^{-1}\right)\mathrm{FJ}^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi^{-1}(w-l),f)

again by Lemma
3.10
.
∎

We put
\Omega=\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}
to simplify notation.
Granted (
3.3
), we normalize the arithmetic
m
th Fourier-Jacobi coefficient of
f
at
\beta\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\beta}(w,f)=\rho_{\underline{k}}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&\Omega^{-1}\end{bmatrix},1\biggl)\overrightarrow{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\beta}(w,f).

When
\beta={\mathbf{d}}(a)
and
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
, we write

\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\beta}(w,f)=\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,f)=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f)_{i}(w)(-1)^{i}\binom{\kappa}{i}X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}.

Then
\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f)_{i}(w)=\Omega^{k_{2}-i}\mathrm{FJ}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f)_{i}(w)
and
\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(f)_{i}(0)\in{O}
by Proposition
4.11
.
Proposition
3.11
shows that for every
l\in{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}

e^{-\pi m\sqrt{-1}l^{c}\delta^{-1}(2w+l)}\rho_{\underline{k}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
-\delta^{-1}\frac{l^{c}}{\Omega}&1\end{bmatrix},1\right)\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w+l)=\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w).

Recall the operator
A_{m}(l)
defined for functions
\Theta
on
{\mathbb{C}}
by

[A_{m}(l)\Theta](w)=e^{-\pi m\sqrt{-1}l^{c}\delta^{-1}(2w+l)}\Theta(w+l){\@index{\@indexphrase{$A_{m}(l)$}}}

(see §
3.7
).
It follows from Lemma
6.5
that

\displaystyle\vec{\theta}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(-l_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})}(w,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)}{\xi^{k_{3}}}e^{-\pi\sqrt{-1}m\delta^{-1}l^{c}(2w+l)}\rho_{\underline{k}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}(\xi^{-1})^{c}&0\\
-\frac{(\xi^{-1}l)^{c}}{\delta\Omega}&1\end{bmatrix},1\right)\vec{\theta}^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\xi^{-1}(w+l),f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)\frac{(\xi^{k_{1}})^{c}}{\xi^{k_{3}}}\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\left[A_{mp^{j}}\left(\frac{l}{\xi}\right)\theta^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{i}\right]\left(\frac{w}{\xi}\right)\binom{\kappa}{i}(-\xi^{c}X)^{i}\biggl(Y+\frac{l^{c}X}{\delta\Omega}\biggl)^{\kappa-i}.

We are now ready to prove Proposition
6.4
.
For our choice of
{B}_{j}
we can apply this formula to
l=\delta b
with
b\in{B}_{j}
to get

\displaystyle\vec{\theta}^{m}_{{\mathbf{d}}(a){\mathbf{n}}(-\delta b_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})}(w,f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{m}(z)\frac{(\xi^{k_{1}})^{c}}{\xi^{k_{3}}}\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\left[A_{mp^{j}}\left(\frac{\delta b}{\xi}\right)\theta^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{i}\right]\left(\frac{w}{\xi}\right)\binom{\kappa}{i}(-\xi^{c}X)^{i}\biggl(Y-\frac{b^{c}X}{\Omega}\biggl)^{\kappa-i}.

Since

{\mathbf{n}}(-\delta b_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}},z)\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}(\beta_{1}^{-j})=\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\left(\begin{bmatrix}p^{j}&b_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right),

we have

(6.1)

\frac{\gamma_{p}^{j}\xi^{k_{3}}}{(\xi^{k_{1}})^{c}}\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\sum_{b\in{B}_{j}}\breve{\phi}(b^{c})\biggl[A_{mp^{j}}\left(\delta\frac{b}{\xi}\right)\theta^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{i}\biggl]\left(\frac{w}{\xi}\right)\binom{\kappa}{i}(-\xi^{c}X)^{i}\biggl(Y-\frac{b^{c}}{\Omega}X\biggl)^{\kappa-i}

if
m
is
p
-integral.
Clearly,
\vec{\theta}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(w,{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)=0
if
m
is not
p
-integral.
We obtain

\frac{\gamma_{p}^{j}\xi^{k_{3}}}{(\xi^{k_{1}})^{c}}\theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)_{0}(w)\\
=\sum_{b\in{B}_{j}}\breve{\phi}(b^{c})\biggl[A_{mp^{j}}\left(\delta\frac{b}{\xi}\right)\theta^{mp^{j}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{0}\biggl]\left(\frac{w}{\xi}\right)

by looking at the coefficient of
Y^{\kappa}
.
Since
A_{mp^{j}}(l)
(
l\in E
) preserves the module of
p
-integral theta functions by Lemma
5.15
, we conclude that
\theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}f)_{0}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}}({O})
. ∎

6.4.
The
{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}
-operator

Let
\chi^{\prime}
be a Dirichlet character of conductor
N^{\prime}
whose prime factors are split in
E
and distinct from
p
.
Take an ideal
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N^{\prime}{\mathbb{Z}}
.
To construct
p
-adic families having tame level
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
and nebentypus
\chi^{\prime}
, we apply the
{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}
-operator which we introduce in
[
HY25
]
by following the construction
[
Sch93
]
of
p
-adic
L
-functions for
\mathrm{GL}_{3}\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}
.
We define

{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f(g)=\sum_{i,j\in(\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}/N^{\prime}\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}})^{\times}}\sum_{y\in\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}/N^{\prime 2}\widehat{{\mathbb{Z}}}}\chi^{\prime}(ij)f\left(g\cdot\varsigma_{NN^{\prime}}\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{i}{N^{\prime}}&\frac{y}{N^{\prime 2}}\\
0&1&\frac{j}{N^{\prime}}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right),{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}$}}}

where
\varsigma_{NN^{\prime}}=(\varsigma_{NN^{\prime},l})\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
is defined by

\varsigma_{NN^{\prime},l}=\begin{cases}\hbox{\bf 1}_{3}&\text{if $l$ does not divide $NN^{\prime}$, }\\
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}^{-1}(\varsigma)&\text{if $l|NN^{\prime}$. }\end{cases}

This operator
{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}
has the following properties:

•

the restriction of
{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f
to
H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
has an appropriate
K
-type;

•

the local integrals at prime factors of
N^{\prime}
attached to
{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f
and newforms of level
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
have a simple formula (see §
7.8
).

6.5.
Shimura’s differential operators

Put

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{T}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{M}_{2,1}({\mathbb{C}}),

\displaystyle\xi(Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\tau^{c}-\tau&-w\\
w^{c}&-\delta\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\varXi(Z)

\displaystyle=(\xi(Z),\eta(Z)){\@index{\@indexphrase{$\varXi(Z),\xi(Z),\eta(Z)$}}}

for
Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}
.
We write
Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
for the vector space of all
{\mathbb{C}}
-multilinear maps of
{\mathfrak{T}}\times\cdots\times{\mathfrak{T}}
(
n
copies) into
V
.
We define the representation
(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n},Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V))
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
by

[(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(a,b)h](u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=\rho(a,b)h(\,^{t}\!au_{1}b,\dots,\,^{t}\!au_{n}b)

for
h\in Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.

Given
f\in S^{G}_{\rho}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
, we define a
Ml_{1}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
-valued function
Df
on
{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

\displaystyle[Df(u)](Z,\beta)

\displaystyle=u_{11}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\tau}(Z,\beta)+u_{21}\frac{\partial f}{\partial w}(Z,\beta),

\displaystyle[Cf(u)](Z,\beta)

\displaystyle=[Df(\,^{t}\!\xi(Z)u\eta(Z))](Z,\beta)

for
\beta\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
u=\begin{bmatrix}u_{11}\\
u_{21}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.
Given a non-negative positive integer
n
, we define
D^{n}f
and
C^{n}f
by

\displaystyle D^{n}f

\displaystyle=D(D^{n-1}f),

\displaystyle D^{0}f

\displaystyle=f,

\displaystyle C^{n}f

\displaystyle=C(C^{n-1}f),

\displaystyle C^{0}f

\displaystyle=f.

Define
Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
-valued function
D_{\rho}^{n}f
on
{\mathfrak{D}}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

D_{\rho}^{n}f=(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(\varXi)^{-1}C^{n}(\rho(\varXi)f).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$D_{\rho}$}}}

Then
D_{\rho}^{n}f
satisfies

D_{\rho}^{n}f(\gamma Z,\gamma\beta k)=(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(J(\gamma,Z))D_{\rho}^{n}f(Z,\beta)

for
\gamma\in G({\mathbb{Q}})
,
\beta\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
k\in{K}_{1}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})
, and

\displaystyle D_{\rho}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=D_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}D_{\rho}^{n}=D^{n}_{\rho\otimes\tau}D_{\rho}

by Proposition 12.10 of
[
Shi00
]
.
Moreover,
D_{\rho}^{n}f
is nearly holomorphic in the sense that
\varPhi_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}(D_{\rho}^{n}f)
is is annihilated by some power of every generator of the antiholomorphic Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{p}}_{-}
.
Put

\displaystyle D_{\underline{k}}

\displaystyle=D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}},

\displaystyle\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}

\displaystyle=(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-n}D_{\underline{k}}^{n}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$D_{\underline{k}},\delta_{\underline{k}}$}}}

Remark 6.6
.

When
r=s=1
, the operator
\delta_{(k_{1};k_{2})}^{n}
coincides with the Maass-Shimura differential operator
\delta_{k_{2}-k_{1}}^{n}
on
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
(cf. (
2.4
)).

Strictly speaking, Shimura deals with only the tube domain
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,r}
in
[
Shi00
]
.
When
r\neq s
, he considers bounded forms
{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
of the symmetric spaces instead of the unbounded domains
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
.
The proof is axiomatic and works generally.
We will prove the analogous results for
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
in §
A.4
by using the isomorphism
{\mathfrak{t}}:{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
, which will be used for the calculation of the archimedean integral in Appendix
B
.

6.6.
Differential operators on
\mathrm{U}(2,1)

Definition 6.7
.

Put
q^{m}=e^{2\pi m\sqrt{-1}}
.
For
\underline{k}^{\prime}=(k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime})
with
k_{1}^{\prime}<k_{2}^{\prime}
and a non-negative integer
t
we denote by
{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
the
space of all
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued functions
f
on
{\mathfrak{H}}\times H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
satisfying the following two conditions:

•

for
\gamma\in H({\mathbb{Q}})
,
\beta\in H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
and
k\in K_{1}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})

f(\gamma\tau,\gamma\beta k)=\chi^{\prime}(k)^{-1}\rho_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(J(\gamma,\tau))f(\tau,\beta)

•

for every
\beta\in H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
there exists
N_{\beta}\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\nu}(\beta)\in{\mathbb{C}}
such that

f(\tau,\beta)=\sum_{\nu=0}^{t}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(\pi\eta(\tau))^{-\nu}\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\nu,\beta}(f)q^{m/N_{\beta}}.

We call
f\in{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
a nearly holomorphic cusp form on
H
of weight
\underline{k}^{\prime}
, level
p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
, degree
t
and nebentypus
\chi^{\prime}
.
For a subfield
\Omega
of
{\mathbb{C}}
we say that
f
is
\Omega
-rational if
\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\nu,\beta}(f)\in\Omega
for all
m,\nu,\beta
.
The space
{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},\Omega)
consists of
\Omega
-rational elements of
{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
.

The
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rationality is characterized in terms of values at CM-points.
The following proposition is a special case of Theorem 14.12(1) of
[
Shi00
]
.

Proposition 6.8

(Shimura)
.

The space
{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
consists of all elements
f\in{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
such that
\frac{f(\tau)}{(\Omega_{\infty}/\pi)^{k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}}\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
for every
\tau\in{\mathfrak{H}}\cap E
.

Put

\displaystyle v_{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\underline{v}_{0}^{n}

\displaystyle=(v_{0},v_{0},\dots,v_{0})\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{n}.

Proposition
A.6
shows that

(6.2)

[D_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}(\jmath(\tau))\\
=\sum_{j=0}^{\min\{n,\kappa-i\}}\frac{(i+j)!}{i!(-\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau))^{j}}\binom{n}{j}\frac{\partial^{n-j}f_{i+j}}{\partial w^{n-j}}(\jmath(\tau)).

The computation of
\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})\circ\jmath
is ad hoc.
So as not to interrupt the flow of of the body of this section, we will do it in Appendix
A
.

We now generalize Proposition
4.11
.

Proposition 6.9
.

If
f\in S^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
with
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3})
, then

(2\pi\sqrt{-1}/\Omega_{\infty})^{n+i-k_{2}}[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath\in{\mathscr{S}}^{[\min\{n,k_{2}-k_{1}-i\}]}_{(k_{1}+i;k_{3}+n)}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi|_{K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})

for
i=0,1,2,\dots,k_{2}-k_{1}
and a non-negative integer
n
.

Remark 6.10
.

This is a special case of Remark 7.10.3 of
[
Har86
]
.

Proof.

Proposition
A.4
says that
[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath
is a nearly holomorphic cusp form on
H
of weight
(k_{1}+i;k_{3}+n)
.
It has degree at most
\min\{n,k_{2}-k_{1}-i\}
by (
6.2
).

To prove the
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rationality, we use the notation of Sections 11 and 26.3 of Shimura’s book
[
Shi00
]
.
Section 11.2 of
[
Shi00
]
defines the
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rationality of modular forms on unitary groups over CM fields in terms of values at CM points.
Since CM points are related to
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-quadruples, one can show that elements of
S^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
are
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational in the sense of Shimura.
Moreover, Theorem 14.7 of
[
Shi00
]
shows that the value of
\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})
at any CM point is algebraic.

Let
Y=E\oplus E\oplus E
be a (split) CM-algebras.
Put
Y^{u}=\{a\in Y\;|\;aa^{c}=1\}
.
Fix
\tau_{0}\in{\mathfrak{H}}\cap E
.
Put
Z_{0}=\begin{bmatrix}\tau_{0}\\
0\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}
.
Define an
E
-linear ring injection
h:Y\to\mathrm{M}_{3}(E)
by

h(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})=B(Z_{0})\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&0&0\\
0&a_{2}&0\\
0&0&a_{3}\end{bmatrix}B(Z_{0})^{-1}.

Since
S_{\delta}\,^{t}\!h(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})^{c}S_{\delta}^{-1}=h(a_{1}^{c},a_{2}^{c},a_{3}^{c})
by (6.1.7) and (6.1.8) of
[
Shi97
]
, we have
h(Y^{u})\subset G({\mathbb{Q}})
.
Moreover,
Z_{0}
is the common fixed point of
h(Y^{u})
, and

\displaystyle\lambda(h(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}),Z_{0})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\iota_{\infty}^{c}(a_{1})&0\\
0&\iota_{\infty}^{c}(a_{2})\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\mu(h(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}),Z_{0})

\displaystyle=\iota_{\infty}(a_{3})

for
(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\in Y^{u}
.

Let
J_{E}=\{\iota_{\infty},\iota_{\infty}^{c}\}
be the set of all isomorphisms of
E
into
{\mathbb{C}}
and
I_{E}
the free abelian group generated by
J_{E}
.
Shimura introduced the period symbol
p_{E}:I_{E}\times I_{E}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}/\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}^{\times}
which is linear with respect to both of the arguments.
Actually,
p_{E}(\iota_{\infty},\iota_{\infty})
coincides with the geometric period of a CM elliptic curve.

Let
\varPhi=(\iota_{\infty}^{c},\iota_{\infty}^{c},\iota_{\infty})
be a ring homomorphism of
Y
into
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Then

(6.3)

p_{Y}(\iota_{\infty}^{c},\varPhi)=p_{Y}(\iota_{\infty},\varPhi)=p_{E}(\iota_{\infty},\iota_{\infty})=\Omega_{\infty}/\pi

by (11.23) of
[
Shi00
]
.
We conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}(Z_{0})

\displaystyle=\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}p_{Y}(\iota_{\infty}^{c},\varPhi)&0\\
0&p_{Y}(\iota_{\infty}^{c},\varPhi)\end{bmatrix},p_{Y}(\iota_{\infty},\varPhi)\biggl)=\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{\pi}&0\\
0&\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{\pi}\end{bmatrix},\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{\pi}\biggl).

Since
\upsilon({\mathfrak{p}}(Z_{0}))\underline{v}_{0}=\Omega_{\infty}^{2}/\pi^{2}
(see §
A.2
for the definition of the action
\upsilon
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
on
{\mathfrak{T}}=\mathrm{M}_{2,1}({\mathbb{C}})
), we have

(\Omega_{\infty}/\pi)^{-2n}\rho_{\underline{k}}({\mathfrak{p}}(Z_{0})^{-1})\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})(Z_{0})\in{L}_{\underline{k}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}).

Equivalently,

(\Omega_{\infty}/\pi)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}-2n}[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}(\jmath(\tau_{0}))\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}

for
i=0,1,2,\dots,k_{2}-k_{1}
.
Our proof is now complete by Proposition
6.8
.
∎

We write
\mathrm{Hol}:{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})\to S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
for the holomorphic projection.
Recall that if
2t<k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}
and
f\in{\mathscr{S}}^{[t]}_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
, then
\mathrm{Hol}(f)\in S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
is the unique cusp form such that

f-\mathrm{Hol}(f)=\sum_{\nu=1}^{t}\delta_{(k_{1}^{\prime}+\nu;k_{2}^{\prime}-\nu)}^{\nu}h_{\nu}

with
h_{\nu}\in S_{(k_{1}^{\prime}+\nu;k_{2}^{\prime}-\nu)}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime})
(cf. Remark
6.6
).

Proposition 6.11
.

Notations and assumptions being as in Proposition
6.9
, we let
\beta={\mathbf{d}}(a)
and put
{\mathfrak{a}}=a{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
.
Then

\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\frac{\mathrm{Hol}([\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath)(\tau,\beta)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1}/\Omega_{\infty})^{k_{2}-i-n}}=\lim_{j\to\infty}\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{i-k_{2}}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}}\frac{\partial^{n}\mathrm{FJ}^{mp^{j!}}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{i}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{n}\;\partial w^{n}}(0)q^{m}.

Remark 6.12
.

Proposition 4.8 of
[
Har21a
]
says that the left hand side coincides with the
p
-adic differential operator
\Theta^{n}
.

Proof.

Put
{F}=\Big(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\Big)^{n+i-k_{2}}[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath
.
Observe that

2\min\{n,k_{2}-k_{1}-i\}<k_{3}+n-k_{1}-i.

We can write

(6.4)

{F}=\mathrm{Hol}({F})+\sum_{\nu=1}^{\min\{n,k_{2}-k_{1}-i\}}\delta_{(k_{1}^{\prime}+\nu;k_{2}^{\prime}-\nu)}^{\nu}h_{\nu}

with
h_{\nu}\in S_{(k_{1}+i+\nu;k_{3}+n-\nu)}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi|_{K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}})})
.
Recall the relation

\delta^{\nu}_{k}=\sum_{a=0}^{\nu}{\nu\choose a}\frac{\Gamma(\nu+k)}{\Gamma(a+k)}(-4\pi y)^{a-\nu}\left(\frac{1}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\right)^{a}

(see
[
Hid93
, (3), p. 311]
and Remark
6.6
).
Since

[D_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}(\jmath(\tau))=\frac{\partial^{n}f_{i}}{\partial w^{n}}(\jmath(\tau))\pmod{\eta(\tau)^{-1}}

by (
6.2
), we see that
\mathrm{Hol}({F})
equals

\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{i-k_{2}}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}_{+}}\frac{\partial^{n}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f)_{i}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{n}\;\partial w^{n}}(0)q^{m}-\sum_{\nu=1}^{\min\{n,k_{2}-k_{1}-i\}}\theta^{\nu}h_{\nu}

by equating the constant terms of the identity (
6.4
) as a polynomial in
\frac{1}{\eta(\tau)}=\frac{1}{2y}
.
Here
\theta
stands for Serre’s operator
\theta(\sum_{m}a_{m}q^{m})=\sum_{m}ma_{m}q^{m}
.
Since
\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\circ\theta=0
, we get the stated formula in view of (
5.2
).
∎

Remark 6.13
.

Define a representation
\rho^{\prime}_{\underline{k}}
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
by

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{\underline{k}}(g,t)

\displaystyle=\rho_{\underline{k}}(\sqrt{T^{\prime}}g\sqrt{T^{\prime}}^{-1},t),

\displaystyle\sqrt{T^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&\sqrt{\frac{|\delta|}{2}}\end{bmatrix}.

Here
|\delta|=\sqrt{D_{E}}
.
Note that
\rho^{\prime}_{\underline{k}}(\overline{\lambda(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})},1)=\rho^{\prime}_{\underline{k}}(\,^{t}\!\lambda(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}})^{-1},1)
for
\alpha\in{K}_{\infty}
by the isomorphism (
2.3
).
Set

f^{\prime}=\rho_{\underline{k}}(\sqrt{T^{\prime}},1)f.

Define the differential operator
D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}^{\prime}}
in the same way as in §
A.2
but with

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}(Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{T^{\prime}}^{-1}\xi(Z)\sqrt{T^{\prime}}^{-1},

\displaystyle u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{T^{\prime}}u

for
u\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.
Observe that

[\delta_{\rho_{\underline{k}}^{\prime}}^{n}f^{\prime}(\underline{v}_{0}^{\prime n})]_{i}=(\rho_{\underline{k}}\otimes\upsilon^{n})(\sqrt{T^{\prime}},1)[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}=\biggl(\frac{2}{|\delta|}\biggl)^{\frac{k_{2}-i-n}{2}}[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}.

6.7.
The work of Bannai and Kobayashi

We fix a number field
F
containing
E
and that for every
\alpha\in E
the morphism
\iota(\alpha)
is also defined over
F
, where
\iota:E\hookrightarrow\mathrm{End}(E_{\mathfrak{a}})\otimes{\mathbb{Q}}
is an embedding.
Fix a nonzero invariant differential one form
\omega_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}}
defined over
F
such that
\iota(\alpha)^{*}\omega_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}}=\iota_{\infty}(\alpha)\omega_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}}
for
\alpha\in E
.
Fix the complex period
\Omega_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
so that the pullback of
\omega_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}}
via
{\mathbb{C}}/\Gamma_{\mathfrak{a}}\to E_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})
is
\mathrm{d}w
, where
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\iota_{\infty}({\mathfrak{a}})\Omega_{\infty}
is called a period lattice of
(E_{\mathfrak{a}},\omega_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}})
and
w
is the canonical basis of
{\mathbb{C}}
.
We associate to
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})
a reduced theta function
\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}
on
{\mathbb{C}}/\Gamma_{\mathfrak{a}}
by

\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}(w):=\theta(w/\Omega_{\infty}).

Bannai and Kobayashi have proved that it has algebraic Taylor coefficients.

Theorem 6.14

(
[
BK10
, Theorem 2.9]
)
.

If
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, then for every
l\in E

\Omega_{\infty}^{-n}\frac{\partial^{n}A_{m}(l)\theta}{\partial w^{n}}(0)\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}.

Remark 6.15
.

We define the translation
A^{\Omega_{\infty}}_{m}(l)
by
l\in E
so that

A^{\Omega_{\infty}}_{m}(l)\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}=[A_{m}(l)\theta]^{\Omega_{\infty}}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$A^{\Omega_{\infty}}_{m}(l)$}}}

It is explained in
[
BK10
,
Hsi21
]
that the translation
A^{\Omega_{\infty}}_{m}(l)
by a torsion point
l
preserves the algebraicity.
Consequently, Definition
3.12
coincides with the notion of algebraicity of theta functions in
[
BK10
]
.

Take a Weierstrass model

{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}:y^{2}=4x^{3}-g_{2}x-g_{3}

which is good for the prime ideal
{\mathfrak{P}}
of
R_{F}
.
Let
F_{\mathfrak{P}}
be the completion of
F
relative to
{\mathfrak{P}}
.
Denote by
{O}_{F_{\mathfrak{P}}}
the maximal compact subring of
F_{\mathfrak{P}}
.
Let
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}
be the formal group associated to
{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}
with respect to the parameter
t=-\frac{2x}{y}
.
We denote by
\lambda(t)
the formal logarithm of
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}
, which is a power series giving a homomorphism of
formal groups
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}\to\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{a}
,
z=\lambda(t)
and is normalized so that
\lambda^{\prime}(0)=1
.
Since the coefficients of the Taylor expansion of
\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}(w)
are algebraic, we can consider its formal composition
\widehat{\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(t)
with
w=\lambda(t)
.

Bannai and Kobayashi proved the
{\mathfrak{p}}
-integrality of the Taylor expansion of reduced theta functions associated to meromorphic sections of line bundles of CM abelian varieties with respect to formal group parameters.
Moreover, they computed the
p
-adic translation by
{\mathfrak{p}}
-power torsion points.
We denote the ring of integers of
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
by
{O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}
and the maximal ideal of
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
by
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}
.
Let

\wp_{p}:\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}})_{\mathrm{tor}}\to E_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}}_{p})_{\mathrm{tor}}=E_{\mathfrak{a}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})_{\mathrm{tor}}=E_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})_{\mathrm{tor}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}E/{\mathfrak{a}}

be the morphism obtained by the complex uniformization
\wp:E_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathfrak{a}}
.
We denote the set of
{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}
-power torsion point of the formal group
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}
by
\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}[{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}]
.
When
{G}
is a formal group, we write
\oplus_{G}
for the formal addition.

Theorem 6.16

(
[
BK10
, Propositions 2.15, 2.16, 2.20]
)
.

Let
\theta\in{\mathbf{T}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({O})
.

(1)

\widehat{\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(t)=\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}(\lambda(t))\in{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}\llbracket{t}\rrbracket
.

(2)

Let
\varepsilon
be an element of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
\varepsilon\equiv 1\pmod{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}
and
\varepsilon\in 2({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{n}
.
Then
\widehat{\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(t\oplus_{\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}})=A^{\Omega_{\infty}}_{m}\yhwidehat{(\varepsilon\wp_{p}(t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}))\theta^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(t)
for every
t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}\in\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}[{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}]
.

Remark 6.17
.

More generally, Bannai and Kobayashi study reduced theta functions associated to meromorphic sections of
{\mathfrak{L}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}
.
Since we consider only holomorphic sections, we can let
f=1
in Propositions 2.15, 2.16 and Theorem 2.9 of
[
BK10
]
.
We here apply Proposition 2.20 of
[
BK10
]
with
v_{0}=0
.

Let
{\mathbb{W}}={\mathbb{W}}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p})
be the ring of Witt vectors with coefficients in
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{p}
.
It is known that there exists an isomorphism
\Xi_{p}:\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}\simeq\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{m}
of formal groups over
{\mathbb{W}}
.
We fix such an isomorphism
\Xi_{p}
, and then
\Xi_{p}
is given by a power series

\Xi_{p}(t)=\exp(\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\lambda(t))-1,

where
\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{W}}^{\times}
is a suitable
p
-adic period.
We denote by
\Upsilon(T)
the inverse of
\Xi_{p}
.
Since
\Xi_{p}
is an isomorphism, the coefficients of
\Upsilon(T)
are also in
{\mathbb{W}}
.
We associate to
g(t)\in{\mathbb{W}}\llbracket{t}\rrbracket
another power series
g(\Upsilon(T))\in{\mathbb{W}}\llbracket{T}\rrbracket
on
\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}}_{m}
.

We briefly recall the bijection between the space
\mathrm{Dist}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p},{O})
of
p
-adic measures on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
and the ring of the power series
{O}\llbracket{T}\rrbracket
given by the
p
-adic Mellin transform. See Section 4 of
[
Lan90
]
for details.
The bijection
g\mapsto\mathrm{d}\mu(g)
is given by

\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}(1+T)^{x}\mathrm{d}\mu(g)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}T^{k}\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\binom{x}{k}\mathrm{d}\mu(g)=g(T).

The power series
g(T)
is called the
p
-adic Mellin transform of
\mathrm{d}\mu(g)
. For every non-negative integer
n
we have

(6.5)

\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}x^{n}\mathrm{d}\mu(g)=D_{T}^{n}g(0),

where
D_{T}=(1+T)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}T}
. For an
{O}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{p}}
-valued function
\phi
on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
we define
\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}g\in{O}\llbracket{T}\rrbracket
by

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}g(T)=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\phi(x)(1+T)^{x}\mathrm{d}\mu(g)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}T^{k}\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\phi(x)\binom{x}{k}\mathrm{d}\mu(g).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}$}}}

If
\phi
factors through
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, then

(6.6)

\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}g(T)=\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{u\in{\mathbb{Z}}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}}\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p^{n}}}\zeta^{-u}\phi(u)g(\zeta(T+1)-1),

where
\mu_{p^{n}}
denotes the group of
p^{n}
th roots of unity in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
.

6.8.
The
\Theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}}
-operator

Let
f\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}},\chi;{O})
.
Fix a locally constant function
\breve{\phi}\in{C}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
.
To simplify notation, we put

\displaystyle f^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f,

\displaystyle\mho

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}.

Remark 6.18
.

The period ratio
\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\Omega_{\infty}}
is not an element of any ring.
The symbol
\mho
has to be interpreted as an operation rather than a number.

Let
j
be sufficiently large such that
\breve{\phi}
factors through
({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{j}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}
.
Put
{\mathfrak{c}}=({\mathfrak{p}}^{c})^{j}{\mathfrak{b}}_{0}
.
Proposition
6.4
shows that

\theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime}):=\frac{\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime})_{0}}{\mho^{k_{2}}}\in{\mathbf{T}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{c}}}^{m}({O}).

Therefore the reduced theta function
\theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime})^{\Omega_{\infty}}
on an elliptic curve
E_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{c}}}({\mathbb{C}})={\mathbb{C}}/\iota_{\infty}({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{c}})\Omega_{\infty}
has algebraic Taylor coefficients by Theorem
6.14
.
Moreover, we define the power series

(6.7)

\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime})(T):=\yhwidehat{\theta^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime})^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(\Upsilon(T)).

Then
\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime})(T)
is a power series with
p
-integral coefficients by Theorem
6.16
(
1
).
Hence we can associate the
p
-adic measure
\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(f^{\prime}))
.

Definition 6.19
.

Let
U
be an open compact subgroup of
G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
which contains
{N}({\mathfrak{c}})
.
We define the theta operator
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}{F}
for
{F}\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U;{O})
with respect to a locally constant function
\phi\in{C}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
by

[\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}{F}](g)=\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{x\in p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\sum_{z\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\phi(z){\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(zx){F}\left(g\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\right),{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}$}}}

provided that
\phi
factors through the quotient
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}\to({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}
.
It is easy to check that this definition is independent of the choice of
n
(cf. (
6.6
)).

Proposition 6.20
.

If
\phi\in{C}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times},{O})
is locally constant, then for
{F}\in S_{\underline{k}}^{G}(U;{O})

\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}{F})=\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({F}).

Proof.

Take a set
{X}_{n}
of representatives for
{\mathfrak{p}}^{-n}{\mathfrak{c}}/{\mathfrak{c}}
.
Given
x\in{\mathbb{E}}
, we write its
p
-component by
x_{p}\in E_{p}
.
Put

{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(x,0)=\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)

for
x\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
By definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{FJ}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}{F})_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{x\in p^{-n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\sum_{z\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\phi(z){\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(zx)\mathrm{FJ}_{{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(x,0){\mathbf{d}}(a)}^{m}({F})_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{x\in{X}_{n}}\sum_{z\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\phi(z){\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}(z\mathrm{T}_{E_{p}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}(x_{p}))[A_{m}(-x)\mathrm{FJ}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}({F})_{0}](w).

Define an isomorphism
\iota_{n}:\widehat{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{c}}}[{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}]\simeq\mu_{p^{n}}
by
\iota_{n}(t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}})=e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{T}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\wp_{p}(t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}))}
.
Then Theorem
6.16
(
2
) shows that

\yhwidehat{\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}{F})^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(t)\\
=\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{c}}}[{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}]}\sum_{z\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\phi(z)\iota_{n}(t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}})^{z}\yhwidehat{\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}({F})^{\Omega_{\infty}}}(t\oplus_{\widehat{{\mathscr{E}}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{c}}}}t_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}).

We conclude by (
6.6
) that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}{F})(T)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{n}}\sum_{\zeta\in\mu_{p^{n}}}\sum_{z\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\phi(z)\zeta^{z}\cdot\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}({F})(T\oplus_{\widehat{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}}(\zeta-1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}({F}),

as claimed.
∎

6.9.
The
p
-adic interpolation of
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}

Let
J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime},m}(f)\in\Lambda_{2}
correspond to the
{O}
-valued distribution

(\phi,\breve{\phi})\mapsto\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\phi(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)).

Namely, for
\underline{Q}=(Q_{1},Q_{2})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda_{2}}
with
k_{\underline{Q}}=(0,0)
and
\epsilon_{\underline{Q}}=(\epsilon_{Q_{1}},\epsilon_{Q_{2}})
, we have

J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime},m}(f)_{\underline{Q}}=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\epsilon_{Q_{2}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)).

Proposition 6.21
.

If
k_{Q_{1}}=0
and
k_{Q_{2}}\geq 0
, then

J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime},m}(f)_{\underline{Q}}=\mho^{k_{Q_{2}}-k_{2}}\frac{\partial^{k_{Q_{2}}}\mathrm{FJ}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)_{0}}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k_{Q_{2}}}\partial w^{k_{Q_{2}}}}(0).

Proof.

By the definition of
\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}}}
in §
6.7
we have

\displaystyle J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime},m}(f)_{\underline{Q}}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}x^{k_{Q_{2}}}\epsilon_{Q_{2}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f))

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}x^{k_{Q_{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\underline{\boldsymbol{\Theta}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}}}\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)).

Proposition
6.20
and (
6.5
) show that
