J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime},m}(f)_{\underline{Q}}=D_{T}^{k_{Q_{2}}}\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)(0).

Since
\lambda(\Upsilon(T))=\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}\log(1+T)
, we conclude that

J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime},m}(f)_{\underline{Q}}=\mho^{-k_{2}}\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{k_{Q_{2}}}\frac{\partial^{k_{Q_{2}}}\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)_{0}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{k_{Q_{2}}}\partial w^{k_{Q_{2}}}}(0)

as claimed.
∎

6.10.
Construction of
p
-adic families

Put

{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\underline{k}}=\{(Q_{1}^{\prime},Q_{2}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\Lambda_{2}}\;|\;k_{1}=k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\leq k_{2},\;k_{3}\leq k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\}.

We write
\chi_{p}|_{K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell})}=\epsilon_{1}^{\uparrow}\epsilon_{3}^{\downarrow}
, where

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}^{\uparrow}(u)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{1}(a),

\displaystyle\epsilon_{3}^{\downarrow}(u)

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{3}(d)

for
u=\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\in K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell})
.

Proposition
6.21
gives an element
J^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3},m}_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}(f)\in\Lambda_{2}
such that

\underline{Q}^{\prime}(J_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3},m}(f))=\mho^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{2}-k_{3}}\frac{\partial^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}}\mathrm{FJ}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{m}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{3}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f)_{0}}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}}\partial w^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}}}(0)

for
\underline{Q}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\underline{k}}
.
Now we define the formal power series
J^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3}}_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}(f)\in\Lambda_{2}\llbracket{q}\rrbracket
by

J^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3}}_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}(f)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\sum_{m\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathfrak{a}}^{+}}J_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3},mp^{j!}}(f)\,q^{m}.

We will see that the limit makes sense in the proof of the following corollary.

Corollary 6.22
.

Notation being as above, if
\underline{Q}^{\prime}=(Q_{1}^{\prime},Q_{2}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\Lambda_{2}}^{\underline{k}}
, then
J^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3}}_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}(f)_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
is the Fourier expansion at
{\mathfrak{a}}
of

\mho^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{2}-k_{3}}\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\mathrm{Hol}\biggl(\biggl[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{3}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f(v_{0}^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}})\biggl]_{0}\circ\jmath\biggl).

In particular, we have

J^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}_{\underline{k}}(f)\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},\Lambda_{2}).

Proof.

Proposition
6.11
shows that

{F}:=\mathrm{Hol}\biggl(\biggl[\delta_{\underline{k}}^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{3}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{1}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f(v_{0}^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{3}})\biggl]_{k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}-k_{1}}\circ\jmath\biggl)

has weight
(k_{Q_{1}^{\prime}};k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}})
.
Moreover, Proposition
6.21
says that

\lim_{j\to\infty}\underline{Q}^{\prime}(J_{{\mathfrak{a}},\underline{k}}^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{3},mp^{j!}}(f))=\mho^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{2}-k_{3}}\mathrm{FC}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}{F})

by (
5.2
).
The open compact subgroup
K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})
acts on
{F}
by the character
(\chi^{\prime}\epsilon_{\underline{Q}})^{-1}
in view of Corollary
8.18
and
[
HY25
, Proposition 6.7(i)]
, which confirms that
J^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}_{\underline{k}}(f)_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\in\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}S^{H}_{k_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}\epsilon_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}},\Lambda_{2}(\underline{Q}^{\prime}))
.
∎

6.11.
Construction of theta elements

We normalize the integrals by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\Phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{H({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash H({\mathbb{A}})}\Phi((h,h))\,\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\Phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{H({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash H({\mathbb{A}})}\Phi^{\prime}((h,h))\,\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h

for
\Phi\in{\mathscr{A}}^{0}(H\times H)
and
\Phi^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{A}}^{0}(G\times H)
, where
\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h
is defined in §
7.3
.
Let
{\mathbf{I}}_{n}
be a normal ring finite flat over
\Lambda_{n}={O}\llbracket{T_{n}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}\rrbracket
.
Let

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{f}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbf{S}}^{G}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbf{I}}_{3}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{g}

\displaystyle\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbf{I}}_{2})

be Hida families.
We define
J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})\in{\mathbf{I}}_{3}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}\llbracket{q}\rrbracket
by

J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})_{{Q}}=J_{{\mathfrak{a}},k_{\underline{Q}}}^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{Q_{1}},\epsilon_{Q_{3}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}

for
{Q}=(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}
.
By (
5.5
) and (
6.7
) we have

J_{{\mathfrak{a}},k_{\underline{Q}}}^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{Q_{1}},\epsilon_{Q_{3}},m}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}=\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}(Q_{2}^{\prime}Q_{3}^{-1})(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\yhwidehat{\mathrm{FJ}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{Q_{1}^{\prime}Q_{1}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}\underline{Q}(\boldsymbol{f}))_{0}^{\Omega_{\infty}}}\circ\Upsilon).

Corollary
6.22
says that
\{J_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})\}_{{\mathfrak{a}}\in C_{E}}
gives an
{\mathbf{I}}_{3}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}
-adic cusp form

J^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},{\mathbf{I}}_{3}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}).

We may assume that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}\in C_{E}
.
Writing
1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}\in{\mathbf{T}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}(N^{\prime},\chi^{\prime},,{\mathbf{I}}_{2})\otimes_{{\mathbf{I}}_{2}}\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}
for the idempotent corresponding to
\boldsymbol{g}
(cf. Proposition
5.8
), we define the theta element
\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}\in{\mathbf{I}}_{3}\widehat{\otimes}\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}
attached to
\boldsymbol{f}
and
\boldsymbol{g}
by

\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}=\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}J^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}$}}}

See Definition
5.11
for the symbol
\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}
.
Let
\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}
be a
{U}^{\prime}_{p}
-eigenform with
p
-unit eigenvalue which is anti-holomorphic and
K_{1}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})
-invariant.

Proposition 6.23
.

If
\ell
is sufficiently large, then

\displaystyle{Q}(\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}})\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}\Big(\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mho^{m_{Q}}{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}\biggl(\delta_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{Q_{3}}}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{Q_{3}}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{Q_{1}}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}\varPhi(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\biggl)

for
{Q}=(\underline{Q},\underline{Q}^{\prime})\in{\mathfrak{Y}}^{\rm crit}_{\mathbf{V}}\cap({\mathfrak{X}}_{R}^{\mathrm{cls}}\times{\mathfrak{X}}_{\boldsymbol{g}}^{\prime\prime})
, where

m_{{Q}}=k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-(k_{Q_{2}}+k_{Q_{3}}).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Proposition 3.7 of
[
Hsi21
]
.
Since

\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}({U}_{p}^{\prime}\varphi,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi^{\prime})=\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}{U}_{p}^{\prime}\varphi^{\prime}),

we have

\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}\varphi,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat})=\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi,\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}).

Since
\varPhi(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})=\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
is a
p
-stabilized ordinary newform, we have

{Q}(\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}})\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ord}}=1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}\varPhi(J^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})_{{Q}})

by multiplicity one for
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
, and for new and ordinary forms (see Remark
4.7
, §
8.2
and
[
Hsi21
, Proposition 2.2]
).
Note that
\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(\boldsymbol{g}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}})\neq 0
by Proposition
5.9
.
Taking the pairing with
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\in\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}
, we get

\displaystyle{Q}(\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}})\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}\Big(\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}\Big(\varPhi(J^{\chi^{\prime}}(\boldsymbol{f})_{Q})\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}\Big(\varPhi\bigl(J_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{\chi^{\prime},\epsilon_{Q_{1}},\epsilon_{Q_{3}}}(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}\bigl)\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mho^{m_{Q}}{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}\biggl(\delta_{k_{\underline{Q}}}^{k_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}-k_{Q_{3}}}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{2}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{Q_{3}}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\epsilon_{Q_{1}^{\prime}}\epsilon_{Q_{1}}^{-1}}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}\varPhi(\boldsymbol{f}_{\underline{Q}})_{0}\otimes\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}\varphi_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\underline{Q}^{\prime}}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\biggl)

by Corollary
6.22
.
∎

The theta element
\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}
is the five variable
p
-adic
L
-function of the title.
Proposition
6.23
is the basis for the comparison of the square of the specialization of
\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}
at classical points with normalized central values of tensor product
L
-functions.
This comparison is carried out in Section
7
, using the Ichino-Ikeda formula.
The calculation of the non-archimedean Euler factors is carried out in Section
8
, and that of the archimedean Euler factor is completed in the appendices.

7.
The central value formulas

7.1.
An outer involution

Put
I_{r,s}=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{r}&\\
&-\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix}
.
Suppose that
\gamma_{0}^{c}=-\gamma_{0}
, that is,
\,{}^{t}\!\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{0}
.
Since
S_{\gamma_{0}}^{c}=-I_{r,s}S_{\gamma_{0}}I_{r,s}
, we can define an outer automorphism of
G
by

(7.1)

g^{\natural}=I_{r,s}g^{c}I_{r,s}=S_{\gamma_{0}}I_{r,s}\,^{t}\!g^{-1}I_{r,s}S_{\gamma_{0}}^{-1}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$g^{\natural}$}}}

Observe that for
\alpha\in G({\mathbb{R}})
and
Z\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
.

\displaystyle\alpha^{\natural}(Z)=-\alpha(-Z^{c})^{c},

\displaystyle\lambda(\alpha^{\natural},-Z^{c})=\lambda(\alpha,Z)^{c},

\displaystyle\mu(\alpha^{\natural},-Z^{c})=\mu(\alpha,Z)^{c}.

It follows that
{K}_{\infty}^{\natural}={K}_{\infty}
, and for
k\in{K}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lambda(k^{\natural},{\mathbf{i}})

\displaystyle=\,^{t}\!(T^{\prime}\lambda(k,{\mathbf{i}})T^{\prime-1})^{-1},

\displaystyle\mu(k^{\natural},{\mathbf{i}})

\displaystyle=\,^{t}\!\mu(k,{\mathbf{i}})^{-1}.

Let
\rho=\rho_{\underline{k}}
.
Put
T=(T^{\prime},\hbox{\bf 1}_{s})
.
Define representations
\rho^{c}
and
\rho^{T}
by

\displaystyle\rho^{c}(h)

\displaystyle=\rho(h^{c}),

\displaystyle\rho^{T}(h)

\displaystyle=\rho(\,^{t}\!(ThT^{-1})^{-1})

for
h\in{\mathscr{H}}(R)=\mathrm{GL}_{r}(R)\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}(R)
.
Let
{}^{\natural}:L_{\underline{k}}(R)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(R)
such that

\displaystyle(\rho_{\underline{k}}^{T}(h)P)^{\natural}

\displaystyle=\rho_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(h)P^{\natural},

\displaystyle\ell_{\underline{k}}(P^{\natural}\otimes Q)=\ell_{\underline{k}}(Q^{\natural}\otimes P).

Let
\chi
be a character of
K_{0}({\mathfrak{N}})
whose restriction to
K_{1}({\mathfrak{N}})
is trivial.
Given
f\in S_{\rho}^{G}({\mathfrak{N}},\chi,{\mathbb{C}})
, we define a smooth function
f^{\natural}:{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}\times G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})\to L_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{C}})
by

f^{\natural}(Z,\beta)=f(-Z^{c},\beta^{\natural})^{\natural}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$f^{\natural}$}}}

We define
\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}=(\tau_{{\mathfrak{N}},l})\in G(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
by

\displaystyle T_{\gamma_{0}}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\\
0&-\gamma_{0}&0\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\tau_{{\mathfrak{N}},l}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}^{-1}\Biggl(T_{\gamma_{0}}\begin{bmatrix}N\hbox{\bf 1}_{r+s-1}&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\Biggl)&\text{if $l|N$, }\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{r+s}&\text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

Note that
(\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}K_{0}({\mathfrak{N}})\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}^{-1})^{\natural}=K_{0}({\mathfrak{N}})
.
Put

\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}f=(r(\tau_{\mathfrak{N}})f)^{\natural}.

Define the function
\varPhi_{\rho^{c}}(\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}f)
as in §
3.4
.
Then

\varPhi_{\rho^{c}}(\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}f)(\gamma g\alpha k)=\rho_{\underline{k}^{\vee}}(J(\alpha,{\mathbf{i}}))^{-1}\varPhi_{\rho^{c}}(\tau_{\mathfrak{N}}f)(g)\chi(k)^{-1}

for
\gamma\in G({\mathbb{Q}})
,
\alpha\in{K}_{\infty}
and
k\in K_{0}({\mathfrak{N}})
.

7.2.
A factorization of the dual representation

For a function on
\varphi
on
G({\mathbb{A}})
we define a function
\varphi^{\natural}:G({\mathbb{A}})\to{\mathbb{C}}
by
\varphi^{\natural}(g)=\varphi(g^{\natural})
.
When
\varphi
is a cusp form on
G
, so is
\varphi^{\natural}
.
Let
\pi^{\vee}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}^{\vee}
denote the contragredient representation of
\pi
.
We define representations
\pi^{\natural}
of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
\pi_{v}^{\natural}
of
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
as the twists
\pi^{\natural}(g)=\pi(g^{\natural})
and
\pi_{v}^{\natural}(g_{v})=\pi_{v}(g_{v}^{\natural})
for
g\in G({\mathbb{A}})
and
g_{v}\in G({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
.
It is well-known that
\pi_{v}^{\vee}\simeq\pi_{v}^{\natural}
(see
[
MVW87
]
).
Since

\varphi^{\natural}(gh)=\varphi((gh)^{\natural})=(\pi^{\natural}(h)\varphi)^{\natural}(g),{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\varphi^{\natural}$}}}

we have
\{\overline{\varphi}\;|\;\varphi\in\pi\}=\{\varphi^{\natural}\;|\;\varphi\in\pi\}
by the global multiplicity one for unitary groups (see Remark
4.7
), where the automorphic form
\overline{\varphi}
is defined by
\overline{\varphi}(g)=\overline{\varphi(g)}
.

For every non-split prime
q
we require that
\pi_{q}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}
-invariant vector.
For each split prime
l
we denote the conductor of
\pi_{l}
by
c(\pi_{l})
in the sense of (
8.2
).
Let
N_{\pi}=\prod_{l}l^{c(\pi_{l})}
be the conductor of
\pi
.
We take ideals
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N_{\pi}{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Let
\varphi_{\pi}\in\pi
be an essential vector with respect to
K_{1}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi})
which has highest weight in the minimal
{K}_{\infty}
-type (see Definition
8.2
).
Define the longest Weyl element
w_{n}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
by

\displaystyle w_{1}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle w_{n}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
w_{n-1}&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle(n

\displaystyle\geq 2).

When
l
is split in
E
, we identify
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
with
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(E_{\mathfrak{l}})
via
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}
, realize
\pi_{l}
in the Whittaker model
{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}}(\pi_{l})
with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}
and denote by
W_{\pi_{l}}\in\pi_{l}
the normalized essential Whittaker vector with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}
, and by
W_{\pi_{l}^{\vee}}\in\pi_{l}^{\vee}
the normalized essential Whittaker vector with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}^{-1}
.
For
W\in\pi_{l}
we define
W^{\natural}\in\pi_{l}^{\vee}
by

(7.2)

W^{\natural}(g)=W(w_{n}\,^{t}\!g^{-1}T_{\gamma_{0}}^{-1})=\pi_{l}^{\vee}(T_{\gamma_{0}})\widetilde{W_{l}}(g){\@index{\@indexphrase{$W^{\natural}$}}}

(cf. (
7.1
)) for
g\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(E_{\mathfrak{l}})
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{W_{l}}(g)

\displaystyle=W_{l}(w_{n}\,^{t}\!g^{-1}),

\displaystyle T_{\gamma_{0}}

\displaystyle=-S_{\gamma_{0}}I_{r,s}\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(E_{\mathfrak{l}}).

It is important to note that

\pi^{\vee}(h)W^{\natural}(g)=W^{\natural}(gh)=W((gh)^{\natural})=(\pi^{\natural}(h)W)^{\natural}(g).

When
q
remains a prime in
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
, we fix
{K}_{q}
-invariant vectors
W_{\pi_{q}}\in\pi_{q}
and
W_{\pi_{q}^{\vee}}\in\pi_{q}^{\vee}
.
Fix a highest weight vector
W_{\pi_{\infty}}
in the minimal
{K}_{\infty}
-type of
\pi_{\infty}
and a lowest weight vector
W_{\pi_{\infty}^{\vee}}
in the minimal
{K}_{\infty}
-type of
\pi_{\infty}^{\vee}
.
Let
{}^{\natural}:\pi_{v}^{\natural}\simeq\pi_{v}^{\vee}
be the
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
-equivariant isomorphism determined by
W_{\pi_{v}}^{\natural}=W_{\pi_{v}^{\vee}}
for
v=q,\infty
.

To apply the Ichino-Ikeda formula to
\varPhi
and
\varPhi^{\natural}
, we need explicate the factorization of
\varPhi^{\natural}
.
Fix isomorphisms
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
and
\pi^{\vee}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}^{\vee}
so that

\displaystyle\varphi_{\pi}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{\pi_{v}},

\displaystyle\varphi_{\pi}^{\natural}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{\pi_{v}}^{\natural}.

Using this factorization
\pi^{\vee}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}^{\vee}
, we define a cusp form
\varphi_{\pi^{\vee}}\in\pi^{\vee}
by

\varphi_{\pi^{\vee}}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{\pi_{v}^{\vee}}.

The following result is nothing but Lemma 4.6 of
[
HY25
]
.

Lemma 7.1
.

If
\varphi=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{v}\in\pi
is factorizable and if
W_{v}=W_{\pi_{v}}
for all non-split places
v
, then
\varphi^{\natural}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{v}^{\natural}
.

7.3.
Measures

We define a
G({\mathbb{R}})
-invariant measure
{\mathbf{d}}Z
on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
by

(7.3)

{\mathbf{d}}Z=\frac{2^{s(r+s)}}{\det\eta(Z)^{r+s}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\left[\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\mathrm{d}Z_{ij}\land\mathrm{d}\overline{Z_{ij}}\right].{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{d}}Z$}}}

We extend
{\mathbf{d}}Z
to a Haar measure
\mathrm{d}g_{\infty}
on
G({\mathbb{R}})
so that

(7.4)

\int_{G}f(g_{\infty}({\mathbf{i}}))\,\mathrm{d}g_{\infty}=\int_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}}f(Z)\,{\mathbf{d}}Z.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathrm{d}g_{\infty}$}}}

From now on we let
r=2
,
s=1
and
\gamma_{0}=\delta
.
We view
H=\mathrm{U}(1,1)
as a subgroup of
G
via the embedding

\iota\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\biggl)=\begin{bmatrix}a&0&b\\
0&1&0\\
c&0&d\end{bmatrix}.

For each finite prime
l
the local Haar measures
\mathrm{d}h_{l}
and
\mathrm{d}g_{l}
are defined so that maximal compact subgroups
K_{l}^{\prime}
and
K_{l}
have volume
1
.
For arithmetic applications it is suitable to use the Haar measures
\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}h_{v}
and
\mathrm{d}_{K}g=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}g_{v}
.
We normalize the Petersson pairing by

\mathrm{Pet}_{K}(\Phi)=\int_{G({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash G({\mathbb{A}})}\Phi((g,g))\,\mathrm{d}_{K}g{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathrm{Pet}_{K}(\Phi)$}}}

for
\Phi\in{\mathscr{A}}^{0}(G\times G)
.
We define the Petersson pairing
\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}
on
{\mathscr{A}}^{0}(H\times H)
relative to
\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h
.

Let
F
be an algebraic number field.
For each place
v
of
F
we normalize the Haar measure
\mathrm{d}a_{v}
of the complete field
F_{v}
in the following way:
if
F_{v}={\mathbb{R}}
, then
\mathrm{d}a_{v}
is the Lebesgue measure;
if
F_{v}={\mathbb{C}}
, then
\mathrm{d}a_{v}=\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{d}a_{v}\mathrm{d}a_{v}^{c}
;
if
v<\infty
, then
\int_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{F_{v}}}\mathrm{d}a_{v}=1
.
Put
\mathrm{d}_{F}a=D_{F}^{-1/2}\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}a_{v}
, where
D_{F}
is the absolute value of the discriminant of
F
.
We define the Haar measure
\mathrm{d}^{\times}a_{v}
of
F_{v}^{\times}
by
\mathrm{d}^{\times}a_{v}=\zeta_{F_{v}}(1)\frac{\mathrm{d}a_{v}}{|a_{v}|_{F_{v}}}
.
Put
\zeta_{F}(s)=\prod_{v}\zeta_{F_{v}}(s)
and
\xi_{F}(s)=D_{F}^{s/2}\zeta_{F}(s)
.
Denote the residue of
\xi_{F}(s)
at
s=1
by
\rho_{F}
.
Then
\mathrm{d}^{\times}_{F}a=\rho_{F}^{-1}\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}^{\times}a_{v}
is the Tamagawa measure.

Let
\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}
be the quadratic Dirichlet character corresponding to
E/{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We denote the complete Dirichlet
L
-function associated to
\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}
by
L(s,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})
, and its finite part by
L_{\mathbf{f}}(s,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})
.
We define the Haar measure
\mathrm{d}t_{v}
of
\mathrm{U}(1)({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
as the quotient measure of the Haar measures of
E_{v}^{\times}
and
{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}^{\times}
.
The Tamagawa measure of
\mathrm{U}(1)({\mathbb{A}})
is given by
\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!t=D_{E}^{-1/2}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})^{-1}\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}t_{v}
.

Let
\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!g
and
\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!h
denote the Tamagawa measures on
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
H({\mathbb{A}})
.
We choose the constants
C_{H}
and
C_{G}
so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!h

\displaystyle=C_{H}\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!g

\displaystyle=C_{G}\mathrm{d}_{K}g.

Lemma 7.2

(
[
Ich07
]
)
.

We have

\displaystyle C_{H}

\displaystyle=2^{2}D_{E}^{-1}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})^{-1}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)^{-1},

\displaystyle C_{G}

\displaystyle=2^{3}D_{E}^{-7/2}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})^{-1}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)^{-1}L(3,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})^{-1}\lambda_{K},

where
\lambda_{K}
is a rational constant depending on
K
.

Proof.

Define the Tamagawa measures of
{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
,
{N}^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
,
{\mathbf{M}}({\mathbb{A}})
,
{N}({\mathbb{A}})
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}_{E}^{\times}a,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}_{\mathbb{Q}}z,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{m}}(a,t)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}_{E}^{\times}a\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{n}}(w,z)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}_{E}w\mathrm{d}_{\mathbb{Q}}z.

Take the Haar measure
\mathrm{d}k_{v}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathrm{d}k_{v}
) of
K_{v}^{\prime}
(resp.
K_{v}
) of total volume
1
.
Let
\mathrm{d}k^{\prime}=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}k_{v}^{\prime}
and
\mathrm{d}k=\prod_{v}\mathrm{d}k_{v}
.
We define the Haar measures

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}_{K^{\prime}}h

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}k^{\prime},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}_{K}g

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{m}}\,\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!{\mathbf{n}}\,\mathrm{d}k.

We choose the constants
C_{H}^{\prime}
and
C_{G}^{\prime}
so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!h

\displaystyle=C_{H}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}^{\tau}h,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!g

\displaystyle=C_{G}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}_{K}^{\tau}g.

Ichino computes the constant
C_{G}^{\prime}
for a good maximal compact subgroup of
G({\mathbb{A}})
.
When we replace it by our open compact subgroup
K
, we have only to change by a rational constant.
Theorems 9.5 and 9.6 of
[
Ich07
]
applied with
F={\mathbb{Q}}
,
m^{\prime}=r-s
,
r_{0}=s
give

\displaystyle C_{H}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=2^{-1}D_{E}^{-1/2}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)^{-1},

\displaystyle C_{G}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=D_{E}^{-1}\rho_{E}\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)^{-1}\xi(3,\epsilon_{E})^{-1}\lambda_{K},

where we put
\xi(s,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})=D_{E}^{s/2}L(s,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})
.

We define the Haar measures of
{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
,
{N}^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
,
{\mathbf{M}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
,
{N}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}(a_{v})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}^{\times}a_{v},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z_{v})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}z_{v},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{m}}(a_{v},t_{v})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}^{\times}a_{v}\mathrm{d}t_{v},

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{n}}(w_{v},z_{v})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{d}w_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{v}.

Recall the measure

{\mathbf{d}}Z=\frac{2^{3}}{\eta(Z)^{3}}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{\tau}\biggl)\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\mathrm{d}w\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{w}\biggl){\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{d}}Z$}}}

on
{\mathfrak{D}}
defined in (
7.3
).
Given
Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}
, we put
a=\sqrt{\eta(Z)/2}
.
Since

Z={\mathbf{n}}(w,\Re\tau){\mathbf{m}}(a,1){\mathbf{i}}

and the measure
\mathrm{d}t_{\infty}
gives
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
the volume 2, we have

\mathrm{d}g_{\infty}=a^{-4}\mathrm{d}^{\times}a(\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{d}w\mathrm{d}w^{c})\mathrm{d}\Re\tau\mathrm{d}k_{\infty}=2^{-3}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{m}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{n}}\mathrm{d}k_{\infty}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mathrm{d}g_{\infty}$}}}

Given
\tau\in{\mathfrak{H}}
, we put
a=\sqrt{\Im\tau}
.
Then

\mathrm{d}h_{\infty}=a^{-2}\mathrm{d}^{\times}a\mathrm{d}\Re\tau\mathrm{d}k_{\infty}^{\prime}=2^{-3}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{m}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}k_{\infty}^{\prime}.

We have seen that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}_{K^{\prime}}h

\displaystyle=2^{3}\rho_{E}^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{\tau}_{K}g

\displaystyle=2^{3}\rho_{E}^{-1}D_{E}^{-1}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{K}g.

It follows that
C_{H}=2^{3}\rho_{E}^{-1}C_{H}^{\prime}
and
C_{G}=2^{3}\rho_{E}^{-1}D_{E}^{-1}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})^{-1}C_{G}^{\prime}
.
Since
\rho_{E}=D_{E}^{1/2}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})
, we get the formula for
C_{H}
.
∎

7.4.
The Ichino-Ikeda formula

Let
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
be an irreducible cuspidal tempered automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
\sigma\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\sigma_{v}
an irreducible cuspidal tempered automorphic representation of
H({\mathbb{A}})
.
We define the product
L
-function associated to
\pi
and
\sigma^{\vee}
by

L(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\mathrm{BC}(\pi)\times\mathrm{BC}(\sigma^{\vee})),

where
\mathrm{BC}(\pi)
(resp.
\mathrm{BC}(\sigma^{\vee})
) is the functorial lift of
\pi
(resp.
\sigma^{\vee}
) to an automorphic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}({\mathbb{E}})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{E}})
).
The right hand side is the
complete

L
-function defined by Jacquet, Piatetski-Shapiro and Shalika in
[
JPSS83
]
, and by Shahidi
[
Sha83
]
.
Assume that both
\pi
and
\sigma
are tempered.
Put

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}(\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})

\displaystyle=\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L(i,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}^{i}),

\displaystyle{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{v}\times\sigma_{v}^{\vee})

\displaystyle=\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{v}\times\sigma_{v}^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi_{v},\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma_{v}^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{i=1}^{3}L(i,\epsilon_{E_{v}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}^{i}),

where
L(s,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})
denotes the adjoint
L
-series for
\sigma^{\vee}
.

Remark 7.3
.

Let
\sigma
be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of a unitary group in
n
variables.
If
n
is even, then
L(s,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})=L(s,\mathrm{BC}(\sigma),\mathrm{As})
is the Asai
L
-series.
If
n
is odd, then
L(s,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})=L(s,\mathrm{BC}(\sigma),\mathrm{As}^{-})
is the twisted Asai
L
-series by Proposition 7.4 of
[
GGP12
]
.
Hence if
l
splits in
E
, then
L(s,\sigma_{l},\mathrm{Ad})=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\sigma_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})
, where
\sigma_{l}
is viewed as a representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
in the right hand side.

Put
{\mathbf{G}}=G\times H
.
We introduce the two Petersson pairings by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}(\varPhi\otimes\varPhi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{G}}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{\mathbf{G}}({\mathbb{A}})}\varPhi(g,h)\varPhi^{\prime}(g,h)\,\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!g\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!h,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}_{K,K^{\prime}}(\varPhi\otimes\varPhi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbf{G}}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash{\mathbf{G}}({\mathbb{A}})}\varPhi(g,h)\varPhi^{\prime}(g,h)\,\mathrm{d}_{K}g\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h

for cusp forms
\varPhi
and
\varPhi^{\prime}
on
{\mathbf{G}}
.
We consider the integrals

\displaystyle{\mathscr{P}}(\varPhi)

\displaystyle=\int_{H({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash H({\mathbb{A}})}\varPhi((h,h))\,\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!h,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi)=\int_{H({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash H({\mathbb{A}})}\varPhi((h,h))\,\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h.

Set
\varPi_{v}=\pi_{v}\otimes\sigma_{v}^{\vee}
.
Fix a
{\mathbf{G}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
-invariant local perfect pairing

\langle\!\langle\quad\rangle\!\rangle_{v}:\varPi_{v}\otimes\varPi_{v}^{\vee}\to{\mathbb{C}}.

If
\pi_{v}
and
\sigma_{v}
are tempered, then the integral

I({\mathbf{W}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{2})=\int_{H({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})}\langle\!\langle\varPi_{v}((h_{v},h_{v})){\mathbf{W}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\rangle\!\rangle_{v}\,\mathrm{d}h_{v}

is convergent for
{\mathbf{W}}_{1}\in\varPi_{v}
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{2}\in\varPi_{v}^{\vee}
.

Ichino and Ikeda
[
II10
]
have refined the global Gross-Prasad conjecture and predicted an explicit relation between the central value and the period for orthogonal groups. The analogue of the Ichino-Ikeda conjecture for unitary groups was formulated in
[
Har14
]
and proved generally in
[
BPLZZ21
,
BPCZ22
]
.

Theorem 7.4

(
[
Zha14
,
BPLZZ21
,
BPCZ22
]
)
.

Let
\pi
(resp.
\sigma
) be an irreducible tempered cuspidal automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
(resp.
H({\mathbb{A}})
).
Put
\varPi=\pi\otimes\sigma^{\vee}
.
If
\varPhi=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}{\mathbf{W}}_{v}\in\varPi
and
\varPhi^{\prime}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime}\in\varPi^{\vee}
are factorizable, then

\frac{{\mathscr{P}}(\varPhi){\mathscr{P}}(\varPhi^{\prime})}{\mathrm{Pet}(\varPhi\otimes\varPhi^{\prime})}=C_{H}\frac{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})}{2^{\varkappa_{\pi}+\varkappa_{\sigma}}}\prod_{v}\frac{I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{v}\times\sigma_{v}^{\vee})\langle\!\langle{\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime}\rangle\!\rangle_{v}},

where
2^{\varkappa_{\pi}}
(resp.
2^{\varkappa_{\sigma}}
) is the order of the component group associated to the
L
-parameter of
\pi
(resp.
\sigma
).

7.5.
The Petersson norm on
\mathrm{U}(1,1)

We will rewrite Theorem
7.4
in a form which is more convenient for our later application.
We use the measures
\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h
and
\mathrm{d}_{K}g
to rewrite the formula as

\frac{{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi){\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi^{\prime})}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K,K^{\prime}}(\varPhi\otimes\varPhi^{\prime})}=C_{G}\frac{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})}{2^{\varkappa_{\pi}+\varkappa_{\sigma}}}\prod_{v}\frac{I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{v}\times\sigma_{v}^{\vee})\langle\!\langle{\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime}\rangle\!\rangle_{v}}.

We substitute the formula for
C_{G}
to get

(7.5)

\frac{{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi){\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi^{\prime})}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K,K^{\prime}}(\varPhi\otimes\varPhi^{\prime})}\\
=2^{3-\varkappa_{\pi}-\varkappa_{\sigma}}D_{E}^{-7/2}\lambda_{K}\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{v}\frac{I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{v}\times\sigma_{v}^{\vee})\langle\!\langle{\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime}\rangle\!\rangle_{v}}.

Let
\varPhi^{\prime}=\varPhi^{\natural}
.
Then
{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi^{\prime})={\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi)
and
{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\prime}={\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\natural}
.
Roughly speaking, this formula tells us that the square of the period integral
{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\varPhi)
is a product of the central value
L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\bigl)
and the local integrals
I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\natural})
.
Thanks to Proposition
6.23
, our task of proving the interpolation formula of
\Theta_{\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{g}}
boils down to (i) choices of test vectors
{\mathbf{W}}_{v}
and (ii) the explicit evaluation of the local integrals
I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\natural})
.
The purpose of this section is to carry out the step (i) and give the explicit formula of
I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\natural})
.
The computations of
I({\mathbf{W}}_{v}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{v}^{\natural})
have been carried out in
[
HY25
]
except at the real and
p
-adic places.
The details of the step (ii) are left to Section
8
for
v=p
and to Appendix
B
for
v=\infty
.

Let
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3})
and
\underline{k}^{\prime}=(k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime})
be sequences of integers such that
k_{1}\leq k_{1}^{\prime}\leq k_{2}
and
k_{3}\leq k_{2}^{\prime}
.
We write
\tau_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}
for the irreducible representation of
{K}_{\infty}
whose highest weight is
-\underline{k}
.
Let
\sigma
be an irreducible tempered cuspidal automorphic representation of
H({\mathbb{A}})
such that
\sigma_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series with minimal
K
-type
-\underline{k}^{\prime}
.
Let
\pi
be an irreducible tempered cuspidal automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
such that
\pi_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series or limit of holomorphic discrete series with minimal
K
-type
{L}_{\underline{k}}({\mathbb{C}})^{\vee}
.
For every non-split prime
q
we assume that
\pi_{q}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}
-invariant vector and
\sigma_{q}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}^{\prime}
-invariant vector.
Put
N=\prod_{l\neq p}l^{c(\pi_{l})}
and
N^{\prime}=\prod_{l\neq p}l^{c(\sigma_{l})}
.
We take ideals
{\mathfrak{N}}
and
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N{\mathbb{Z}}
and
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/N^{\prime}{\mathbb{Z}}
.
We define open subgroups
K_{0}^{\prime}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})
of
K^{\prime}
as in Section
5.1
.

Lemma
5.1
implies that
\sigma
is
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
-generic.
Thus
\sigma^{\vee}
is
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}
-generic.
We realize the local representation
\sigma_{v}
(resp.
\sigma_{v}^{\vee}
) in its Whittaker model
{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}}(\sigma_{v})
(resp.
{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}^{-1}}(\sigma_{v}^{\vee})
) with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}
(resp.
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}^{-1}
).
For each place
v
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
we normalize the vector
W_{\sigma_{v}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}}(\sigma_{v})
in the following way:
if
v
splits in
E
, then
W_{\sigma_{v}}
is the normalized essential vector (see Definition
8.2
);
if
v
is finite and does not split in
E
, then
W_{\sigma_{v}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}}(\sigma_{v})
is the spherical vector normalized by
W_{\sigma_{v}}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})=1
;
let
W_{\sigma_{\infty}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}}(\sigma_{\infty})
be the lowest weight vector normalizedby
W_{\sigma_{\infty}}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})=e^{-2\pi}
.

Let
\sigma\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\sigma_{v}
and
\sigma^{\vee}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\sigma_{v}^{\vee}
be the isomorphisms that are compatible with the factorization of the Whittaker functionals
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}
and
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}
respectively.
Namely, if
\varphi=\otimes_{v}W_{v}\in\sigma
is factorizable, then
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\sigma(h)\varphi)=\prod_{v}W_{v}(h_{v})
for
h=(h_{v})\in H({\mathbb{A}})
.
For
h\in H
we have

h^{\natural}=w_{2}\,^{t}\!h^{-1}w_{2}=(\det h)^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}h\begin{bmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$g^{\natural}$}}}

We will show that the factorization
\sigma^{\vee}\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\sigma_{v}^{\vee}
is compatible with
\natural
.
Observe that

\displaystyle W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\sigma(h)\varphi^{\natural})=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}}\varphi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&-z\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}h^{\natural}\right)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(z)}\,\mathrm{d}_{\mathbb{Q}}z

\displaystyle=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\sigma^{\vee}(h^{\natural})\varphi)=\prod_{v}W_{v}(h^{\natural}_{v}).

Let
\varphi_{\sigma}\in\sigma
be the newform associated to
\sigma
(see Definition
5.10
).
By uniquness of essential vectors the newform
\varphi_{\sigma}
is factorizable, and by our normalization
\varphi_{\sigma}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{\sigma_{v}}
.
If
l
is split in
E
, then
W_{l}^{\natural}(h_{l})=W_{l}(h_{l}^{\natural})
for all
W_{l}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}}(\sigma_{v})
by the definition (
7.2
) of
W_{l}^{\natural}
.
If
v
does not split in
E
, then
W_{\sigma_{v}}^{\natural}(h_{v})=W_{\sigma_{v}}(h_{v}^{\natural})
for our choice of
W_{\sigma_{v}}
.
Thus
\varphi_{\sigma}^{\natural}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{\sigma_{v}}^{\natural}
.
It follows that
\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}W_{\sigma_{v}^{\vee}}
is the
K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})
-fixed vector on which
K_{\infty}^{\prime}
acts by the character
\underline{k}^{\prime}
, normalized by
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}})=e^{-2\pi}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}=\overline{\varphi_{\sigma}}
.

Proof.

Since
\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}
and
\overline{\varphi_{\sigma}}
are proportional by multiplicity one for the unitary group
H
(see §6.2 of
[
LM15
]
) and essential vectors for generic representations of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
, Lemma
7.5
follows from the observation that
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}})=e^{-2\pi}=\overline{W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\varphi_{\sigma})}
.
∎

Taking Lemma
7.5
into account, we will write

\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}^{2}=\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi_{\sigma}\otimes\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}$}}}

For each place
v
we define the
{\mathbf{G}}({\mathbb{Q}}_{v})
-equivariant isomorphism
{}^{\natural}:\varPi_{v}^{\natural}\simeq\varPi_{v}^{\vee}
by

(W\otimes W^{\prime})^{\natural}=W^{\natural}\otimes W^{\prime\natural}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$W^{\natural}$}}}

7.6.
Algebraicity of central values

We denote the complexified Lie algebra of
G
by
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
f
be a Picard cusp form of weight
\underline{k}
associated to
\pi
defined over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
Put

\displaystyle n_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=k^{\prime}_{2}-k_{3},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{i}

\displaystyle=X^{k_{2}-k_{1}-i}Y^{i}

for
i=0,1,2,\dots,k_{2}-k_{1}
.
Let
\varphi=\varPhi_{\underline{k}}(f)_{{\mathbf{v}}_{k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{1}}}
.
In Appendix
A
we define a differential operator
\delta_{\underline{k}}
associated to an element
{Y}_{+}:=\frac{Y^{C}_{+}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\in\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
such that the restriction of
{Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi
to
H({\mathbb{A}})
has weight
\underline{k}^{\prime}
(cf. Proposition 2.6 of
[
Har21a
]
).

Assuming that
\varphi
is factorizable, we write
\varphi\otimes\varphi_{\sigma}=\otimes_{v}^{\prime}{\mathbf{W}}_{v}
.
Fix a non-zero
{K}_{\infty}
-invariant vector
W_{\infty}^{{K}_{\infty}}\in\pi_{\infty}\otimes\pi_{\infty}^{\vee}
and a non-zero
{K}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-invariant vector
W_{\infty}^{{K}_{\infty}^{\prime}}\in\sigma_{\infty}^{\vee}\otimes\sigma_{\infty}
.
Let

\displaystyle\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=W_{\infty}^{{K}_{\infty}}\otimes_{l}(W_{\pi_{l}}\otimes W_{\pi_{l}^{\vee}})\in\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee},

\displaystyle\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}\otimes\varphi_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=W_{\infty}^{{K}_{\infty}^{\prime}}\otimes_{l}(W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{l}})\in\sigma^{\vee}\otimes\sigma

be
{K}_{\infty}
and
{K}_{\infty}^{\prime}
invariant algebraic cusp forms.

For each place
v
we put

\displaystyle{\mathbf{W}}_{\varPi_{v}}

\displaystyle=W_{\pi_{v}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{v}^{\vee}}\in\varPi_{v},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{W}}_{\varPi_{v}^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=W_{\pi_{v}^{\vee}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{v}}\in\varPi_{v}^{\vee}.

Put

I_{\infty}=\frac{I(({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}){\mathbf{W}}_{\infty}\otimes(({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}){\mathbf{W}}_{\infty})^{\natural})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{\infty}\times\sigma_{\infty}^{\vee})\langle\!\langle W_{\infty}^{{K}_{\infty}}\otimes W_{\infty}^{{K}_{\infty}^{\prime}}\rangle\!\rangle_{\infty}}.

Proposition
B.3
and Remark
A.3
give

(7.6)

I_{\infty}=2^{2k_{3}-2k_{2}^{\prime}}\biggl(-\frac{2}{|\delta|}\biggl)^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}.

Since
{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\Phi)={\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\Phi^{\natural})
for
\Phi\in{\mathscr{A}}^{0}({\mathbf{G}})
, one can deduce the following formula from Lemma
7.1
and (
7.5
):

(7.7)

\frac{{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi\otimes\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}})^{2}}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}\Big)\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}}}\\
=\frac{D_{E}^{-7/2}\lambda_{K}}{2^{\varkappa_{\pi}+\varkappa_{\sigma}-3}}I_{\infty}\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{l}\frac{I({\mathbf{W}}_{l}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{l}^{\natural})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})\langle\!\langle{\mathbf{W}}_{\varPi_{l}}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{\varPi_{l}^{\vee}}\rangle\!\rangle_{l}}.

Let
\Omega_{\infty}
be a complex period of a CM elliptic curve (see §
6.7
).
Applying Proposition
6.9
with
i=k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{1}
and
n=n_{1}^{*}
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 7.6
.

The holomorphic projection of the restriction of

\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}{Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi

to
H({\mathbb{A}})
is associated to a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational form of weight
\underline{k}^{\prime}
.

The symbol
\mathrm{Hol}
stands for the holomorphic projection from nearly holomorphic functions on
{\mathfrak{H}}
.
Observe that

{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi\otimes\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}})={\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\sigma}\mathrm{Hol}\;{Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi\otimes\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}),

where
1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\sigma}
denotes the projection from the space of holomorphic cusp forms on
H
to the space of
\sigma
-isotypic modular forms.
Then
{Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi
is an algebraic nearly holomorphic Picard modular form by Theorem 14.7 of
[
Shi00
]
.
If the restriction of
{Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi
to
H({\mathbb{A}})
has level
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma}
, then Lemma
7.6
gives an algebraic number
C_{\varphi}
such that

\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\sigma}\mathrm{Hol}\;{Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi=C_{\varphi}\varphi_{\sigma}.

It follows that

\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{\infty}}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\biggl)^{2(k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})}C_{\varphi}^{2}\frac{\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}^{2}}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}\Big)}\\
=\frac{D_{E}^{-7/2}\lambda_{K}}{2^{\varkappa_{\pi}+\varkappa_{\sigma}-3}}I_{\infty}\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{l}\frac{I({\mathbf{W}}_{l}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{l}^{\natural})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})\langle\!\langle{\mathbf{W}}_{\varPi_{l}}\otimes{\mathbf{W}}_{\varPi_{l}^{\vee}}\rangle\!\rangle_{l}}.

Note that
L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})=L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})
.
Since
\frac{L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})}{\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}^{2}}
is an algebraic number by Corollary
C.2
, we get the following result:

Theorem 7.7
.

Notations and assumptions being as above, we have

\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2(k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})}\frac{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}\Big)L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}^{4}}\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}

7.7.
An application of the splitting lemma

If
q
and
pNN^{\prime}
are coprime, then

(7.8)

\frac{I((W_{\pi_{q}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{q}^{\vee}})\otimes(W_{\pi_{q}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{q}^{\vee}})^{\natural})}{\langle W_{\pi_{q}},W_{\pi_{q}^{\vee}}\rangle_{q}\langle W_{\sigma_{q}},W_{\sigma_{q}^{\vee}}\rangle_{q}^{\prime}}={\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{q}\times\sigma_{q}^{\vee})

by
[
Har14
, Theorem 2.12]
and
[
HY25
, Proposition C.1]
.
We put

\displaystyle B_{\pi_{l}}

\displaystyle=\langle W_{\pi_{l}},W_{\pi_{l}^{\vee}}\rangle_{l},

\displaystyle B_{\sigma_{l}}

\displaystyle=\langle W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}\rangle_{l}^{\prime},

(7.9)

\displaystyle{B}_{\pi_{l}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta_{l}(3)}{L^{\mathrm{GL}}(1,\pi_{l}\times\pi_{l}^{\vee})}B_{\pi_{l}},

\displaystyle{B}_{\sigma_{l}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta_{l}(2)}{L^{\mathrm{GL}}(1,\sigma_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})}B_{\sigma_{l}}

for each split prime
l
, where the local pairings
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{l}
and
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{l}^{\prime}
are constructed by (
8.3
).
It is well-known that
{B}_{\pi_{l}}={B}_{\sigma_{l}}=1
if
\pi_{l}
and
\sigma_{l}
are unramified.
To avoid possible confusion, we recall that

\displaystyle L(s,\pi_{l},\mathrm{Ad})

\displaystyle=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi_{l}\times\pi_{l}^{\vee}),

\displaystyle L(s,\sigma_{l}^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})

\displaystyle=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\sigma_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee}).

We regard
\pi_{l}
and
\sigma_{l}
as representations of unitary groups in the left hand side and representations of general linear groups in the right hand side.

Let
\varphi=\otimes_{v}W_{v}\in\pi
and
\varphi^{\prime}=\otimes_{v}W_{v}^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\vee}
.
If
W_{l}=W_{\pi_{l}}
and
W_{l}^{\prime}=W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}
unless
pNN^{\prime}
is divisible by
l
, then by (
7.7
) and (
7.8
)

(7.10)

\frac{{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi\otimes\varphi^{\prime})^{2}}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}\Big)}\\
=\frac{D_{E}^{-7/2}\lambda_{K}}{2^{\varkappa_{\pi}+\varkappa_{\sigma}-3}}I_{\infty}\frac{\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}}L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{l|pNN^{\prime}}\frac{I(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime}\otimes(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})^{\natural})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})B_{\pi_{l}}B_{\sigma_{l}}}.

Fix a split rational prime
l
.
We regard
\pi_{l}
and
\sigma_{l}
as representations of general linear groups via
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}
and
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}^{\prime}
.
Put
\zeta_{l}(s)=(1-l^{-s})^{-1}
.
Using the Whittaker models with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}
, we define the local pairings
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{l}
and
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{l}^{\prime}
by (
8.3
).
Lemma
8.5
gives

\displaystyle I(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime}\otimes(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})^{\natural})

\displaystyle=J(\pi_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l},\pi^{\vee}_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l}^{\natural},W_{l}^{\prime\natural},W_{l}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\zeta_{l}(1)Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l},W_{l}^{\prime}\biggl)Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi^{\vee}_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l}^{\natural},W_{l}^{\prime\natural}\biggl)

where

\varsigma=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&0&1\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}.

Since
\varsigma T_{\delta}=-\delta\begin{bmatrix}-\delta^{-1}w_{2}&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\varsigma
, we have

\displaystyle Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi^{\vee}_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l}^{\natural},W_{l}^{\prime\natural}\biggl)

\displaystyle=Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi^{\vee}_{l}(\varsigma T_{\delta})\widetilde{W_{l}},\sigma_{l}(w_{2})\widetilde{W_{l}^{\prime}}\biggl)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}(-\delta)}{\omega_{\pi_{l}}(-\delta)}Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi^{\vee}_{l}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W_{l}},\widetilde{W_{l}^{\prime}}\biggl)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}(-\delta)}{\omega_{\pi_{l}}(-\delta)}\gamma^{\mathrm{GL}}\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\biggl)Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l},W_{l}^{\prime}\biggl)

by (
7.2
),
and the invariance and the functional equation (
8.4
) of the JPSS integrals.
We can rewrite the identity above as

\frac{I(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime}\otimes(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})^{\natural})}{{\mathscr{L}}(\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})B_{\pi_{l}}B_{\sigma_{l}}}=\frac{{\mathscr{I}}(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta){B}_{\pi_{l}}{B}_{\sigma_{l}}},

where

{\mathscr{I}}(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})=\gamma^{\mathrm{GL}}\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\biggl)\frac{Z\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l},W_{l}^{\prime}\bigl)^{2}}{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee}\bigl)}.

We use Corollary
C.2
to rewrite (
7.10
) as

\frac{{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi\otimes\varphi^{\prime})^{2}}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}\Big)}\\
=\frac{D_{E}^{-5/2}\lambda_{K}}{2^{\varkappa_{\pi}-1+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}}I_{\infty}\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{l|pNN^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathscr{I}}(W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta){B}_{\pi_{l}}}.

Definition 7.8

(Periods on
\mathrm{U}(2,1)
)
.

Put

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}(\pi)

\displaystyle=2^{\varkappa_{\pi}}L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})\prod_{l|N_{\pi}}{B}_{\pi_{l}},

\displaystyle\Xi_{\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}}

\displaystyle=(-2)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}}\frac{\mathrm{Pet}(\pi)}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}\Big)}.

We now get the following formula from (
7.6
):

Corollary 7.9
.

Let
\pi\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\pi_{v}
be an irreducible cuspidal tempered automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
whose archimedean part
\pi_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series or limit of holomorphic discrete series and such that
\pi_{q}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}
-invariant vector for every non-split prime
q
.
Let
\sigma\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\sigma_{v}
be an irreducible cuspidal tempered automorphic representation of
H({\mathbb{A}})
whose archimedean part
\sigma_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series and such that
\sigma_{q}
admits a non-zero
K_{q}^{\prime}
-invariant for every non-split prime
q
.
Let

\displaystyle\varphi^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{v}W_{v}^{\dagger}\in\pi,

\displaystyle\varphi^{\flat}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{v}W_{v}^{\flat}\in\sigma^{\vee}.

If
W_{l}^{\dagger}=W_{\pi_{l}}
and
W_{l}^{\flat}=W_{\sigma_{l}}
unless
pNN^{\prime}
is divisible by
l
, then

\displaystyle{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi^{\dagger}\otimes\varphi^{\flat})^{2}\Xi_{\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2k_{1}^{\prime}-2k_{2}^{\prime}}(-1)^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}}}{|\delta|^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}}2D_{E}^{-5/2}\lambda_{K}L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)\prod_{l|pNN^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathscr{I}}(W_{l}^{\dagger}\otimes W_{l}^{\flat})}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta)}.

7.8.
The local integral at a ramified split prime
l

Let
l\neq p
be a rational prime that is split in
E
.
We write
\omega_{\sigma_{l}}
for the central character of
\sigma_{l}
, which is viewed as a character of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}^{\times}
via
\imath_{\mathfrak{l}}
.
Observe that

{\mathscr{I}}(\pi_{l}(\varsigma)W_{l}\otimes W_{l}^{\prime})=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\biggl)\Biggl(\frac{Z\bigl(\frac{1}{2},W_{l},W_{l}^{\prime}\bigl)}{L^{\mathrm{GL}}\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee}\bigl)}\Biggl)^{2}.

If
\sigma_{l}
is unramified, then the essential vector
W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}^{-1}}(\sigma_{l}^{\vee})
is the normalized spherical vector and so by Théoremè on p. 208 of
[
JPSS81
]
, we have

Z(s,W_{\pi_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}})=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee}).

We see that

{\mathscr{I}}(\pi_{l}(\varsigma)W_{\pi_{l}}\otimes W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}})=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\right)=:{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}.

We consider the case when
\sigma_{l}
is ramified.
Put
f_{l}=c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})
.
We define
{\mathbf{U}}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}}(\pi_{l})
by

{\mathbf{U}}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}}=\sum_{i,j\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}/l^{f_{l}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})^{\times}}\sum_{y\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}/l^{2f_{l}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}(ij)^{-1}\pi_{l}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{i}{l^{f_{l}}}&\frac{y}{l^{2f_{l}}}\\
0&1&\frac{j}{l^{f_{l}}}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)W_{\pi_{l}}.

Put
\tau_{N^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
N^{\prime}&0\end{bmatrix}
.
We consider the embedding

\displaystyle\iota^{\prime}

\displaystyle:\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{3}(F),

\displaystyle\iota^{\prime}(g)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}g&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}.

Proposition 6.8 of
[
HY25
]
tells us that

Z(s,{\mathbf{U}}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}(\tau_{N^{\prime}}^{\prime})W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}})=\frac{l^{3c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}(l)^{2c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}\varepsilon\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\bigl)}{\varepsilon\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\bigl)^{2}[\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}):{K}_{0}^{(2)}(l^{2c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})]}

if
c(\sigma_{l})\leq 2c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})
.
Put

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{l}(\iota^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}})^{-1}\varsigma)\mathrm{U}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\right)\frac{l^{6c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}(l)^{4c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}\varepsilon\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\sigma_{l}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\bigl)^{2}}{\varepsilon\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}\bigl)^{4}L^{\mathrm{GL}}\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee}\bigl)^{2}}.

It follows that

{\mathscr{I}}(\pi_{l}(\varsigma){\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}})=\frac{{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}}{[\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{l}):{K}_{0}^{(2)}(l^{2c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}{\mathbb{Z}}_{l})]^{2}}.

Put
{\mathfrak{M}}=\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Notations and assumptions being as in Corollary
7.9
, if
c(\sigma_{l})\leq 2c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})
,
W_{l}^{\flat}=W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}
and
W_{l}^{\dagger}=\pi_{l}(\varsigma){\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}}
for every
l\neq p
, then

\displaystyle|\delta|^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}(\omega_{\pi_{p}}\omega_{\sigma_{p}}^{-1})(-\delta){\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi^{\dagger}\otimes\varphi^{\flat})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{1+2k_{1}^{\prime}-2k_{2}^{\prime}}(-1)^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}}}{D_{E}^{5/2}[K^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]^{2}}\lambda_{K}\frac{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\bigl)}{\Xi_{\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}}}{\mathscr{I}}(W_{p}^{\dagger}\otimes W_{p}^{\flat})\prod_{l|NN^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta)}.

7.9.
The local integral at
p
and the modified
p
-factor

Assume that
\pi_{p}
is the irreducible generic constituent of
I(\nu_{p},\rho_{p},\mu_{p})
and that
\sigma_{p}
is the irreducible generic constituent of
I(\mu^{\prime}_{p},\nu^{\prime}_{p})
, where
\nu_{p}
,
\rho_{p}
,
\mu_{p}
,
\mu_{p}^{\prime}
,
\nu_{p}^{\prime}
are characters of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
(see §
8.4
for notations).
Put

\displaystyle\alpha_{p}

\displaystyle=\nu_{p}(p),

\displaystyle\beta_{p}

\displaystyle=\rho_{p}(p),

\displaystyle\gamma_{p}

\displaystyle=\mu_{p}(p),

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}_{p}

\displaystyle=\mu^{\prime}_{p}(p),

\displaystyle\beta^{\prime}_{p}

\displaystyle=\nu^{\prime}_{p}(p).

Assume that
\pi
is
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-ordinary and
\sigma
is
p
-ordinary with respect to
\iota_{p}
, that is,

\displaystyle\iota_{p}(p^{-k_{1}}\gamma_{p});

\displaystyle\iota_{p}\bigl(p^{-k_{2}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{p}^{\prime}\bigl),

\displaystyle\iota_{p}\bigl(p^{-k_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\beta_{p}^{\prime}\bigl)

are
p
-units, where
\mathrm{ord}_{p}\gamma_{p}\leq\min\{\mathrm{ord}_{p}\alpha_{p},\mathrm{ord}_{p}\beta_{p}\}
.
Let
W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi_{p}}\in\pi_{p}
and
W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma_{p}}\in\sigma_{p}
be the ordinary vectors defined in §
8.4
.
Define
W^{\flat}_{\sigma_{p}^{\vee}}\in\sigma_{p}^{\vee}
by
W^{\flat}_{\sigma_{p}^{\vee}}=(W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma_{p}})^{\natural}
.

Definition 7.11
.

Define the modified
p
-factor
{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})
by

\frac{1}{{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})}=L\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}\times\sigma_{p}^{\vee}\biggl)\\
\times\gamma\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}\otimes\mu_{p}^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}\biggl)\gamma\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\pi^{\vee}_{p}\otimes\nu^{\prime}_{p},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}_{p}\biggl)\gamma\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}\biggl)^{2}.

Define the
{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}
-depletion
{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1}}W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
of
W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
by

{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1}}W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}=\sum_{y\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{j}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\sum_{z\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{j}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\frac{(\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1})(y)}{p^{j}\gamma_{p}^{j}}\pi_{p}\left(\begin{bmatrix}p^{j}&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}},

and the
{\mathfrak{p}}
-depletion
W_{p}^{\dagger}=\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{p}\mu_{p}^{\prime-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1}}W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
by

W_{p}^{\dagger}=\sum_{x\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\sum_{a\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})^{\times}}\frac{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}\bigl(\frac{ax}{p^{n}}\bigl)}{p^{n}(\mu_{p}^{-1}\nu_{p}^{\prime})(a)}\pi_{p}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&\frac{x}{p^{n}}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right){\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1}}W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}},

where
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
is an arbitrarily fixed large natural number (cf. Lemma
8.10
).

Put
t_{\ell}=\imath_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime-1}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
p^{\ell}&0\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\in H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
.
If
\ell
is sufficiently large, then Theorem
8.19
below gives

\displaystyle{\mathscr{I}}(W^{\dagger}_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\flat}_{\sigma_{p}^{\vee}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{\mathrm{GL}}\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}\times\sigma_{p}^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}\bigl)}{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}\times\sigma_{p}^{\vee}\bigl)}\left(\frac{\zeta_{p}(2)}{p^{\ell/2}\zeta_{p}(1)}\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)^{\ell}\frac{\gamma\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\mu_{p}^{-1}\nu_{p}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}_{p}\bigl)}{\gamma\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi_{p}\otimes\mu_{p}^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p}\bigl)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{p}(2)^{2}}{p^{\ell}\zeta_{p}(1)^{2}}\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)^{2\ell}{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})=[K^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})]^{-2}p^{\ell}\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)^{2\ell}{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})

in view of the multiplicativity and functional equation of the gamma factor.

7.10.
An explicit central value formula

We say that an irreducible representation of
G({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
(resp.
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{q})
) is unramified if it admits a non-zero
K_{q}
(resp.
K_{q}^{\prime}
) invariant vector.
Let
\pi\simeq\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\pi_{v}
be an irreducible tempered cuspidal automorphic representation of
G({\mathbb{A}})
and
\sigma\simeq\otimes^{\prime}_{v}\sigma_{v}
that of
H({\mathbb{A}})
satisfying the following conditions:

(
H_{1}
)

\pi_{q}
and
\sigma_{q}
are unramified for every non-split rational prime
q
;

(
H_{2}
)

\sigma_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series with minimal
K
-type
-(k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime})
and
\pi_{\infty}
is a holomorphic discrete series or limit of holomorphic discrete series with minimal
K
-type
-(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3})
such that

\displaystyle k_{1}

\displaystyle\leq k_{1}^{\prime}\leq k_{2},

\displaystyle k_{3}

\displaystyle\leq k_{2}^{\prime};

(
H_{3}
)

c(\sigma_{l})\leq 2c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})
for every split rational prime
l\neq p
;

(
H_{4}
)

\pi_{p}
is a generic constituent of a principal series
I(\nu_{p},\rho_{p},\mu_{p})
;

\sigma_{p}
is a generic constituent of a principal series
I(\mu_{p}^{\prime},\nu_{p}^{\prime})
.

Recall that
N=\prod_{l\neq p}l^{c(\pi_{l})}
and
N^{\prime}=\prod_{l\neq p}l^{c(\sigma_{l})}
, where
c(\pi_{l})=c(\sigma_{l})=0
if
l
does not split in
E
.
Put
M=\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}l^{c(\omega_{\sigma_{l}})}
.
Take an ideal
{\mathfrak{M}}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
such that
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}/{\mathfrak{M}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/M{\mathbb{Z}}
.
We define
\varphi^{\dagger}=\otimes_{v}W_{v}^{\dagger}\in\pi
in the following way:
Set

\displaystyle W_{\infty}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=W_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{\sigma}},

\displaystyle W_{p}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{p}\mu_{p}^{\prime-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1}}W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}.

Let
W_{q}^{\dagger}=W_{\pi_{q}}
be a spherical or an essential vector unless
pNN^{\prime}
is divisible by
q
.
For each prime factor
l
of
NN^{\prime}
we set
W_{l}^{\dagger}=\pi_{l}(\iota(\xi^{\prime}_{l})\varsigma)\varTheta^{\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}}W_{\pi_{l}}
.

Let
\varphi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{ord}}=\otimes_{v}W_{v}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in\sigma
be such that
W_{v}^{\mathrm{ord}}=W_{\sigma_{v}}
for
v\neq p
and
W_{p}^{\mathrm{ord}}=W_{\sigma_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in\sigma_{p}
is the normalized
p
-stabilized new vector with respect to
\beta^{\prime}=\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)
(see Remark

).
Let

be such that

for

and
W_{p}^{\flat}=W_{\sigma_{p}^{\vee}}^{\flat}\in\sigma_{p}^{\vee}
.

Definition 7.12

(The
p
-modified period on
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
)
.

Put

\Omega(\sigma)=2^{\varkappa_{\sigma}}(\sqrt{-1})^{k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{2}^{\prime}}[K^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})]L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad}){E}(\sigma_{p},\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})\prod_{l|N_{\sigma}}{B}_{\sigma_{l}},

-factor

.

Remark 7.13
.

We renormalize the Petersson norm by setting

\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})}=[K^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})]\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}}.

Corollary
C.2
gives

\Omega(\sigma)=D_{E}^{-1}2^{2}(-2\sqrt{-1})^{k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})}{E}(\sigma_{p},\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})

(cf. Definition 3.12 of
[
Hsi21
]
).

Lemma 7.14
.

If
\sigma_{p}
is
p
-ordinary, then

\frac{\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{ord}}\otimes\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell}^{-1})\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}^{\flat})}{\mu_{p}^{\prime}(-1)p^{\ell/2}\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)^{\ell}}=\frac{\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\sigma})}{E}(\sigma_{p},\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})}{[K^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime})]}.

Proof.

Since
\varphi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{ord}}
is obtained by replacing
W_{\sigma_{p}}
with
W_{\sigma_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
in the definition of
\varphi_{\sigma}
and since
\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}^{\flat}
is obtained by replacing
W_{\sigma_{p}^{\vee}}
with
W_{\sigma_{p}^{\vee}}^{\flat}
in the definition of
\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}
, we have

\frac{\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{ord}}\otimes\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell}^{-1})\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}^{\flat})}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi_{\sigma}\otimes\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}})}=\frac{{B}_{\sigma_{p}^{\mathrm{ord}}}^{[\ell]}}{{B}_{\sigma_{p}}}=\frac{\mu_{p}^{\prime}(-1)p^{\ell/2}\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)^{\ell}}{[K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\sigma_{p})}):K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})]}{E}(\sigma_{p},\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{p})

by Lemma
8.8
if
\ell
is sufficiently large.
∎

We will use the following formula to prove Theorem
1.2
.

Proposition 7.15
.

Notations and assumptions being as in Corollary
7.10
, we set
W_{p}^{\dagger}=\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{p}\mu_{p}^{\prime-1}}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\mu_{p}\nu_{p}^{\prime-1}}W_{\pi_{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}
.
If
\ell
is sufficiently large, then

\displaystyle|\delta|^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}(\omega_{\pi_{p}}\omega_{\sigma_{p}}^{-1})(-\delta)\frac{{\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}(\delta^{\underline{k},\underline{k}^{\prime}}\varphi^{\dagger}\otimes\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell}^{-1})\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}^{\flat})^{2}}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K^{\prime}}(\varphi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{ord}}\otimes\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell}^{-1})\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}^{\flat})^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{5}}{D_{E}^{9/2}[K^{\prime}_{0}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}):K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]^{2}}\lambda_{K}\frac{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\bigl)}{\Xi_{\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}}\Omega(\sigma)^{2}}{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})\prod_{l|NN^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta)}.

Proof.

Corollary
7.10
and (

) give

\displaystyle|\delta|^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}(\omega_{\pi_{p}}\omega_{\sigma_{p}}^{-1})(-\delta){\mathscr{P}}_{K^{\prime}}({Y}_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\varphi^{\dagger}\otimes\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell}^{-1})\varphi_{\sigma^{\vee}}^{\flat})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{1+2k_{1}^{\prime}-2k_{2}^{\prime}}(-1)^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}}}{D_{E}^{5/2}[K^{\prime}:K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}{\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]^{2}}\lambda_{K}\frac{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\bigl)}{\Xi_{\varPhi_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}^{{K}_{\infty}}}}p^{\ell}\nu_{p}^{\prime}(p)^{2\ell}{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})\prod_{l|NN^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta)}.

By multiplying

we see that the left hand side of the stated identity is equal to

\displaystyle\times{E}(\pi_{p},\sigma_{p}^{\vee})\prod_{l|NN^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}}{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}\omega_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1})(-\delta)}

The Petersson norm on
\mathrm{U}(2,1)

We shall translate the periods in Definition

into more classical terminology.

Given

.
We write

\displaystyle=\rho_{\kappa}(J)P(X_{2}^{\prime},Y_{2}^{\prime})=P(-Y_{2}^{\prime},X_{2}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\

Definition 7.19
.

be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of

with its

-power multiple.

corresponds to the

-valued distribution

\phi\mapsto\int_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}^{\times}}\phi(x)x^{-k_{3}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\widetilde{\mathrm{FJ}}^{m}_{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{0}{\mathbf{U}}^{\chi^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}}f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}))

).

Let

.
In the first step we assume (

) and define the theta element
\Theta_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}
attached to
f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}
and
\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}
by

\Theta_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}=\mathrm{FC}^{1}_{{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}}(1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}{J}^{\chi^{\prime}}(f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}})).

We normalize this theta element by

{\mathscr{L}}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}=\sqrt{\frac{D_{E}^{9/2}}{2^{5}\lambda_{K}}}[-\delta]_{\mathbf{I}}^{1/2}\frac{[K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}):K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]}{\sqrt{(-\delta)^{k_{2}+k_{3}}(\omega_{\pi}^{-1}\omega_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})(-\delta){\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}}\Theta_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}},

where

\sqrt{(\omega_{\pi}^{-1}\omega_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}})(-\delta){\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\vee}}}=\prod_{l|NN^{\prime}}\sqrt{(\omega_{\pi_{l}}^{-1}\omega_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}})(-\delta){\mathfrak{f}}_{\pi_{l},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}^{\vee}}}.

The same computation as in §
7.12
shows that for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbf{I}}
with
k_{Q}\geq k_{3}

\frac{Q({\mathscr{L}}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}})^{2}}{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}}=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}\frac{\Gamma({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime},0)L({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime},0)}{\Xi_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}}\Omega(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q})^{2}}{E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime}){E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime}).

Next we remove the assumption (
H_{3}
).
Let
M
be a prime-to-
p

odd
integer divisibly only by prime factors of
N^{\prime}
.
Take an ideal
{\mathfrak{M}}
of
{\mathfrak{r}}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}/{\mathfrak{M}}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}/M{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Fix a primitive character
\varrho
of
({\mathfrak{r}}/{\mathfrak{M}})^{\times}
such that
\chi^{\prime}\varrho^{-2}
has conductor
M
.
We extend
\varrho
to an automorphic character
\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}=\prod_{v}\varrho_{v}
of
\mathrm{U}(1)({\mathbb{A}})
.

Since
\boldsymbol{g}_{Q}^{\prime}
has tame level
{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}
for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{\mathbf{I}}
, there exists a positive integer
A
such that if
m\geq A
and
M
is divisible by
N^{\prime m}
, then
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q}\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}
has tame conductor
M^{2}
and
(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q,l}\otimes\varrho_{l})_{u}
is trivial for all
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{\mathbf{I}}
and prime factors
l
of
N^{\prime}
and
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{\mathbf{I}}
by (
8.2
) and stability of the epsilon factor (cf.
[
JS85
]
).
We enlarge
M
so that
(\pi_{l}\otimes\varrho_{l})_{u}
is trivial for prime factors
l
of
N^{\prime}
.
Then
B_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q,l}\otimes\varrho_{l}}=B_{\pi_{l}\otimes\varrho_{l}}=1
for all prime factors
l
of
N^{\prime}
by Proposition 6.4 of
[
HY25
]
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\Omega(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q}\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})

\displaystyle=[K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}):K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]\Omega^{(N^{\prime})}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q})\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}\zeta_{l}(2),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pet}(\pi\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})

\displaystyle=2^{\varkappa_{\pi}}L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})\prod_{q|pN,q\nmid N^{\prime}}{B}_{\pi_{q}}\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}\frac{\zeta_{l}(3)}{L^{\mathrm{GL}}(1,\pi_{l}\times\pi_{l}^{\vee})}.

Since

\frac{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi^{{K}_{\infty}}_{(\pi\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})\otimes(\pi\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})^{\vee}}\Big)}{\mathrm{Pet}_{K}\Big(\varPhi^{{K}_{\infty}}_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}\Big)}=\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}\frac{B_{\pi_{l}\otimes\varrho_{l}}}{B_{\pi_{l}}}=\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}\frac{1}{B_{\pi_{l}}},

we have
\Xi_{\varPhi^{{K}_{\infty}}_{(\pi\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})\otimes(\pi\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})^{\vee}}}=\Xi_{\varPhi^{{K}_{\infty}}_{\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}}}
.

Fix
Q_{0}\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbf{I}}
with
k_{Q_{0}}\geq k_{3}
.
Let

\displaystyle f_{\pi\otimes\varrho}^{\mathrm{ord}}

\displaystyle\in\boldsymbol{e}S^{G}_{\underline{k}}(p{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi}{\mathfrak{M}}^{3},\chi\varrho^{-3},{O}),

\displaystyle g_{\varrho}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\in\boldsymbol{e}^{\prime}S^{H}_{(k_{1};k_{Q_{0}})}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{M}}^{2},\chi^{\prime}\varrho^{-2},{O})

be
p
-stabilized newforms associated to
\pi\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q_{0}}\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}}
.
We extend
g_{\varrho}^{\prime}
to a cusp form on
{H}
by (
5.3
) and lift it to a Hida family
\boldsymbol{g}_{\varrho}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{S}}^{H}_{\mathrm{ord}}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2},\chi^{\prime}\varrho^{-2},{\mathbf{I}})
by Theorem
4.4
.
For our choice of
\varrho
we see that
\boldsymbol{g}_{\varrho}^{\prime}
satisfies (
H_{3}
).
We have seen that

\displaystyle\frac{Q({\mathscr{L}}_{f_{\pi\otimes\varrho}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}_{\varrho}^{\prime}})^{2}}{\Omega_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}\frac{\Gamma(0,{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})L(0,{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})}{\Xi_{f_{\pi\otimes\varrho}^{\mathrm{ord}}}\Omega(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q}\otimes\varrho_{\mathbb{A}})^{2}}{E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime}){E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggl)^{2m_{Q}^{\prime}}\frac{\Gamma(0,{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})L(0,{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime}){E}_{\infty}({\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime}){E}_{p}(\mathrm{Fil}^{+}{\mathbf{V}}_{Q}^{\prime})}{\Xi_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}}\Omega^{(N^{\prime})}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{Q})^{2}[K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}):K_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]^{2}\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}\zeta_{l}(2)^{2}}

for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbf{I}}
with
k_{Q}\geq k_{3}
.
Therefore

{L}_{f_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}^{\prime}}^{2}=[{K}_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime}):{K}_{0}^{\prime}({\mathfrak{M}}^{2})]{\mathscr{L}}_{f_{\pi\otimes\varrho}^{\mathrm{ord}},\boldsymbol{g}_{\varrho}^{\prime}}\prod_{l|N^{\prime}}\zeta_{l}(2)\in\mathrm{Frac}{\mathbf{I}}_{2}

satisfies the interpolation formula in Theorem
1.4
for
Q\in{\mathfrak{X}}_{\mathbf{I}}
with
k_{Q}\geq k_{3}
.

8.
The non-archimedean computation

8.1.
The Gauss sum

Let
F
be a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
which contains the integer ring
{\mathfrak{o}}
having a single prime ideal
{\mathfrak{p}}
.
We denote the order of the residue field
{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}
by
q
.
The absolute value
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{F}=|\cdot|
on
F
is normalized via
|\varpi|=q^{-1}
for any generator
\varpi
of
{\mathfrak{p}}
, where
q
denotes the order of the residue field
{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}
.
We denote by
B_{n}
the group of upper triangular matrices in
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
, and by
N_{n}
its unipotent radical.
Let
\mathrm{d}g
be the Haar measure on
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
giving
\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}})
volume
1
.

We choose a non-trivial additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
of
F
so that the maximal fractional ideal on which it is trivial is
{\mathfrak{o}}
.
We write
{S}(F)
for the space of locally constant compactly supported functions on
F
.
The Fourier transform of
\phi\in{S}(F)
is defined by

\widehat{\phi}(y)=\int_{F}\phi(x){\boldsymbol{\psi}}(-xy)\,\mathrm{d}x.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\widehat{\phi}$}}}

The measure
\mathrm{d}x
is chosen so that
\widehat{\widehat{\phi}}(x)=\phi(-x)
.
Put

\displaystyle{\mathscr{N}}_{n}

\displaystyle=N_{n}\cap\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}}),

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{bmatrix}.

Let
\chi
be a character of
F^{\times}
.
When
\chi
is ramified, the conductor
c(\chi)
of
\chi
is defined as the smallest positive integer
n
such that
\chi
is trivial on
1+{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}
.
When
\chi
is unramified, we set
c(\chi)=0
.

If
c(\chi)\geq 1
, then the Gauss sum is defined by

{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})=\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\chi)})^{\times}}\chi(a)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\biggl(-\frac{a}{\varpi^{c(\chi)}}\biggl).{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})$}}}

When
c(\chi)=0
, we formally set
{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})=1
.

Remark 8.1
.

The Gauss sum is related to the root number in the following way:

\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{2},\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}\right)=q^{-c(\chi)/2}\chi(\varpi)^{c(\chi)}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}).

8.2.
Essential vectors

Let
\pi
be an irreducible admissible generic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{m+1}(F)
.
We write
{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
for the Whittaker model of
\pi
with respect to the additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
of
F
.
Given an open compact subgroup
\Gamma
of
\mathrm{GL}_{m+1}(F)
and its character
{X}:\Gamma\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, we put

{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi,\Gamma,{X})=\{W\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)\;|\;\pi(\gamma)W={X}(\gamma)W\text{ for }\gamma\in\Gamma\}.

Assume that
n\geq 1
.
For a positive integer
\ell
the subgroup
{K}_{0}^{(m+1)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})
consists of matrices of the form

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}A&B\\
C&d\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle(A

\displaystyle\in\mathrm{GL}_{m}({\mathfrak{o}}),\;B\in{\mathfrak{o}}^{m},\;\,^{t}\!C\in({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})^{m},\;d\in{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}).

When
\ell=0
, we set
{K}_{0}^{(m+1)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})=\mathrm{GL}_{m+1}({\mathfrak{o}})
.
Given a character
\omega
of
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}
, we define the characters
\omega^{\downarrow}:{K}_{0}^{(m+1)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
and
\omega^{\uparrow}:{K}_{0}^{(2)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})\to{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
by

(8.1)

\displaystyle\omega^{\downarrow}\left(\begin{bmatrix}A&B\\
C&d\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\omega(d),
