\displaystyle\omega^{\uparrow}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\omega(a).

We write
\omega_{\pi}
for the central character of
\pi
.
Let
c(\pi)
denote the exponent of the conductor of
\pi
, i.e., the epsilon factor of
\pi
is of the form

(8.2)

\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}\left(s+\frac{1}{2},\pi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\right)=q^{-c(\pi)s}\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\right).

Théoremè on p. 211 of
[
JPSS81
]
says that

\dim{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}\bigl(\pi,{K}_{0}^{(m+1)}\bigl({\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\pi)}\bigl),\omega^{\downarrow}_{\pi}\bigl)=1.

Theorem 3.1 of
[
Mat13
]
enables us to normalize a basis vector of this one-dimensional space in the following way:

Definition 8.2

(essential vectors)
.

There exists a unique vector
W_{\pi}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}\bigl(\pi,{K}_{0}^{(m+1)}\bigl({\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\pi)}\bigl),\omega^{\downarrow}_{\pi}\bigl)
which satisfies
W_{\pi}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{m+1})=1
.
This vector
W_{\pi}
is called a normalized essential Whittaker vector of
\pi
with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
.

Given
W\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
, we define
\widetilde{W}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\pi^{\vee})
by
\widetilde{W}(g)=W(w_{n}\,^{t}\!g^{-1})
.
Put
\xi_{m,\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}\varpi^{-\ell}\hbox{\bf 1}_{m}&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}
.
Then
\pi^{\vee}(\xi_{m,c(\pi)})\widetilde{W}_{\pi}
is an essential vector of
\pi^{\vee}
.
The following result is Proposition 6.2 of
[
HY25
]
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible admissible generic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{m+1}(F)
.
Let
W_{\pi^{\vee}}
be the essential vector of
\pi^{\vee}
with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}
.
Then
\pi^{\vee}(\xi_{m,c(\pi)})\widetilde{W}_{\pi}=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\bigl)^{m}W_{\pi^{\vee}}
.

8.3.
The JPSS integrals

Let
\pi
be an irreducible admissible generic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
.
One can define an invariant perfect pairing

\langle\;,\;\rangle:{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)\otimes{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\pi^{\vee})\to{\mathbb{C}}

by

(8.3)

\langle W_{1},W_{2}\rangle=\int_{N_{n-1}\backslash\mathrm{GL}_{n-1}(F)}W_{1}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}h&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)W_{2}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}h&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\,\mathrm{d}h.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\langle W_{1},W_{2}\rangle$}}}

Let
m
be a positive integer which is less than
n
.
Put
l=n-m-1
.
Let
\sigma
be an irreducible admissible generic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(F)
whose central character is
\omega_{\sigma}
.
We associate to Whittaker functions
W\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
and
W^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\sigma^{\vee})
the local zeta integrals

\displaystyle Z(s,W,W^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{N_{m}\backslash G_{m}}W\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}h&\\
&\hbox{\bf 1}_{l+1}\end{bmatrix}\biggl)W^{\prime}(h)|\det h|^{s-\frac{l+1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}h,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}(s,\widetilde{W},\widetilde{W^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int_{N_{m}\backslash G_{m}}\int_{\mathrm{M}_{l,m}(F)}\widetilde{W}\left(\begin{bmatrix}h&&\\
x&\hbox{\bf 1}_{l}&\\
&&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\widetilde{W^{\prime}}(h)|\det h|^{s-\frac{l+1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}h,

which converge absolutely for
\Re s\gg 0
.

We write
L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})
,
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
and
\gamma^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
for the
L
, epsilon and gamma factors associated to
\pi
and
\sigma^{\vee}
.
These local factors are studied extensively in
[
JPSS83
]
.
The gamma factor is defined as the proportionality constant of the functional equation

(8.4)

Z(1-s,\pi^{\vee}(w_{n,m})\widetilde{W},\widetilde{W^{\prime}})=\omega_{\sigma}(-1)^{n-1}\gamma^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\widetilde{Z}(s,W,W^{\prime}),

where

w_{n,m}=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{r}&\\
&w_{n-m}\end{bmatrix}.

Remark 8.4
.

When we view
\pi
and
\sigma
are representations of unitary groups over the split quadratic algebra
F\oplus F
,

L(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi^{\vee}\times\sigma).

When
m=1
and
\chi
is a character of
F^{\times}
, we will write

\displaystyle L(s,\pi\otimes\chi)

\displaystyle=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\chi),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(s,\pi\otimes\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}),

\displaystyle\gamma(s,\pi\otimes\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})

\displaystyle=\gamma^{\mathrm{GL}}(s,\pi\times\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}).

Let
\pi
be an irreducible admissible tempered representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
and
\sigma
that of
\mathrm{GL}_{n-1}(F)
.
We consider the integral

J(W_{1},W_{2},W_{1}^{\prime},W_{2}^{\prime})=\int_{\mathrm{GL}_{n-1}(F)}\biggl\langle\pi\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}h&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)W_{1},W_{2}\biggl\rangle\langle W_{1}^{\prime},\sigma^{\vee}(h)W_{2}^{\prime}\rangle^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}h,

which is convergent for

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi),

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\pi^{\vee}),

\displaystyle W_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\sigma),

\displaystyle W_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(\sigma^{\vee}).

The following result is called a splitting lemma and was proved by Wei Zhang in Proposition 4.10 of
[
Zha14
]
by using the work of Lapid and Mao
[
LM14
]
.
It is worth noting that Proposition 4.10 of
[
Zha14
]
uses unnormalized local Haar measures (cf. §2.1 of
[
Zha14
]
) while we here use normalized ones.

Lemma 8.5
.

Notation being as above, we have

J(W_{1},W_{2},W_{1}^{\prime},W_{2}^{\prime})=Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},W_{1},W_{2}^{\prime}\biggl)Z\biggl(\frac{1}{2},W_{2},W_{1}^{\prime}\biggl)\prod_{i=1}^{m-2}\zeta_{F}(i).

8.4.
Ordinary vectors of representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)

For a subset
S
we write
{\mathbb{I}}_{S}
for the characteristic function of
S
.
For a compact subgroup
\Gamma
of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
and a representation
(\pi,V)
of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
we let

V^{\Gamma}=\{v\in V\;|\;\pi(\gamma)v=v\text{ for }\gamma\in\Gamma\}

be the space of
\Gamma
-invariant vectors in
V
.
Let
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n}
be quasi-characters of
F^{\times}
.
The space
V
of
\pi=I(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
consists of functions
h:\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)\to{\mathbb{C}}
which satisfy

\displaystyle h(tug)

\displaystyle=h(g)\wp_{n}(t)^{1/2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mu_{i}(t_{i}),

\displaystyle\wp_{n}(t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}|t_{i}|^{n+1-2i}

for
t=\mathrm{diag}[t_{1},t_{2},\dots,t_{n}]\in T_{n}
,
u\in N_{n}
and
g\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
.

We define a function
h^{\mathrm{ord}}
on
B_{n}w_{n}N_{n}
by

h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}(tuw_{n}v)={\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathscr{N}}_{n}}(v)\wp_{n}(t)^{1/2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mu_{i}(t_{i}){\@index{\@indexphrase{$h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}$}}}

for
t=\mathrm{diag}[t_{1},t_{2},\dots,t_{n}]\in T_{n}
and
u,v\in N_{n}
, where
{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathscr{N}}_{n}}
denotes the characteristic function of
{\mathscr{N}}_{n}=N_{n}\cap\mathrm{GL}_{n}({\mathfrak{o}})
.
Since
B_{n}w_{n}N_{n}
is the cell of the longest Weyl element
w_{n}
in the Bruhat decomposition of
\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
, we can extend
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
by zero to an element of
V

(cf.
[
Car79
, (B), p. 138]
).
We call
h_{\pi}^{\mathrm{ord}}\in V^{{\mathscr{N}}_{n}}
the normalized ordinary vector with respect to
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
.

Put
\alpha_{i}=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{n-i}&\\
&\varpi\hbox{\bf 1}_{i}\end{bmatrix}\in T_{n}
for
i=1,2,\dots,n
.
Define the operator
U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{i})
on
V^{{\mathscr{N}}_{n}}
by

U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{i})h=\sum_{u\in{\mathscr{N}}_{n}/\alpha_{i}^{-1}{\mathscr{N}}_{n}\alpha_{i}}\pi(u\alpha_{i}^{-1})h.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{1})$}}}

Put
\beta_{j}=\frac{\alpha_{n-j}}{\varpi}
for
1\leq j\leq n-1
.
We similarly define the operator
U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{j})
.

Proposition 8.6
.

\displaystyle U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{i})h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{\frac{i(n-i)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=1}^{i}\mu_{l}(\varpi)}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi},

\displaystyle U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{j})h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{\frac{j(n-j)}{2}}}{\prod_{l=n-j+1}^{n}\mu_{l}(\varpi)^{-1}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}.

Proof.

Let
g\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(F)
be such that
[U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{i})h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}](g)\neq 0
.
There exists
u\in{\mathscr{N}}_{n}
such that
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}(gu\alpha_{i}^{-1})\neq 0
.
We have
gu\alpha_{i}^{-1}\in B_{n}w_{n}{\mathscr{N}}_{n}
, and hence
g\in B_{n}w_{n}{\mathscr{N}}_{n}
.
By the characterization of
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
we see that
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
is an eigenvector of
U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{i})
with eigenvalue

[U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{i})h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}](w_{n})=\sum_{u\in{\mathscr{N}}_{n}/\alpha_{i}^{-1}{\mathscr{N}}_{n}\alpha_{i}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}(w_{n}u\alpha_{i}^{-1})=h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}(w_{n}\alpha_{i}^{-1}),

from which we obtain the first formula.
Since
U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\beta_{j})=U_{\mathfrak{p}}(\alpha_{n-j})\omega_{\pi}(\varpi)
, where
\omega_{\pi}=\prod_{l=1}^{n}\mu_{l}
is the central character of
\pi
, the second formula follows from the first.
∎

Definition 8.7
.

Let
\sigma
be the irreducible generic quotient of
I(\mu,\nu)
.

\frac{1}{{E}(\sigma,\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})}=L^{\mathrm{GL}}(1,\sigma_{u}\times\sigma_{u}^{\vee})\gamma(1,\mu^{-1}\nu,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\times\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\zeta_{F}(1)^{2}}&\text{if $c(\sigma)=0$, }\\
\frac{q^{c(\sigma)}}{\zeta_{F}(1)}&\text{if $c(\sigma)>0$ }\end{cases}

(cf. Definition 6.5 of
[
HY25
]
).
Here we write
\sigma_{u}
for the unramified component of the first non-zero spherical Bernstein-Zelevinsky derivative of
\pi
(see Definition 1.3 of
[
Mat13
]
for the precise definition).

Define the additive character of
N_{n}
by

{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(u)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}(u_{1,2}+u_{2,3}+\cdots+u_{n-1,n})

for
u=(u_{i,j})\in N_{n}
.
We redefine
\pi
as the irreducible generic constituent of
I(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
.
For
h\in I(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
we define
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h)\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
by

W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(g,h)=\int_{N_{n}}h\left(w_{n}ug\right)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(u)}\,\mathrm{d}u.

Put
W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma}=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma})
.
For positive integers
\ell
we define
t_{\ell}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)
by

(8.5)

t_{\ell}=\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
\varpi^{\ell}&0\end{bmatrix}.

By analogy with the formulas in (
7.9
), we set

{B}_{\sigma^{\mathrm{ord}}}^{[\ell]}=\frac{\zeta_{F}(2)}{L^{\mathrm{GL}}(1,\sigma\times\sigma^{\vee})}\langle W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma},\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\mathrm{ord}\natural}_{\sigma}\rangle.

The following result is Corollary 6.6 of
[
HY25
]
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
\sigma
be the irreducible generic quotient of
I(\mu,\nu)
.
We denote the exponent of the conductor of
\sigma
by
c(\sigma)
.
If
\ell
is sufficiently large, then

\frac{{B}_{\sigma^{\mathrm{ord}}}^{[\ell]}}{{B}_{\sigma}}=\frac{q^{\ell/2}\nu(\varpi)^{\ell}}{[{K}_{0}^{(2)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\sigma)}):{I}_{0}^{(2)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})]}{E}(\sigma,\mathrm{Ad},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\mu(-1).

8.5.
Depleted vectors of representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}(F)

Let
\mu,\nu,\rho
be characters of
F^{\times}
.
The space
V
of
\pi=I(\nu,\rho,\mu)
consists of functions
h:\mathrm{GL}_{3}(F)\to{\mathbb{C}}
which satisfy

h\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&y&z\\
0&b&x\\
0&0&c\end{bmatrix}g\right)=\nu(a)\rho(b)\mu(c)|ac^{-1}|h(g)

for
a,b,c\in F^{\times}
,
x,y,z\in F
and
g\in\mathrm{GL}_{3}(F)
.

Let
{P}_{1,2}=\{(g_{ij})\in\mathrm{GL}_{3}(F)\;|\;g_{21}=g_{31}=0\}
be a maximal parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}(F)
.
Given
h^{\prime\prime}\in I(\rho,\mu)
and
\phi_{1},\phi_{3}\in{S}(F)
, we define
h_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}\in I(\nu,\rho,\mu)
by

h_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&\\
&g\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0&z\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)=\frac{|a|\nu(a)}{|\det g|^{1/2}}h^{\prime\prime}(g)\phi_{1}(x)\phi_{3}(z)

for
g\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)
,
a\in F^{\times}
,
x,z\in F
, and by setting
h_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}(g)=0
unless
g\in{P}_{1,2}w_{3}N_{3}
.
Given
\phi_{2}\in{S}(F)
, we define
h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu}\in I(\rho,\mu)
by

h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&x\\
0&d\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&z\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)=|ad^{-1}|^{1/2}\rho(a)\mu(d)\phi_{2}(z)

for
d\in F^{\times}
and by setting
h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu}(g)=0
unless
g\in B_{2}w_{2}N_{2}
.
Put

h_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}=h_{\phi_{1},h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}.

Bear in mind that the ordinary vector
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}\in I(\nu,\rho,\mu)
with respect to
(\nu,\rho,\mu)
is given by

h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}=h_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}.

Remark 8.9
.

Given
\phi\in{S}(F)
and
t\in F^{\times}
, we define
\phi^{t}\in{S}(F)
by
\phi^{t}(x)=\phi(tx)
for
x\in F
.
One can show that for
a,b,c\in F^{\times}

\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&0&0\\
0&b&0\\
0&0&c\end{bmatrix}\right)h_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}=\nu(c)\rho(b)\mu(a)|ac^{-1}|h_{\phi_{1}^{c/b},\phi_{2}^{b/a},\phi_{3}^{c/a}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}

(cf. Remark 2.3 of
[
HY24b
]
).
In particular,
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
and
h_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}^{\rho,\mu}
are eigenvectors of
U_{p}
-operators (cf. Proposition 5.4 of
[
HY25
]
).
The vector
h_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}^{\rho,\mu}
is called the normalized
p
-stabilized new vector with respect to
\mu(\varpi)
.

8.6.
p
-depletion

Define subgroups of
{\mathscr{N}}_{3}
by

\displaystyle{\mathscr{N}}_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left.\begin{bmatrix}1&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right|\;y,z\in{\mathfrak{o}}\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathscr{N}}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\left\{\left.\begin{bmatrix}1&0&z\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right|\;x,z\in{\mathfrak{o}}\right\}.

Fix a prime element
\varpi
of
{\mathfrak{o}}
.
The space
{C}({\mathfrak{o}}^{\times},{\mathbb{C}})
consists of
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued locally constant functions on
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}
.
Given
\breve{\phi}\in{C}({\mathfrak{o}}^{\times},{\mathbb{C}})
, we define
{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}\in V^{{\mathscr{N}}_{2,1}}
by

{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}=\frac{1}{q^{j}\mu(\varpi)^{j}}\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j})^{\times}}\sum_{z\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j}}\breve{\phi}(y)\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}\varpi^{j}&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi},{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}$}}}

where
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
is such that
\breve{\phi}
factors through the quotient
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}\to({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j})^{\times}
.

Lemma 8.10
.

The definition of
{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\breve{\phi}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
is independent of the choice of
j
.

Proof.

If
\breve{\phi}\in{C}({\mathfrak{o}}^{\times},{\mathbb{C}})
factors through
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}\to({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j})^{\times}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j+1})^{\times}}\sum_{z\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j+1}}\breve{\phi}(y)\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}\varpi^{j+1}&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j})^{\times}}\sum_{z\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{j}}\breve{\phi}(y)\sum_{b,c\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}}\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}\varpi^{j}&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\varpi&b&c\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}.

The inner sum is
q\mu(\varpi)\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}\varpi^{j}&y&z\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
(cf. Remark
8.9
).
∎

Let
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
be characters of
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}
.
We define an operator on
V^{{\mathscr{N}}_{1,2}}
by

\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h=\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}})^{\times}}\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{y}{\varpi}&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h

if
c(\chi^{\prime})=0
, and

\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h={\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})^{-1}\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\chi^{\prime})})^{\times}}\chi^{\prime}(y)\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{y}{\varpi^{c(\chi^{\prime})}}&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h

otherwise.
For positive integers
\ell
we define

{W}_{\ell}h=q^{-\ell}\sum_{z\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}}\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&\frac{z}{\varpi^{\ell}}\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h.

Put
n^{\prime}=\{1,c(\chi^{\prime})\}
and

\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}=\begin{bmatrix}\varpi&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}.

Then we can write
{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
as

{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}=\mu(\varpi)^{-n^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\pi(\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{n^{\prime}}){W}_{n^{\prime}}\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}.

Given
\phi\in{C}({\mathfrak{o}}^{\times},{\mathbb{C}})
, we define
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}:V^{{\mathscr{N}}_{2,1}}\to V
by

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}h=q^{-n}\sum_{x\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}}\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{n})^{\times}}\phi(a){\boldsymbol{\psi}}\left(\frac{ax}{\varpi^{n}}\right)\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&\frac{x}{\varpi^{n}}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h,{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}$}}}

provided that
\phi
factors through the quotient
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}\to({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{n})^{\times}
.
Since

\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{n+1})^{\times}}\phi(a){\boldsymbol{\psi}}\left(\frac{ax}{\varpi^{n+1}}\right)=q{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(x)\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{n})^{\times}}\phi(a){\boldsymbol{\psi}}\left(\frac{ax}{\varpi^{n+1}}\right),

the definition of
\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\phi}
is independent of the choice of
n
.
If
c(\chi)\geq 1
, then we define an operator on
\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}:V^{{\mathscr{N}}_{2,1}}\to V
by

\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}h={\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})^{-1}\sum_{x\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\chi)})^{\times}}\chi(x)^{-1}\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&\frac{x}{\varpi^{c(\chi)}}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h,

and if
\chi
is trivial, then

\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}h=h-q^{-1}\sum_{x\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}}\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1&\frac{x}{\varpi}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h.

Lemma 8.11
.

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}=\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}
.

Proof.

If
c(\chi)\geq 1
, then the relation follows from the equalities

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}){\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})

\displaystyle=q^{c(\chi)}\chi(-1),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-y})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}(y)\chi(-y)^{-1}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})

for
y\in{\mathfrak{o}}
.
One can easily prove the case when
c(\chi)=0
.
∎

Definition 8.12

(
p
-depletion)
.

Put

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle=\max\{1,c(\chi)\},

\displaystyle n^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\max\{1,c(\chi^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=n+n^{\prime}.

For
\ell\geq c
we define the
p
-depletion of
h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}
by

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{p,\ell}^{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

\displaystyle={W}_{\ell-n^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}

\displaystyle=\mu(\varpi)^{-n^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\cdot\pi(\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}})^{n^{\prime}}{W}_{\ell}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}.

8.7.
A description of the
p
-depleted vector

Define
\varphi_{\chi},\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}\in{S}(F)
by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\chi}(x)

\displaystyle=\chi(x){\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}(x),

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\varphi_{\chi^{\prime-1}}&\text{if $c(\chi^{\prime})\geq 1$, }\\
q{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{p}}-{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}&\text{if $c(\chi^{\prime})=0$ }\end{cases}

for characters
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
of
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}
.

Proposition 8.13
.

If
\ell\geq n+n^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{p,\ell}^{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

\displaystyle=\mu(\varpi)^{-n^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\cdot\pi(\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{n^{\prime}})h^{\ddagger},

\displaystyle h^{\ddagger}

\displaystyle=q^{-\ell}h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},\widehat{\varphi_{\chi^{\prime}}^{\prime}},{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{$h^{\ddagger}$}}}

Define subspaces
{S}({\mathfrak{o}})
and
{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})
of
{S}(F)
by

\displaystyle{S}({\mathfrak{o}})

\displaystyle=\{\phi\in{S}(F)\;|\;\phi(x)=0\text{ for }x\notin{\mathfrak{o}}\},

\displaystyle{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})

\displaystyle=\{\phi\in{S}(F)\;|\;\phi(x+c)=0\text{ for }x\in F\text{ and }c\in{\mathfrak{o}}\}.

The operator
\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}:{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})\to{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})
is defined by

\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi(x)=\phi(x)-q^{-1}\sum_{a\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}}\phi(x+a\varpi^{-1})

if
c(\chi)=0
, and by

\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi(x)={\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})^{-1}\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\chi)})^{\times}}\chi(a)^{-1}\phi(x+a\varpi^{-c(\chi)})

if
c(\chi)\geq 1
.
We define the operator
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}:{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})\to{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})
by

\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}\phi(x)=\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}})^{\times}}\phi(x+a\varpi^{-1})

if
c(\chi^{\prime})=0
, and by
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}=\theta^{\chi^{\prime-1}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
if
c(\chi^{\prime})\geq 1
.
We shall prove the following lemma to prove Proposition
8.13
.

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
\chi,\chi^{\prime}
be characters of
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}
.
Let
\phi\in{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})
.
Then for
y\in F

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi}(y)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\chi}(-y)\cdot\widehat{\phi}(y),

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}\phi}(y)

\displaystyle=\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}(-y)\cdot\widehat{\phi}(y).

Proof.

Note that
\phi\in{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})\Leftrightarrow\widehat{\phi}\in{S}({\mathfrak{o}})
.
Observe that if
c(\chi)=0
, then

\widehat{\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi}(y)=\widehat{\phi}(y)-q^{-1}\sum_{a\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(ay\varpi^{-1})\widehat{\phi}(y)={\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}(y)\widehat{\phi}(y),

while if
c(\chi)\geq 1
, then

\widehat{\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi}(y)={\mathfrak{g}}(\chi,{\boldsymbol{\psi}})^{-1}\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\chi)})^{\times}}\chi(a)^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(ay\varpi^{-c(\chi)})\widehat{\phi}(y)=\chi(-y){\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}(y)\widehat{\phi}(y).

Thus we obtain the first identity.
If
c(\chi^{\prime})=0
, then

\widehat{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}\phi}(y)=\sum_{a\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}})^{\times}}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(ay\varpi^{-1})\widehat{\phi}(y)=\varphi_{\chi^{\prime}}^{\prime}(-y)\widehat{\phi}(y),

which proves the second identity.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
\phi_{1}\in{S}(F/{\mathfrak{o}})
,
\phi_{3}\in{S}(F)
and
h^{\prime\prime}\in I(\rho,\mu)
.

(1)

Take
m
so that
\phi_{1}(x)=0
unless
x\in{\mathfrak{p}}^{-m}
.
If
\ell\geq n^{\prime}+m
, then

{W}_{\ell}\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}=q^{-\ell}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\prime\prime},{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}},

where
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\prime\prime}(g)=\displaystyle\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}})^{\times}}h^{\prime\prime}\left(g\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{y}{\varpi}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)
if
c(\chi^{\prime})=0
, and

\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h={\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})^{-1}\sum_{y\in({\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{c(\chi^{\prime})})^{\times}}\chi^{\prime}(y)h^{\prime\prime}\left(g\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{y}{\varpi^{c(\chi^{\prime})}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)

otherwise.

(2)

\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}=h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}
.

Remark 8.16
.

If
c(\chi^{\prime})\geq 1
, then
h^{\prime\prime}\mapsto\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\prime\prime}
coincides with the twisting operator associated to
\chi^{\prime-1}
(cf. (2.12) of
[
Hsi21
]
).
Clearly,
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu}=h_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show the equality as a function on the big cell because it is an open dense subset of
G
.
Observe that

\begin{bmatrix}1&0&z\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{b}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}&\frac{c}{\varpi^{\ell}}\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{b}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0&z-\frac{bx}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}+\frac{c}{\varpi^{\ell}}\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}.

Therefore, if
\ell\geq n^{\prime}+m
and
b\in{\mathfrak{o}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{c\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}}\pi\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{b}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}&\frac{c}{\varpi^{\ell}}\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&g\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0&z\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{c\in{\mathfrak{o}}/{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}}\frac{\phi_{1}(x)}{|\det g|^{1/2}}h^{\prime\prime}\biggl(g\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{b}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl){\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}\biggl(z-\frac{bx}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}+\frac{c}{\varpi^{\ell}}\biggl)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\phi_{1}(x)}{|\det g|^{1/2}}h^{\prime\prime}\biggl(g\begin{bmatrix}1&\frac{b}{\varpi^{n^{\prime}}}\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl){\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}(z),

from which (
1
) follows.
The proof for (
2
) is easier by Lemma
8.11
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
8.13
.
By the definition of
h_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}
Lemma
8.15
gives

\displaystyle{W}_{\ell}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},\phi_{2},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}{W}_{\ell}\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu},{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}

\displaystyle=q^{-\ell}\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\phi_{1},h_{\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu},{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}}=q^{-\ell}h^{\nu,\rho,\mu}_{\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\phi_{1},\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}\phi_{2},{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}}.

Since
\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}=\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}}
and
\theta_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}=\widehat{\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}}
by Lemma
8.14
, we get the stated relation.

8.8.
The
K
-type of
{\mathbf{U}}_{p,\ell}^{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

For a positive integer
\ell
we define an open subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}({\mathfrak{o}})
by

{I}^{(3)}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})=\begin{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}&{\mathfrak{o}}&{\mathfrak{o}}\\
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}&{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}&{\mathfrak{o}}\\
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}&{\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell}&{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}\end{bmatrix}.{\@index{\@indexphrase{${I}_{0}^{(n)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})$}}}

Define the characters
(\mu,\rho,\nu)_{0}
of
{I}^{(3)}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})
and
(\mu,\rho)_{0}
of
{K}^{(2)}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\ell})
by

\displaystyle(\mu,\rho,\nu)_{0}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&y&z\\
v&b&x\\
w&u&c\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu(a)\rho(b)\nu(c),

\displaystyle(\mu,\rho)_{0}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&y\\
v&b\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu(a)\rho(b).

Proposition 8.17
.

Put
\phi_{3}=q^{-\ell}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}
.
If
\ell\geq\max\{n,c(\nu)\}
and
h^{\prime\prime}\in I(\rho,\mu)
satisfies
\pi^{\prime}(k^{\prime})h^{\prime\prime}=(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})_{0}(k^{\prime})h^{\prime\prime}
for
k^{\prime}\in{K}_{0}^{(2)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{2\ell})
, then for
k\in{I}_{0}^{(3)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{3\ell})

\pi(k)h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}=(\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\chi,\nu\chi^{-1})_{0}(k)h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
HY25
, Lemma 2.5]
.
We write

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{u}}^{-}(y)\begin{bmatrix}k^{\prime}&\\
&d\end{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{u}}(b),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{u}}(b)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&b\\
&1\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{u}}^{-}(y)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\\
\,{}^{t}\!y&1\end{bmatrix}

for
b\in{\mathfrak{o}}^{\oplus 2}
,
\,{}^{t}\!y\in({\mathfrak{p}}^{3\ell})^{\oplus 2}
,
d\in{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}
and
k^{\prime}\in{K}_{0}^{(2)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{3\ell})
.
Define
\Psi\in{S}(F^{2})
by
\Psi\Big(\begin{matrix}x_{1}\\
x_{2}\end{matrix}\Big)=\phi_{3}(x_{1})\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}}(x_{2})
.
Since
\Psi
is invariant under the translation by elements of
{\mathfrak{o}}^{\oplus 2}
, we have

\pi({\mathfrak{u}}(b))h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}=h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}.

Put
\pi^{\prime}=I(\rho,\mu)
.
Since
\Psi(dk^{\prime-1}x)=\chi(d^{-1}k^{\prime}_{2,2})\Psi(x)
by (2.11) of
[
HY25
]
,

\pi\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}k^{\prime}&\\
&d\end{bmatrix}\biggl)h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&g\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{u}}(x)\biggl)=\frac{\nu(d)\chi(k^{\prime}_{2,2})\Psi(x)}{\chi(d)|\det g|^{1/2}}\pi^{\prime}(k^{\prime})h^{\prime\prime}(g)

by definition.
We conclude that

\pi\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}k^{\prime}&\\
&d\end{bmatrix}\biggl)h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}=(\chi_{1},\chi_{2}\chi,\nu\chi^{-1})_{0}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}k^{\prime}&\\
&d\end{bmatrix}\biggl)h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}.

If
h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}(g{\mathfrak{u}}^{-}(y))\neq 0
, then there is an element
x\in({\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell})^{\oplus 2}
such that
g{\mathfrak{u}}^{-}(y)\in{P}_{1,2}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{u}}(x)
.
Since

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{u}}(x){\mathfrak{u}}^{-}(-y)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}-x\,^{t}\!y&x\\
-\,^{t}\!y&1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}-x\,^{t}\!y&0\\
-\,^{t}\!y&1+\,^{t}\!y(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}-x\,^{t}\!y)^{-1}x\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}-x\,^{t}\!y)^{-1}x\\
0&1\end{bmatrix},

we can write
g=\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&g^{\prime}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{u}}(x)
with
x\in({\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell})^{\oplus 2}
and
g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)
.
Since
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}+x\,^{t}\!y\in{K}^{(2)}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}^{2\ell})
, we see that

\displaystyle h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&g^{\prime}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{u}}(x){\mathfrak{u}}^{-}(y)\biggl)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\nu(1-\,^{t}\!y(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}+x\,^{t}\!y)^{-1}x)}{|\det g^{\prime}|^{1/2}}\pi^{\prime}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}+x\,^{t}\!y)h^{\prime\prime}(g^{\prime})\Psi((\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}+x\,^{t}\!y)^{-1}x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det g^{\prime}|^{-1/2}h^{\prime\prime}(g^{\prime})\Psi(x)=h_{\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}1&\\
&g^{\prime}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}{\mathfrak{u}}(x)\biggl)

by the assumption and definition above.
∎

Corollary 8.18
.

Put
\ell=\max\{n,n^{\prime},c(\nu),c(\rho),c(\mu)\}
.
If
m\geq 3\ell
, then

\pi(k){\mathbf{U}}_{p,\ell}^{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}=(\mu\chi^{\prime-1},\rho\chi\chi^{\prime},\nu\chi^{-1})_{0}(k){\mathbf{U}}_{p,\ell}^{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

for every
k\in{I}^{(3)}_{0}({\mathfrak{p}}^{m})
.

Proof.

One can easily show that for every
k^{\prime}\in{K}_{0}^{(2)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{2\ell})

\pi^{\prime}(k^{\prime})h_{\widehat{\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}}}^{\rho,\mu}=(\mu\chi^{\prime-1},\rho\chi^{\prime})_{0}(k^{\prime})h_{\widehat{\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}}}^{\rho,\mu}

(see Remark
8.9
).
Proposition
8.17
applied to
h^{\prime\prime}=h_{\widehat{\varphi^{\prime}_{\chi^{\prime}}}}^{\rho,\mu}
gives

\pi(k)h^{\ddagger}=(\mu\chi^{\prime-1},\rho\chi\chi^{\prime},\nu\chi^{-1})_{0}(k)h^{\ddagger}

for
k\in{I}_{0}^{(3)}({\mathfrak{p}}^{3\ell})
, which is equivalent to Corollary
8.18
in view of Proposition
8.13
.
∎

8.9.
The zeta integral of
{\mathbf{U}}_{p,\ell}^{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}

Let
\pi
be the irreducible generic constituent of the principal series
I(\nu,\rho,\mu)
and
\sigma
that of
I(\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
.
Put
\omega_{\sigma}=\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}
.
The element
t_{\ell}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)
is defined in (
8.5
).
Recall that
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h)\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
is associated to
h\in I(\nu,\rho,\mu)
by

W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(g,h)=\int_{F^{3}}h\left(w_{3}\begin{bmatrix}1&y&z\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}g\right)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(x+y)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z.

Similarly,
W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h^{\prime})\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\sigma)
is defined for
h^{\prime}\in I(\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime})
.

Theorem 8.19
.

Let
\chi=(\nu\mu^{\prime-1})|_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}
and
\chi^{\prime}=(\mu\nu^{\prime-1})|_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}
.
Put

\displaystyle W^{\dagger}=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\boldsymbol{\Theta}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}{\mathbf{U}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{\chi^{\prime}}h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\pi}),

\displaystyle h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma}=h_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}}^{\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}},

\displaystyle W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma}=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma}),

\displaystyle W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}}=(W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma})^{\natural}.

If
\ell
is sufficiently large, then

Z\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi(\varsigma)W^{\dagger},\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}}\right)^{2}=\Biggl(\frac{\zeta_{F}(2)}{q^{\ell/2}\zeta_{F}(1)}\nu^{\prime}(\varpi)^{\ell}\frac{\gamma\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\mu^{-1}\nu^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}\bigl)}{\gamma\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\otimes\mu^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\bigl)}\Biggl)^{2}.

8.10.
Proof of Theorem
8.19

Since
t_{\ell}{\mathbf{u}}(x)t_{\ell}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
\varpi^{\ell}x&1\end{bmatrix}
, we have

Z(s,\pi(\varsigma)W,\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\prime})=Z(s,\pi(\varsigma){W}_{m}W,\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\prime})

for
m\ll\ell
and
W\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
.
Put
W^{\ddagger}=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h^{\ddagger})
.
Then

{W}_{\ell-n^{\prime}}W^{\dagger}=\mu(\varpi)^{-n^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})\cdot\pi(\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{n^{\prime}})W^{\ddagger}

by Proposition
8.13
, and hence

Z(s,\pi(\varsigma)W^{\dagger},\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\prime})=\mu(\varpi)^{-n^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})Z(s,\pi(\varsigma\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{n^{\prime}})W^{\ddagger},\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\prime}).

Observe that

\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}t_{\ell}&\\
\hline\cr&1\end{array}\right]\varsigma\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{n^{\prime}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\varpi^{\ell+n^{\prime}}\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\\
\hline\cr&1\end{array}\right]{J}_{\ell+n^{\prime}},

where

{J}_{\ell}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}0&1&0\\
\varpi^{\ell}&0&0\\
\hline\cr 0&0&1\end{array}\right]^{-1}\varsigma=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&\frac{1}{\varpi^{\ell}}\\
1&0&0\\
0&1&0\end{bmatrix}.

The invariance of the JPSS integral gives

Z(s,\pi(\varsigma\varkappa_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{c}}^{n^{\prime}})W^{\ddagger},\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\prime})=\omega_{\sigma}(\varpi^{\ell+n^{\prime}})Z(s,\pi({J}_{\ell+n^{\prime}})W^{\ddagger},W^{\prime}).

We conclude that

Z(s,\pi(\varsigma)W^{\dagger},\sigma^{\vee}(t_{\ell})^{-1}W^{\prime})=\frac{\omega_{\sigma}(\varpi^{\ell+n^{\prime}})}{\mu(\varpi)^{n^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{g}}(\chi^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})Z(s,\pi({J}_{\ell+n^{\prime}})W^{\ddagger},W^{\prime}).

We deduce Theorem
8.19
from Remark
8.1
and the following lemma:

Lemma 8.20
.

Notation being as in Theorem
8.19
, we have

\frac{Z\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi({J}_{\ell+n^{\prime}})W^{\ddagger},W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}}\bigl)^{2}}{(\mu\omega_{\sigma}^{-1})(\varpi)^{2n^{\prime}}\frac{\zeta_{F}(2)^{2}}{\zeta_{F}(1)^{2}}}=\frac{q^{-\ell-c(\chi^{\prime})}\mu^{\prime}(\varpi^{-2\ell})L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\mu\nu^{\prime-1}\bigl)^{2}}{(\mu\nu^{\prime-1})(\varpi)^{2c(\chi^{\prime})}\gamma\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\pi\otimes\mu^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\bigl)^{2}L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\mu^{-1}\nu^{\prime}\bigl)^{2}}.

8.11.
Proof of Lemma
8.20

Recall the local zeta integrals associated to Whittaker functions
W\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\pi)
and a character
\mu^{\prime}
of
F^{\times}

\displaystyle Z(s,W,\mu^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{F^{\times}}W\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a&\\
&\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\mu^{\prime}(a)|a|^{s-1}\,\mathrm{d}a,

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}(s,W,\mu^{\prime-1})

\displaystyle=\int_{F^{\times}}\int_{F}W\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&&\\
x&1&\\
&&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\mu^{\prime}(a)^{-1}|a|^{s-1}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}a,

which converge absolutely for
\Re s\gg 0
.
Fix
h^{\prime\prime}\in I(\rho,\mu)
.
Put

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=h_{\phi_{1},h^{\prime\prime},\phi_{3}}^{\nu,\rho,\mu},

\displaystyle h_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{ord}}

\displaystyle=h^{\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}}_{{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}},

\displaystyle W^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=\pi({J}_{\ell})W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h).

Substituting the integral expression of
W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}}=(W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma}))^{\natural}
, we get

Z(s,W,W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=\int_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)}W\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}g&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)h^{\mathrm{ord}}_{\sigma}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}g^{\natural}\biggl)|\det g|^{s-\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}g.

Recall the integration formula

\int_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)}{F}(g)\,\mathrm{d}g=\frac{\zeta_{F}(2)}{\zeta_{F}(1)}\int_{F^{\times 2}\times F^{2}}{F}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a&\\
x&b\end{bmatrix}{\mathbf{u}}(y)\biggl)\,\mathrm{d}y\frac{\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}b}{|b|}

for an integrable function
{F}
on
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F)
(cf.
[
MV10
, 3.1.6, p. 206]
).
Since
g^{\natural}=w_{2}\,^{t}\!g^{-1}w_{2}
, we see that
\frac{\zeta_{F}(1)}{\zeta_{F}(2)}Z(s,W,W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}})
equals

\displaystyle\int_{F^{\times 2}\times F^{2}}W\left(\begin{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}a&\\
x&b\end{bmatrix}{\mathbf{u}}(y)&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\biggl|\frac{b}{a}\biggl|^{1/2}{\mathbb{I}}_{\mathfrak{o}}(y)\frac{|ab|^{s-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mu^{\prime}(a)\nu^{\prime}(b)}\,\mathrm{d}y\frac{\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}b}{|b|}.

Since
\pi\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}{\mathbf{u}}(y)&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)W=W
for
y\in{\mathfrak{o}}
for sufficiently large
\ell
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{F}(1)}{\zeta_{F}(2)}Z(s,W,W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=

\displaystyle\int_{F^{\times 2}\times F}W\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}a&&\\
\hline\cr x&b&\\
&&1\end{array}\right]\right)\frac{|ab|^{s-1}}{\nu^{\prime}(b)\mu^{\prime}(a)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}a\mathrm{d}b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F^{\times}}\widetilde{Z}(s,W_{b},\mu^{\prime-1})\nu^{\prime}(b)^{-1}|b|^{s-1}\mathrm{d}b,

where

W_{b}=\pi\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}1&&\\
\hline\cr&b&\\
&&1\end{array}\right]\right)W.

By the functional equation (
8.4
) we get

\widetilde{Z}(s,W_{b},\mu^{\prime-1})=\gamma(s,\pi\otimes\mu^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})^{-1}Z(1-s,\pi^{\vee}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W}_{b},\mu^{\prime}).

In summary we have

Z(s,W,W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=\frac{\frac{\zeta_{F}(2)}{\zeta_{F}(1)}}{\gamma(s,\pi\otimes\mu^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})}\int_{F^{\times}}Z(1-s,\pi^{\vee}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W}_{b},\mu^{\prime})\frac{|b|^{s-1}}{\nu^{\prime}(b)}\,\mathrm{d}b.

Observe that

\displaystyle\pi^{\vee}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W}_{b}(g)=

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}_{b}(g\varsigma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W_{b}(w_{3}\,^{t}\!g^{-1}\varsigma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}\left(w_{3}\,^{t}\!g^{-1}\varsigma\begin{bmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&b&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}{J}_{\ell},h\right).

We have

\pi^{\vee}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W}_{b}\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)=W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}b&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
\hline\cr 0&0&-(\varpi^{\ell}a)^{-1}\end{array}\right],h\right).

Now we need the following formula:

Lemma 8.21
.

Let
a,b\in F^{\times}
.
Then

W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}b&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
\hline\cr 0&0&a^{-1}\end{array}\right],h\right)\\
=\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)|b|^{1/2}W^{\prime\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\cdot|a|\nu(a)^{-1}\widehat{\phi_{1}}(a).

Proof.

Since

w_{3}\begin{bmatrix}1&y&z+xy\\
0&1&x\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}b&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&a\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}a&\\
&w_{2}{\mathbf{u}}(y)\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0&\frac{az}{b}\\
0&1&ax\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}

for
x,y,z\in F
, we get

\displaystyle W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}b&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&a\end{bmatrix},h\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F^{3}}h\left(\begin{bmatrix}a&\\
&w_{2}{\mathbf{u}}(y)\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}&1\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}&\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}1&0&\frac{a}{b}z\\
0&1&ax\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix}\right)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(x+y)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F^{3}}\frac{|a|\nu(a)}{|b|^{1/2}}h^{\prime\prime}\biggl(w_{2}{\mathbf{u}}(y)\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\phi_{1}(ax)\phi_{3}\biggl(\frac{a}{b}z\biggl)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(x+y)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F^{3}}\frac{|b|^{1/2}\nu(a)}{|a|}h^{\prime\prime}\biggl(w_{2}{\mathbf{u}}(y)\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\phi_{1}(x)\phi_{3}(z)\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\biggl(\frac{x}{a}+y\biggl)}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|b|^{1/2}\nu(a)}{|a|}\widehat{\phi_{1}}(a^{-1})\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)W^{\prime\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl).

We obtain the stated formula by replacing
a
by
a^{-1}
.
∎

For
\phi\in{S}(F)
and a character
\chi
of
F^{\times}
we define Tate’s local integral by

Z(s,\phi,\chi)=\int_{F^{\times}}\phi(a)\chi(a)|a|^{s}\,\mathrm{d}a.

Proposition 8.22
.

\frac{\zeta_{F}(1)}{\zeta_{F}(2)}Z(s,\pi({J}_{\ell})W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(h),W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}})
equals

\frac{\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)q^{-\ell s}\mu^{\prime}(-\varpi^{-\ell})}{\gamma(s,\pi\otimes\mu^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})}Z(1-s,\widehat{\phi_{1}},\nu^{-1}\mu^{\prime})Z(s,W^{\prime\prime},\nu^{\prime-1}).

Proof.

By Lemma
8.21
we get

\displaystyle Z(1-s,\pi^{\vee}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W}_{b},\mu^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)|b|^{1/2}W^{\prime\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\int_{F^{\times}}|-\varpi^{\ell}a|\nu(-\varpi^{\ell}a)^{-1}\widehat{\phi_{1}}(-\varpi^{\ell}a)\mu^{\prime}(a)\,\frac{\mathrm{d}a}{|a|^{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)|b|^{1/2}W^{\prime\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\int_{F^{\times}}|a|\nu(a)^{-1}\widehat{\phi_{1}}(a)\frac{\mu^{\prime}(-\varpi^{-\ell}a)}{q^{\ell s}|a|^{s}}\,\mathrm{d}a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-\ell s}\mu^{\prime}(-\varpi^{-\ell})\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)|b|^{1/2}W^{\prime\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)Z(1-s,\widehat{\phi_{1}},\nu^{-1}\mu^{\prime}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\int_{F^{\times}}Z(1-s,\pi^{\vee}(\varsigma)\widetilde{W}_{b},\mu^{\prime})\frac{|b|^{s-1}}{\nu^{\prime}(b)}\,\mathrm{d}b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-\ell s}\mu^{\prime}(-\varpi^{-\ell})\widehat{\phi_{3}}(0)Z(1-s,\widehat{\phi_{1}},\nu^{-1}\mu^{\prime})Z(s,W^{\prime\prime},\nu^{\prime-1})

as claimed.
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
8.20
.
Set

\displaystyle h^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=h_{\phi_{2}}^{\rho,\mu},

\displaystyle\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\varphi_{\chi}},

\displaystyle\phi_{2}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\varphi_{\chi^{\prime}}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle=(\nu\mu^{\prime-1})|_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}},

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(\mu\nu^{\prime-1})|_{{\mathfrak{o}}^{\times}}.

By definition we see that

W^{\prime\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}b&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)=\frac{\mu(b)}{|b|^{1/2}}\int_{F}h^{\prime\prime}(w_{2}{\mathbf{u}}(b^{-1}x))\overline{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x=\mu(-b)|b|^{1/2}\widehat{\phi_{2}}(b)

for
b\in F^{\times}
.
It follows that

Z(s,W^{\prime\prime},\nu^{\prime-1})=\mu(-1)Z(s,\widehat{\phi_{2}},\mu\nu^{\prime-1}).

Then

\displaystyle Z(s,\widehat{\phi_{1}},\nu^{-1}\mu^{\prime})

\displaystyle=(\nu\mu^{\prime})(-1),

\displaystyle Z(s,\widehat{\phi_{2}},\mu\nu^{\prime-1})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(\mu\nu^{\prime})(-1)&\text{if $c(\chi^{\prime})\geq 1$, }\\
\frac{(\mu\nu^{\prime-1})(\varpi)L(s,\mu\nu^{\prime-1})}{q^{s-1}L(1-s,\mu^{-1}\nu^{\prime})}&\text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

Letting
\phi_{3}=q^{-\ell}{\mathbb{I}}_{{\mathfrak{p}}^{-\ell}}
, we obtain

Z(s,\pi({J}_{\ell})W^{\ddagger},W^{\flat}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=\pm\frac{q^{-\ell s}\mu^{\prime}(\varpi^{-\ell})\frac{\zeta_{F}(2)}{\zeta_{F}(1)}}{\gamma(s,\pi\otimes\mu^{\prime-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}})}\times\begin{cases}1&\text{if $c(\chi^{\prime})\geq 1$, }\\
\frac{(\mu\nu^{\prime-1})(\varpi)L(s,\mu\nu^{\prime-1})}{q^{s-1}L(1-s,\mu^{-1}\nu^{\prime})}&\text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

Lemma
8.20
follows by replacing
\ell
by
\ell+n^{\prime}
in view of
n^{\prime}=\max\{1,c(\chi^{\prime})\}
.

Part III
Appendices

A.
Differential operators on symmetric spaces

A.1.
Bounded symmetric domains and factors of automorphy

For a Hermitian matrix
X
we write
X>0
to indicate that
X
is positive definite.
For non-negative integers
r,s
we define the Lie group
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}(r,s)

\displaystyle=\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{r+s}({\mathbb{C}})\;|\;gI_{r,s}\,^{t}\!g^{c}=I_{r,s}\},

\displaystyle I_{r,s}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{r}&\\
&-\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix}.

The group
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
acts transitively on the bounded symmetric domain

{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}=\{z\in\mathrm{M}_{r,s}({\mathbb{C}})\;|\;1_{s}-\,^{t}\!z^{c}z>0\}{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}$}}}

by

gz=(Az+B)(Cz+D)^{-1}

where
z\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
and
g=\begin{bmatrix}A&B\\
C&D\end{bmatrix}\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
with
D
of size
r
.

Let
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
be the zero matrix.
Then

K=\{g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)\;|\;g{\mathbf{o}}={\mathbf{o}}\}

is the maximal compact subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
.
We identify
{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
with
\mathrm{U}(r,s)/K
via the map
g\mapsto g{\mathbf{o}}
.
Put

\displaystyle\lambda(g,z)=B^{c}\,{}^{t}\!z+A^{c},

\displaystyle\mu(g,z)=Cz+D,

\displaystyle J(g,z)=(\lambda(g,z),\mu(g,z)),

\displaystyle\xi(z^{\prime},z)=\hbox{\bf 1}_{r}-z^{\prime c}\,{}^{t}\!z,

\displaystyle\eta(z^{\prime},z)=\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}-\,^{t}\!z^{\prime c}z,

\displaystyle B(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\hbox{\bf 1}_{r}&z\\
\,{}^{t}\!z^{c}&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\xi(z)=\xi(z,z),

\displaystyle\eta(z)=\eta(z,z),

\displaystyle\varXi(z)=(\xi(z),\eta(z))

for
z,w\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
and
g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
.
Recall the equalities

(A.1)

\displaystyle gB(z)=B(gz)\begin{bmatrix}\lambda(g,z)^{c}&\\
&\mu(g,z)\end{bmatrix},

\,{}^{t}\!B(z^{\prime})^{c}I_{r,s}B(z)=\begin{bmatrix}\xi(z^{\prime},z)^{c}&z-z^{\prime}\\
\,{}^{t}\!z^{\prime c}-\,^{t}\!z^{c}&-\eta(z^{\prime},z)\end{bmatrix}

from which we have for
g,g^{\prime}\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
z\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}

\displaystyle\lambda(gg^{\prime},z)=\lambda(g,g^{\prime}z)\lambda(g^{\prime},z),

\displaystyle\mu(gg^{\prime},z)=\mu(g,g^{\prime}z)\mu(g^{\prime},z),

\,{}^{t}\!\lambda(g,z)^{c}\xi(gz)\lambda(g,z)=\xi(z),

\,{}^{t}\!\mu(g,z)^{c}\eta(gz)\mu(g,z)=\eta(z).

Therefore the isomorphism
K\simeq\mathrm{U}(r)\times\mathrm{U}(s)
is given by
k\mapsto J(k,{\mathbf{o}})
.
Its inverse map is given by

(A.2)

(A,D)\mapsto\begin{bmatrix}\,{}^{t}\!A^{-1}&\\
&D\end{bmatrix}.

The group
\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
acts on
{\mathfrak{T}}=\mathrm{M}_{r,s}({\mathbb{C}})
by

\displaystyle(A,D)u

\displaystyle=Au\,^{t}\!D

\displaystyle(A

\displaystyle\in\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}}),\;D\in\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}}),\;u\in\mathrm{M}_{r,s}({\mathbb{C}})).

By a representation
(\rho,V)
of
\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
we mean a pair formed by a finite dimensional complex vector space
V
and a complex analytic homomorphism
\rho
of
\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
into
\mathrm{GL}(V)
.
Given a
C^{\infty}
-manifold
M
, we write
C^{\infty}(M,V)
for the space of all
V
-valued
C^{\infty}
functions on
M
and
C^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(r,s),\rho)
the space of all
V
-valued
C^{\infty}
functions on
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
such that for
g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
k\in K

h(gk)=\rho(J(k,{\mathbf{o}}))^{-1}h(g).

For
g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},V)
we define
f\|_{\rho}g\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},V)
by

f\|_{\rho}g(z)=\rho(J(g,z))^{-1}f(gz).

We associate
f^{\rho}\in C^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(r,s),\rho)
to
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},V)
by

f^{\rho}(g)=\rho(J(g,{\mathbf{o}}))^{-1}f(g{\mathbf{o}})=f\|_{\rho}g({\mathbf{o}}).

The map
f\mapsto f^{\rho}
is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear bijection of
C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},V)
onto
C^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(r,s),\rho)
.

A.2.
Differential operators on
{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}

Denote the Lie algebra of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
by
{\mathfrak{g}}
and the subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
corresponding to
K
by
{\mathfrak{k}}
.
The Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
acts on the space of
C^{\infty}
functions on
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
by

[Yf](g)=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}f(g\exp(tY))\biggl|_{t=0}

for
Y\in{\mathfrak{g}}
.
This action is extended to that of the universal enveloping algebra
{\mathfrak{U}}
of the complexified Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
\mathrm{Ad}
denote the adjoint representation of
K
on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
For
k\in K
the action of
\mathrm{Ad}(k)
can be extended to
{\mathfrak{U}}
, which we denote also by
\mathrm{Ad}(k)
.

Since we have fixed complex structure of
{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}=\mathrm{U}(r,s)/K
, we have the Harish-Chandra decomposition

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}={\mathfrak{k}}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}\oplus{\mathfrak{p}}_{-},

where

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{M}_{r+s}({\mathbb{C}}),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{k}}_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\begin{bmatrix}a&0\\
0&d\end{bmatrix}\biggl|\;a\in\mathrm{M}_{r}({\mathbb{C}}),\;d\in\mathrm{M}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})\biggl\}

and the
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear bijections
\iota_{\pm}
of
{\mathfrak{T}}=\mathrm{M}_{r,s}({\mathbb{C}})
onto
{\mathfrak{p}}_{\pm}
are defined by

\displaystyle\iota_{+}(u)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&u\\
0&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\iota_{-}(u)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\,{}^{t}\!u&0\end{bmatrix}

for
u\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.
Note that
Xh=-\mathrm{d}\rho(X)h
for every
X\in{\mathfrak{k}}
and
h\in C^{\infty}(\rho)
.
Since
[{\mathfrak{p}}_{\pm},{\mathfrak{p}}_{\pm}]=\{0\}
, the symmetric algebra
{\mathfrak{S}}({\mathfrak{p}}_{\pm})
can be embedded in
{\mathfrak{U}}
and is stable under
\mathrm{Ad}(k)
for
k\in K
.

We write
Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
for the vector space of all
{\mathbb{C}}
-multilinear maps of
{\mathfrak{T}}\times\cdots\times{\mathfrak{T}}
(
n
copies) into
V
.
We define the representation
(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n},Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V))
of
\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
by

\displaystyle[(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(a,b)h](u_{1},\dots,u_{n})

\displaystyle=\rho(a,b)h(\,^{t}\!au_{1}b,\dots,\,^{t}\!au_{n}b){\@index{\@indexphrase{$\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}$}}}

for
(a,b)\in\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
,
h\in Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.

Given
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},V)
, we define
Df\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},Ml_{1}({\mathfrak{T}},V))
by

\displaystyle[Df(u)](z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sum_{j=1}^{s}u_{ij}\frac{\partial f}{\partial z_{ij}}(z),

\displaystyle[Cf(u)](z)

\displaystyle=[Df(\,^{t}\!\xi(z)u\eta(z))](z)

for
z=(z_{ij})\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
and
u=(u_{ij})\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.
Given a non-negative positive integer
n
, we define
D^{n}f
and
C^{n}f
by

\displaystyle D^{n}f

\displaystyle=D(D^{n-1}f),

\displaystyle D^{0}f

\displaystyle=f,

\displaystyle C^{n}f

\displaystyle=C(C^{n-1}f),

\displaystyle C^{0}f

\displaystyle=f.

These have values in
Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V)
in the sense that

C^{n}f(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=C[C^{n-1}f(u_{1},\dots,u_{n-1})](u_{n}).

Define
D_{\rho}^{n}f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V))
by

D_{\rho}^{n}f=(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(\varXi)^{-1}C^{n}(\rho(\varXi)f).

Proposition 12.10 and (A8.6a) of
[
Shi00
]
say that

\displaystyle D_{\rho}^{n+1}=D_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}D_{\rho}^{n}=D^{n}_{\rho\otimes\tau}D_{\rho},

\displaystyle D_{\rho}^{n}(f\|_{\rho}g)=(D_{\rho}^{n}f)\|_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}g,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\iota_{+}(u_{1})\cdots\iota_{+}(u_{n})f^{\rho}=[D_{\rho}^{n}f]^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})

for
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s},V)
,
g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.

A.3.
Hermitian symmetric spaces

We retain the notation in Sections
2.1
–
2.6
.
Put

\displaystyle\varXi(Z)

\displaystyle=(\xi(Z),\eta(Z)),

\displaystyle B(Z)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\,{}^{t}\!\tau^{c}&\,{}^{t}\!w^{c}&\tau\\
0&\gamma_{0}&w\\
\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix},{\@index{\@indexphrase{$B(Z)$}}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\varXi(Z),\xi(Z),\eta(Z)$}}}

where

\displaystyle\xi(Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\tau^{c}-\,^{t}\!\tau&-\,^{t}\!w\\
w^{c}&-\,^{t}\!\gamma_{0}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\eta(Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}(\,^{t}\!\tau^{c}-\tau-\,^{t}\!w^{c}\gamma_{0}^{-1}w).

It is well-known that

(A.4)

\displaystyle\alpha B(Z)=B(\alpha Z)\begin{bmatrix}\lambda(\alpha,Z)^{c}&\\
&\mu(\alpha,Z)\end{bmatrix},{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\lambda(\alpha,Z)$}}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\mu(\alpha,Z)$}}}

\,{}^{t}\!B(Z^{\prime})^{c}S_{\gamma_{0}}^{-1}B(Z)=\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{-1}\xi(Z^{\prime},Z)^{c}&Z-Z^{\prime}\\
\,{}^{t}\!(Z-Z^{\prime})^{c}&\sqrt{-1}\eta(Z^{\prime},Z)\end{bmatrix}

(see (6.3.1) and (6.6.1) of
[
Shi97
]
) from which we have

\displaystyle\lambda(\alpha\beta,Z)=\lambda(\alpha,\beta Z)\lambda(\beta,Z),

\displaystyle\mu(\alpha\beta,Z)=\mu(\alpha,\beta Z)\mu(\beta,Z),

(A.5)

\,{}^{t}\!\lambda(\alpha,Z)^{c}\xi(\alpha Z)\lambda(\alpha,Z)=\xi(Z),

\,{}^{t}\!\mu(\alpha,Z)^{c}\eta(\alpha Z)\mu(\alpha,Z)=\eta(Z)

for
\alpha,\beta\in G
and
Z\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
.
Recall the maximal compact subgroup
{K}
of
G

\displaystyle{K}

\displaystyle=\{\alpha\in G\;|\;\alpha{\mathbf{i}}={\mathbf{i}}\},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{i}}=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{-1}\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\\
\hbox{\bf 0}_{t,s}\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}.

Let
C^{\infty}(G,\rho)
the space of all
V
-valued
C^{\infty}
functions on
G
that satisfy

h(gk)=\rho(J(k,{\mathbf{i}}))^{-1}h(g)

for
g\in G
and
k\in{K}
.
The map
f\mapsto f^{\rho}
is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear bijection of
C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
onto
C^{\infty}(G,\rho)
.
Given
g\in G
and
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
, we define
f\|_{\rho}g\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
by

f\|_{\rho}g(Z)=\rho(J(g,Z))^{-1}f(gZ).

We define
f^{\rho}\in C^{\infty}(G,\rho)
by

f^{\rho}(g)=\rho(J(g,{\mathbf{i}}))^{-1}f(g{\mathbf{i}})=f\|_{\rho}g({\mathbf{i}}).

Given
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
, we define
Df\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},Ml_{1}({\mathfrak{T}},V))
by

\displaystyle[Df(u)](Z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{s}u_{ij}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\tau_{ij}}(Z)+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\sum_{j=1}^{s}u_{s+i,j}\frac{\partial f}{\partial w_{ij}}(Z),

\displaystyle[Cf(u)](Z)

\displaystyle=[Df(\,^{t}\!\xi(Z)u\eta(Z))](Z)

for
Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s}
and
u=(u_{ij})\in{\mathfrak{T}}
.
Given a non-negative positive integer
n
, we define
D^{n}f
and
C^{n}f
by

\displaystyle D^{n}f

\displaystyle=D(D^{n-1}f),

\displaystyle D^{0}f

\displaystyle=f,

\displaystyle C^{n}f

\displaystyle=C(C^{n-1}f),

\displaystyle C^{0}f

\displaystyle=f.

Define
D_{\rho}^{n}f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},Ml_{n}({\mathfrak{T}},V))
by

D_{\rho}^{n}f=(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(\varXi)^{-1}C^{n}(\rho(\varXi)f).{\@index{\@indexphrase{$D_{\rho}$}}}

Proposition 12.10 of
[
Shi00
]
shows that

(A.6)

\displaystyle D_{\rho}^{n+1}

\displaystyle=D_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}D_{\rho}^{n}=D^{n}_{\rho\otimes\tau}D_{\rho},

\displaystyle D_{\rho}^{n}(f\|_{\rho}g)

\displaystyle=(D_{\rho}^{n}f)\|_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}g.

A.4.
Translation

We take
\beta\in\mathrm{GL}_{t}({\mathbb{C}})
such that
\sqrt{-1}\beta\,^{t}\!\beta^{c}=2\gamma_{0}
and put

\displaystyle{C}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{-1}\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0&\sqrt{-1}\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\\
0&\beta&0\\
-\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}&0&\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}\end{bmatrix},

following
[
Shi97
, A2.2]
.
Since
{C}I_{r,s}\,^{t}\!{C}^{c}=-2\sqrt{-1}S_{\gamma_{0}}
, we define the isomorphism

\displaystyle\varphi

\displaystyle:\mathrm{U}(r,s)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}G,

\displaystyle\varphi(g)

\displaystyle={C}g{C}^{-1}.

Lemma A2.3 of
[
Shi97
]
gives a holomorphic bijection

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{t}}

\displaystyle:{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{t}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}z_{1}\\
z_{2}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{-1}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}+z_{1})(\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}-z_{1})^{-1}\\
\beta z_{2}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{s}-z_{1})^{-1}\end{bmatrix}

such that
{\mathfrak{t}}(gz)=\varphi(g){\mathfrak{t}}z
.
Define holomorphic maps
\kappa:{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}\to\mathrm{GL}_{r}({\mathbb{C}})
and
\nu:{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}\to\mathrm{GL}_{s}({\mathbb{C}})
by

{C}B(z)=B({\mathfrak{t}}z)\begin{bmatrix}\kappa(z)^{c}&\\
&\nu(z)\end{bmatrix}

(see (A2.3.4) of
[
Shi97
]
).
We see from (
A.1
) and (
A.4
) that

\displaystyle\lambda(\varphi(g),{\mathfrak{t}}z)=\kappa(gz)\lambda(g,z)\kappa(z)^{-1},

\displaystyle\mu(\varphi(g),{\mathfrak{t}}z)=\nu(gz)\mu(g,z)\nu(z)^{-1}.

Moreover, the proof of Lemma A2.3 shows that

\displaystyle 2\xi(z^{\prime},z)

\displaystyle=\,^{t}\!\kappa(z^{\prime})^{c}\xi({\mathfrak{t}}z^{\prime},{\mathfrak{t}}z)\kappa(z),

\displaystyle 2\eta(z^{\prime},z)

\displaystyle=\,^{t}\!\nu(z^{\prime})^{c}\eta({\mathfrak{t}}z^{\prime},{\mathfrak{t}}z)\nu(z),

Put
\varkappa(z)=(\kappa(z),\nu(z))
.
We rewrite the formulas as

(A.7)

\displaystyle J(\varphi(g),{\mathfrak{t}}z)

\displaystyle=\varkappa(gz)J(g,z)\varkappa(z)^{-1},

\displaystyle 2\varXi(z)

\displaystyle=\,^{t}\!\varkappa(z)^{c}\varXi({\mathfrak{t}}z)\varkappa(z).

Let
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
.
Define
\tilde{f}\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
by

\tilde{f}_{\rho}(Z)=\rho(\varkappa({\mathfrak{t}}^{-1}Z))f(Z).

Let
\hat{\mathfrak{g}}
denote the Lie algebras of
G
.
Define the isomorphism
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
by
X\mapsto{C}X{C}^{-1}
.
For
u\in{\mathfrak{T}}
we define
\iota_{+}^{C}(u)\in\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
by
\iota_{+}^{C}(u)={C}\iota_{+}(u){C}^{-1}
.

Proposition A.1
.

Given
g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{r,s},V)
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\rho}\|_{\rho}\varphi(g)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}}\|_{\rho}g\circ{\mathfrak{t}}^{-1})_{\rho}},

\displaystyle(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{(D_{\rho}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})\circ{\mathfrak{t}}^{-1})_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}}.

Proof.

Let
g\in\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
z\in{\mathfrak{B}}_{r,s}
.
It follows from (
A.7
) that

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{\rho}\|_{\rho}\varphi(g)({\mathfrak{t}}z)

\displaystyle=\rho(J(\varphi(g),{\mathfrak{t}}z))^{-1}\tilde{f}_{\rho}(\varphi(g){\mathfrak{t}}z)

\displaystyle=\rho(\varkappa(gz)J(g,z)\varkappa(z)^{-1})^{-1}\tilde{f}_{\rho}({\mathfrak{t}}(gz))=\rho(\varkappa(z))(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}}\|_{\rho}g(z)).

Shimura has proved that

D(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(\,^{t}\!\kappa(z)u_{1}\nu(z))=-2\sqrt{-1}[Df(u_{1})]\circ{\mathfrak{t}}

for
u_{1}\in{\mathfrak{T}}
in §26.2 of
[
Shi00
]
.
It follows from (
A.7
) that

\displaystyle 4[C(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(u_{1})](z)

\displaystyle=4[D(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(\,^{t}\!\xi(z)u_{1}\eta(z))](z)

\displaystyle=[D(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(\,^{t}\!\kappa(z)\,^{t}\!\xi({\mathfrak{t}}z)\kappa(z)^{c}u_{1}\,^{t}\!\nu(z)^{c}\eta({\mathfrak{t}}z)\nu(z))]({\mathfrak{t}}z)

\displaystyle=-2\sqrt{-1}[Cf(\kappa(z)^{c}u_{1}\,^{t}\!\nu(z)^{c})]({\mathfrak{t}}z).

Since
\kappa(z)^{c}
and
\nu(z)^{c}
are anti-holomorphic, we have

4^{n}[C^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(\underline{u})](z)\\
=(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}[C^{n}f(\kappa(z)^{c}\underline{u}\,^{t}\!\nu(z)^{c})]({\mathfrak{t}}z)

for
\underline{u}=(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{n}
.
We get

\displaystyle[D_{\rho}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(\underline{u})](z)=

\displaystyle\rho(\Xi({\mathfrak{t}}z)\varkappa(z))^{-1}[C^{n}(\rho(\varXi)\tilde{f}_{\rho})\circ{\mathfrak{t}}(\,^{t}\!\xi(z)^{-1}\underline{u}\eta(z)^{-1})](z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}\rho(\varkappa(z))^{-1}[D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}(\,^{t}\!\kappa(z)^{-1}\underline{u}\nu(z)^{-1})]({\mathfrak{t}}z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(\varkappa(z))^{-1}[D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}(\underline{u})]({\mathfrak{t}}z)

again by (
A.7
) as claimed.
∎

Corollary A.2
.

(1)

(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})^{\rho}=\rho(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))^{-1}(\tilde{f}_{\rho})^{\rho}\circ\varphi
.

(2)

[D_{\rho}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})]^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}=(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}(\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n})(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))^{-1}[D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}]^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}\circ\varphi
.

(3)

For
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}\in{\mathfrak{T}}

\iota_{+}^{C}(u_{1})\cdots\iota_{+}^{C}(u_{n})(\tilde{f}_{\rho})^{\rho}=(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}\upsilon^{n}(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))^{-1}D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}.

Proof.

Since
{\mathbf{i}}={\mathfrak{t}}{\mathbf{o}}
, we obtain (
1
) by specializing the first identify of Proposition
A.1
.
Proposition
A.1
combined with (
A.6
) gives

(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}(D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho})\|_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}\varphi(g)=\widetilde{(D_{\rho}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})\|_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}g\circ{\mathfrak{t}}^{-1})}_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}

from which we obtain (
2
).
By definition we have

(\iota_{+}^{C}(u_{1})\cdots\iota_{+}^{C}(u_{n})(\tilde{f}_{\rho})^{\rho})\circ\varphi=\iota_{+}(u_{1})\cdots\iota_{+}(u_{n})((\tilde{f}_{\rho})^{\rho}\circ\varphi).

The right hand side is

\iota_{+}(u_{1})\cdots\iota_{+}(u_{n})(\rho(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})^{\rho})=\rho(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))[D_{\rho}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})]^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}(\underline{u})

by (
1
) and (
A.3
), where
\underline{u}=(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})
.
Since

\rho(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))[D_{\rho}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})](\underline{u})^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}=(-2\sqrt{-1})^{n}\upsilon^{n}(\varkappa({\mathbf{o}}))^{-1}D_{\rho}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}(\underline{u})^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}\circ\varphi

by (
2
), we have completed the proof of (
3
).
∎

A.5.
Differential operators on
{\mathfrak{B}}_{2,1}

Letting
r=2
,
s=1
and
\gamma_{0}=\delta
, we retain the notation of §
4.4
.
Put

\displaystyle v_{0}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{T}},

\displaystyle Y_{+}

\displaystyle=\iota_{+}(v_{0})=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}0&0&0\\
0&0&1\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0\end{array}\right]\in{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}.

Let
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3})
and
{\mathbf{v}}_{i}=X^{\kappa-i}Y^{i}\in{L}_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}
be a non-zero vector such that

\rho^{\vee}_{\underline{k}}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&0\\
0&a_{2}\end{bmatrix},a_{3}\biggl){\mathbf{v}}_{i}=a_{1}^{k_{1}+i}a_{2}^{k_{2}-i}a_{3}^{-k_{3}}{\mathbf{v}}_{i}

for
i=0,1,2,\dots,\kappa
and
\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}a_{1}&0\\
0&a_{2}\end{bmatrix},a_{3}\biggl)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
.
Given
{\mathbf{v}}\in{L}_{\underline{k}}^{\vee}
, we associate to
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{B}}_{2,1},V)
and
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathrm{U}(2,1),\rho)
functions
f_{\mathbf{v}}:{\mathfrak{B}}_{2,1}\to{\mathbb{C}}
and
h_{\mathbf{v}}:\mathrm{U}(2,1)\to{\mathbb{C}}
defined by

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{v}}(z)

\displaystyle=\ell_{\rho}({\mathbf{v}}\otimes f(z)),

\displaystyle h_{\mathbf{v}}(g)

\displaystyle=\ell_{\rho}({\mathbf{v}}\otimes h(g)).

For
g\in\mathrm{U}(2,1)
and
t=\mathrm{diag}[t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}]\in K
we see that

h_{{\mathbf{v}}_{i}}(gt)=\ell_{\rho}\biggl(\rho^{\vee}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}t_{1}^{-1}&0\\
0&t_{2}^{-1}\end{bmatrix},t_{3}\biggl){\mathbf{v}}_{i}\otimes h(g)\biggl)=h_{{\mathbf{v}}_{i}}(g)t_{1}^{-k_{1}-i}t_{2}^{-k_{2}+i}t_{3}^{-k_{3}}

(cf. (
A.2
)).
Since
\mathrm{Ad}(t)Y_{+}=t_{2}t_{3}^{-1}Y_{+}
, we see that

Y_{+}^{n}f^{\rho}_{{\mathbf{v}}_{i}}(gt)=(Y_{+}^{n}f^{\rho})_{{\mathbf{v}}_{i}}(gt)=Y_{+}^{n}f^{\rho}_{{\mathbf{v}}_{i}}(g)t_{1}^{-k_{1}-i}t_{2}^{-k_{2}+i+n}t_{3}^{-k_{3}-n}.

It follows from (
A.3
) that

Y_{+}^{n}f^{\rho}=D_{\rho}^{n}f(v_{0},v_{0},\dots,v_{0})^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}.

A.6.
The restriction to
{\mathfrak{D}}_{1,1}

We retain the notation of §
2.9
.
It follows from (
2.6
) that for
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1},V)

\displaystyle f\|_{\rho}\iota(h)\circ\jmath

\displaystyle=f\circ\jmath\|_{\rho\circ\iota^{\prime}}h,

\displaystyle f^{\rho}\circ\iota

\displaystyle=(f\circ\jmath)^{\rho\circ\iota^{\prime}}.

Recall that for
Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1}

\displaystyle\eta(Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}(\tau^{c}-\tau-w^{c}\delta^{-1}w),

\displaystyle\eta(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}(\tau^{c}-\tau)=\eta(\jmath(\tau)),

\displaystyle\xi(\jmath(\tau))

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}\eta(\tau)&0\\
0&-\sqrt{-1}\delta\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\varXi(\tau)

\displaystyle=(\xi(\tau),\eta(\tau)).

Put
\underline{v}_{0}^{n}=(v_{0},v_{0},\dots,v_{0})\in{\mathfrak{T}}^{n}
.
We see from (
A.6
) that

(A.8)

\displaystyle[D_{\rho}^{n}(f\|_{\rho}\iota(h))(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))=

\displaystyle[D_{\rho}^{n}f\|_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}\iota(h)(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(D_{\rho}^{n}f)\circ\jmath\|_{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}\circ\iota}h(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu(h,\tau)^{-n}[D_{\rho}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]\circ\jmath\|_{\rho\circ\iota}h(\tau).

Put
Y_{+}^{C}={C}Y_{+}{C}^{-1}\in\hat{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
.
We will consider the differential operator

(Y_{+}^{C})^{n}f^{\rho}=[D_{\rho}^{n}f]^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})

for
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1},V)
(cf. Corollary
A.2
(
3
)).

We write
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1},{L}_{\underline{k}}({\mathbb{C}}))
as

f(Z)=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}f_{i}(Z)X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}.

Remark A.3
.

We take
\beta=\sqrt{2|\delta|}
.
Then
\varkappa({\mathbf{o}})=\left(\begin{bmatrix}-1&0\\
0&\sqrt{-2/|\delta|}\end{bmatrix},1\right)
.
We can rewrite Corollary
A.2
(
2
) as

[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}\tilde{f}_{\rho}(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}\circ\varphi=\frac{(-\sqrt{-1})^{i+2k_{1}+k_{2}}}{(-2)^{n}}\sqrt{2/|\delta|}^{n-k_{2}+i}[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}(f\circ{\mathfrak{t}})(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}^{\rho\otimes\upsilon^{n}}

for
i=0,1,2,\dots,\kappa
.
The dependance on
\delta
is compatible with Remark
6.13
.

Given
k,\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
g\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{1,1},{\mathbb{C}})
and
h\in H
, we define

g\|_{(\ell,k)}h(\tau)=\lambda(h,\tau)^{\ell}\mu(h,\tau)^{-k}g(h\tau).

Proposition A.4
.

For
h\in H
we have

[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}(f\|_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}\iota(h))(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath=[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath\|_{(k_{1}+i,k_{3}+n)}h.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}(f\|_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}\iota(h))(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}(\jmath(\tau))

\displaystyle=\mu(h,\tau)^{-n}([D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]\circ\jmath\|_{\rho_{\underline{k}}\circ\iota}h)_{i}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\lambda(h,\tau)^{k_{1}+i}\mu(h,\tau)^{-n-k_{3}}[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})]_{i}\circ\jmath(h\tau)

by (
A.8
).
∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1},V)
.
We have

[D_{\rho}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))=\rho(\varXi(\jmath(\tau)))^{-1}\left[\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}-\frac{w^{c}}{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\right)^{n}(\rho(\varXi)f)\right](\jmath(\tau)).

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle[Cf(v_{0})](Z)

\displaystyle=\left[Df\left(\,{}^{t}\!\xi(Z)\begin{bmatrix}0\\
1\end{bmatrix}\eta(Z)\right)\right](Z)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{-1}\eta(Z)\left(w^{c}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}-\delta\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\right)f(Z)

for
Z=\begin{bmatrix}\tau\\
w\end{bmatrix}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1}
.
Since

(A.9)

\left(w^{c}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}-\delta\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\right)\eta(Z)=0,

we have

[C^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](Z)=(\sqrt{-1}\eta(Z))^{n}\left(w^{c}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}-\delta\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\right)^{n}f(Z).

Since
\,{}^{t}\!\xi(\jmath(\tau))^{-1}v_{0}\eta(\tau)^{-1}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\delta\eta(\tau)}v_{0}
, we have

[D_{\rho}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))=\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{\delta\eta(\tau)}\right)^{n}\rho(\varXi(\tau))^{-1}[C^{n}(\rho(\varXi)f)(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau)),

which proves the stated formula.
∎

Proposition A.6
.

Given
f\in C^{\infty}({\mathfrak{D}}_{2,1},{L}_{\underline{k}}({\mathbb{C}}))
, we have

[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\sum_{j=0}^{\min\{n,i\}}\frac{i!}{(i-j)!}\binom{n}{j}\frac{\partial^{n-j}f_{i}}{\partial w^{n-j}}(\jmath(\tau))\frac{X^{i-j}Y^{\kappa-i+j}}{(-\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau))^{j}}.

In particular,

[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\frac{\partial^{n}f_{i}}{\partial w^{n}}(\jmath(\tau))X^{i}Y^{\kappa-i}\pmod{\eta(\tau)^{-1}}.

Proof.

Recall that
\xi(Z)=\begin{bmatrix}\eta(\tau)&-\sqrt{-1}w\\
\sqrt{-1}w^{c}&-\sqrt{-1}\delta\end{bmatrix}
.
Since

\displaystyle\det\xi(Z)

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{-1}\delta\eta(Z),

\,{}^{t}\!\xi(Z)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta\eta(Z)}\begin{bmatrix}\delta&w^{c}\\
-w&\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau)\end{bmatrix},

we have

(\rho_{\underline{k}}(\varXi)f)(Z)=\eta(Z)^{k_{3}}\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}f_{i}(Z)\frac{(\delta X-wY)^{i}(w^{c}X+\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau)Y)^{\kappa-i}}{(-\sqrt{-1}\delta\eta(Z))^{k_{1}}(\delta\eta(Z))^{\kappa}}.

It follows again from (
A.9
) that

\displaystyle\frac{(-\sqrt{-1})^{k_{1}}\delta^{k_{2}}}{\eta(Z)^{k_{3}-k_{2}}}\left[\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}-\frac{w^{c}}{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\right)^{n}(\rho_{\underline{k}}(\varXi)f)\right](Z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}-\frac{w^{c}}{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\right)^{n}f_{i}(Z)(\delta X-wY)^{i}(w^{c}X+\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau)Y)^{\kappa-i}.

Now we evaluate at
w=0
.
Then since
w^{c}=0
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{(-\sqrt{-1})^{k_{1}}\delta^{k_{2}}}{\eta(\tau)^{k_{3}-k_{2}}}\left[\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}-\frac{w^{c}}{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\right)^{n}(\rho_{\underline{k}}(\varXi)f)\right](\jmath(\tau))\Big|_{w=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial w^{n}}f_{i}(Z)(\delta X-wY)^{i}(\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau)Y)^{\kappa-i}\Big|_{w=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}(\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau)Y)^{\kappa-i}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\binom{n}{j}\frac{\partial^{n-j}f_{i}}{\partial w^{n-j}}(Z)\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial w^{j}}(\delta X-wY)^{i}\Big|_{w=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}(\sqrt{-1}\eta(\tau)Y)^{\kappa-i}\sum_{j=0}^{\min\{n,i\}}\binom{n}{j}\frac{\partial^{n-j}f_{i}}{\partial w^{n-j}}(\jmath(\tau))\frac{i!(-Y)^{j}}{(i-j)!}(\delta X)^{i-j}.

Finally, we see from Lemma
A.5
that
[D_{\rho_{\underline{k}}}^{n}f(\underline{v}_{0}^{n})](\jmath(\tau))
equals

\displaystyle\frac{(-\sqrt{-1}\delta\eta(\tau))^{k_{1}}}{\eta(\tau)^{k_{3}}}\rho_{\kappa}(\xi(\jmath(\tau)))\left[\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}-\frac{w^{c}}{\delta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\right)^{n}(\rho_{\underline{k}}(\varXi)f)\right](\jmath(\tau))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\kappa}\frac{(\eta(\tau)\delta Y)^{\kappa-i}}{(\delta\eta(\tau))^{\kappa}}\sum_{j=0}^{\min\{n,i\}}\binom{n}{j}\frac{\partial^{n-j}f_{i}}{\partial w^{n-j}}(\jmath(\tau))\frac{i!(\sqrt{-1}\delta Y)^{j}}{(i-j)!}(\delta\eta(\tau)X)^{i-j},

which proves our formula.
∎

B.
Archimedean computations

B.1.
Discrete series representations of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)

Put

1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{n}=(1,1,\dots,1)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}.

Let
r
and
s
be non-negative integers such that
r+s=n
.
Discrete series of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
are parametrized by Harish-Chandra parameters.
Let
(\lambda,z)
be a Harish-Chandra parameter for
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
, where
\lambda\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}+\frac{n-1}{2}1\hskip-2.5pt{\rm{l}}_{n}
is a sequence of distinct integers or half integers and
z\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
is a sequence of
+
and
-
corresponding to each entry in
\lambda
.
Here the total number of
+
’s must be
r
and the total number of
-
’s must be
s
.

Let
D(\lambda,z)
denote the discrete series of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
with Harich-Chandra parameter
(\lambda,z)
.
We also interpret
z
as a partition of
\lambda
into
\lambda^{+}\in\bigl(\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}\bigl)^{r}
and
\lambda^{-}\in\bigl(\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}\bigl)^{s}
, and write
D_{(\lambda^{+};\lambda^{-})}=D(\lambda,z)
.

B.2.
Branching laws for
\mathrm{U}(r,s)\times\mathrm{U}(r-1,s)

Definition B.1
.

We say that two Harish-Chandra parameters
(\lambda,z)
and
(\mu,t)
of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
and
\mathrm{U}(r-1,s)
respectively, satisfy the GGP interlacing relation, if one can line up
\lambda
and
\mu
in the descending ordering such that the corresponding sequence of signs from
z
and
t
only has the following eight adjacent pairs

(\oplus+),\;(+\oplus),\;(-\ominus),\;(\ominus-),\;(+-),\;(-+),\;(\oplus\ominus),\;(\ominus\oplus).

Here
\oplus
and
\ominus
represent
+1
and
-1
in
t
, and
\pm
represents
\pm 1
in
z
.

Theorem B.2

(He
[
He17
]
)
.

D(\mu,t)
appears as a subrepresentation of the restriction of
D(\lambda,z)
to
\mathrm{U}(r-1,s)
if and only if
(\lambda,z)
and
(\mu,t)
satisfy the GGP interlacing relation.

B.3.
Archimedean local factors

Let
{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
denote the group of complex numbers of absolute value
1
.
Define the character
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}:{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
by
{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x)=\frac{x}{|x|}
.
We view it as a character of any unitary group via composition with the determinant character.
Fix tuples
\lambda_{1}>\cdots>\lambda_{n}
and
\mu_{1}>\cdots>\mu_{n-1}
of half integers such that
\lambda_{i}-\frac{n+1}{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\mu_{j}-\frac{n}{2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let

\displaystyle(\lambda,z)

\displaystyle=(\underset{\oplus}{\lambda_{1}},\dots,\underset{\oplus}{\lambda_{r}};\underset{\ominus}{\lambda_{r+1}},\dots,\underset{\ominus}{\lambda_{n}}),

\displaystyle(\mu,t)

\displaystyle=(\underset{+}{\mu_{1}},\dots,\underset{+}{\mu_{r-1}};\underset{-}{\mu_{r}},\dots,\underset{-}{\mu_{n-1}})

be Harish-Chandra parameters of
\mathrm{U}(V)
and
\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})
.
We identify the maximal compact subgroup
K
of
\mathrm{U}(V)
with
\mathrm{U}(r)\times\mathrm{U}(s)
via the map

\begin{bmatrix}A&\\
&D\end{bmatrix}\mapsto(A,D)

(cf. (
A.2
)).
Let
\pi
be a discrete series of
\mathrm{U}(r,s)
with Harish-Chandra parameter
(\lambda,z)
and
\sigma
a discrete series of
\mathrm{U}(r-1,s)
with Harish-Chandra parameter
(\mu,t)
.
The minimal
K
-types of
\pi
and
\sigma
are

\displaystyle-\underline{k}=

\displaystyle\biggl(\lambda_{1}+\frac{s-(r-1)}{2},\dots,\lambda_{r}+\frac{s+(r-1)}{2};

\displaystyle\quad\lambda_{r+1}-\frac{r+(s-1)}{2},\dots,\lambda_{n}-\frac{r-(s-1)}{2}\biggl),

\displaystyle-\underline{k}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\biggl(\mu_{1}+\frac{s-(r-2)}{2},\dots,\mu_{r-1}+\frac{s+(r-2)}{2};

\displaystyle\quad\mu_{r}-\frac{r-1+(s-1)}{2},\dots,\mu_{n-1}-\frac{r-1-(s-1)}{2}\biggl).

The
L
-parameters of
\pi
and
\sigma
restricted to
W_{\mathbb{C}}={\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\subset W_{\mathbb{R}}
are given by

\displaystyle\phi_{\pi}|_{W_{\mathbb{C}}}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{2\lambda_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{2\lambda_{n}},

\displaystyle\phi_{\sigma}|_{W_{\mathbb{C}}}

\displaystyle={\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{2\mu_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{2\mu_{n-1}}.

One can easily compute the adjoint
L
-factors, combining Remark
7.3
with
[
Pra92
, Lemma 7.1]
.
The
L
-factors are defined by

\displaystyle L(s,\pi\times\sigma^{\vee})=

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+|\lambda_{i}-\mu_{j}|),

\displaystyle L(s,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+1)^{n}\prod_{i<j}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}),

(B.1)

\displaystyle L(s,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s+1)^{n-1}\prod_{i<j}\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s+\mu_{i}-\mu_{j}),

where
\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(s)=\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma\bigl(\frac{s}{2}\bigl)
and
\Gamma_{\mathbb{C}}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)
.
Suppose that

\lambda_{1}>\mu_{1}>\cdots>\mu_{r-1}>\lambda_{r};\qquad\lambda_{r+1}>\mu_{r}>\cdots>\lambda_{n}>\mu_{n-1}.

Then
\sigma
appears as a subrepresentation of
\pi|_{\mathrm{U}(r-1,s)}
, and

(B.2)
