\displaystyle\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}=

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{n-1}\Gamma\bigl(\frac{1}{2}+|\lambda_{i}-\mu_{j}|\bigl)}{\prod_{i<j}\Gamma(1+\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})\prod_{i<j}\Gamma(1+\mu_{i}-\mu_{j})}

\displaystyle\times 2^{-n}(2\pi)^{\frac{n(n+1)}{2}-2\sum_{j=1}^{s}(\lambda_{r+j}-\mu_{r-1+j})}.

B.4.
The Ichino-Ikeda integral

We let
V=W
and equip the space
W={\mathbb{C}}^{3}
with the Hermitian form on
(\;,\;)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{-1}}\langle\;,\;\rangle_{W}
.
Fix an orthogonal basis
\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{1}
of
V
so that

\displaystyle(\varepsilon,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=(\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{0})=1,

\displaystyle(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{1})

\displaystyle=-1.

Put

\displaystyle V^{\prime}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\varepsilon+{\mathbb{C}}\varepsilon_{1},

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\varepsilon,

\displaystyle L_{0}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\varepsilon_{0},

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\varepsilon_{1},

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle=L+L_{0}.

Define the unitary groups of
V
and
V^{\prime}
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}(V)

\displaystyle=\{g\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(V)\;|\;(gx_{1},gx_{2})=(x_{1},x_{2})\text{ for }x_{1},x_{2}\in V\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\{h\in\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(V^{\prime})\;|\;(hy_{1},hy_{2})=(y_{1},y_{2})\text{ for }y_{1},y_{2}\in V^{\prime}\}.

We view
\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})
as a subgroup of
\mathrm{U}(V)
.
Let

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle=\mathrm{U}(L_{2})\times\mathrm{U}(L_{1}),

\displaystyle K^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{U}(L)\times\mathrm{U}(L_{1})

be maximal compact subgroups of
\mathrm{U}(V)
and
\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})
.
Let

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle=D_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2};\lambda_{3})},

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=D_{(\mu_{1};\mu_{2})}.

The minimal
K
-type of
\pi
is
-(k_{1},k_{2};k_{3}):=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}+1;\lambda_{3}-1)
.
The minimal
K^{\prime}
-type of
\sigma
is
-(k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime}):=\bigl(\mu_{1}+\frac{1}{2};\mu_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\bigl)
.

We identify the space of the minimal
K
-type

{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2};\lambda_{3})}\simeq\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}+1)}\boxtimes{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\lambda_{3}-1}=\rho_{(-k_{1},-k_{2})}\boxtimes{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-k_{3}}{\@index{\@indexphrase{$\rho_{\underline{k}}$}}}

with
{L}_{k_{2}-k_{1}}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Let
v_{\pi}^{(i)}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2};\lambda_{3})}
and
\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(i)}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2};\lambda_{3})}^{\vee}
be the vectors identified with

\displaystyle v_{\pi}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=X^{k_{2}-k_{1}-i}Y^{i},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(j)}

\displaystyle=X^{\prime j}(-Y^{\prime})^{k_{2}-k_{1}-j}.

Note that

\displaystyle\pi(\iota(t_{1},t_{2}))v_{\pi}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=t_{1}^{-k_{1}-i}t_{2}^{-k_{3}}v_{\pi}^{(i)},

\displaystyle\pi^{\vee}(\iota(t_{1},t_{2}))\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=t_{1}^{k_{1}+i}t_{2}^{k_{3}}\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(i)}

for
(t_{1},t_{2})\in K^{\prime}
and
i=0,1,2,\dots,k_{2}-k_{1}
.
Let

{\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}}=(XY^{\prime}-YX^{\prime})^{k_{2}-k_{1}}\in\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}{\@index{\@indexphrase{${\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}}$}}}

be a
K
-invariant vector.

Fix non-zero vectors
v_{\sigma}\in\sigma
and
\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}}\in\sigma^{\vee}
such that for
t_{1},t_{2}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1}

\displaystyle\sigma(t_{1},t_{2})v_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=t_{1}^{-k_{1}^{\prime}}t_{2}^{-k_{2}^{\prime}}v_{\sigma},

\displaystyle\sigma^{\vee}(t_{1},t_{2})\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=t_{1}^{k_{1}^{\prime}}t_{2}^{k_{2}^{\prime}}\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}}.

Let
{}^{\natural}:\pi^{\natural}\simeq\pi^{\vee}
and
{}^{\natural}:\sigma^{\natural}\simeq\sigma^{\vee}
be the equivariant isomorphisms determined by
v_{\pi}^{(0)\natural}=\bar{v}_{\pi^{\vee}}^{(0)}
and
v_{\sigma}^{\natural}=\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}}
.
Fix perfect pairings
\ell_{\pi}:\pi\otimes\pi^{\vee}\to{\mathbb{C}}
and
\ell_{\sigma}:\sigma\otimes\sigma^{\vee}\to{\mathbb{C}}
.
We consider the integral

J(W,W^{\prime})=\int_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})}\ell_{\pi}(h,W\otimes W^{\natural})\cdot\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(h,W^{\prime\natural}\otimes W^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}h,

which converges absolutely for
W\in\pi
and
W^{\prime}\in\sigma
.
Set

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{v}}_{\pi}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=v_{\pi}^{(i)}\otimes\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(i)},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=v_{\sigma}\otimes\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}}

\displaystyle=\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}}\otimes v_{\sigma}.

Proposition B.3
.

If the relation
\lambda_{1}>\mu_{1}>\lambda_{2}\geq\lambda_{3}>\mu_{2}
holds, then

\frac{J(Y_{+}^{k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{3}}v_{\pi}^{(k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{1})},v_{\sigma})}{(2\pi)^{2n_{1}^{*}}\ell_{\underline{k}}({\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}})\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})}=\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2)^{2}\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(4)\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}.

Remark B.4
.

(1)

If the interlacing relation
\lambda_{1}>\mu_{1}>\lambda_{2}>\lambda_{3}>\mu_{2}
holds, then
\sigma
must satisfy
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\geq 2
and hence
k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}\geq 3
.
Proposition
B.3
includes the case
k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}=2
,
and in this case,
\pi
is a limit of discrete series with minimal
K
-type
-(k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime};k_{2}^{\prime})
(cf. Remark
B.5
).

(2)

We consider the additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{-2}(x)=e^{2\pi(x^{c}-x)}
.
Since

\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{2},{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{2\kappa},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{-2}\right)=\begin{cases}-1&\text{if $\kappa>0$, }\\
1&\text{if $\kappa<0$ }\end{cases}

for
\kappa\in\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{Z}}\setminus{\mathbb{Z}}
, if
\lambda_{1}>\mu_{1}>\lambda_{2}\geq\lambda_{3}>\mu_{2}
, then

\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{-2}\right)=\prod_{i,j}\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{2},{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{2(\lambda_{i}-\mu_{j})},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\mathbb{C}}_{-2}\right)=1.

B.5.
Weil representations

We will take the auxiliary additive character
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
defined by
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}(x)=e^{-\sqrt{-1}x}
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
so that the correspondences in §
B.7
hold.
This choice makes our computations simpler.
Given a Hermitian space
(V,(\;,\;))
of signature
(r,s)
and a skew Hermitian space
({H},\langle\;,\;\rangle)
of dimension
m
, we let
{\mathbb{W}}=V\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{H}
be the symplectic space over
{\mathbb{R}}
of dimension
2(r+s)m
equipped with the symplectic form

\ll v_{1}\otimes w_{1},v_{2}\otimes w_{2}\gg=\mathrm{T}_{{\mathbb{C}}/{\mathbb{R}}}((v_{1},v_{2})\langle w_{1},w_{2}\rangle)

for
v_{1},v_{2}\in V
and
w_{1},w_{2}\in{H}
.
Let
Sp({\mathbb{W}})
be the symplectic group of
{\mathbb{W}}
and
\mathrm{Mp}({\mathbb{W}})
the metaplectic
{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
-cover of
Sp({\mathbb{W}})
.

Once we choose characters
\chi_{V},\chi_{H}:{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{1}
such that
\chi_{V}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}}=\mathrm{sgn}^{r+s}
and
\chi_{H}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}}=\mathrm{sgn}^{m}
, the natural homomorphism
\mathrm{U}(V)\times\mathrm{U}({H})\to Sp({\mathbb{W}})
has a lift
\iota_{V,{H},\chi_{V},\chi_{H},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}:\mathrm{U}(V)\times\mathrm{U}({H})\to\mathrm{Mp}({\mathbb{W}})
.
Composing this with the Weil representation
\Omega_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}
of
\mathrm{Mp}({\mathbb{W}})
relative to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}
, we obtain a representation

\Omega_{V,{H},\chi_{V},\chi_{H},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}=\Omega_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}\circ\iota_{V,{H},\chi_{V},\chi_{H},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}

of
\mathrm{U}(V)\times\mathrm{U}({H})
.
When
n
is even, we will take
\chi_{H}=1
and write

\Omega_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}=\Omega_{V,{H},\chi_{V},1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}.

Let
\omega_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}\subset\Omega_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}
denote the Harish-Chandra module.

B.6.
Fock model

Assume that
n
is even and that
\mathrm{U}({H})
is compact.
Let
{P}={P}_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}
be the Fock model of the Weil representation
\omega_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}
of
\mathrm{U}(V)\times\mathrm{U}({H})
relative to the data
(\chi_{V},1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime})
, where
{P}_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}
is the space of polynomials in
(r+s)m
variables
x_{ik},y_{jk}

(1\leq i\leq r,\;1\leq j\leq s,\;1\leq k\leq m)
.

Let
\chi_{V}={\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}}
with
n_{0}\equiv r+s\pmod{2}
.
Then

\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}(h)\varPhi\begin{pmatrix}x\\
y\end{pmatrix}=(\det h)^{(r-s+n_{0})/2}\varPhi\begin{pmatrix}xh\\
y\,^{t}\!h^{-1}\end{pmatrix}

for
h\in\mathrm{U}({H})
,
x\in\mathrm{M}_{r,m}({\mathbb{C}})
and
y\in\mathrm{M}_{s,m}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Let
\epsilon=1
or
\epsilon=-1
according to whether the Hermitian form
\frac{1}{\sqrt{-1}}\langle\;,\;\rangle
is positive or negative definite.
Then for
t=(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathrm{U}(r)\times\mathrm{U}(s)

\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}(t)\varPhi\begin{pmatrix}x\\
y\end{pmatrix}=(\det t_{1})^{\epsilon m/2}(\det t_{2})^{-\epsilon m/2}\varPhi\begin{pmatrix}\,{}^{t}\!t_{1}x\\
t_{2}^{-1}y\end{pmatrix}.

Observe that for
\bar{x}\in\mathrm{M}_{r,m}({\mathbb{C}})
and
\bar{y}\in\mathrm{M}_{s,m}({\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle(\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}})^{\vee}(h)\varPhi^{\prime}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bar{x}\\
\bar{y}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=(\det h)^{(r-s-n_{0})/2}\varPhi^{\prime}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bar{x}\,^{t}\!h^{-1}\\
\bar{y}h\end{bmatrix}\right),

\displaystyle(\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}})^{\vee}(t)\varPhi^{\prime}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bar{x}\\
\bar{y}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=(\det t_{1})^{-\epsilon m/2}(\det t_{2})^{\epsilon m/2}\varPhi^{\prime}\left(\begin{bmatrix}t_{1}^{-1}\bar{x}\\
\,{}^{t}\!t_{2}\bar{y}\end{bmatrix}\right)

(see Lemma 5.2 of
[
KK07
]
).
Define an invariant pairing

\ell_{\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}}:{P}_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}\otimes({P}_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}})^{\vee}\to{\mathbb{C}}

by

\ell_{\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}}(Q\otimes P)=\pi^{-(r+s)m}\int_{\mathrm{M}_{r+s,m}({\mathbb{C}})}Q(z)P(\bar{z})e^{-\mathrm{tr}(z\,^{t}\!\bar{z})}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}\bar{z}.

One can easily see that

\ell_{\omega_{V,{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{0}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}}(Q\otimes P)=\left[Q\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{ik}}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{jk}}\end{pmatrix}P\right]\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},

using polar coordinates.
Following
[
KV78
, p. 38]
, we put

\Delta_{ij}=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{ik}\partial y_{jk}}.

The space of pluriharmonic polynomials is defined by

{\mathfrak{H}}_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}=\{{F}\in{P}_{V,{H}}^{\chi_{V},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}\;|\;\Delta_{ij}{F}=0\;(1\leq i\leq r,\;1\leq j\leq s)\}.

B.7.
Theta correspondence

We write
{\mathbb{H}}
for the Hamilton quaternion algebra over
{\mathbb{R}}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}\cdot{\boldsymbol{j}},

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{j}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-1,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{j}}^{-1}\cdot\sqrt{-1}\cdot{\boldsymbol{j}}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{-1}.

We denote the main involution of
{\mathbb{H}}
also by
\bar{\cdot}
and set
{\boldsymbol{\nu}}(x)=x\bar{x}
for
x\in{\mathbb{H}}
.
Define a skew Hermitian form
\langle\;,\;\rangle
on
{\mathbb{H}}
by letting
\langle x,y\rangle
be the projection of
\sqrt{-1}x\bar{y}
onto the
{\mathbb{C}}
-factor via the decomposition above.
Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})\times{\mathbb{C}}^{1})/\Delta\mu_{2},

where
\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})=\{h\in{\mathbb{H}}\;|\;{\boldsymbol{\nu}}(h)=1\}
, and
\Delta\mu_{2}
is the diagonally embedded copy of
\{\pm 1\}
in
{\mathbb{H}}^{\times}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
.

Let
r=2
,
s=1
and
n_{0}=1
.
Lemma 7.10 of
[
Ich22
]
states that

\displaystyle{P}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{L_{0},{\mathbb{H}}}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{l=0}^{\infty}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{l+1}\boxtimes\rho_{(l+1,1)},

\displaystyle{P}^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{b,c\geq 0}D_{\bigl(b+\frac{1}{2};-c-\frac{1}{2}\bigl)}\boxtimes\rho_{(b,-c)},

\displaystyle{P}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{a\geq a^{\prime}\geq 1}D_{(a+1,a^{\prime};0)}\boxtimes\rho_{(a+1,a^{\prime}+1)}\oplus\bigoplus_{a,d\geq 0}D_{(a+1,0;-d)}\boxtimes\rho_{(a+1,1-d)}.

Remark B.5
.

In the decomposition above the representation
D_{(a+1,0;0)}
, which is a theta lift of
\rho_{(a+1,1)}
, is a limit of discrete series (cf.
[
Pau00
]
).

The space
{P}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}
consists of polynomials in
z=\begin{bmatrix}x_{11}&x_{12}\\
x_{21}&x_{22}\\
y_{1}&y_{2}\end{bmatrix}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{g}},{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{k}},{\mathfrak{k}}^{\prime}
be the Lie algebras of
\mathrm{U}(V),\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime}),K,K^{\prime}
, respectively.
Let

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{p}}_{+}\oplus{\mathfrak{k}}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus{\mathfrak{p}}_{-},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{+}\oplus{\mathfrak{k}}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{-}

be the Harish-Chandra decompositions.
Recall that
\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}({\mathfrak{p}}_{-})
is spanned by

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{11}\partial y_{1}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{12}\partial y_{2}},

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{21}\partial y_{1}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{22}\partial y_{2}}.

We write
{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda^{+};\lambda^{-})}
for the space of minimal
K
-type of
D_{(\lambda^{+};\lambda^{-})}
.
Then

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{H}}^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{b,c\geq 0}{\mathfrak{H}}_{\bigl(b+\frac{1}{2};-c-\frac{1}{2}\bigl)}\boxtimes\rho_{(b,-c)},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{H}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{a\geq a^{\prime}\geq 1}{\mathfrak{H}}_{(a+1,a^{\prime};0)}\boxtimes\rho_{(a+1,a^{\prime}+1)}\oplus\bigoplus_{a,d\geq 0}{\mathfrak{H}}_{(a+1,0;-d)}\boxtimes\rho_{(a+1,1-d)}.

Following (4.14) of
[
KK07
]
, we put

\displaystyle U_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}0&0&0\\
1&0&0\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0\end{array}\right]\in{\mathfrak{k}}_{\mathbb{C}},

\displaystyle Y_{+}

\displaystyle=Y_{2,1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}0&0&0\\
0&0&1\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0\end{array}\right]\in{\mathfrak{p}}_{+},

\displaystyle U_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}0&-1&0\\
0&0&0\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0\end{array}\right]\in{\mathfrak{k}}_{\mathbb{C}},

\displaystyle X_{+}

\displaystyle=X_{1,2}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc|c}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
\hline\cr 0&1&0\end{array}\right]\in{\mathfrak{p}}_{-}.

Proposition 4.4(iii) of
[
KK07
]
shows that

\displaystyle\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}(Y_{+})

\displaystyle=-(x_{21}y_{1}+x_{22}y_{2}),

\displaystyle\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}(U_{2,1})

\displaystyle=x_{21}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{11}}+x_{22}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{12}},

\displaystyle\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V,-{\mathbb{H}}}(X_{+})

\displaystyle=-(\bar{x}_{21}\bar{y}_{1}+\bar{x}_{22}\bar{y}_{2}),

\displaystyle\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V,-{\mathbb{H}}}(U_{1,2})

\displaystyle=\bar{x}_{21}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{x}_{11}}+\bar{x}_{22}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{x}_{12}}.

B.8.
Formal degree

Let
\tau
be an irreducible square-integrable representation of a unitary group
\mathrm{U}({H})
.
Fix a
\mathrm{U}({H})
-invariant pairing
\ell_{\tau}:\tau\otimes\tau^{\vee}\to{\mathbb{C}}
.
Given
{F}_{1}\otimes{F}_{2}\in\tau\otimes\tau^{\vee}
, we have a matrix coefficient

\ell_{\tau}(h,{F}_{1}\otimes{F}_{2})=\ell_{\tau}(\tau(h){F}_{1}\otimes{F}_{2}).

The formal degree
\mathrm{d}(\tau)
of
\tau
is a positive real number characterized by

\int_{\mathrm{U}({H})}\ell_{\tau}(h,{F}_{1}\otimes{F}_{2})\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{F}_{1}^{\prime}\otimes{F}_{2}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}h=\mathrm{d}(\tau)^{-1}\ell_{\tau}({F}_{1}\otimes{F}_{1}^{\prime})\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}({F}_{2}\otimes{F}_{2}^{\prime})

for
{F}_{1}\otimes{F}_{2}\in\tau\otimes\tau^{\vee}
and
{F}_{1}^{\prime}\otimes{F}_{2}^{\prime}\in\tau^{\vee}\otimes\tau
.

When
\mathrm{U}({H})
is compact and
\mathrm{d}h
has total volume 1, we have

\mathrm{d}(\tau)=\dim\tau.

When
\sigma=D_{(\mu_{1};\mu_{2})}
and
\mathrm{d}h
is defined by (
7.4
), we have

\mathrm{d}(\sigma)=(4\pi)^{-1}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})

by Proposition A.7 of
[
KL19
]
and its third remark.

B.9.
Representations of
\mathrm{U}(2)

Fix a commutative integral domain
A
of characteristic zero.
Let
{P}^{\prime}(A)
be the set of polynomials in
\begin{bmatrix}x_{11}&x_{12}\\
y_{1}&y_{2}\end{bmatrix}
.
The decomposition
{P}^{\prime}(A)=\oplus_{b,c\geq 0}{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
holds, where the submodule
{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
of
{P}^{\prime}(A)
consists of homogeneous polynomials of degree
b
in
x_{11},x_{12}
and of degree
c
in
y_{1},y_{2}
.
The group
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(A)
acts on the module
{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
by

\rho(h)P\left(\begin{matrix}x\\
y\end{matrix}\right)=P\left(\begin{matrix}xh\\
y\,^{t}\!h^{-1}\end{matrix}\right).

Define a submodule
{T}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
of
{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
by

{T}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)=(x_{11}y_{1}+x_{12}y_{2}){P}^{\prime}_{b-1,c-1}(A).

Since
{T}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
is stable under the action of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(A)
, the group
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(A)
acts on the quotient module
{\mathfrak{H}}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)={P}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)/{T}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)
.

We define a bilinear form

l_{{P}^{\prime}}:{P}^{\prime}(A)\otimes{P}^{\prime}(A)\to A

by

l_{{P}^{\prime}}(P\otimes Q)=\left[Q\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{1}}&\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{2}}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{11}}&\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{12}}\end{pmatrix}P\right]\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

More explicitly, the bilinear form
l_{{P}^{\prime}}
is given by

l_{{P}^{\prime}}\biggl(\prod_{j=1}^{2}x_{1j}^{n_{j}}y_{j}^{m_{j}}\otimes x_{1j}^{\prime n_{j}^{\prime}}y_{j}^{\prime m_{j}^{\prime}}\biggl)=\begin{cases}\prod_{j=1}^{2}n_{j}!m_{j}!&\text{if $n_{j}=m_{j}^{\prime}$ and $m_{j}=n_{j}^{\prime}$, }\\
0&\text{otherwise. }\end{cases}

Put
{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}(A)=\{P\in{P}_{b,c}^{\prime}(A)\;|\;\Delta^{\prime}P=0\}
, where

\Delta^{\prime}=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{11}\partial y_{1}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{12}\partial y_{2}}.

If
A
is a field, then since

{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}(A)=\{P\in{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}(A)\;|\;l_{{P}^{\prime}}(P\otimes Q)=0\text{ for }Q\in{T}^{\prime}_{c,b}(A)\},

the restriction of
\rho
to
{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}(A)
is the contragredient representation of
{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime}(A)
.
The linear form
l_{{P}^{\prime}}
induces a perfect pairing

l_{{P}^{\prime}}:{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}(A)\otimes{\mathfrak{H}}^{\prime}_{c,b}(A)\to A.

Remark B.6
.

When we view
{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}({\mathbb{C}})
as a representation of
\mathrm{U}(2)
, it is a theta lift of the discrete series of
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
with Harish-Chandra parameter
\bigl(b+\frac{1}{2};c-\frac{1}{2}\bigl)
(see §
B.7
).
Hence
{\mathfrak{H}}^{\prime}_{c,b}({\mathbb{C}})
is an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{C}})
with highest weight
(c,-b)
.

One can apply the same computation as in the proofs of Lemmas A.2 and A.4 of
[
HY25
]
to prove the following lemma.

Lemma B.7
.

(1)

For
P\in{P}^{\prime}(A)
,
Q\in{P}^{\prime}(A)
and
h\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(A)

l_{{P}^{\prime}}(\rho(h)Q\otimes\rho(h)P)=l_{{P}^{\prime}}(Q\otimes P).

(2)

{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{Q}})
is a direct sum of
{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}({\mathbb{Q}})
and
{T}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{Q}})
.

Let
{\mathbf{P}}:{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{Q}})\to{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}({\mathbb{Q}})
be the projection given by Lemma
B.7
(
2
).
Define a homomorphism
\wp:{P}^{\prime}_{c,b}(A)\to{L}_{b+c}(A)
by

\wp(P)=P\begin{pmatrix}X&Y\\
-Y&X\end{pmatrix}.

Since
\wp(\rho(h)P)=(\det h)^{-b}\rho_{b+c}(h)\wp(P)
, it factors though an equivariant morphism
{\mathfrak{H}}^{\prime}_{c,b}(A)\to\rho_{(c,-b)}(A)
and its restriction gives an equivariant morphism
{\mathfrak{H}}_{b,c}^{\prime\vee}(A)\to\rho_{(c,-b)}(A)
.

In view of (
4.1
) we normalize perfect pairings
\ell_{\pi},\ell_{\sigma}
and
\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}
by

(B.3)

\ell_{\pi}({\mathfrak{v}}_{\pi}^{(0)})=\ell_{\sigma}({\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma})=\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=1.

Then

(B.4)

\ell_{\pi}({\mathfrak{v}}_{\pi}^{(i)})=\binom{k_{2}-k_{1}}{i}^{-1}.

Lemma B.8
.

For
P\in{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{Q}})
and
Q\in{P}^{\prime}_{c,b}({\mathbb{Q}})
we have

l_{{P}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{P}}(P)\otimes Q)=(-1)^{c}b!c!\ell_{b+c}(\wp(P)\otimes\wp(Q)).

Proof.

Since both sides give invariant pairings on
\rho_{(b,-c)}\otimes\rho_{(c,-b)}
, they are proportional.
Let
P=x_{11}^{b}y_{2}^{c}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{c,b}^{\prime\vee}({\mathbb{Q}})
and
Q=x_{12}^{c}y_{1}^{b}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{b,c}^{\prime\vee}({\mathbb{Q}})
.
Then

\displaystyle l_{{P}^{\prime}}(P\otimes Q)

\displaystyle=b!c!,

\displaystyle\wp(P)

\displaystyle=X^{b+c},

\displaystyle\wp(Q)

\displaystyle=Y^{\prime c}(-Y)^{\prime b}.

Since
\ell_{b+c}(\wp(P)\otimes\wp(Q))=(-1)^{c}
, the proof is complete.
∎

We define the isomorphism
{}^{\natural}:{P}_{b,c}^{\prime}(A)\to{P}_{c,b}^{\prime}(A)
by

P^{\natural}\begin{pmatrix}x_{11}&x_{12}\\
y_{1}&y_{2}\end{pmatrix}=P\begin{pmatrix}y_{1}^{\prime}&y_{2}^{\prime}\\
x_{11}^{\prime}&x_{12}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

and the action
\rho^{\natural}
by
\rho^{\natural}(h)=\rho(\,^{t}\!h^{-1})
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(A)
.
Then

\displaystyle(\rho^{\natural}(h)P)^{\natural}

\displaystyle=\rho(h)P^{\natural},

\displaystyle\ell_{\omega_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}}(P\otimes Q)

\displaystyle=l_{{P}^{\prime}}(P\otimes Q^{\natural}).

Lemma B.9
.

Let
P,Q\in{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{C}})
.
We view
P
(resp.
Q
) as an element of
{P}^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}
(resp.
({P}^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}})^{\vee}
).
Put
\sigma=D_{\bigl(b+\frac{1}{2};-c-\frac{1}{2}\bigl)}
.
Then for
h\in\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})}\ell_{\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),P\otimes Q)\frac{\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(g,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})}{b!c!}\,\mathrm{d}g=\frac{(-1)^{c}}{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)}\ell_{b+c}(h,\wp(P)\otimes\wp(Q^{\natural})).

Proof.

Define the character
\chi
of
K^{\prime}
by
\chi(t_{1},t_{2})=t_{1}^{b+1}t_{2}^{-c-1}
.
Given a representation
(\varPi,V)
of
\mathrm{U}(1,1)
, we write

V^{\chi}=\{v\in V\;|\;\varPi(k)v=\chi(k)v\text{ for }k\in K^{\prime}\}.

Recall that

{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{C}})={P}^{\prime}({\mathbb{C}})^{\chi}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m}\sigma_{i}^{\chi}\boxtimes\rho_{(b_{i},-c_{i})},

where
\sigma_{i}=D_{\bigl(b_{i}+\frac{1}{2};-c_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\bigl)}
are discrete series of
\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})
such that
\sigma_{i}^{\chi}\neq\{0\}
.
Set
\sigma_{0}=\sigma
.
We write
P=\sum_{i=0}^{m}P_{i}
with
P_{i}\in\sigma_{i}^{\chi}\boxtimes\rho_{(b_{i},-c_{i})}
.
Since
\chi
is the minimal
K
-type of
\sigma_{0}
, we have
P_{0}={\mathbf{P}}(P)
.
Lemma
B.8
and (
B.3
) give

\displaystyle\ell_{\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{\prime}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),P_{0}\otimes Q)

\displaystyle=\ell_{\sigma}(g,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma})l_{{P}^{\prime}}(h,P_{0}\otimes Q^{\natural})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{c}b!c!\ell_{\sigma}(g,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma})\ell_{b+c}(h,\wp(P)\otimes\wp(Q^{\natural})).

The proof is complete by the definition of the formal degree.
∎

B.10.
Reduction to the trilinear form

To prove Proposition
B.3
, we may assume that
\lambda_{2}=0
, replacing
\pi
by
\pi\otimes{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-\lambda_{2}}
and
\sigma
by
\sigma\otimes{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-\lambda_{2}}
.
Recall the interlacing relation:

\lambda_{1}=-k_{1}>\mu_{1}=-k_{1}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}>\lambda_{2}=-k_{2}-1=0\geq\lambda_{3}=1-k_{3}>\mu_{2}=-k_{2}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}.

Equivalently, we rewrite it as

\displaystyle k_{1}\leq k_{1}^{\prime}\leq k_{2}=-1,

\displaystyle 1\leq k_{3}\leq k_{2}^{\prime}.

We include the case
\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{3}
, i.e.,
k_{3}-k_{2}=2
.

Define non-negative integers
n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}
by

\displaystyle n_{1}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{3}-1,

\displaystyle n_{2}

\displaystyle=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}-1,

\displaystyle n_{3}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{3}-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}-1.

Observe that

\displaystyle n_{1}+n_{2}

\displaystyle\geq n_{3},

\displaystyle n_{2}+n_{3}

\displaystyle\geq n_{1},

\displaystyle n_{3}+n_{1}

\displaystyle\geq n_{2}.

Define non-negative integers by
n_{i}^{*}=\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}-n_{i}
.
Note that

\displaystyle n_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{3},

\displaystyle n_{2}^{*}

\displaystyle=k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{1}.

It is worth noting that

\displaystyle n_{1}^{*}+n_{2}^{*}

\displaystyle=n_{3},

\displaystyle n_{2}^{*}+n_{3}^{*}

\displaystyle=n_{1},

\displaystyle n_{3}^{*}+n_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=n_{2}.

Define non-negative integers
b
and
c
by

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=-k_{1}^{\prime}-1,

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=k_{2}^{\prime}-1.

It follows from (
B.2
) that

\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}\\
=4\pi^{5}\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}+2\bigl)\Gamma(n_{1}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{2}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}^{*}+1)\Gamma(-k_{1}^{\prime})\Gamma(k_{2}^{\prime})}{(2\pi)^{2n_{1}^{*}}\Gamma(n_{1}+2)\Gamma(n_{2}+2)\Gamma(1-k_{1})\Gamma(k_{3})}.

Define the representations
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3}
of
\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})
by

\displaystyle\tau_{1}

\displaystyle=\rho_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{3}+1)},

\displaystyle\tau_{2}

\displaystyle=\rho_{(-k_{1}^{\prime}-1,-k_{2}^{\prime}+1)},

\displaystyle\tau_{3}

\displaystyle=\rho_{(n_{3}+1,1)}.

Given an irreducible representation
\tau
of dimension
n+1
, we put

\displaystyle v_{\tau}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=X^{n-i}Y^{i},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{\tau}^{(j)}

\displaystyle=X^{\prime j}(-Y^{\prime})^{n-j},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau}^{(i,j)}

\displaystyle=v_{\tau}^{(i)}\otimes\bar{v}_{\tau^{\vee}}^{(j)}.

Recall that

(B.5)

\ell_{\tau}({\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau}^{(i,j)})=\binom{n}{i}^{-1}\delta_{i,j}

by (
4.1
), where
\delta_{i,j}
denotes the Kronecker’s delta.

We now consider the following seesaw:

The Ichino-Ikeda integral is related to the trilinear form.

Lemma B.10
.

Notations and assumptions being as above, we have

\displaystyle J(Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}v_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})},\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=

\displaystyle\frac{b!c!n_{3}!(n_{1}+1)}{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)(k_{3}-1)!(-k_{1}-1)!}\sum_{i,j=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}(-1)^{i+j}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{j}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})}\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}^{(n_{1},n_{1})})\ell_{\tau_{2}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{2}}^{(i,j)})\ell_{\tau_{3}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{3}}^{(n_{1}^{*}-i,n_{1}^{*}-j)})\,\mathrm{d}h.

Proof.

We write
-{\mathbb{H}}
for the space
{\mathbb{H}}
equipped with the skew Hermitian form
-\langle\;,\;\rangle
.
We employ the idea of
[
Xue23
]
, which uses the theta correspondence.
Let

\displaystyle w_{0}

\displaystyle=x_{11}^{\lambda_{1}-1}y_{2}^{-\lambda_{3}}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},0;\lambda_{3})}\boxtimes\tau_{1}\subset{\mathfrak{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}},

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{0}

\displaystyle=\bar{x}_{11}^{\lambda_{1}-1}\bar{y}_{2}^{-\lambda_{3}}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},0;\lambda_{3})}^{\vee}\boxtimes\tau_{1}^{\vee}\subset{\mathfrak{H}}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,-{\mathbb{H}}}

(cf. Proposition 6.1 of
[
KV78
]
).
Put

\displaystyle w_{n_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-k_{1}^{\prime})!}{(\lambda_{1}-1)!}\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}(U_{2,1})^{n_{2}^{*}}w_{0}=x_{11}^{-k_{1}^{\prime}-1}x_{21}^{n_{2}^{*}}y_{2}^{k_{3}-1}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},0;\lambda_{3})}\boxtimes\tau_{1},

\displaystyle\bar{w}_{n_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-k_{1}^{\prime})!}{(\lambda_{1}-1)!}\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-1},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,-{\mathbb{H}}}(U_{1,2})^{n_{2}^{*}}\bar{w}_{0}=\bar{x}_{11}^{-k_{1}^{\prime}-1}\bar{x}_{21}^{n_{2}^{*}}\bar{y}_{2}^{k_{3}-1}\in{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},0;\lambda_{3})}^{\vee}\boxtimes\tau_{1}^{\vee},

\displaystyle\upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}(Y_{+})^{n_{1}^{*}}w_{n_{2}^{*}}=(-x_{21}y_{1}-x_{22}y_{2})^{n_{1}^{*}}x_{11}^{-k_{1}^{\prime}-1}x_{21}^{n_{2}^{*}}y_{2}^{k_{3}-1}\in\pi\boxtimes\tau_{1},

\displaystyle\bar{\upsilon}_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,-{\mathbb{H}}}(X_{+})^{n_{1}^{*}}\bar{w}_{n_{2}^{*}}=(-\bar{x}_{21}\bar{y}_{1}-\bar{x}_{22}\bar{y}_{2})^{n_{1}^{*}}\bar{x}_{11}^{-k_{1}^{\prime}-1}\bar{x}_{21}^{n_{2}^{*}}\bar{y}_{2}^{k_{3}-1}\in\pi^{\vee}\boxtimes\tau_{1}^{\vee},

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}\otimes\bar{\upsilon}_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}.

Note that
\bar{\upsilon}_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}\in{P}_{b,n_{3},c}({\mathbb{C}})
.
Given
Q\otimes P\in{P}_{b,n_{1}^{*},c}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes{P}_{b,n_{3},c}({\mathbb{C}})
, we set

(Q\otimes P)^{\natural}=Q\otimes P^{\natural}\in{P}_{b,n_{1}^{*},c}({\mathbb{C}})\otimes{P}_{c,n_{3},b}({\mathbb{C}}).

Since
\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}(w_{n_{2}^{*}}\otimes\bar{w}_{n_{2}^{*}})=b!n_{2}^{*}!(k_{3}-1)!
,

\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),w_{n_{2}^{*}}\otimes\bar{w}_{n_{2}^{*}})=b!n_{2}^{*}!(k_{3}-1)!\binom{-k_{1}-1}{n_{2}^{*}}\ell_{\pi}(g,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})})\ell_{\tau_{1}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}}^{(0,0)})

by (
B.4
) and (
B.5
), and hence

\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}})=(k_{3}-1)!(-k_{1}-1)!\ell_{\pi}(g,Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}v_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})}\otimes Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})})\ell_{\tau_{1}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}}^{(0,0)}).

By the definition of the formal degree of
\tau_{1}
, for
g\in\mathrm{U}(V)
we have

\int_{\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})}\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}^{(n_{1},n_{1})})\frac{\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}})}{(k_{3}-1)!(-k_{1}-1)!}\,\mathrm{d}h=\frac{\ell_{\pi}(g,Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}v_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})}\otimes Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}\bar{v}_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})})}{\mathrm{d}(\tau_{1})},

where
\mathrm{d}(\tau_{1})=n_{1}+1
.
We get

\displaystyle(k_{3}-1)!(-k_{1}-1)!\frac{J(Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}v_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})},\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}})}{n_{1}+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})}\int_{\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})}\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}^{(n_{1},n_{1})})\cdot\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}})\,\mathrm{d}h\cdot\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(g,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})\,\mathrm{d}g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})}\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}^{(n_{1},n_{2})})\int_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})}\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}})\cdot\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(g,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})\,\mathrm{d}g\mathrm{d}h,

switching the order of integration.

Recall the orthogonal decomposition
V=V^{\prime}\oplus L_{0}
and

\displaystyle\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}|_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})}

\displaystyle\simeq\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}\otimes\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{L_{0},{\mathbb{H}}},

\displaystyle(\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}})^{\vee}

\displaystyle\simeq\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V^{\prime},-{\mathbb{H}}}.

We write
\upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}\otimes\upsilon^{\prime\prime}_{i}
and
\bar{\upsilon}_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}}=\sum_{j=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{j}\bar{\upsilon}_{j}^{\prime}\otimes\bar{\upsilon}^{\prime\prime}_{j}
with

\displaystyle\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=x_{11}^{b}y_{1}^{i}y_{2}^{c-i}\in{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{C}}),

\displaystyle\upsilon^{\prime\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=x_{21}^{n_{2}^{*}+i}x_{22}^{n_{1}^{*}-i}\in{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{n_{3}+1}\boxtimes\tau_{3}\subset{P}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{L_{0},{\mathbb{H}}},

\displaystyle\bar{\upsilon}_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\bar{x}_{11}^{b}\bar{y}_{1}^{j}\bar{y}_{2}^{c-j}\in{P}^{\prime}_{b,c}({\mathbb{C}}),

\displaystyle\bar{\upsilon}^{\prime\prime}_{j}

\displaystyle=\bar{x}_{21}^{n_{2}^{*}+j}\bar{x}_{22}^{n_{1}^{*}-j}\in{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-n_{3}-1}\boxtimes\tau_{3}^{\vee}\subset{P}^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{L_{0},-{\mathbb{H}}}.

Then
\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*},n_{2}^{*}})
is equal to

\sum_{i,j=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{j}\ell_{\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\upsilon_{i}^{\prime}\otimes\bar{\upsilon}_{j}^{\prime})\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{L_{0},{\mathbb{H}}}}(h,\upsilon^{\prime\prime}_{i}\otimes\bar{\upsilon}^{\prime\prime}_{j})

for
g\in\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})
and
h\in\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})
.
Observe that

\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{L_{0},{\mathbb{H}}}}(h,\upsilon^{\prime\prime}_{i}\otimes\bar{\upsilon}^{\prime\prime}_{j})=n_{3}!\ell_{\tau_{3}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{3}}^{(n_{1}^{*}-i,n_{1}^{*}-j)}).

Since

\displaystyle\wp(\upsilon^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle=X^{b+c-i}(-Y)^{i}=(-1)^{i}v_{\tau_{2}}^{(i)},

\displaystyle\wp(\bar{\upsilon}^{\prime\natural}_{j})

\displaystyle=\wp(y_{1}^{b}x_{11}^{j}x_{12}^{c-j})=(-1)^{b}X^{\prime j}Y^{\prime c-j+b}=(-1)^{c-j}\bar{v}_{\tau_{2}}^{(j)},

Lemma
B.9
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})}\ell_{\omega^{1,{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V^{\prime},{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\upsilon^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\bar{\upsilon}^{\prime}_{j})\frac{\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(g,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})}{b!c!}\,\mathrm{d}g=\frac{(-1)^{i+j}}{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)}\ell_{\tau_{2}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{2}}^{(i,j)}).

We conclude that

\int_{\mathrm{U}(V^{\prime})}\ell_{\omega^{{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}_{V,{\mathbb{H}}}}((g,h),\Upsilon_{n_{1}^{*}})\frac{\ell_{\sigma^{\vee}}(g,\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})}{b!c!n_{3}!}\,\mathrm{d}g\\
=\frac{1}{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)}\sum_{i,j=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}(-1)^{i+j}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{j}\ell_{\tau_{2}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{2}}^{(i,j)})\ell_{\tau_{3}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{3}}^{(n_{1}^{*}-i,n_{1}^{*}-j)})

for
h\in\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})
.
Hence
\frac{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)(k_{3}-1)!(-k_{1}-1)!}{b!c!n_{3}!(n_{1}+1)}J(Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}{\mathfrak{v}}_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})},\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{\sigma^{\vee}})
is the sum of

(-1)^{i+j}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{j}\int_{\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})}\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}^{(n_{1},n_{1})})\ell_{\tau_{2}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{2}}^{(i,j)})\ell_{\tau_{3}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{3}}^{(n_{1}^{*}-i,n_{1}^{*}-j)})\,\mathrm{d}h.

Since the product of the central characters of
\tau_{2}
and
\tau_{3}
coincides with the central character of
\tau_{1}
, we can replace
\mathrm{U}({\mathbb{H}})
by
\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})
.
∎

B.11.
Computation of the trilinear form

Put

\displaystyle{L}_{\underline{n}}(A)

\displaystyle={L}_{n_{1}}(A)\otimes{L}_{n_{2}}(A)\otimes{L}_{n_{3}}(A),

\displaystyle\ell_{\underline{n}}

\displaystyle=\ell_{n_{1}}\otimes\ell_{n_{2}}\otimes\ell_{n_{3}}.

Therefore

\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathbb{H}}^{\times}}(\tau_{1}\otimes\tau_{2}\otimes\tau_{3},{\mathbb{C}})=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})}(\rho_{\underline{n}},{\mathbb{C}})=1.

Define
P_{\underline{n}}\in{L}_{\underline{n}}({\mathbb{C}})
by

P_{\underline{n}}=(X_{1}Y_{2}-X_{2}Y_{1})^{n_{3}^{*}}(X_{3}Y_{1}-X_{1}Y_{3})^{n_{2}^{*}}(X_{2}Y_{3}-X_{3}Y_{2})^{n_{1}^{*}}.

Then
P_{\underline{n}}
is a basis of the line
{L}_{\underline{n}}({\mathbb{C}})
fixed by
\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})
.
2
2

2

There are misprints in (4.9) of
[
Hsi21
]
.

Lemma B.11
.

Notations and assumptions being as above, we have

\int_{\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})}\ell_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{1}^{\vee}}^{(n_{1},n_{1})})\ell_{\tau_{2}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{2}}^{(i,j)})\ell_{\tau_{3}}(h,{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{3}}^{(n_{1}^{*}-i,n_{1}^{*}-j)})\,\mathrm{d}h=\frac{(-1)^{i+j}a_{i}a_{j}}{\ell_{\underline{n}}(P_{\underline{n}}\otimes P_{\underline{n}})},

where
a_{i}=\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}\binom{n_{2}}{i}^{-1}\binom{n_{3}}{n_{1}^{*}-i}^{-1}
.

Proof.

Recall that

{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau^{\vee}_{1}}^{(n_{1},n_{1})}\otimes{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{2}}^{(i,j)}\otimes{\mathfrak{v}}_{\tau_{3}}^{(n_{1}^{*}-i,n_{1}^{*}-j)}\\
=(-1)^{\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}}Y_{1}^{n_{1}}X_{2}^{n_{2}-i}Y_{2}^{i}X_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+i}Y_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-i}\otimes X_{1}^{n_{1}}X_{2}^{j}Y_{2}^{n_{2}-j}X_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-j}Y_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+j}.

Since
{L}_{\underline{n}}({\mathbb{C}})^{\mathrm{SL}({\mathbb{H}})}={\mathbb{C}}P_{\underline{n}}
, the left hand side equals

\displaystyle\frac{\ell_{\underline{n}}(Y_{1}^{n_{1}}X_{2}^{n_{2}-i}Y_{2}^{i}X_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+i}Y_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-i}\otimes P_{\underline{n}})\ell_{\underline{n}}(P_{\underline{n}}\otimes X_{1}^{n_{1}}X_{2}^{j}Y_{2}^{n_{2}-j}X_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-j}Y_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+j})}{(-1)^{\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}}\ell_{\underline{n}}(P_{\underline{n}}\otimes P_{\underline{n}})}

(cf. (4.20) of
[
Hsi21
]
).
Since

\displaystyle P_{\underline{n}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\sum_{l_{2}=0}^{n_{2}^{*}}\sum_{l_{3}=0}^{n_{3}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{l_{1}}\binom{n_{2}^{*}}{l_{2}}\binom{n_{3}^{*}}{l_{3}}(-1)^{n_{1}^{*}+n_{2}^{*}+n_{3}^{*}-l_{1}-l_{2}-l_{3}}

\displaystyle\times X_{1}^{n_{2}^{*}-l_{2}+l_{3}}Y_{1}^{n_{3}^{*}+l_{2}-l_{3}}X_{2}^{n_{3}^{*}+l_{1}-l_{3}}Y_{2}^{n_{1}^{*}-l_{1}+l_{3}}X_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-l_{1}+l_{2}}Y_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+l_{1}-l_{2}},

we have

\displaystyle\ell_{\underline{n}}(Y_{1}^{n_{1}}X_{2}^{n_{2}-i}Y_{2}^{i}X_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+i}Y_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-i}\otimes P_{\underline{n}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}(-1)^{n_{3}+i}}{(-1)^{n_{2}-i}\binom{n_{2}}{i}(-1)^{n_{2}^{*}+i}\binom{n_{3}}{n_{1}^{*}-i}},

\displaystyle\ell_{\underline{n}}(P_{\underline{n}}\otimes X_{1}^{n_{1}}X_{2}^{j}Y_{2}^{n_{2}-j}X_{3}^{n_{1}^{*}-j}Y_{3}^{n_{2}^{*}+j})

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{n_{1}^{*}-j}(-1)^{n_{3}^{*}+j}}{(-1)^{n_{2}-j}\binom{n_{2}}{j}(-1)^{n_{2}^{*}+j}\binom{n_{3}}{n_{1}^{*}-j}},

from which we get the stated formula.
∎

B.12.
Proof of Proposition
B.3

Lemmas
B.10
and
B.11
give

J(Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}v_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})},\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}})=\frac{b!c!n_{3}!(n_{1}+1)\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}a_{i}\Big)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)(k_{3}-1)!(-k_{1}-1)!\ell_{\underline{n}}(P_{\underline{n}}\otimes P_{\underline{n}})}.

For non-negative integers
A
,
B
and
C
, Lemma 5 of
[
DGdO13
]
gives the formula

\sum_{i=0}^{A}\binom{A+B-i}{B}\binom{C+i}{C}=\binom{A+B+C+1}{A}.

This formula applied with
A=n_{1}^{*}
,
B=n_{3}^{*}
and
C=n_{2}^{*}
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}a_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{1}^{*}}{i}^{2}\binom{n_{2}}{i}^{-1}\binom{n_{3}}{n_{1}^{*}-i}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(n_{1}^{*}+1)^{2}\Gamma(n_{2}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}^{*}+1)}{\Gamma(n_{2}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}+1)}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}^{*}}\binom{n_{2}-i}{n_{3}^{*}}\binom{n_{2}^{*}+i}{n_{2}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}+2\bigl)\Gamma(n_{1}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{2}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}^{*}+1)}{\Gamma(n_{1}+2)\Gamma(n_{2}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}+1)}.

Lemma 4.11 of
[
Hsi21
]
gives the formula

\ell_{\underline{n}}(P_{\underline{n}}\otimes P_{\underline{n}})=\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}+2\bigl)\Gamma(n_{1}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{2}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}^{*}+1)}{\Gamma(n_{1}+1)\Gamma(n_{2}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}+1)}.

Put
c_{H}^{\prime}=\frac{\mathrm{d}(\sigma)}{n_{2}+1}
.
Since

(B.6)

\ell_{\underline{k}}({\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}})=\dim{\mathfrak{H}}_{(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2};\lambda_{3})}=-k_{1}

by Lemma A.1 of
[
HY25
]
, we finally get

\displaystyle J(Y_{+}^{n_{1}^{*}}v_{\pi}^{(n_{2}^{*})},\bar{v}_{\sigma^{\vee}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}{2}+2\bigl)\Gamma(n_{1}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{2}^{*}+1)\Gamma(n_{3}^{*}+1)\Gamma(-k_{1}^{\prime})\Gamma(k_{2}^{\prime})}{c_{H}^{\prime}\Gamma(n_{1}+2)\Gamma(n_{2}+2)\Gamma(-k_{1})\Gamma(k_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2\pi)^{2n_{1}^{*}}}{4\pi^{5}c_{H}^{\prime}}\ell_{\underline{k}}({\mathbf{P}}_{\underline{k}})\frac{L\left(\frac{1}{2},\pi\times\sigma^{\vee}\right)}{L(1,\pi,\mathrm{Ad})L(1,\sigma^{\vee},\mathrm{Ad})}.

C.
The Petersson norm of newforms on
\mathrm{U}(1,1)

C.1.
The formula of Lapid and Mao

Recall the maximal compact subgroup
K^{\prime}=\prod_{l}K_{l}^{\prime}
of
H(\widehat{{\mathbb{Q}}})
defined by
K_{l}^{\prime}=\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathfrak{o}}_{E,l})\cap H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
Define the Haar measure of
H({\mathbb{A}})
by
\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h=\mathrm{d}h_{\infty}\prod_{l}\mathrm{d}h_{l}
, where
\mathrm{d}h_{\infty}
is defined in (
7.4
) and where
\mathrm{d}h_{l}
is the Haar measure of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
giving
K^{\prime}_{l}
volume 1.
On the other hand, let
\mathrm{d}^{\tau}h
be the Tamagawa measure on
H({\mathbb{A}})
.
Lemma
7.2
gives

\mathrm{d}_{K^{\prime}}h=2^{-2}D_{E}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)\,\mathrm{d}^{\tau}\!h.

We normalize the Petersson norm of
\varphi\in{\mathscr{A}}^{0}(H)
by

\|\varphi\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}}=2^{-2}D_{E}L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)\,\|\varphi\|^{2}.

Let
\sigma\simeq\otimes_{v}^{\prime}\sigma_{v}
be an irreducible
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
-generic unitary cuspidal automorphic representation of
H({\mathbb{A}})
generated by a cusp form in
S_{\underline{k}^{\prime}}^{H}(p^{\ell}{\mathfrak{N}}^{\prime},\chi^{\prime};{\mathbb{C}})
.
Then
\sigma
is tempered by the Ramanujan–Petersson conjecture in view of Remark
5.2
.

Fix a perfect Hermitian paring
{B}_{v}:\sigma_{v}\times\sigma_{v}\to{\mathbb{C}}
for each place
v
of
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
We define the local Whittaker period
I_{v}:\sigma_{v}\times\sigma_{v}\to{\mathbb{C}}
by

I_{v}(W_{v},W_{v}^{\prime})=\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}^{\rm st}{B}_{v}(\sigma_{v}({\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z_{v}))W_{v},W_{v}^{\prime}){\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{v}(z_{v})^{-1}\mathrm{d}z_{v}

Define the normalized local period by

I^{\sharp}_{v}(W_{v})=\frac{L(1,\sigma_{v},\mathrm{Ad})}{L(1,\epsilon_{E_{v}/{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}})\zeta_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{v}}(2)}I_{v}(W_{v},W_{v}).

The following result is a special case of the conjecture of Lapid and Mao, studied in §6.2 of
[
LM15
]
.

Theorem C.1

(Lapid-Mao)
.

Notation being as above, if
\varphi=\otimes_{v}W_{v}\in\sigma
is not zero, then

\frac{|W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\varphi)|^{2}}{\|\varphi\|^{2}}=2^{-1-\varkappa_{\sigma}}\frac{L(1,\epsilon_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}})\zeta_{\mathbb{Q}}(2)}{L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{v}\frac{I^{\sharp}_{v}(W_{v})}{{B}_{v}(W_{v},W_{v})},

where
\varkappa_{\sigma}=0
if the functorial lift of
\sigma
to
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{E}})
is cuspidal and
\varkappa_{\sigma}=1
otherwise.

Let
\varphi_{\sigma}\in\sigma
be the normalized newform associated to
\sigma
defined in Definition
5.10
.
Let
l
a split prime.
We denote by
{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}}(\sigma_{l})
the Whittaker model of
\sigma_{l}
with respect to
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}
, by
W_{\sigma_{l}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}}(\sigma_{l})
the normalized essential Whittaker vector (see Definition
8.2
) and by
\langle\;,\;\rangle_{l}^{\prime}:{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}}(\sigma_{l})\times{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{l}^{-1}}(\sigma_{l}^{\vee})\to{\mathbb{C}}
the pairing defined in (
8.3
).
The quantity
{B}_{\sigma_{l}}
is defined in (
7.9
).

Corollary C.2
.

Notation being as above, we have

\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{K^{\prime}}=2^{-2+\varkappa_{\sigma}+k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{2}^{\prime}}D_{E}L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})\prod_{l|N_{\sigma}}{B}_{\sigma_{l}}.

Remark C.3
.

Though
\varkappa_{\sigma}=0
by (splt), we include the factor
2^{\varkappa_{\sigma}}
.

Proof of Corollary
C.2

Lemma C.4
.

If
q
does not split in
E
, then
I_{q}^{\sharp}(W_{\sigma_{q}})={B}_{q}(W_{\sigma_{q}},W_{\sigma_{q}})
.

Proof.

Write
E=E_{q}
,
F={\mathbb{Q}}_{q}
,
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}={\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{q}
and
\sigma=\sigma_{q}
for simplicity.
Then
\sigma
is the unramified component of an unramified principal series
I^{\prime}(\chi)
for some unramified character
\chi
of
E^{\times}
(see §
5.2
).
Define the Whittaker functional
J_{\boldsymbol{\psi}}:I^{\prime}(\chi)\to{\mathbb{C}}
by

J_{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(f)=\int_{F}f(w{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z)){\boldsymbol{\psi}}(z)^{-1}\mathrm{d}z.

We also define the invariant pairing
{B}^{\prime}:I^{\prime}(\chi)\times I^{\prime}(\chi^{-1})\to{\mathbb{C}}
by

{B}^{\prime}(f,f^{\prime})=\int_{F}f(w{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z))f^{\prime}(w{\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z))\,\mathrm{d}z.

By uniqueness of Whittaker functionals we have

\frac{I^{\sharp}(f,f^{\prime})}{{B}(f,f^{\prime})}=\frac{L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})}{L(1,\epsilon_{E/F})\zeta_{F}(2)}\cdot\frac{J_{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(f)J_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{-1}}(f^{\prime})}{{B}^{\prime}(f,f^{\prime})}

for
f\in I^{\prime}(\chi)
and
f^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}(\chi^{-1})
, where

L(s,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})=\zeta_{F}(s)L(s,\epsilon_{E/F})L(s,\chi|_{F^{\times}})L(s,\chi^{-1}|_{F^{\times}}).

If
f
and
f^{\prime}
are both spherical elements in
I^{\prime}(\chi)
and
I^{\prime}(\chi^{-1})
with
f(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})=f^{\prime}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})=1
, then
J_{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(f)=1-\chi(q)|q|_{E}
and
{B}^{\prime}(f,f^{\prime})=\frac{\zeta_{F}(1)}{\zeta_{F}(2)}
.
∎

Next we consider the split case.

Lemma C.5
.

If
l
is split in
E
, then
I_{l}^{\sharp}(W_{\sigma_{l}})={B}_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}{B}_{l}(W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}})
.

Proof.

In this case
\sigma_{l}
is a unitary generic irreducible representation of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{Q}}_{l})
.
Define
{B}_{l}
by

{B}_{l}(W,W^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\zeta_{l}(1)}\int_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{l}^{\times}}W\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}t_{l}&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\overline{W^{\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}t_{l}&\\
&1\end{bmatrix}\biggl)}\,\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{l}

for
W,W^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\sigma_{l})
.
Then Lemma 4.4 of
[
LM15
]
gives

I_{l}(W,W^{\prime})=W(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2})W^{\prime}(\hbox{\bf 1}_{2}).

Note that

{B}_{l}(W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}})=\frac{1}{\zeta_{l}(1)}\langle W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}\rangle_{l}^{\prime}.

It follows that

I^{\sharp}(W_{\sigma_{l}})=\frac{L(1,\sigma_{l},\mathrm{Ad})}{\zeta_{l}(1)\zeta_{l}(2)}\cdot\frac{I_{l}(W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}})}{{B}_{l}(W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}})}=\frac{L^{\mathrm{GL}}(1,\sigma_{l}\times\sigma_{l}^{\vee})}{\zeta_{l}(2)\langle W_{\sigma_{l}},W_{\sigma_{l}^{\vee}}\rangle_{l}^{\prime}}={B}_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1}

(cf. Remark
7.3
).
∎

Finally, we consider the archimedean case.

Lemma C.6
.

I^{\sharp}_{\infty}(W_{\sigma_{\infty}})=2^{1+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}e^{-4\pi}{B}_{\infty}(W_{\sigma_{\infty}},W_{\sigma_{\infty}})
.

Proof.

Put
k=k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}
and
w=-(k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime})
.
The normalized Whittaker function
W_{\sigma_{\infty}}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}}(\sigma_{\infty})
of minimal
K
-type
-\underline{k}^{\prime}
is given by

W_{\sigma_{\infty}}\left({\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z){\mathbf{d}}^{\prime}(t)\begin{bmatrix}\cos\theta_{1}&\sin\theta_{1}\\
-\sin\theta_{1}&\cos\theta_{1}\end{bmatrix}e^{\sqrt{-1}\theta_{2}}\right)={\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}(z)t^{k}e^{-2t^{2}\pi}e^{\sqrt{-1}(k\theta_{1}+w\theta_{2})}

for
z,\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
t\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}_{+}
(cf.
[
Hsi21
, (2.10)]
).
Define
{B}_{\infty}
by

{B}_{\infty}(W,W^{\prime})=2\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}_{+}}W\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}t_{\infty}&\\
&t_{\infty}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\biggl)\overline{W^{\prime}\biggl(\begin{bmatrix}t_{\infty}&\\
&t_{\infty}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}\biggl)}\,\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{\infty}

for
W,W^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{W}}_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}}(\sigma_{\infty})
.
Then

\displaystyle{B}_{\infty}(\sigma_{\infty}({\mathbf{n}}^{\prime}(z_{\infty}))W_{\sigma_{\infty}},W_{\sigma_{\infty}})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}_{+}}t_{\infty}^{k}e^{-4\pi t_{\infty}}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}_{\infty}(t_{\infty}z_{\infty})\,\mathrm{d}^{\times}t_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Gamma(k)(2\pi(2-\sqrt{-1}z_{\infty}))^{-k}

and

I_{\infty}(W_{\sigma_{\infty}},W_{\sigma_{\infty}})=(-2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{-k}\Gamma(k)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}z_{\infty}}}{(z_{\infty}+2\sqrt{-1})^{k}}\,\mathrm{d}z_{\infty}=e^{-4\pi}.

We have

\frac{I^{\sharp}_{\infty}(W_{\sigma_{\infty}})}{{B}_{\infty}(W_{\sigma_{\infty}},W_{\sigma_{\infty}})}=\frac{L(1,\sigma_{\infty},\mathrm{Ad})}{\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(1)\Gamma_{\mathbb{R}}(2)}\cdot\frac{e^{-4\pi}}{(4\pi)^{-k}\Gamma(k)}=2^{1+k}e^{-4\pi}

by (
B.1
).
∎

We are now ready to prove Corollary
C.2
.
Theorem
C.1
gives

\frac{|W_{{\boldsymbol{\psi}}}(\varphi_{\sigma})|^{2}}{\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}^{2}}=\frac{2^{1-\varkappa_{\sigma}}}{D_{E}L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})}\prod_{v}\frac{I^{\sharp}_{v}(W_{\sigma_{v}})}{{B}_{v}(W_{\sigma_{v}},W_{\sigma_{v}})}.

This combined with Lemmas
C.4
,
C.5
and
C.6
yield

\frac{e^{-4\pi}}{\|\varphi_{\sigma}\|_{K^{\prime}}^{2}}=\frac{2^{1-\varkappa_{\sigma}}}{D_{E}L(1,\sigma,\mathrm{Ad})}2^{1+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}^{\prime}}e^{-4\pi}\prod_{l|N_{\sigma}}{B}_{\sigma_{l}}^{-1},

which complete our proof of Corollary
C.2
.

D.
Comparison of periods

Let
\Pi
and
\Pi^{\prime}
be cohomological conjugate self-dual cuspidal automorphic representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{3}({\mathbb{E}})
and
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{E}})
.
In this appendix we explicate the periods introduced in
[
GHL
]
for
\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime}
and make a comparison with the period in Theorem
7.7
when
\Pi_{\infty}
and
\Pi^{\prime}_{\infty}
satisfy the intertwining relation in Remark
B.4
.
For
a,b\in{\mathbb{C}}
we write
a\sim b
if
b\neq 0
and
a/b\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
.
We write

L(s,\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime})=L(s,\Pi_{\infty}\times\Pi^{\prime}_{\infty})L_{\rm fin}(s,\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime})

for the Rankin-Selberg
L
-function defined in
[
JPSS83
]
.

Let
\chi
be a Hecke character with conductor
{\mathfrak{f}}
and infinity type
(k,-k)
, where
k
is a non-zero integer.
In other words
\chi((\alpha))=(\alpha/\alpha^{c})^{k}
if
\alpha\equiv 1\pmod{{\mathfrak{f}}}
.
The Hecke
L
-series associated to
\chi
is defined by

L_{\rm fin}(s,\chi)=\sum_{({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{f}})=1}\frac{\chi({\mathfrak{a}})}{N({\mathfrak{a}})^{s}},

where the sum is over integral ideals of
{\mathfrak{o}}_{E}
prime to
{\mathfrak{f}}
.
This series is absolutely convergent if
\Re s>1
.
The Hecke
L
-series is known to have a meromorphic continuation to the whole complex
s
-plane.

We fix embeddings
\iota=\iota_{\infty}:E\hookrightarrow\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
\Omega_{\infty}
be the CM period defined in §
6.7
.
Let
p(\chi,\iota)
be the period associated to
\chi
in
[
GHL
, §2.1.1]
.
Theorem 2.3 of
[
GHL
]
says that if
k>0
, then

p(\widecheck{\chi},\iota^{c})\sim L_{\rm fin}(0,\chi),

where
\widecheck{\chi}:=(\chi^{c})^{-1}
.
If
k<0
, then

p(\widecheck{\chi},\iota)\sim p(\widecheck{\chi}^{c},\iota^{c})\sim L_{\rm fin}(0,\chi^{c}).

By Damerell’s theorem
[
Dam70
,
Dam71
]
, if
k>0
, then

L_{\rm fin}(0,\chi)\sim\pi^{-k}\Omega_{\infty}^{2k}

(cf.
[
BK10
, Corollary 2.12]
).
In any case we have

p(\widecheck{\chi},\iota)\sim\pi^{k}\Omega_{\infty}^{-2k}.

We denote the central characters of
\Pi
and
\Pi^{\prime}
by
\omega_{\Pi}
and
\omega_{\Pi^{\prime}}
.
Under certain assumptions, Theorem 2.6 of
[
GHL
]
associates to
\Pi
local arithmetic automorphic periods
\{P^{(i)}(\Pi,\iota)\}_{i=0,1,2,3}
such that

\displaystyle P^{(0)}(\Pi,\iota)

\displaystyle\sim p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi},\iota^{c}),

\displaystyle P^{(3)}(\Pi,\iota)

\displaystyle\sim p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi},\iota)

and such that

L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})\sim(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{6}P^{(0)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(1)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(2)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(3)}(\Pi,\iota)

by Theorem 5.14 of
[
GHL
]
.
Similarly, there are periods
\{P^{(i)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)\}_{i=0,1,2}
such that

\displaystyle P^{(0)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)

\displaystyle\sim p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi^{\prime}},\iota^{c}),\qquad\quad P^{(2)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)\sim p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi^{\prime}},\iota),

\displaystyle L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})

\displaystyle\sim(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}P^{(0)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)P^{(1)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)P^{(2)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota).

Put
G=\mathrm{U}(2,1)
.
Let
\pi(0)
be a square-integrable automorphic representations of
G({\mathbb{A}})
whose functorial lift is
\Pi
and whose archimedean part
\pi_{\lambda,0}
is holomorphic (see Lemma 4.9 of
[
GHL
]
).
Let
Q(\pi(0))
be the automorphic
Q
-period defined in
[
GHL
, §4.3]
.
By definition
Q(\pi(0))
is the Petersson norm of a
deRham rational
automorphic form in
\pi(0)
.
Theorem 6.3 and Lemma 2.2(c) of
[
GHL
]
give

P^{(1)}(\Pi,\iota)=P^{(0)}(\Pi,\iota)Q(\pi(0))p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi},\iota)=Q(\pi(0)).

It follows that

\displaystyle P^{(0)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(2)}(\Pi,\iota)

\displaystyle\sim\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})}{\pi^{6}P^{(1)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(3)}(\Pi,\iota)}\sim\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})}{\pi^{6}Q(\pi(0))p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi},\iota)},

\displaystyle P^{(0)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)P^{(1)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)^{2}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}{\pi^{6}P^{(0)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)P^{(2)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)^{2}}\sim\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}{\pi^{6}p(\widecheck{\omega}_{\Pi^{\prime}},\iota)}.

Let

(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})=(-k_{1},-k_{2}-1,1-k_{3});\quad(b_{1},b_{2})=\left(k_{2}^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2},k_{1}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}\right)

be the Langlands parameters of
\Pi_{\infty}
and
\Pi^{\prime}_{\infty}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\omega_{\Pi,\infty}(z/z^{c})

\displaystyle=(z/z^{c})^{-(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})},

\displaystyle\omega_{\Pi^{\prime},\infty}(z/z^{c})

\displaystyle=(z/z^{c})^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}}

for
z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
.
The interlacing relation is

a_{1}>-b_{2}>a_{2}>a_{3}>-b_{1}.

The split indices in
[
GHL
, Definition 2.12]
are given by

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(0,\Pi;\Pi^{\prime},\iota)={\rm sp}(2,\Pi;\Pi^{\prime},\iota)=1,\qquad{\rm sp}(1,\Pi;\Pi^{\prime},\iota)={\rm sp}(3,\Pi;\Pi^{\prime},\iota)=0;

\displaystyle{\rm sp}(0,\Pi^{\prime};\Pi,\iota)=1,\qquad{\rm sp}(1,\Pi^{\prime};\Pi,\iota)=2,\qquad{\rm sp}(2,\Pi^{\prime};\Pi,\iota)=0.

Theorem 5.19 of
[
GHL
]
gives

L_{\rm fin}\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime}\biggl)\sim(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{3}P^{(0)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(2)}(\Pi,\iota)P^{(0)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)P^{(1)}(\Pi^{\prime},\iota)^{2}.

We conclude that

\displaystyle L_{\rm fin}\biggl(\frac{1}{2},\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime}\biggl)

\displaystyle\sim\pi^{3}\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})}{\pi^{6}Q(\pi(0))p(\omega^{\vee}_{\Pi},\iota)}\cdot\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}{\pi^{6}p(\omega_{\Pi^{\prime}}^{\vee},\iota)}

\displaystyle\sim\pi^{-9}\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})}{(\pi^{-1}\Omega_{\infty}^{2})^{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}}Q(\pi(0))}\cdot\frac{L_{\rm fin}(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}{(\pi^{-1}\Omega_{\infty}^{2})^{-k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{2}^{\prime}}}.

By (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) we have

\frac{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\Pi_{\infty}\times\Pi^{\prime}_{\infty}\bigl)}{L(1,\Pi_{\infty},\mathrm{As}^{-})L(1,\Pi_{\infty}^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}\sim\pi^{6-2(a_{3}+b_{1})}\pi^{3+b_{1}-b_{2}}=\pi^{7+2k_{3}-k_{1}^{\prime}-k_{2}^{\prime}}.

Putting these together, we find that

\displaystyle\frac{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime}\bigl)}{L(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})L(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}

\displaystyle\sim\pi^{2k_{3}-2-(k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime})}\frac{(\pi^{-1}\Omega_{\infty}^{2})^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}}{Q(\pi(0))}

\displaystyle=\pi^{-2+k_{3}-k_{1}-k_{2}}\frac{(\pi^{-2}\Omega_{\infty}^{2})^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}}{Q(\pi(0))}.

Suppose that
\pi(0)
is cuspidal and that
\pi(0)
and
\Pi^{\prime}
are unramified at all non-split primes.
Let
f^{\circ}\in S^{G}_{\underline{k}}({\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi(0)},\chi,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
be a
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
-rational newform associated to
\pi(0)
.
Theorem
7.7
, Corollary
C.2
, (
7.12
) and Remark
7.3
give

\frac{L\bigl(\frac{1}{2},\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime}\bigl)}{L(1,\Pi,\mathrm{As}^{-})L(1,\Pi^{\prime},\mathrm{As})^{2}}\sim\frac{(\pi^{-2}\Omega_{\infty}^{2})^{k_{1}^{\prime}+k_{2}^{\prime}-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}}}{(f^{\circ},f^{\circ})_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi(0)}}}.

We therefore see that

\pi^{k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3}}Q(\pi(0))\sim\pi^{-2}(f^{\circ},f^{\circ})_{{\mathfrak{N}}_{\pi(0)}}\sim\langle f^{\circ},f^{\circ}\rangle,

where
\langle f^{\circ},f^{\circ}\rangle
is the pairing defined by replacing the classical measure by the Tamagawa measure (see Lemma
7.2
).

Remark D.1
.

Let
\sigma
be a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathbb{A}})
whose archimedean component
\sigma_{\infty}
is a discrete series with extremal weight
\pm k
.
Let
g^{\circ}
be the normalized newform associated to
\sigma
.
Then
\pi^{k/2}g^{\circ}
is deRham rational in view of
[
Har90
, (1.6.5)]
.
We expect that
\pi^{(k_{3}-k_{1}-k_{2})/2}f^{\circ}
is deRham rational.
